# GreenKube data-quality audit

> **Status:** in progress. **Baseline:** `dev` @ `43eb25f1`. **Started:** 2026-11-15.
> **Audience:** the maintainer and the developers who will fix the findings. Each finding is written as a specification: mechanism, effect on the numbers, evidence, fix and acceptance tests.

## 1. Scope and method

The audit asks one question: can the numbers GreenKube collects, stores, derives and shows be trusted, on-premises and in the cloud, over months of operation?

Each dimension was audited by an independent reviewer who read the code path end to end, ran the real code against fakes or a temporary SQLite database, and compared stored rows with Prometheus and OpenCost on live clusters:

- **Minikube** (1 node, cri-dockerd): an end-to-end install with a 30 s step, and a soak install with production defaults (5 min step, 24 h backfill) and hourly compression after 1 h.
- **kind** (3 nodes, containerd, Cilium): an end-to-end install with a 30 s step.

**Confidence labels.** `confirmed-live`: seen in live data. `confirmed-sim`: reproduced by running the real code. `confirmed-code`: established by reading the code path. `likely` / `plausible`: needs a check before fixing.

**Limits of this revision.**
- Findings have not yet been re-checked by a second reviewer. Treat each fix as test-first: the regression test must fail on `dev` before the fix.
- Several findings describe the same root cause seen from different angles. Section 3 groups them; fix the group, not each finding separately.
- Long-run evidence comes from single-node Minikube only, and the host slept during the night, so overnight data is sparse.
- Evidence scripts and raw outputs cited as `audit-workspace/...` are kept outside this repository, in the maintainer's audit workspace.

### Dimensions

| Dimension | State |
|---|---|
| Usage collection | audited |
| Cost | audited |
| Energy and carbon | audited |
| History reconstruction (experiment) | audited |
| Storage write path | audited |
| Rollups, compression, retention | audited |
| Change measurement and verdicts | audited |
| Recommendation engine | audited (gates and lifecycle code only partly read) |
| Impact projection | audited (fixed-node clusters with OpenCost only; no autoscaler cluster) |
| Savings ledger | audited (ledgers empty live; PostgreSQL paths read, not run) |
| API read path | audited (raw/hourly seam tested in simulation only) |
| Sources and consensus | planned |
| GitOps delivery | planned |
| Long-term reliability and operations | planned |
| Frontend, security, architecture, tests, docs, portability, GitOps planning | set aside for a later pass |

## 2. Verdict so far

**The numbers cannot be trusted yet.** The collection design (half-open windows, watermarks, idempotent upserts, provenance columns, reservation-aware allocation) is sound, and several earlier defects are really fixed. But stored history is degraded in ways the product does not record or show, and the core promise (a decided verdict on a merged change) has not been observed.

What was measured directly and holds:

- **Hourly compression conserves sums.** On the soak PostgreSQL database, cost, energy, CO2e and embodied CO2e of the hourly table equal the raw sums for all 14 compressed hours, one hourly row per pod. Not covered by this check: container rows, averaged fields, late rows.
- **A controller outage inside the backfill horizon is recovered.** The soak controller was stopped for 50 minutes; after restart every 5-minute step of the outage was present.
- **No step is skipped or duplicated** in normal operation (237 distinct timestamps over 237 steps on the end-to-end install).

## 3. Blocking points

These groups must be fixed before any number is published as reliable. IDs refer to section 5.

| # | Blocking point | Effect on numbers | Findings |
|---|---|---|---|
| B1 | **History is built from the Kubernetes state at collection time.** Requests, owner, zone, PUE and embodied profile come from the live API, not from the time of the sample. | Rows of deleted pods get request 0 and no owner (30 to 50 % of rows in a 24 h backfill on the soak install); up to 73 % less energy for those rows; CO2e up to 19 times off for removed nodes; a re-collection overwrites good rows with degraded ones. | COL-01, RECON-01, COST-07, NRG-10, NRG-11, RECON-05, RECON-06, COL-07, MEA-12, STO-01 |
| B2 | **Holes and dropped rows are invisible.** Windows beyond the backfill horizon, windows where Prometheus returns nothing, pods without a CPU sample and node-steps whose intensity lookup fails are all committed as collected. | Totals over a period with holes are silently low; nothing in a table, the API or the UI says so. | COL-04, RECON-04, COL-03, COL-08, RECON-12, NRG-08, API-08, API-13 |
| B3 | **The watermark key can change.** The cluster key falls back to the node name when the node list fails at start. | Every controller reschedule can fork the watermark and re-collect the whole horizon over existing rows (which triggers B1). | COL-02, COST-04, RECON-10, LED-02 |
| B4 | **Cost degrades silently.** An OpenCost outage switches the window to default rates and it is never re-priced; zero-duration allocations are stored as trusted zeros; cost depends on how the window was chunked. | +10.8 % in simulation for an outage window; partial-lifetime pods over-costed by 1.17x or more in catch-up windows. | COST-01, RECON-09, COST-05, RECON-11, COL-10, RECON-08 |
| B5 | **Container-level data is wrong.** Pod cost is split between containers by CPU usage, not reservation; on cri-dockerd, container rows are Metrics API snapshots labelled `prometheus` and biased low; one missing container sample removes all container rows of the pod. | Rightsizing evidence and projection unit prices are wrong for multi-container pods; container CPU -8.8 % on Minikube. | COST-02, COL-05, RECON-07, COL-17, IMP-02 |
| B6 | **Embodied carbon is a constant.** The Boavizta response model does not match the real API, so every node gets the 100 kg default, unflagged. | Embodied CO2e is wrong for every node on every provider. | NRG-01, NRG-07 |
| B7 | **Totals exclude the idle share of nodes.** Idle rows are written but never read, never rolled up, and deleted at raw retention. | Cluster cost, energy and CO2e shown by the API and UI are lower than the cluster's modelled footprint. Live: the Minikube dashboard shows 54 % of the modelled cluster cost; on kind 4.5 %, but that figure is inflated because the three kind nodes share one host and each is charged its full capacity (NRG-15). | NRG-02, COST-13, RECON-13, NRG-09, API-04 |
| B8 | **Carbon intensity falls back to defaults too often.** The hourly job never fetches the default zone; the bundled defaults table is empty for 251 of 349 zones. | On-prem clusters never get live intensity even with a token; 19 of 51 mapped cloud zones use 500 gCO2e/kWh. | NRG-03, NRG-04 |
| B9 | **Energy, CO2e and cost are stored as 4-byte floats in PostgreSQL.** | Sums drift by percents beyond about 1 M rows and stop growing beyond about 17 M rows. | NRG-06 |
| B9b | **PostgreSQL identifiers and time zone.** Row ids are 4-byte serials and every upsert consumes one; time buckets use the session time zone, which is never pinned to UTC. | Extrapolated, not measured: at 20,000 pods the id sequence is exhausted after about one year. On a database not in UTC, daily, monthly and yearly figures are shifted and mislabelled. | STO-05, STO-10, ROL-08 |
| B10 | **The raw/hourly boundary loses data.** Rows written into an already compressed hour are never re-aggregated; readers use `now - compression age` instead of the compression watermark. | Up to about 2 hours missing from summaries and time series; late or repaired rows never reach the API. | RECON-02, RECON-03, ROL-01, ROL-02, STO-02, API-03 |
| B11 | **Projections do not use the model that measures.** The projector assumes 1 W / 10 W per core where collection uses per-vCPU coefficients; PV and LoadBalancer costs never come from OpenCost. | Projected and measured savings cannot agree; orphan-resource savings always use flat defaults. | NRG-05, COST-03 |
| B12 | **No change has ever reached a decided verdict, and the stated reason is misleading.** All measured changes on both end-to-end installs ended `inconclusive` with "no trusted allocated cost in the baseline window". OpenCost cost was in fact trusted: the accelerated windows cannot match an hour of the week and the only baseline hour had under 50 % coverage (0.49 on Minikube, 0.23 on kind). | The proof step and the ledger are unexercised (0 ledger entries); the accelerated quickstart cannot produce a verdict by design, and the user is told the wrong cause. | MEA-10, MEA-11 |
| B12b | **On-premises without OpenCost or Kubecost, no cost verdict is possible at all.** Rows priced with configured rates (`custom_rate`) are never trusted by measurement. Two verification systems with different rules also write the same `verified` / `not_realized` labels. | Every on-prem install without OpenCost ends every cost measurement `inconclusive`; the ledger stays empty by construction. | MEA-06, MEA-16 |
| B13 | **The verdict rules and statistics are not valid.** One baseline week is compared with one post week and the bootstrap only sees within-week noise; `realized` needs only an interval above zero, with no noise floor; health gates have no baseline and zero restart tolerance over 7 days. | With a true saving of zero, 11.5 to 50 % of simulated changes are declared `realized` against a nominal 5 %; one unrelated restart yields a false `rollback_review`. | MEA-01, MEA-09, MEA-07 |
| B14 | **Unrelated events are booked as savings or end a measurement.** Price and grid-intensity moves between the two windows count as savings; any pod-template change (an image deployment) contaminates a running measurement and marks a realized change "regressed". | A 30 % price drop inflates the saving by 30 %; a lower-intensity week inflates CO2e 2.3 times; accrual stops at the next release. | MEA-02, MEA-03, MEA-08 |
| B15 | **Hourly rollups distort what is built on them.** Hours where pods were replaced overstate cost and replicas; one untrusted sample makes the whole hour untrusted; lookback percentiles are percentiles of hourly percentiles; `greenkube recompute` leaves hourly tables on the old model. | Measurements change once their window is compressed (18.0 per hour instead of 12.0; 2 to 6 times in the worst case); 24 % (kind) and 40 % (Minikube) of live pod-hours would become untrusted; a true p95 of 100 m reported as 900 m. Based on simulation and emulated rollup rows, not a live compressor run. | ROL-03, ROL-04, ROL-06, MEA-04, MEA-05 |
| B16 | **Recommendations can be unsafe, stale or permanently blocked.** Rightsizing fails open when OOM and restart signals are unknown; a decrease is not checked against the increase trigger; OOM kills are counted over the whole lookback whatever the limit was; once a container's rightsizing is `applied`, later recommendations for it are dropped; recommendations outlive deleted workloads. | A crash-looping container can receive CPU and memory decreases; workloads with occasional peaks oscillate (1000m, 307m, 480m, 307m) and memory decreases land below the observed peak; a fixed OOM workload keeps getting increases (64 to 107 Mi proposed after 12 h without restart); an OOM increase after a GreenKube decrease is never proposed. | REC-01 (confirmed-sim), REC-02 (confirmed-live), REC-03 (confirmed-sim), REC-04 (confirmed-sim), REC-05 (confirmed-live) |
| B17 | **The ledger does not yet account correctly, and what users see is not the ledger.** Projected savings accrue on expected-billable cost, measured savings on allocated cost; projected accruals before a `not_realized` or `inconclusive` verdict stay forever and a measured loss is never booked; the savings report, UI and Prometheus counters read the old mutable table, not ledger v2; the hash chain has no key or external anchor; no loop feeds realized-versus-projected back into projections. | With realization factor 0 (on-prem, single node) nothing accrues before verification: 0.24 booked of 0.96 earned in simulation; user-facing totals disagree with ledger v2 by the same ratio; a database writer can re-chain the ledger and pass verification. Both live ledgers are empty, so no accrued number was checked live. | LED-01 (confirmed-live for the zero projections and empty ledgers only; the basis mismatch and the 0.24 / 0.96 figure are confirmed-sim), LED-03 (confirmed-sim), LED-04 (confirmed-sim), LED-05 (confirmed-sim), LED-07 (confirmed-code) |
| B18 | **Exports and Prometheus metrics publish wrong or unstable figures.** An aggregated report export is aggregated per 7-day read chunk; the exposition publishes the amounts of one collection step as unit-less `*_total` gauges that the bundled Grafana labels "Cost"; the exposition is cleared and refilled during each scrape. | The same (namespace, pod, period) appears several times in an export ("2026-11" three times in simulation); two installs on one cluster expose 0.000715 and 0.00716 for the same thing because their steps differ; overlapping scrapes returned 5 of 20 namespaces. | API-01 (confirmed-sim), API-02 (confirmed-live), API-09 (confirmed-sim) |
| B19 | **On fixed-capacity clusters every rightsizing projects zero.** Cost and CO2e savings are multiplied by a realization factor that is 0 without a node autoscaler, while workload deletions keep a factor of 1. | All live rightsizing recommendations show 0 cost and 0 CO2e; all 88 expected values are negative; 30 request increases sit in the savings lane; idle-namespace deletions outrank every rightsizing. Whether zero is the right figure to show on fixed capacity is a product decision. | IMP-03 (confirmed-live), IMP-05 (confirmed-live), API-12, LED-01 |
| B20 | **Projected savings shown can be stale or biased low.** Revision hysteresis freezes savings and evidence at an earlier scan while the proposal moves by 5 % or less; annualization divides by the span since the first observed hour, so collection gaps lower projections. | Minikube: a 500 MiB to 45 MiB memory cut shows 0.14 per year instead of about 16.5; a 6.6 h overnight gap gave -49 % (kind) and -56 % (Minikube). | IMP-01 (confirmed-live), IMP-04 (confirmed-live), REC-12 |

## 3b. Proposed answer to B19, B12b and B17: released capacity as a third quantity

**Decision proposed by the maintainer (2026-11-15):** next to cost and CO2e, show the resources a change releases, projected and measured.

**Why it fits.** A rightsizing pull request changes reservations. Released reservation is therefore the one effect that is exact, needs no price, no OpenCost and no autoscaler, and can be verified from the workload spec alone. On fixed-capacity clusters it is the honest result: the bill does not move, but capacity is freed for other workloads or for removing a node later.

**Scope.**

| Resource | Include | Reason |
|---|---|---|
| CPU requests (cores, core-hours) | yes | What the change edits; exact. |
| Memory requests (GiB, GiB-hours) | yes | Same. |
| Storage (GiB) | only for actions that change it: PersistentVolume deletion or resize | A request change does not free storage. |
| LoadBalancers (count) | only for deletion actions | Same. |
| Network bandwidth | no | Not a reservation, and no current action changes it; a figure would be noise. |

**Rules.**
1. Released capacity is never converted to money silently and never added to cost savings. It is a separate column, labelled "released capacity".
2. Projected: reuse the `capacity_released` object the Impact Model already stores on every recommendation (`cpu_core_hours`, `mem_gib_hours`, `replica_hours`, `node_hours`; checked on the live Minikube database). It is not shown by the UI today. State its period basis explicitly: it inherits the annualization bias of IMP-04. Also show the instantaneous figure `(current request - proposed request) x replicas` in cores and GiB, which needs no period.
3. Measured: requested capacity over the post-window minus the baseline, reported per replica and in total so that replica changes (HPA, manual scaling) are visible and not booked as a release. **No reliable source exists today:** `workload_spec_observations` stores digests only, and the request columns of metric rows are wrong for replaced pods (B1). Record per-container requests and limits with each spec observation, and measure from those.
4. Verdict: a capacity verdict (`released`, `not_released`, `partially_released`) is decided from specs alone. It must be available when the cost verdict is `inconclusive`, including on-prem without OpenCost.
5. Ledger: accrue core-hours and GiB-hours as their own quantities, with the same projected / measured separation.
6. Increases are negative releases and are shown as such, not in a savings lane (see IMP-03).
7. Optional derived figure, clearly labelled as an estimate: node-equivalents freed, from released capacity and node allocatable capacity.

**Size.** The projected side is small: the number exists and only needs surfacing in the API contract, UI, pull-request text and exports. The measured side, the capacity verdict and the ledger quantities are real development.

**Depends on:** recording request values at observation time (rule 3), B1, and B15 (hours with pod replacement overstate replicas in hourly rows).

## 4. What was checked and found correct

**Usage collection**

- Container-scoped selectors exclude the pod cgroup and the pause container; on kind at 2026-11-14 22:30:00 all 34 pods match Prometheus exactly for CPU millicores, memory bytes and node, and the container-level sum is 0.986 of the pod-cgroup sum (no double count).
- Half-open windows: the query runs from window.start + step to window.end and the sample evaluated at t is stored at t - step with duration = step; on kind every Prometheus evaluation with data has its stored step.
- Rate window = max(step, 4 x scrape interval); with a window longer than the step usage is smoothed over neighbouring steps but core-seconds are conserved on average.
- Units: cores are converted to millicores with rounding, memory stays in bytes, requests are parsed with Decimal quantities, Metrics API nanocore and Ki/Mi values parse correctly (utils/k8s_quantities.py).
- A Prometheus query error on CPU or on any of the eight per-chunk queries raises RangeCollectionError and the watermark does not advance (code path read: _collect_cpu, asyncio.gather block, MetricsCollectionJob.run).
- NaN/Inf samples are dropped and negative values clamped with a counter (_sample_value); samples outside the window are dropped.
- The collection loop fires at k x step + COLLECTION_SAFETY_LAG and plans windows oldest first in chunks of about one hour; an explicit --last range that is not contiguous with the watermark does not advance it.
- Metrics API usage is attached only to the latest sample of a current window and keeps the sample timestamp.
- Restart counts are rolled up with MAX and network/disk rates with AVG in the hourly compression SQL, consistent with their semantics.
- On minikube (pod-cgroup fallback) the stored CPU of all 55 pods at 22:30:00 equals the pod-cgroup rate query; memory equals it for 48 pods and differs by under 1% for 7 (late sample, see COL-04).
- On kind (kube-prometheus-stack) cAdvisor series carry the node label and no container has duplicate series at an instant; the kube_pod_info fallback for a missing node label exists.
- Native sidecars and limits are collected by PodCollector; classic init containers are excluded from steady-state sums.

**History reconstruction (experiment)**

- Steady window, same Prometheus replica as the live controller: the reconstruction reproduces the live rows exactly for presence (2204 of 2204 keys), CPU, memory, joules, CO2e, embodied CO2e, requests, owner, node, zone, PUE, intensity, restart count, estimation flags and reasons; only cost fields differ (at most 0.35% per row, 0.03% in total). The 40 node idle rows are identical at 1e-6.
- E2 idempotence: re-running a window into the same database keeps the row counts (2205 pod rows, 40 idle rows) and every value; the only differences are float noise of 1 unit in the last place (relative 4e-16) on cost_ram and total_cost.
- E3 split invariance of usage, energy, CO2e, requests and owners: bit-identical sums for 1 x 20 min, 4 x 5 min and 40 x 30 s (joules 8600.63968902832 in all three); only cost depends on chunking (RECON-08).
- E4 step invariance: 30 s, 60 s and 300 s steps conserve CPU core-seconds within -3.8% to +2.6% on 20-minute windows, node energy (pods + idle) within 0.4%, pod cost within 1.9%.
- Windows are half-open and aligned: 40 steps for 20 minutes, no duplicate key, no step skipped, row timestamp T describes [T, T + step).
- Pod cgroup fallback counts the pod once (BUG-01): Prometheus rate 12.93 mCPU for the pod cgroup series, stored 13 mCPU.
- Node totals are conserved when requests are lost: pods + idle = 14186 J live versus 14198 J reconstructed (+0.08%) on the churning window; only the split between pods and idle is wrong (RECON-01).
- OpenCost allocations are keyed by (namespace, pod) and deleted pods still get their OpenCost cost in a reconstruction (cost ratio 0.975 for the 140 rows of deleted pods); CPU and RAM components are stored.
- A Prometheus outage fails the window and keeps the watermark: the real job returned 'RangeCollectionError: Prometheus CPU usage is unavailable', windows_collected 0, watermark unchanged.
- Compression itself is lossless and idempotent: hourly sums of cost, cost_cpu, cost_ram, joules, CO2e, embodied CO2e, duration and sample count equal the raw sums exactly for both tables; a second run compresses nothing and leaves the hourly table identical; pruning is hour-aligned and never removes uncompressed hours.
- Hourly averaged fields are per-presence averages with the weight stored next to them: a pod present 15 minutes gets cpu_usage_avg 400, duration_seconds 900, sample_count 3; a pod at 50 m then 350 m gets avg 200 and max 350.
- A manual re-collection repairs raw rows completely after an OpenCost outage (0 differing fields at 1e-9), which makes the automatic repair of RECON-09 feasible.
- kube-state-metrics history in Prometheus holds the owner chain and the requests of deleted pods (kube_pod_owner, kube_replicaset_owner, kube_pod_container_resource_requests), which makes the fix of RECON-01 feasible.

**Cost**

- Steady-state conservation holds live: minikube 2026-11-14 22:00-23:00 UTC, 46 OpenCost-priced pods, sum of 120 stored 30 s step costs per pod equals the OpenCost allocation of the hour within 0.6% per pod and 0.02% in total (0.117060 vs 0.11704).
- Same check on kind 2026-11-14 22:30-23:30 UTC: 34 pods, total ratio 0.9977; largest per-pod gaps are OpenCost's own 5-decimal rounding of tiny hourly figures and -2.9% for greenkube-postgres-0 (usage above request is only partly visible to OpenCost in 30 s windows; with the default 5-minute step this effect should be smaller, not verified).
- opencost_step_share computes a rate (cost divided by running minutes), so pods that lived only part of a window get the right per-step cost even when OpenCost quantises minutes (5-minute resolution on 1 h queries): oversized-web-685f69cd4f-vbk97 is stored at 0.00011861 per 30 s step = 400m x 0.031611 + 0.375 GiB x 0.004237, exactly its request at OpenCost prices (BUG-41 fixed).
- The OpenCost cost map is keyed by (namespace, pod) and the query aggregates by namespace,pod: no cross-namespace collision (BUG-04 fixed); cpu, ram, storage, network and lb components are stored and container rows sum back to their pod row.
- OpenCost pseudo-allocations (__idle__, __unallocated__, __unmounted__) are skipped by the collector; <namespace>-unmounted-pvcs pseudo-pods never get a row because they have no CPU series.
- The sub-precision rebuild (opencost_derived) works as documented when node prices are known: 13,433 derived rows on minikube reproduce OpenCost hourly totals; rebuilt per-step cost of a 100m/128Mi pod is 0.00003076 against 0.00003068 from the hourly allocation.
- A modeled cost is never labelled opencost: rows without an observed cost are re-priced at catalog or custom rates (rows.py modeled_prices and _rescale) and labelled pricing_catalog or custom_rate; live, pods unknown to OpenCost (no requests, cri-dockerd) are stored as custom_rate.
- Without OpenCost, pod rows plus the idle row equal capacity x unit price by construction (reservation_shares); live CPU side on minikube: 0.09305 + 0.09548 = 0.18853 against 6 cores x 0.031611 = 0.18967 (-0.6%, OpenCost rounding).
- Allocatable equals capacity in the collection path (node_profile never sets cpu_allocatable_cores), so modeled pod cost is not inflated by a capacity/allocatable factor.
- NaN, infinite, negative and missing OpenCost costs are not trusted (broken_cost_reason, is_cost_trusted); a zero total for a pod that ran with requests and minutes > 0 is replaced by the modeled cost.
- Rollups sum cost columns and aggregate cost_source with one rule in Python and SQL (combine_cost_sources, cost_source_aggregate_sql): any mix other than opencost + opencost_derived becomes mixed, which measurement does not trust.
- Deletion measurement never books a flat default (custom_rate) as a measured saving (verify/deletion.py).
- Catalog prices that exist are correct and recent (generated 2026-11-07): AWS us-east-1 m5.large 0.096, m6i.large 0.096, m7g.large 0.0816, t3.medium 0.0416, r5.large 0.126, eu-west-3 m5.large 0.112; Azure westeurope D2s_v5 0.115, D4s_v5 0.23, B2ms 0.096, E4s_v5 0.304, F4s_v2 0.194. Lookups are region-aware; apportion_node_price preserves the node price with the OpenCost CPU/RAM ratio.
- The UI formats every amount with the currency of GET /config (IMPACT_PRICING_CURRENCY); format.js has no hard-coded dollar sign.
- Bare-metal LoadBalancers are not charged the flat default: the kind orphan LoadBalancer (Cilium LB-IPAM) is projected at 0 with cost_source none.
- Container rows add up to their pod row: minikube 18,733 pod-steps, 0 mismatches on total_cost and cost_cpu (kind: 21 mismatches, all explained by COST-04). GPU, shared and external OpenCost cost stay inside total_cost only (no column of their own).

**Energy and carbon**

- Units end to end: watts × seconds = joules; joules / 3.6e6 = kWh; kWh × PUE × gCO2e/kWh = grams; embodied = kg × 1000 / lifespan hours × hours × share. Checked in code and by hand on live rows (co2e_grams == joules/3.6e6 × pue × grid_intensity for every sampled row).
- Per-step allocation is internally exact on live data: an independent recompute of step 2026-11-15 06:49:00Z (and of 2026-11-14 22:30:00Z) reproduces every pod row's joules to 1e-7 relative on minikube (59 and 55 pods) and on the 3 kind nodes, and Σ pods + __idle__ equals the modelled node energy, operational CO2e and embodied emissions.
- Node utilisation denominator is the node CPU capacity reported by the kubelet (capped at 1); idle power and embodied emissions follow max(request, usage) / capacity; dynamic power follows usage share; memory power = 0.392 W/GiB × node memory (15.33 W for 39.1 GiB live); over-commit is normalised by the total reservation.
- BUG-07 fix holds: without a token the intensity history stays empty on both live clusters, rows are tagged intensity_source=zone_default with an estimation reason, defaults are never persisted, and INTENSITY_MAX_AGE is applied in get_for_zone_at_time and in the recompute.
- BUG-06 fix holds for container rows: the consolidation recompute updates pod and container rows together and relabels realtime -> consolidated (simulation: 12 pod + 12 container rows).
- Unknown instance types get a capacity-derived profile with truthful provenance (power_source=node_capacity_derived + reason) in collection; the 1 vCPU / 1–10 W DEFAULT_INSTANCE_PROFILE is only reached when neither capacity nor instance type is known and is flagged (power_source=default_profile, reason, greenkube_unknown_power_profile_nodes).
- `greenkube recompute` is idempotent for energy: re-allocating current rows leaves joules of pods and idle rows unchanged (simulation); only embodied is affected (NRG-07).
- Projection arithmetic is consistent with its inputs: live recommendation 95 reproduces idle_operational = core-hours × W/core / 1000 × PUE × intensity and embodied = core-hours × (node g/h / cores) exactly; annualisation uses 8760 h.
- Zone mapping: zone-suffix heuristics for AWS/GCP/Scaleway/OVH, the 'nova' exclusion and direct Electricity Maps codes on labels behave as coded (minikube label FR -> FR live); every zone the region mapping can produce exists as a row in the defaults table (19 of them without a value, NRG-04).
- PUE is applied once (to CO2e, not to stored joules) with the per-provider table, and an unknown provider uses the operator's DEFAULT_PUE with an explicit estimation reason.
- Idle rows are committed with the pod rows after the leader-fence check and a failed idle write stops the watermark (collection.py), as documented.

**Storage write path**

- PostgreSQL metric timestamps are all TIMESTAMPTZ and the session time zone is UTC; no naive/aware mix found on the write path.
- Stored co2e_grams equals joules / 3.6e6 x pue x grid_intensity for pod, container and idle rows on both live installs (ratio within 1e-7), so the carbon recompute formula is consistent with the writer.
- Watermark advance is monotonic (GREATEST / MAX) and a forced `--last` range that does not touch the watermark does not move it; the processor resets last_idle_metrics at the start of each run_range, so idle rows of a previous window are not rewritten.
- PostgreSQL executemany batches are atomic per table (a failed batch stores nothing for that table) and a failed container or idle write prevents the watermark from advancing.
- A SQLite database created by the current migrations has exactly the same column set as the live PostgreSQL for the seven metric, snapshot and watermark tables.
- Live schema matches the migrations for the metric tables (columns, types, unique keys); both dialects ship the same migration versions; the runner takes an advisory lock and applies each version once.
- 0.3.1 already wrote step-normalised UTC timestamps with the same `Z` format and DEFAULT_COST=0, so the first post-upgrade run really overwrites the last 24 h and migration 0039's cost_source backfill does not label fallback costs as opencost.
- SQLite connection scopes roll back a leaked transaction (BUG-50) and nested transactions use savepoints.
- Live data shows no partial window: every step with pod rows has its idle rows on both installs (785/785 and 841/841 steps); the 82 minikube steps without container rows are the runtime issue of RECON-06/COL-05, not a failed write.
- Weekly buckets agree between dialects (SQLite `weekday 0, -6 days` and PostgreSQL date_trunc('week') both start on Monday) when the PostgreSQL session is UTC.
- Migration scripts 0000-0037 are byte-identical to v0.3.1 (git diff shows additions only).
- No timestamp is NULL in the live raw tables; StatefulSet pod-name reuse is safe because the timestamp is part of the key.

**Rollups, compression, retention**

- Compression of closed hours conserves additive quantities: SUM(total_cost, joules, embodied) and sample counts in combined_metrics_hourly equal the raw sums below the watermark (sim1.py step B: 576 samples / 576.0 / 57.6).
- Re-running the compressor is idempotent (0 hours compressed, totals unchanged) and compression + watermark advance are one transaction per chunk on both dialects.
- Only hours ending at or before floor_hour(now - AGE) are compressed; raw pruning is hour-aligned and never passes the compression watermark, so an hour is never half-pruned (BUG-02 mechanism is gone).
- After raw pruning, hourly totals equal the totals of rows that were present at compression time (872 samples kept after the 8-day run).
- workload_usage_hourly re-rolls hours that receive new raw rows (late-hour detection through row ids works on SQLite; rollup hour showed 180 = raw).
- Network and disk columns are rates (bytes/s), so AVG in the hourly table is the right aggregation; gpu_power_watts is duration-weighted.
- Carbon-intensity recompute updates pod and container rows in one transaction (BUG-06 for containers) and its 24 h window matches the default compression age.
- Change-measurement cost/CO2e rates divide by covered seconds, so full hours of stable pods give identical results from raw and hourly rows (sim2.py second hour: 12.0 in both).
- At refresh time metrics_summary matches direct SQL on the live databases up to the last collection step (minikube 1h: 0.130048 vs 0.132319; all: 0.734096 vs 0.736366).
- The Postgres compressor and rollup bucket hours with AT TIME ZONE 'UTC' (independent of session time zone); SQLite uses UTC ISO strings.
- cli/db.py advances identity sequences after copying and excludes cache tables; watermarks and rollup marks are copied together with metric tables under --include-metrics.

**Change measurement and verdicts**

- Live stored baseline snapshots equal a hand recomputation from combined_metrics (minikube 0335c712: cost 0.0033098/h, core_hours 0.0864; kind 5ee15bd2: cost 0.0106/h) and the stored inconclusive verdicts follow from the code's rules.
- Windows are half-open [start, end) in UTC; rows are filtered with start <= t < end; post-window starts at rollout completion + warm-up; no time-zone conversion error found.
- A decision is taken once per (change_id, sequence): UNIQUE(change_id, sequence) with ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING, and _decide reuses the stored measurement; decided changes are re-measured only on an explicit request (BUG-31 behaviour removed).
- The bootstrap is seeded from (change_id, sequence, quantity): the same evidence reproduces the same interval.
- Per-replica health gates (per-pod p95, per-pod restart deltas) and the wired health collector work on the live clusters (gates carry real values).
- Failed metric reads raise MeasurementReadError and postpone the decision instead of freezing empty evidence.
- Component attribution caps component claims at the net allocated saving (attribution.component_cap) and CO2e is split by projected share, so no saving is claimed twice inside a change.
- Per-replica cost (cost / replicas) is robust to the rollup-row coverage defect, so the drift monitor's per-replica rates are not distorted by it.
- A realized deletion is not wrongly closed by the drift monitor (the workload probe returns None for Service/PV).
- The conservative rule (smaller of per-replica and workload-total saving, realized only if both are) prevents crediting an unrelated replica drop or an HPA scale-out.

**Recommendation engine**

- Stored values of live recommendations match the documented formulas when recomputed by hand from workload_usage_hourly (minikube): kustomize-controller CPU 100m->12m; greenkube-postgres CPU ->13m and memory 128->205Mi (p99 170.12Mi x 1.2 rounded up); metrics-server memory 200->39Mi and CPU floored to 10m; greenkube-api memory ->234Mi; test-postgres CPU capped at 2x (400m) and memory capped at 512Mi; e2e-oom 64->107Mi (limit 71Mi x 1.5).
- Weighted average usage in the summary equals the sample average of raw container_metrics (kustomize-controller: 5.23m both ways).
- Statistics are per replica and per container (one point per pod/container row; never summed across replicas); the pod-level summary is only used when no container series exist, and then never for an increase (pod_level_usage_only skip) and never for an automatic patch (build_patch requires a container name).
- Units are consistent: CPU in integer millicores, memory in bytes, memory proposals rounded up to whole MiB (_round_up_mib) and emitted as Mi/Gi quantities by _humanize_bytes when divisible.
- Memory increases are capped at RIGHTSIZING_MAX_INCREASE_FACTOR x request unless OOM-backed; limit advice keeps limit >= request and raises the memory limit with the request when the request would reach it (headroom_memory_limit).
- A container whose statistics exceed its pod's by more than 5% + slack is bounded by the pod statistics for increases and gets no decrease (fail closed).
- Orphaned LoadBalancer / PersistentVolume analyzers fail closed without evidence or without a stored first-seen time, require DELETION_MIN_UNUSED_DAYS, word the advice as a review, and project no saving for bare-metal allocators (live kind: Cilium LB-IPAM record id 32 with 0 saving).
- Idle-namespace analyzer returns nothing when namespace metadata cannot be read, and skips infrastructure, opted-out, terminating and young namespaces.
- Reconciliation only marks a recommendation stale when all its supporting sources completed (may_mark_stale); an incomplete source keeps the last known, safer proposal (_preserve_last_known).
- Scheduled scans exist and are single-writer (controller Lease, enqueue_if_due, abandoned-run reaping); API processes only enqueue.
- Apply detection requires the live value to have moved from the baseline to within tolerance of the proposal, a completed rollout, and uses the rollout completion time as applied_at; a rollout completed before the recommendation existed is retired as no_effective_change.
- Scan memory is bounded by targets on SQL backends: summaries are computed in SQL from workload_usage_hourly (one row per target container), not from raw rows.

**Impact projection**

- Projection arithmetic is reproducible from stored rows: 40 of 41 live CPU rightsizing projections (kind and minikube) recomputed independently in SQL from workload_usage_hourly match allocated_cost_annual to 3 digits (the 41st is the clamp defect); 6 kind memory projections match within the non-linearity of max(request, usage).
- Annualization constants are consistent: HOURS_PER_YEAR = 8760 everywhere in the model; the PR body's monthly figure is annual / 12 (730 h); no 720/744/8766 mix found.
- Per-replica versus per-workload: samples carry replica-hours (duration_seconds summed over replicas), so a 2-replica workload projects twice a single replica (live coredns 8862 vs 4463 replica-hours per year); no extra multiplication by replica count.
- A request cut moves only reservation-allocated terms: idle power share, memory power and embodied share; dynamic_operational is 0 in every live container_resources projection (BUG-16 is fixed).
- Projection and allocation use the same per-reserved-core rates: live kind embodied rate stored on rows (0.20385 g per reserved core-hour) equals the rate implied by the projection (81.71 g / 400.8 core-h).
- Pod-level price anchoring: for single-container pods the price per reserved core-hour derived from the sample's observed cost equals the node price (0.0316 USD/core-h on both clusters), and a CPU plus a memory cut cannot exceed the sample's observed cost.
- Request increases produce negative allocated deltas (live: -24.4/yr for 200m -> 400m), and an increase that stays below observed usage projects 0 (reservation is max(request, usage)).
- CO2e of storage and LoadBalancer deletions is reported as not modeled (co2e_modeled false, potential_savings_co2e_grams NULL) rather than invented; a bare-metal LoadBalancer (Cilium LB-IPAM) is projected at cost 0 with source 'none'.
- Scores are absolute per recommendation (no batch normalization): BUG-23 is fixed; active lists are ordered by ranking_score, not by the TEXT priority (BUG-20).
- impact.to_dict() labels every projection method 'projected' and the savings API keeps projected, measured and user-declared blocks apart (never summed server side).
- The controller refreshes the realization context before its scans in normal operation: every live request-change projection records realization_factor_source 'realization_context' with its reasons.
- Limits-only advice does not enter the projection (only request changes are priced).
- Without an observed cost the projection uses the configured custom rates exactly (simulation: 254.04 expected and obtained, confidence 0.7); a replica schedule removing 8 of 24 hours projects one third of the deletion; node removal equals capacity x unit prices x 8760 (1611.84); cutting CPU and memory to zero (262.80) stays below the deletion of the same workload (297.84); a healthy Cluster Autoscaler yields factor 0.6.

**Savings ledger**

- Accrual is idempotent on the fixed UTC hour grid: unique (recommendation_id, measurement_method, slot_start) plus a compare-and-swap of accrual_watermarks in the same transaction (PostgreSQL and SQLite); reruns write 0 rows (seen in simulations)
- Downtime is backfilled from the watermark (168 slots per run, later runs continue); a never-accrued recommendation is caught up from applied_at within 7 days
- The slot containing verified_at is split exactly: projected before, measured after; no overlap between projected and measured rows of one recommendation
- Accrual stops for not_realized, inconclusive, rollback_review, reverted, failed and regressed recommendations (no new rows)
- One invalid recommendation does not block the others (per-record try/except, greenkube_attribution_errors_total)
- v2 appends are serialized per cluster (BEGIN IMMEDIATE / pg_advisory_xact_lock), seq is gap-free, entry_key makes appends idempotent, the projection watermark moves in the same transaction
- Canonical JSON for the hash is deterministic (sorted keys, UTC microsecond timestamps, float repr); value is DOUBLE PRECISION on PostgreSQL so the float round trip is exact; chain verification detects a single modified, removed or reordered row and a naive tail truncation (existing tests; structure confirmed by reading)
- Projected, measured_allocated, user_declared and realized_billed are never summed in /api/v2/ledger/summary (one total per method, quantity and unit) nor in SavingsReport blocks; annual run-rates and accrued totals are separate blocks with a `basis` field
- Windowed totals prorate entries overlapping the window boundary the same way in v1 breakdown, v2 summary and export window_share (non-zero-length periods)
- Restatement arithmetic: reversal = -projection, restatement = measured annual rate x seconds / 31,557,600; both in the restatement book so the accrued book still equals v1 (SIM2: 0.216 / -0.216 / +0.72)
- The savings ledger is no longer compressed or pruned by MetricsCompressor (only VACUUM)
- GET /recommendations/savings returns 503 when the ledger read fails (no silent fallback)
- CSV/FOCUS exports guard string cells against formula injection and write numbers with repr() (no rounding)
- Cluster identity: first resolution wins and is stored; transient API errors skip the ledger pass instead of keying the chain by name (fail closed)

**API read path**

- With raw rows only (both live installs), /metrics/summary, /metrics/by-namespace, /metrics/top-pods, /metrics/timeseries (day) and /report/summary equal direct SQL sums on combined_metrics to float4 precision.
- metrics_summary rows equal the API /metrics/dashboard-summary response, and the sum of the 24h cache buckets equals the 24h summary row (0.86672 versus 0.86682 on minikube, 0.56641 versus 0.56640 on kind).
- Container rows are never added to pod rows: all read aggregates use combined_metrics / combined_metrics_hourly only.
- Repository reads are half-open [start, end) on both tables; time_range_from_last returns aware UTC datetimes; date-only report bounds are interpreted in UTC.
- Compression never double counts with the read split: the hourly branch stops at the cutoff and the compressor only writes closed hours (the defect is a gap, not a duplicate).
- /recommendations/savings keeps projected, measured and user-declared blocks separate and answers 503 when the ledger cannot be read (BUG-42); inconclusive and not-realized records contribute to no block (live: 6 applied, all inconclusive, all blocks 0).
- Weekly buckets are Monday-based on both backends (PostgreSQL date_trunc('week'), SQLite 'weekday 0','-6 days').
- The UI formats cost with the configured currency and converts joules to kWh with 3.6e6; it does not extrapolate footprint totals to monthly or yearly projections.
- The CSV export sanitises formula-leading text cells, and the range is validated before the stream starts.
- POST /config/services is removed (410); data-source settings are read-only in the API.
- /api/v2/recommendations returns `total` next to the truncated page; v1 list endpoints are not truncated.

## 5. Findings

| ID | Severity | Confidence | Effort | Finding |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| NRG-01 | critical | confirmed-sim | M | The Boavizta response model does not match the real API (gwp.embedded/use are objects), so every lookup fails and every node gets the flat DEFAULT_EMBODIED_EMISSIONS_KG=100 kg fallback, unflagged and re-queried every cycle. |
| COL-01 | high | confirmed-live | L | Requests, owners and node zone/provider/PUE are read from the Kubernetes API at collection time, so rows of deleted pods and removed nodes get request 0, owner NULL, default zone/PUE and no container rows, and re-collecting a window overwrites good rows with these degraded values. |
| COL-02 | high | confirmed-live | M | The cluster key of the collection watermark falls back to the node name when the node list fails at start, so each controller reschedule forks the watermark and re-collects the whole backfill horizon over existing rows. |
| COL-03 | high | confirmed-sim | M | Rows are created only for pods that have a CPU sample at the step; memory, network and OpenCost cost of every other pod or step are dropped without trace and no reconciliation against the OpenCost total exists. |
| COL-04 | high | confirmed-live | L | Holes are not recorded anywhere: windows older than the backfill horizon only increment an in-memory counter, and a window for which Prometheus returns no series is committed as collected with zero rows. |
| COL-05 | high | confirmed-live | M | On clusters whose cAdvisor exports only pod-level series (cri-dockerd: Minikube Docker driver, Docker Desktop, Mirantis), container rows are Metrics API snapshots stored with usage_source=prometheus, and catch-up windows get no container rows at all. |
| RECON-01 | high | confirmed-live | L | Backfilled or re-collected rows take pod requests, owner and PVC data from the Kubernetes API as it is at collection time: rows of pods that no longer exist lose their owner, carry request 0 and 73% less energy, and a re-collection overwrites correct live rows with these degraded values. |
| RECON-02 | high | confirmed-sim | M | Raw rows written into an already compressed hour (late backfill, greenkube start --last, greenkube recompute, repaired cost) are never re-aggregated, the API never reads them, and they are deleted at raw retention; the collection-watermark guard of the compressor is not wired in production. |
| RECON-03 | high | confirmed-sim | M | Every aggregate that spans the raw/hourly boundary uses `now - METRICS_COMPRESSION_AGE_HOURS` instead of the compression watermark: between 0 and about 2 hours of data (1 hour on average) are missing from summaries, time series, namespace and top-pod totals, and the result changes with the minute of the query. |
| RECON-04 | high | confirmed-live | M | Windows older than COLLECTION_MAX_BACKFILL are abandoned although Prometheus still holds them, and the hole is recorded nowhere: no table, no API field, no UI flag; totals over a period with holes are silently low. |
| RECON-05 | high | confirmed-sim | M | When a node is no longer in the Kubernetes API at collection time, its historical rows fall back to DEFAULT_ZONE, the default PUE and no embodied profile although node_snapshots_scd holds the node's history: CO2e was 19 times too high and embodied carbon zero in the simulation. |
| RECON-06 | high | confirmed-live | L | Container rows are produced only for pods that still exist at collection time and, on runtimes without container-level cAdvisor series (cri-dockerd), only for the latest step of a live window: backfilled, caught-up and deleted-pod steps have no container usage at all. |
| RECON-07 | high | confirmed-live | M | Container usage filled from the Metrics API is stored with usage_source = prometheus and is clamped one-sidedly to the pod's averaged value, so on runtimes without container cAdvisor series container CPU is biased low (-8.8% overall, -31% to -38% for bursty pods) and carries the wrong provenance. |
| COST-01 | high | confirmed-live | L | An OpenCost outage, empty answer or rejected request silently switches the window to list-price or default-rate cost, the watermark still advances, the window is never re-priced, and a re-collection overwrites good OpenCost cost. |
| COST-02 | high | confirmed-live | M | The pod cost is split between containers by CPU usage share instead of reservation, so container costs and the unit prices of rightsizing projections are wrong for every multi-container pod. |
| COST-03 | high | confirmed-live | M | collect_pv_costs and collect_lb_costs never return a cost with a real OpenCost (wrong query parameter and response fields), so orphan PV and LoadBalancer savings always use the flat defaults and their deletion can never be measured. |
| COST-04 | high | confirmed-live | M | The collection watermark is keyed by the cluster name, which on the kind install fell back to the controller's node name; when the name changed, the controller re-collected a range it had already collected and overwrote stored cost, requests and provenance. |
| NRG-02 | high | confirmed-live | L | Cluster totals (API, UI Footprint page, yearly report) silently exclude the node __idle__ share; idle rows are write-only, never rolled up and deleted after raw retention. |
| NRG-03 | high | confirmed-live | S | The hourly carbon-intensity job never fetches DEFAULT_ZONE: clusters whose nodes carry no mappable zone/region label (on-prem, kind, k3s, kubeadm) never get live intensity even with a valid token, and the job returns before the recompute. |
| NRG-04 | high | confirmed-code | M | The bundled zone-default intensity table is a one-day snapshot with 251 of 349 zones empty and malformed rows (ES, SE, NO, DK, AU): 19 of the 51 zones cloud regions map to fall back to 500 gCO2e/kWh. |
| NRG-05 | high | confirmed-live | S | Projections use a different node power model from collection: for every node without a bundled instance profile the projector assumes 1 W idle / 10 W max per core (DEFAULT_INSTANCE_*), while stored rows use the CCF per-vCPU coefficients (0.6–0.8 / 2.8–4.3 W). |
| NRG-06 | high | confirmed-live | M | Energy, CO2e and cost are stored as float4 (REAL) in PostgreSQL and summed with SUM(real): totals drift by percents beyond ~1M rows and stop growing beyond ~17M rows. |
| NRG-07 | high | confirmed-live | M | Two writers flip node_snapshots_scd.embodied_emissions_kg between NULL and the value every cycle: the SCD table grows by ~2 versions per node per cycle, and `greenkube recompute` zeroes embodied emissions when the latest version is the NULL one. |
| STO-01 | high | confirmed-sim | L | Every metrics upsert overwrites all columns unconditionally and never removes rows the new pass did not produce, so any re-collection of a stored window replaces good values with degraded ones and leaves a mix of two collection generations. |
| ROL-01 | high | confirmed-sim | M | Every reader that combines raw and hourly tables splits at wall-clock now - METRICS_COMPRESSION_AGE_HOURS instead of the compression watermark, so data between the watermark and that cutoff is read from neither table. |
| ROL-02 | high | confirmed-sim | L | Rows written or updated in an already compressed hour never reach combined_metrics_hourly / container_metrics_hourly and are deleted by raw retention; the collection-watermark guard described in the compressor is never wired. |
| ROL-03 | high | confirmed-sim | L | Change measurements built from compressed hourly rows overstate cost rate, replicas and core-hours for every hour in which the workload's pods were replaced, so the same window gives different numbers before and after compression. |
| MEA-01 | high | confirmed-sim | L | A change verdict compares one baseline week with one post week, and the block bootstrap only sees within-week noise, so week-to-week variation yields false 'realized' (and false 'not_realized') verdicts far above the nominal 5%. |
| MEA-02 | high | confirmed-sim | M | Price moves between the two windows are booked as savings (or erase them) whenever the change resized both CPU and memory and the nodes carry no pool label: the measurement values requests at the prices of each window instead of at constant prices. |
| MEA-03 | high | confirmed-sim | M | The measured CO2e saving is the raw difference of two weeks' emissions with no normalisation for grid carbon intensity (or PUE), so a windier or calmer post week is booked as a carbon saving or loss of the change. |
| MEA-04 | high | confirmed-sim | M | Hour buckets built from hourly rollup rows (all production data older than 24 h) take the longest single pod as the covered time, so any hour in which pods were replaced gets an overstated cost rate and replica count, or is dropped as under-covered. |
| MEA-05 | high | confirmed-sim | M | One untrusted cost sample makes the whole pod-hour untrusted once the hour is compressed (cost_source becomes 'mixed'), so measurements read from hourly rows lose every such hour; on the live clusters 24% (kind) and 40% (minikube) of pod-hours contain at least one such sample. |
| MEA-06 | high | confirmed-code | M | Without OpenCost or Kubecost no change can ever get a cost verdict: rows priced with configured rates (custom_rate) or the default are never trusted, so every on-prem measurement ends 'inconclusive' with the reason 'no trusted allocated cost in the baseline window'. |
| MEA-07 | high | confirmed-code | M | Health gates of the final decision have no baseline comparison and a zero restart tolerance over the whole 7-day post-window, so one unrelated container restart or pre-existing CPU throttling sends a correctly realized change to rollback_review and stops its savings. |
| MEA-08 | high | confirmed-sim | M | Any pod-template change that leaves the requests untouched (an image deployment, an env change, a rollout restart) contaminates a running measurement and, once the change is realized, closes it and marks its recommendations 'regressed', so savings on actively deployed workloads are never verified or stop accruing at the next release. |
| REC-01 | high | confirmed-sim | S | Once a rightsizing recommendation of a container is in status 'applied', every later recommendation of the same type for that container is silently discarded by upsert_recommendations (even a different proposal such as an OOM-driven increase after a GreenKube decrease), for as long as the row stays 'applied', which is permanent after an inconclusive or not_realized verdict. |
| REC-02 | high | confirmed-live | M | OOM kills are counted over the whole lookback regardless of request/limit changes, so after an OOM fix is applied the old kills keep 'proving' that the NEW limit was passed and drive a further unjustified memory increase on every scan. |
| REC-03 | high | confirmed-sim | S | The decrease target is not checked against the engine's own increase trigger: a decrease to max(p95,(avg+max)/2)x1.2 lands below p99/0.9 for any workload with an occasional peak, so GreenKube proposes a decrease, then an increase, then the same decrease again, forever, and memory decreases go below the observed peak. |
| REC-04 | high | confirmed-sim | M | Rightsizing fails open when reliability signals are unknown: with oom_kill_count/restarts_in_window = None (no kube-state-metrics, Prometheus error or timeout) a crash-looping or OOM-killed container is offered CPU and memory decreases, although the rollup itself holds its restart count. |
| REC-05 | high | confirmed-live | M | Recommendations are generated for workloads and containers that no longer exist: any target with one hour of usage inside the lookback gets a summary, there is no 'still running' check, so deleted/renamed Deployments, removed containers and finished Jobs keep active (and re-refreshed) rightsizing recommendations for up to the whole lookback. |
| IMP-01 | high | confirmed-live | M | Revision hysteresis freezes a recommendation's projected savings (impact, potential_savings_*, evidence) at the values of an earlier scan for as long as the proposal moves by 5% or less. |
| IMP-02 | high | confirmed-live | L | Container rows receive the pod's CPU cost by CPU-usage share (not reservation share), so per-container request cuts are priced with a per-core price that can be 100x too low or several times too high. |
| IMP-03 | high | confirmed-live | M | On clusters without a detected autoscaler every rightsizing projection is 0 cost and 0 CO2e, reliability increases are classified in the 'savings' lane, and every expected value is negative, so ranking and the savings totals carry no information. |
| LED-01 | high | confirmed-live | L | The ledger accrues 'projected' savings on the expected-billable basis (allocated x realization factor, 0 on fixed-capacity clusters) and 'measured' savings on the allocated basis, so the two methods are not comparable, nothing accrues before verification on-prem, and restatement cannot credit the pre-verification period. |
| LED-02 | high | confirmed-live | M | The v1 ledger, the accrual watermark and the v2 projection are keyed by CLUSTER_NAME, which falls back to the pod's node name; when the controller pod moves to another node the savings history is split and the rows written under the old name are never reported nor projected. |
| API-01 | high | confirmed-sim | M | An aggregated report export (aggregate=true) is aggregated per 7-day read chunk, so the file holds several rows for the same (namespace, pod, period) key and the preview row count is wrong. |
| API-02 | high | confirmed-live | M | The Prometheus exposition publishes the cost, CO2e and energy of ONE collection step as unit-less gauges named *_total, so every greenkube_pod_*, greenkube_namespace_*_total and greenkube_cluster_*_total value scales with the collection step and the bundled Grafana panels show them as 'Cost' and 'CO2e'. |
| API-03 | high | confirmed-sim | M | Every API aggregate that spans more than 24 hours reads hourly rows up to wall-clock now - 24 h and raw rows after it, while the compressor only fills hourly rows up to the last closed hour: 0 to about 2 hours of data are missing from the 7d, 30d, 1y and YTD totals, charts, namespace and pod breakdowns and reports. |
| API-04 | high | confirmed-live | L | No API total, chart, breakdown, report or Prometheus series includes the node idle share (node_idle_metrics is read by no endpoint), so 'Total Cost', 'Total Energy' and 'Total CO2' are the allocated-to-pods part only, without saying so. |
| COL-06 | medium | confirmed-sim | S | A single failed instant query makes PrometheusCollector replace the configured Prometheus URL with a discovered one for the rest of the process, and discovery probes send the configured bearer token or basic credentials to every candidate service. |
| COL-07 | medium | confirmed-sim | S | A failed Kubernetes pod or node list is swallowed (collectors return an empty result), and the window is stored with request 0, owner NULL, no container rows and default zone/PUE, then committed. |
| COL-08 | medium | confirmed-sim | S | When the carbon-intensity lookup raises, every pod row of the (step, node) group is skipped without log, metric or error and the window is committed. |
| COL-09 | medium | confirmed-sim | S | The pod-cgroup fallback is used only when the container query returns nothing for the whole cluster, so in a cluster mixing both kinds of nodes or runtimes every pod that has pod-level series only disappears, together with the idle rows of its node. |
| COL-10 | medium | confirmed-sim | M | In multi-step (catch-up or backfill) windows each row of a pod gets OpenCost cost x step / running time, so pods that ran only part of the window are over-costed. |
| COL-11 | medium | confirmed-live | M | CPU is aggregated with max over all series of a container, so consecutive container instances inside one rate window (restart, crash loop, pod recreated under the same name) are not added and CPU is under-counted. |
| COL-12 | medium | confirmed-live | M | Memory is stored as one instant value per step (the working set at the end of the step), so peaks between two evaluation points are invisible to rightsizing. |
| COL-13 | medium | confirmed-sim | S | Persistent-volume request and usage per pod are joined by PVC name without the namespace, so same-named claims in different namespaces overwrite each other. |
| COL-14 | medium | confirmed-live | M | Network figures of hostNetwork pods are the node's traffic, repeated for every hostNetwork pod of the node, and the stored values are bytes per second in columns and metrics named *_bytes. |
| COL-15 | medium | confirmed-sim | S | Cloud provider detection classifies GKE nodes in us-* regions, and Linode, Oracle, IBM and Alibaba nodes, as AWS, and misses AWS nodes without EKS labels outside five region prefixes. |
| COL-16 | medium | confirmed-code | M | Workload owners are derived by cutting the last dash-separated token of the ReplicaSet name and calling the result a Deployment, and Job pods keep the Job as owner, so Argo Rollouts and bare ReplicaSets get a non-existent Deployment and every CronJob run is a separate workload. |
| COL-17 | medium | confirmed-sim | S | Container rows are all-or-nothing per pod: when one container of the pod spec has no usage sample at the step, no container row is written for any container of that pod. |
| RECON-08 | medium | confirmed-sim | M | The OpenCost cost of a pod is spread over its Prometheus rows with `step / (minutes x 60)`, so the stored cost depends on how the window was chunked: catch-up chunks over-count pods that start or stop inside the chunk (x1.17 to x1.5 at a 5-minute step, +2.8% for the whole cluster in a busy hour) and cost_source flips between opencost and opencost_derived. |
| RECON-09 | medium | confirmed-sim | M | An OpenCost outage or lag is stored as modelled custom_rate cost (+10.8% in the simulation), the window is marked complete, and nothing re-collects it when OpenCost is back. |
| RECON-10 | medium | confirmed-live | M | The collection watermark is keyed by the auto-detected cluster name, and a missing watermark row is treated as a first run: any name change triggers a full re-backfill that overwrites live rows with reconstructed ones and counts no gap. |
| RECON-11 | medium | confirmed-live | S | OpenCost allocations with zero duration (minutes = 0, start = end) are stored as a trusted OpenCost cost of 0 for pods that run with non-zero requests. |
| RECON-12 | medium | confirmed-code | S | Rows can be dropped without any trace while the window is committed: a failed intensity lookup skips every pod of a node-step (the skipped_carbon counter is never reported), and the SQLite writer swallows per-row errors. |
| COST-05 | medium | confirmed-live | S | OpenCost allocations that carry no usable cost (minutes = 0, or sub-precision figures that cannot be rebuilt) are stored with the trusted label opencost, as an exact zero or a rounded value. |
| COST-06 | medium | confirmed-sim | S | The OpenCost credentials (OPENCOST_BEARER_TOKEN, OPENCOST_USERNAME, OPENCOST_PASSWORD) are sent only by the discovery health probe, never by the allocation requests, so an authenticated OpenCost or Kubecost silently yields no cost. |
| COST-07 | medium | confirmed-live | L | Historical windows are priced with the requests and nodes read from the live API at collection time: rows of pods that no longer exist get request 0, which collapses their modeled cost and makes OpenCost cost inconsistent with the recorded reservation. |
| COST-08 | medium | confirmed-live | M | Cost provenance stops at the database: the API and UI show one cost figure that mixes OpenCost costs, list prices, generic default rates and zero rows, with no indication of the share that is estimated, and the OpenCost health check does not test the allocation call. |
| COST-09 | medium | confirmed-code | L | The bundled price catalogs cover a small part of the fleets (GCP empty, AWS 35 families, Azure 100 sizes, missing the default AKS sizes), ignore spot and discounts, have no age check, and yet pricing_catalog is trusted to book measured savings. |
| COST-10 | medium | likely | S | The OpenCost query has no cluster filter and aggregates by namespace and pod only, so a multi-cluster OpenCost or Kubecost merges same-named pods of different clusters into one cost. |
| NRG-08 | medium | confirmed-sim | M | Lookup and Kubernetes API errors are swallowed inside run_range: an intensity lookup error drops every pod of the node-step, a failed node/pod list writes rows with zero requests, default zone, default PUE and no embodied — and the watermark advances, so the window is never repaired. |
| NRG-09 | medium | confirmed-sim | S | The intensity consolidation recompute updates pod and container rows but not node_idle_metrics, so idle rows keep the provisional or default intensity and node totals stop conserving. |
| NRG-10 | medium | confirmed-sim | M | Zone and PUE are taken from the live node list only: pods of a node that left the cluster before its window is collected (autoscaler scale-down, spot reclaim, backfill) are computed with DEFAULT_ZONE / the 500 g global default and the default PUE although the node snapshot knows zone and provider. |
| NRG-11 | medium | confirmed-live | M | Reservation shares use the requests of pods alive at collection time: rows of terminated pods (last steps after a rollout, finished jobs, any backfill) get cpu_request=0 / memory_request=0, shifting their idle energy, CO2e and embodied share to __idle__. |
| NRG-12 | medium | confirmed-live | M | Node CPU utilisation is the sum of pod CPU only: kubelet, container runtime, kernel and other non-pod CPU are ignored, so dynamic node power is under-estimated (live: pods account for 36% of the node's CPU activity on minikube). |
| NRG-13 | medium | confirmed-code | M | GPU energy is counted only when DCGM series exist; GPU nodes without the DCGM exporter are neither detected nor flagged, so their dominant power draw is silently missing. |
| NRG-14 | medium | confirmed-code | L | On-prem and bare-metal nodes are modelled with hyperscaler per-vCPU averages and the operator cannot supply real server power: DEFAULT_INSTANCE_* is dead configuration for any node whose capacity is known, and architecture is ignored. |
| NRG-15 | medium | confirmed-live | M | Nodes that share one physical host (kind, k3d, minikube multi-node, nested clusters) are each charged the full host capacity: idle power, memory power and embodied emissions are multiplied by the node count. |
| STO-02 | medium | confirmed-sim | M | Hourly compression is not bounded by the collection watermark in production (the provider is never passed), so hours are closed before their rows are collected and the late rows are never aggregated. |
| STO-03 | medium | confirmed-code | M | A collection window is committed in four independent transactions (pod rows, container rows, idle rows, watermark) and PostgreSQL node snapshots in one autocommit statement per step, so a failure or a concurrent reader sees a partial window. |
| STO-04 | medium | confirmed-sim | S | The SQLite metrics writer swallows per-row errors and still commits, so the window is reported written and the watermark advances over missing rows. |
| STO-05 | medium | confirmed-live | S | combined_metrics.id and combined_metrics_hourly.id are 32-bit SERIAL and every upsert consumes a sequence value even when it updates, so large or long-lived installs stop collecting when the sequence is exhausted. |
| STO-06 | medium | confirmed-code | M | Node snapshot versions (SCD2) are compared against float4 columns and written without a transaction or a uniqueness constraint, so any non-float4-exact capacity or embodied value creates a new version on every cycle and two writers can leave zero or two current rows. |
| STO-07 | medium | confirmed-code | M | No read path uses calculation_version or model_version, the hourly tables do not keep model_version, and rows written before the model changes (documented as about 2x inflated CPU and energy) are summed with current rows without any flag. |
| STO-08 | medium | confirmed-live | L | Unknown requests, cost and energy are stored as 0 (model and schema defaults) instead of NULL, so SUM, AVG and MAX treat missing measurements as real zeros and hourly rows erase the difference. |
| STO-09 | medium | confirmed-code | M | Metric rows carry no cluster identity and nothing prevents two installs from writing the same database schema; keys are (pod_name, namespace, timestamp) and (node, timestamp). |
| STO-10 | medium | confirmed-code | S | PostgreSQL time buckets and report years are computed with date_trunc/EXTRACT in the session time zone, which the pool never sets to UTC, so on a database whose default time zone is not UTC daily, weekly, monthly and yearly figures are shifted and labelled with the wrong date. |
| ROL-04 | medium | confirmed-sim | L | Lookback usage percentiles used for rightsizing are nearest-rank percentiles of hourly percentiles, and with a 5-minute step an hourly p95/p99 is the hourly maximum, so p95 is overstated whenever short peaks occur in more than 5% of the hours. |
| ROL-05 | medium | confirmed-sim | S | Compressed hours read back with a larger PROMETHEUS_QUERY_RANGE_STEP are counted as covering sample_count x new step, dividing measured cost and CO2e rates by the step ratio. |
| ROL-06 | medium | confirmed-code | M | `greenkube recompute` rewrites raw rows only: hourly aggregates and (on PostgreSQL) the usage rollup keep the old model's numbers, the recomputed values vanish when raw rows are pruned, and hourly tables have no model_version column to tell versions apart. |
| ROL-07 | medium | confirmed-sim | M | node_idle_metrics has no hourly aggregate and is deleted with raw retention, so node totals (pods + idle) cannot be reconstructed after 7 days and idle carbon is never consolidated. |
| ROL-08 | medium | confirmed-code | S | Day, week and month buckets are computed with date_trunc in the PostgreSQL session time zone, which the connection pool never pins to UTC, so on a non-UTC database buckets are cut at local midnight and labelled with the wrong UTC day. |
| ROL-09 | medium | likely | L (split: rollup retention + validators S; gauges S; daily tier L) | With METRICS_AGGREGATED_RETENTION_DAYS = -1 (default) three per-hour tables grow forever, including workload_usage_hourly which is only read for the recommendation lookback, with no size guard against the default 10Gi volume. |
| ROL-10 | medium | confirmed-sim | S | Report/export aggregation averages CPU and memory usage with one vote per row, so a compressed hourly row weighs like one raw 5-minute row, and namespace grouping reports the maximum pod request and the mean pod usage instead of namespace totals. |
| MEA-09 | medium | confirmed-sim | S | A saving is declared 'realized' as soon as its interval is above zero, without having to exceed the noise floor, while 'not_realized' uses the floor: a 0.3% drift below the 2% floor is a verified saving. |
| MEA-10 | medium | confirmed-live | M | The accelerated end-to-end settings can never produce a decided verdict, so the statistics, attribution, drift and ledger-accrual code of the Prove step has never run against a real cluster; all 5 live changes are inconclusive for that reason and the stored reason text misnames the cause. |
| MEA-11 | medium | confirmed-live | S | The OOM-kill baseline rate of a reliability increase divides kills counted over the whole scan window by an exposure reconstructed from the number of usage samples, which is tiny for a crash-looping container: live rates of 11,527 and 2,058 kills/day come from 28 and 5 kills over 58 minutes divided by 3.5 minutes. |
| MEA-12 | medium | confirmed-live | M | Rows of dead or terminating pods that carry no owner and zero requests are attached to the workload by pod-name inference and enter the baseline, diluting requested capacity and adding phantom replicas (live: baseline CPU request 0.086 cores instead of 0.300). |
| MEA-13 | medium | confirmed-sim | M | Hours are rated per covered time and hours without rows are dropped, so a workload that runs part of each hour (batch, scale-to-zero) is annualised as if it ran continuously, and the projection it is compared with uses a different annualisation (observed replica-hours x 8760 vs rate x 8766). |
| MEA-14 | medium | confirmed-code | M | A reliability increase that works is still reported as 'not_realized' (recommendation verification 'failed'), because the change outcome is the cost outcome and a request increase always costs more. |
| MEA-15 | medium | confirmed-code | M | A deletion is 'measured' by restating its projection: the pre-window cost of a Service or PersistentVolume cannot be read from pod metrics, so the realized amount, its zero-width interval and coverage 1.0 are the projected rate, and absence is checked once and never again. |
| MEA-16 | medium | confirmed-code | M | Two verification systems with different rules write the same 'verified' / 'not_realized' labels: recommendations applied by hand (PATCH /apply) or with CHANGE_MEASUREMENT_ENABLED=false are judged by the interim verifier (72 h before/after means, no counterfactual, no interval, no confounders). |
| REC-06 | medium | confirmed-sim | S | Ignoring a recommendation does not survive the next scan: upsert_recommendations only matches rows in status 'active' (then pr_open/applied family), so the same advice is inserted again as a new active row while the ignored row stays behind; un-ignoring later yields two active rows with one fingerprint. |
| REC-07 | medium | confirmed-live | L | Lookback 'p95/p99' are nearest-rank percentiles of hourly percentiles (themselves nearly hourly maxima), so the value shown and used as 'p99' is close to the window maximum and is not a percentile of the samples; one 5-minute spike a day makes 'p99' equal to the spike. |
| REC-08 | medium | confirmed-sim | S | The decrease coverage gate measures the span between the first and last observed hour, not how much of it was observed: 24 samples in two hours 8 days apart pass the 7-day requirement, and RECOMMENDATION_MIN_SAMPLES is not enforced by any analyzer. |
| REC-09 | medium | confirmed-live | S | Static (mirror) control-plane pods are merged into one fake workload 'Node/<node name>' and receive rightsizing and 'use an HPA' recommendations; the pod-level statistics of that target mix etcd, kube-apiserver, scheduler and controller-manager as if they were replicas. |
| REC-10 | medium | confirmed-code | M | Zombie and idle-namespace detection compare an absolute energy total over a variable window (1000 J) instead of a rate, so they almost never fire with 7+ days of dense data and fire for every namespace when data are sparse or short; the 'unused for N days' evidence is a first-to-last span. |
| REC-11 | medium | confirmed-live | M | Off-peak 'scale to zero N h/day' recommendations are issued from a single day of data (6 samples in total suffice): each hour of day needs only one observation, unobserved hours silently cut the idle run, and a one-off startup spike is the 'peak', so always-idle workloads, a database and a Git server were told to scale to zero at night. |
| REC-12 | medium | confirmed-live | M | Revision hysteresis freezes the whole record (evidence, sample count, coverage, description statistics, projected savings, impact, priority) at the first proposal while updated_at keeps advancing, so active recommendations show and are gated on statistics that can be many scans old (live: a recommendation still backed by 'sample_count 1' twelve hours and 729 samples later). |
| REC-13 | medium | confirmed-live | S | Evidence coverage_ratio divides replica-samples by single-series expected steps, so a workload with N replicas reports N times its real coverage (capped at 1.0), and a computed coverage of 0 is stored as 1.0; confidence and the automation minCoverage gate rely on it. |
| REC-14 | medium | confirmed-live | S | AUTOSCALING_CANDIDATE is driven by relative noise on near-zero CPU: 3 samples suffice, there is no minimum absolute usage, warm-up spikes count, and any kind is eligible, so idle workloads (0.01-1m average), single-replica databases and operators are told to 'use an HPA'; each threshold crossing also inserts a new history row. |
| REC-15 | medium | confirmed-code | M | Native rightsizing ignores HorizontalPodAutoscalers: a CPU/memory request change on an HPA-scaled workload moves the HPA's utilisation ratio (usage / request), the scan only knows 'an HPA exists' (metrics unknown, risk unchanged), and savings are projected as if the replica count stayed constant. |
| IMP-04 | medium | confirmed-live | M | Projections are annualized over the span since the target's first observed hour, so collection gaps and the hour-floor of the first observation bias every projection low (live: -49%). |
| IMP-05 | medium | confirmed-live | S | Workload deletions (zombie pods, idle namespaces) are projected with a realization factor of 1 even on fixed nodes, so their 'expected billable' saving is overstated and they outrank every rightsizing. |
| IMP-06 | medium | confirmed-live | M | The 'projected' savings of applied changes and the user-declared bound use the realization-scaled (billable) projection, while measurement and the ledger use allocated values, so pending and realized panels are on different bases. |
| IMP-07 | medium | confirmed-code | M | The UI sums the projected savings of overlapping recommendations (namespace deletion, workload deletion, off-peak, rightsizing, node consolidation) and the two totals disagree on negative values. |
| IMP-08 | medium | confirmed-code | XL | There is no feedback loop from measured outcomes to projections: realization factors, the incident cost and the annualization are constants, and the measured projection accuracy is never used. |
| IMP-09 | medium | confirmed-live | S | Minimum-request clamping is applied after the projection, so a clamped recommendation stores one value and the projection (and description) of another. |
| IMP-10 | medium | confirmed-live | M | The native node analyzer emits OVERPROVISIONED_NODE without any projection (and for control-plane nodes), although node removal is the only change that lowers the bill on fixed nodes and the projector can price it. |
| LED-03 | medium | confirmed-sim | M | Projected savings accrued before a NOT REALIZED or INCONCLUSIVE verdict stay in the ledger for ever (never reversed, superseded or restated), and a measured loss is never booked. |
| LED-04 | medium | confirmed-sim | L | The numbers users actually see (GET /recommendations/savings, overview hero, Prometheus counters and dashboard gauges) come from the mutable v1 ledger and never include restatements, so they disagree with the hash-chained v2 ledger and understate realized savings by the whole pre-verification period. |
| LED-05 | medium | confirmed-sim | L | The hash chain is not tamper-evident against anyone who can write to the database: there is no secret or external anchor, the verified head is stored in the same database, and a full re-chain plus an edit of ledger_chain_status passes every verification. |
