| LED-06 | medium | confirmed-sim | S | A forward clock jump makes the attributor accrue up to 168 future hourly slots per run and then freezes all accrual until real time catches up; rows are written with the current status for periods that have not happened. |
| LED-07 | medium | confirmed-code | XL (split: 1 calibration table + refresh job (M); 2 Impact Model use and versioning (M); 3 API/UI (M); 4 track record per change (S)) | The 'compare realized to projected to refine the projection model' loop is not implemented: only a binary realized/not-realized count per source feeds the ranking probability; measured magnitudes never calibrate the projections. |
| LED-08 | medium | confirmed-sim | M | Ledger corrections on rollback are a one-shot best-effort side effect: a failed supersede call is never retried, late accrual rows written after it stay counted, and dismissing a false-positive rollback review leaves the whole earlier history reversed. |
| LED-09 | medium | confirmed-code | L | The ledger grows without bound at one v1 row and two v2 entries per accruing recommendation per hour, with full-table anti-join sweeps, an all-time summary scan per API call and a daily full re-hash; there is no rollup or archival design. |
| LED-10 | medium | confirmed-code | S | The FOCUS export books projected and user-declared amounts as negative EffectiveCost 'Credit' rows, so summing the standard cost column mixes methods and subtracts unverified projections from spend. |
| API-05 | medium | confirmed-sim | S | The report export loses the rows stamped exactly on the first 7-day chunk boundary (a whole hour of every pod for date-aligned ranges), so the file total is lower than the /report/summary preview of the same range. |
| API-06 | medium | confirmed-live | M | The sustainability score is computed from one row per pod without node or HPA data: three of its seven dimensions are constants (50, 50, 100), two depend on the collection step, and an empty database scores 50. |
| API-07 | medium | confirmed-sim | M | GET /api/v1/metrics pages with LIMIT/OFFSET ordered by timestamp only, and its cursor mode reads a different data set (raw table only) that no response ever hands a cursor for. |
| API-08 | medium | confirmed-sim | S | When no metric row is younger than 20 minutes, /prometheus/metrics publishes cluster cost, CO2e, energy and pod count as 0 and a sustainability score of 50 instead of dropping the series; when a refresh step fails, the previous values are served again with HTTP 200. |
| API-09 | medium | confirmed-sim | M | The exposition is rebuilt inside each scrape by clearing shared gauges and refilling them across awaits, so overlapping scrapes (an HA Prometheus pair) or a UI request to /metrics/dashboard-summary return a partial set of series. |
| API-10 | medium | confirmed-live | S | pod_count is the number of distinct pod names seen in the whole window, but the UI shows it as 'Active Pods' and divides the footprint by it for 'Avg CO2/Pod'. |
| API-11 | medium | confirmed-live | M | The Footprint page mixes KPI cards read from an hourly cache with breakdowns computed live, and the cache keeps rows that are no longer refreshed, so figures shown together cover different periods. |
| API-12 | medium | confirmed-live | M | On clusters without a node autoscaler every rightsizing recommendation has potential_savings_cost = 0 and potential_savings_co2e_grams = 0, so /recommendations/top (default metrics), the Prometheus savings gauges and the Grafana 'Top Actionable Recommendations' table are empty or zero while dozens of recommendations are active. |
| API-13 | medium | confirmed-code | L | Read endpoints carry no coverage or freshness information: no collected-until time, no hole indicator, no estimated share, partial first and last buckets, and health and setup checks stay 'ok' when collection has stopped. |
| COL-18 | low | confirmed-code | S | Sum-based queries (network, disk, restarts, ephemeral storage) double when the same series is visible twice, and partial responses of Thanos/Mimir are accepted as complete. |
| COL-19 | low | confirmed-live | M | Ephemeral-storage usage is never collected on CRI runtimes, and network and disk are absent on cri-dockerd; the product does not tell the user that these dimensions are unavailable. |
| COL-20 | low | confirmed-code | S | restart_count stores the lifetime restart counter of the pod, and consumers read it as restarts of the observed period. |
| COL-21 | low | confirmed-code | M | Tests cannot detect PromQL semantic errors: the fake Prometheus takes pre-computed rates and the alternative label names handled in _series_identity can never occur. |
| RECON-13 | low | confirmed-sim | M | Node idle rows have no hourly aggregate and are deleted at raw retention, and the carbon-intensity recompute skips them: node totals (pods + idle) cannot be rebuilt beyond 7 days and idle CO2e keeps provisional intensities. |
| RECON-14 | low | confirmed-sim | S | aggregate_timeseries returns CPU and memory as sums of samples: the raw part of a bucket is steps-per-bucket times larger than the hourly part (4800 versus 700 in the simulation); the values are not exposed by the API today. |
| RECON-15 | low | confirmed-live | S | Against a non-deduplicated HA Prometheus pair the stored usage depends on which replica answers: row values differ by up to 100% between replicas and short-lived pods exist on one replica only, so a re-collection through another replica does not reproduce history. |
| COST-11 | low | confirmed-sim | S | Currencies are labels, not checked values: the USD default rates are relabelled with IMPACT_PRICING_CURRENCY, OpenCost's currency is never read, and recommendation texts print a dollar sign. |
| COST-12 | low | confirmed-sim | S | Projections read every stored positive cost as an observed cost: default-rate rows get confidence 0.7 instead of 0.5 and mixed hourly aggregates are presented as OpenCost with confidence 0.9. |
| COST-13 | low | confirmed-live | M | Idle cost is always priced with the model, does not reconcile with OpenCost pod costs (pods + idle differ from the node cost) and is read by no API, UI or report. |
| NRG-16 | low | confirmed-code | M | Wattnet's 15-minute intensities are stored but only the :00 point of each hour is ever used (default hour normalisation); sub-zone proxies are unflagged and one null value aborts a zone's collection. |
| NRG-17 | low | confirmed-code | S | Methodology docs and code disagree, and dead legacy estimator code with a different model is still shipped. |
| STO-11 | low | likely | S | The PostgreSQL carbon recompute compares a float4 column with a float8 intensity (`IS DISTINCT FROM`), so every row whose intensity is not float4-exact is rewritten on every hourly run. |
| STO-12 | low | confirmed-sim | S | SQLite stores timestamps as ISO text with variable fractional precision, so range bounds that fall in the same second as a row select the wrong rows. |
| STO-13 | low | confirmed-code | S | pod_count in summaries is COUNT(DISTINCT pod_name) without the namespace, so same-named pods of different namespaces are counted once. |
| STO-14 | low | confirmed-code | S | The hourly tables drop columns of the raw rows (ephemeral storage, grid_intensity_timestamp, model_version, period) and compression rounds differently per dialect. |
| STO-15 | low | likely | M | All pending migrations run in one transaction that takes ACCESS EXCLUSIVE locks on the metric tables and then rewrites them (0039 backfill), with the statement timeout disabled. |
| STO-16 | low | confirmed-code | S | `greenkube db copy` drops all metric history by default and the documentation says it is re-collected, although only the last COLLECTION_MAX_BACKFILL (24 h) can be. |
| STO-17 | low | confirmed-live | S | CPU usage is stored as integer millicores (and hourly averages are cast to INTEGER), so pods using less than 0.5 m are stored as exactly 0 while carrying energy and cost. |
| STO-18 | low | plausible | S | Neither CombinedMetric nor the metric tables reject NaN, infinite or negative values, and a single NaN stored in a PostgreSQL float column turns every SUM that includes the row into NaN. |
| ROL-11 | low | confirmed-sim | M (S for the short-term rule) | A usage-rollup hour rebuilt from compressed rows uses each pod's hourly maximum as its only point, so p50-p99 of that hour all equal the peak. |
| ROL-12 | low | confirmed-code | M | Hourly compression silently drops or approximates per-row information: estimation reasons, model version, intensity timestamp, ephemeral-storage columns, and it resolves owner/node/zone and rounding differently on SQLite and PostgreSQL. |
| ROL-13 | low | confirmed-code | S | Dashboard caches can serve stale data without saying so: the time-series cache keeps old buckets when a window becomes empty, is replaced without a transaction, and KPI windows are up to one hour old and end at the refresh time, not at 'now'. |
| MEA-17 | low | confirmed-live | S | The baseline snapshot is frozen in the detection pass, before the last baseline samples are written and before provisional values are restated, and the recorded rollout start equals the rollout completion when the GitOps sync is observed late. |
| MEA-18 | low | likely | S | read_combined_metrics_smart splits raw and hourly data at a cutoff that is not hour-aligned, so each measurement read that spans the cutoff misses up to one hour of rows (the hour not yet compressed and older than the cutoff). |
| REC-16 | low | confirmed-code | S | OVERPROVISIONED_NODE compares only pod usage with capacity: it ignores requests, has no coverage requirement, flags control-plane and single nodes, and in a namespaced scan the node usage is computed from that namespace's pods only while the cluster-scoped record is still written. |
| REC-17 | low | confirmed-live | S | The minimum-request floor is applied to increases after the step cap, so containers with tiny requests get 'reliability' increases straight to 10m / 16Mi (beyond the 2x cap) for a few millicores of usage. |
| IMP-11 | low | confirmed-code | M | Orphaned PersistentVolumes on non-cloud CSI or NFS storage are projected at the cloud default rate as an expected billable saving, and OpenCost PV/LB costs are annualized over the whole lookback. |
| IMP-12 | low | confirmed-code | M | The realization factor silently defaults to 1.0 until the controller process has completed a first autoscaler read (or when that read fails), and to 0.0 for any autoscaler that is not detected. |
| IMP-13 | low | confirmed-live | S | The 5% revision hysteresis is a one-step relative band without memory: proposals that alternate between two nearby values create a new revision at every flip. |
| LED-11 | low | confirmed-code | M | Ledger entries cannot be reproduced from their own provenance: price_card_version, intensity_g_per_kwh and intensity_source are never filled, projected entries carry only a generic method_version, and the v1 ledger has no currency. |
| LED-12 | low | confirmed-code | M | Minor ledger inconsistencies: drift reversals with mixed signs are projected as accruals, zero-length periods are dropped from windowed summaries, and a record that errors or is skipped for some runs loses those slots for good. |
| API-14 | low | confirmed-live | S | The duration suffix 'm' means months: last=5m returns 150 days of data, and the parser's own docstring presents '5m' as a dashboard duration. |
| API-15 | low | confirmed-code | M | Prometheus exposition naming and labels: gauges named *_total, a currency unit hard-coded as dollars, measured values inside label values, rank labels, and 'implemented' counts that include changes that were not realized. |
| API-16 | low | confirmed-code | S | CSV/JSON export details: negative numbers are written as text with a leading apostrophe, a failure in the middle of a stream produces a truncated file after HTTP 200, and the UI formats negative CO2e amounts in milligrams. |

### 5.1 Usage collection

**Coverage.** Read in full: collectors/promql.py, prometheus_collector.py, metrics_server_collector.py, pod_collector.py, node_collector.py, collectors/discovery/prometheus.py, core/historical_range_processor.py, core/collection.py, core/collection_telemetry.py, core/prometheus_resource_mapper.py, utils/time.py, utils/k8s_quantities.py; the collection parts of cli/start.py and cli/utils.py (loop registration, alignment, entry point); MetricAssembler.assemble_container_metrics, cost_fields and build_estimation_flags; opencost_step_share; Config cluster-name detection; node_zone_mapper; the metrics upsert statements. Skimmed only: collectors/k8s_object_reader.py (pagination and callers), core/loops.py (LoopSpec), discovery/base.py and discovery/opencost.py not read. Ran: simulations with the real HistoricalRangeProcessor and MetricsCollectionJob on SQLite with fakes (work/usage-collection/sim_collection.py scenarios a-h, sim_provider.py, sim_pvc.py, sim_url_switch.py; outputs next to them); live read-only checks on both clusters (Prometheus label sets and counts, the exact catalogue queries, max versus sum at a container handover, SQL on combined_metrics, container_metrics and collection_watermarks, health endpoint, one read-only node-list request from the kind controller pod). NOT covered: DCGM/GPU collection, OpenCost collector internals, node snapshot (SCD) repository correctness, the Impact Model allocation itself, compression and rollups, Windows nodes, real Thanos/Mimir/managed Prometheus behaviour (code reading only), kubelet /metrics/resource scraping (not present live), scale behaviour of unpaginated pod and node lists, the `--last` CLI path end to end, and no pytest run.

**Earlier defects not fully fixed.**

- BUG-03 (partially_fixed): Read-first shortcut gone, half-open windows and watermark work; but gaps and empty windows are still not recorded or exposed (COL-04) and the watermark key can fork (COL-02).
- BUG-05 (partially_fixed): Fill limited to the latest fresh sample and timestamp kept, but filled rows are labelled usage_source=prometheus (COL-05).
- BUG-41 (partially_fixed): Single-step windows get the exact OpenCost allocation; multi-step catch-up windows over-cost partial-lifetime pods (COL-10).

#### COL-01 · Requests, owners and node zone/provider/PUE are read from the Kubernetes API at collection time, so rows of deleted pods and removed nodes get request 0, owner NULL, default zone/PUE and no container rows, and re-collecting a window overwrites good rows with these degraded values.

**Severity** high · **Kind** data-integrity · **Confidence** confirmed-live · **Effort** L

**Where.** `src/greenkube/core/historical_range_processor.py:508-539`; `src/greenkube/core/historical_range_processor.py:563`; `src/greenkube/core/historical_range_processor.py:738-742`; `src/greenkube/core/historical_range_processor.py:789-812`; `src/greenkube/core/metric_assembler.py:196-212`; `src/greenkube/storage/postgres/repository.py:274-294`; `src/greenkube/storage/sqlite/repository.py:335-355`

**Mechanism.** run_range builds pod_request_map / pod_mem_map / pod_owner_map from one PodCollector.collect() call (current pods) and nodes_info / node_contexts from one NodeCollector.collect() call (current nodes), then applies them to every historical sample of the window: `cpu_request=pod_request_map.get(pod_key, 0)`, `owner = pod_owner_map.get(pod_key)`, `provider = _ni.cloud_provider if _ni else None`, `zone = node_context.emaps_zone if node_context else DEFAULT_ZONE`, `node_zone=(_ni.zone if _ni else None)`. Only the power profile uses the stored node history (get_node_info_at). assemble_container_metrics builds container rows only for pods of the current pod list, so a deleted pod gets none. A window [T, T+step) is collected at T+step+safety lag, so a pod deleted before that instant is already gone. The upsert then writes `owner_kind = EXCLUDED.owner_kind`, `cpu_request = EXCLUDED.cpu_request`, so re-collecting a window (watermark lost, `--last`, catch-up) replaces previously correct rows; container rows of that window are not rewritten, so pod and container rows diverge.

**Effect on numbers.** Live kind: pod oversized-worker-6c99bffdf4-gkqpv has 33 rows; the last 4 have cpu_request 0 (was 300), memory_request 0 (was 256Mi), owner NULL (was Deployment/oversized-worker). Pods shorter than the collection delay are 100% orphaned (gkup-greenkube-ready-check-d477j 4/4 rows, gkup-greenkube-api-7f6d67db4c-gx9kk 4/4). Overwrite: greenkube-controller-d85c4df5f-zvrqt has 25/25 pod rows with owner NULL, request 0 and 7.05 J at 21:38:30 while its container row of the same timestamp still says Deployment/greenkube-controller, request 100, 9.29 J (a container with more energy than its pod). With defaults (5 min step, 1 min lag) the last 5-minute row of every deleted pod, every row of pods deleted on completion (CI runners, workflow steps, spot evictions) and, after a backfill of N hours, every pod replaced in those N hours are affected. Consequences: usage, cost and CO2e are not attributed to the workload; the allocation uses reserved = max(request, usage) (impact/types.py:368, impact/pricing.py:401), so a pod requesting 1000m and using 50m keeps 1/20 of its share and the rest moves to other pods or idle; removed nodes get DEFAULT_ZONE and the default PUE (simulation S3: emaps_zone 'unknown' instead of FR, PUE 1.3 instead of 1.15, default power profile), which changes CO2e by the ratio of the two grid intensities (several times between a low-carbon and a default zone); container_metrics has holes exactly around rollouts.

**Affected environments.** All. Worst with frequent rollouts, Jobs/CI pods, HPA, cluster autoscaler/Karpenter/spot nodes, and after any controller downtime or first-install backfill.

**Evidence.** Live kind SQL: pods with both NULL and non-NULL owner (14 pods listed), and pod-vs-container comparison for greenkube-controller-d85c4df5f-zvrqt. Simulation audit-workspace/audit/work/usage-collection/sim_collection.py scenario c (S3), output sim_collection.out: 'pod web-7d9f-gone pod_rows=12 container_rows=0 cpu_request=0 memory_request=0 owner=None/None ... emaps_zone=unknown node_zone=None instance_type=None pue=1.3 power_source=default_profile' versus 'pod alive ... cpu_request=500 owner=Deployment/alive emaps_zone=FR pue=1.15'.

**Fix.** 1) Add a pod metadata history: table pod_snapshots (cluster_uid, namespace, pod, pod_uid, owner_kind, owner_name, node, containers JSON with name/kind/requests/limits, first_seen, last_seen), refreshed at every collection tick (later by a watch). run_range resolves requests, limits, owner and container list as of the sample time from this table. 2) For pods the API poll never saw, read the same facts from kube-state-metrics range queries on the chunk (kube_pod_owner, kube_pod_container_resource_requests, kube_pod_container_resource_limits, kube_pod_info) and prefer them when present, because they are exact per step. 3) Node provider, zone, emaps zone, PUE and instance type must come from get_node_info_at(node, ts) (node_snapshots_scd), falling back to the current node only when no snapshot exists; build zone contexts for snapshot nodes too. 4) Never store 0/NULL as a default: add a column metadata_source (k8s_snapshot | kube_state_metrics | missing), store requests as NULL when missing, add estimation reason pod_metadata_missing, and let the allocator use the last known request of that pod. 5) Upsert rule: a row whose new metadata_source is 'missing' must not overwrite non-null owner/request columns; when a window is re-collected, rewrite its container rows in the same transaction (delete rows of pods that produce none) so pod and container rows agree. 6) Migration: repair stored rows with owner NULL or request 0 from other rows of the same (namespace, pod) and from kube-state-metrics while Prometheus retention allows, bump CALCULATION_VERSION and re-run the allocation of the repaired windows. Document the behaviour in docs/methodology/impact-model.md.

**Acceptance.** a) Fixture: pod P (Deployment d, request 500m) has CPU series until 10:07 and is absent from the pod list at collection time, with a snapshot or KSM series available: rows 10:00 and 10:05 carry owner Deployment/d, cpu_request 500 and container rows exist. b) Without any metadata: request is NULL, metadata_source='missing', the row is flagged, and the allocator uses the last known request. c) A node absent from the API but present in node_snapshots_scd yields its real zone, provider PUE and instance type. d) Re-running a window after the pod is deleted leaves owner/request unchanged and pod joules equal the sum of its container joules. e) The 14 live kind pods have no NULL-owner rows after the migration.

#### COL-02 · The cluster key of the collection watermark falls back to the node name when the node list fails at start, so each controller reschedule forks the watermark and re-collects the whole backfill horizon over existing rows.

**Severity** high · **Kind** reliability · **Confidence** confirmed-live · **Effort** M

**Where.** `src/greenkube/core/config.py:1105-1138`; `src/greenkube/core/config.py:1141-1184`; `src/greenkube/core/collection.py:221`; `src/greenkube/core/collection.py:153-155`

**Mechanism.** MetricsCollectionJob keys the watermark with `cluster_uid or config.CLUSTER_NAME or 'default'`. CLUSTER_NAME is auto-detected once, synchronously, at configuration load: _list_nodes_in_cluster has a 5 s budget and returns None on any error, and _auto_detect_cluster_name then returns K8S_NODE_NAME. The docstring says the name 'must not depend on the node a pod runs on', but this fallback does. A controller that starts on another node finds no watermark under its new key, so plan_collection starts at now - COLLECTION_MAX_BACKFILL and re-collects every window with the current pod/node state (see COL-01).

**Effect on numbers.** Live kind (3 nodes, Cilium, CLUSTER_NAME empty): collection_watermarks holds two rows, 'gk-cilium-worker' (stopped 21:49:00) and 'gk-cilium-worker2' (current); both controller pods took the node name although the node list works from the pod now (HTTP 200), so it is a start-up race (2 starts out of 2). The second controller re-collected the horizon and rewrote 25 pod rows of the previous controller pod with owner NULL, request 0 and 7.05 J instead of 9.29 J. With the default 24 h horizon each move re-queries 288 windows of Prometheus and OpenCost and degrades up to 24 h of history. Every other state keyed by CLUSTER_NAME (GitOps bindings, savings, ledger identity) forks the same way.

**Affected environments.** Multi-node clusters without a well-known cluster-name node label (kubeadm, kind, k3s, RKE2, OVH, Scaleway, bare metal) when the API is not reachable in the first seconds of the pod (Cilium and other CNIs that program the pod network late, slow API servers). Not affected: CLUSTER_NAME set explicitly, single-node clusters.

**Evidence.** live.sh sql 'select * from collection_watermarks' on kind: two rows keyed by node names. Controller log context: "cluster": "gk-cilium-worker2" on 400/400 lines. Read-only node list from inside the controller pod returns 200 with 2 items and a continue token. ConfigMap greenkube-config: CLUSTER_NAME = ''. Pod-vs-container row comparison quoted in COL-01.

**Fix.** 1) Remove the K8S_NODE_NAME fallback. 2) Resolve the cluster identity before the controller loops start, with retries and backoff: the kube-system namespace UID (already used by verify/ledger/identity.py) becomes cluster_uid for watermarks and every cluster-keyed table; CLUSTER_NAME stays a display label. If the identity cannot be resolved, the collection loop does not start and readiness reports it. 3) Persist the resolved identity in the database and reuse it at later starts. 4) Migration at start: when watermark rows exist under other keys in the same database, adopt max(last_complete_end) of the rows whose key is a node name of this cluster or 'default', then delete the legacy rows. 5) Helm: document config.clusterName and warn in NOTES.txt when it is empty on a multi-node cluster. 6) plan_collection with watermark None must not blindly re-collect: start at the newest stored combined_metrics timestamp + step when rows already exist.

**Acceptance.** a) Unit: _auto_detect_cluster_name with a failing node list never returns the node name. b) Integration: two controller instances started with K8S_NODE_NAME=a then b against one database use the same watermark row and the second plans zero already collected windows. c) With an existing database holding rows up to T and no watermark, the first plan starts at T + step. d) Live kind after upgrade: one watermark row.

#### COL-03 · Rows are created only for pods that have a CPU sample at the step; memory, network and OpenCost cost of every other pod or step are dropped without trace and no reconciliation against the OpenCost total exists.

**Severity** high · **Kind** data-integrity · **Confidence** confirmed-sim · **Effort** M

**Where.** `src/greenkube/core/historical_range_processor.py:660-697`; `src/greenkube/core/historical_range_processor.py:759-776`

**Mechanism.** `groups` (the list of pod rows to build per sample and node) is filled only from `samples`, the parsed CPU rate results: `for pod_key in pod_map: groups[(sample_start, node)].append(pod_key)`. Memory, network, restart, storage and cost maps are only looked up for those keys. `rate()` needs two scrapes of the same container series inside the rate window, so a pod has no CPU sample when it lived less than about two scrape intervals, when its containers restart faster than that (each restart is a new cgroup series), or during its first step. Its memory sample and its OpenCost allocation exist but are never stored.

**Effect on numbers.** Simulation S1 (12 steps, three pods costing 1.20 each): pod 'late' without a CPU sample at 3 steps gets 9/12 rows and 0.90 of 1.20; pod 'memonly' with memory and cost but no CPU series gets 0 rows; stored cost 2.10 versus OpenCost 3.60 (-42%). In production the loss is the first step of every pod, all of pods shorter than about 30-60 s (CI steps, short CronJobs), and pods in CrashLoopBackOff, whose requests OpenCost bills for the whole pod lifetime: on a CI or batch cluster this can be tens of percent of cluster cost, on a steady cluster well below 1%. Cluster totals shown by GreenKube are therefore lower than OpenCost's by an unknown amount.

**Affected environments.** All; largest on batch, CI and crash-looping workloads. With OpenCost the cost gap is visible against OpenCost; without OpenCost the modeled cost and energy of those pods are missing too.

**Evidence.** audit-workspace/audit/work/usage-collection/sim_collection.py scenario a, output: 'pod late pod_rows= 9/12 cost_stored=0.900 (OpenCost window total 1.200) memory_samples=9', 'pod memonly pod_rows= 0/12 cost_stored=0.000', 'stored cost total=2.100 vs OpenCost total=3.600'. Code: src/greenkube/core/historical_range_processor.py:692-697.

**Fix.** 1) Build the pod set of each sample as the union of: CPU keys, pod-cgroup CPU keys, memory keys, running pods from kube_pod_info / kube_pod_status_phase when kube-state-metrics exists, and OpenCost allocations with minutes > 0. 2) A pod without a CPU sample gets a row with cpu_usage_millicores NULL (never 0), reason no_cpu_sample, and still takes part in the allocation through its requests. 3) Recover CPU of short-lived series: add a second query `max by (namespace,pod,container,node,name) (max_over_time(container_cpu_usage_seconds_total{...}[step]))` and, for a series whose first sample lies inside the step, use counter_value / step as core usage (the counter starts at 0 with the container). 4) Reconcile every window: compare the sum of stored OpenCost-sourced cost with the OpenCost window total; store the difference as an explicit __unattributed__ row per namespace (so totals match) and export greenkube_collection_cost_unattributed_ratio. 5) Record per window the number of pods with memory but no CPU (see COL-04 window table).

**Acceptance.** Fixture S1: 'late' has 12 rows (3 with NULL CPU and reason no_cpu_sample), 'memonly' has 12 rows, total stored cost = 3.60 +/- 1%. A container that lives 40 s and burns 20 CPU-seconds yields a row with about 67 millicores for a 300 s step. The reconciliation metric is 0 for the fixture and equals the missing share when a pod is removed from the Prometheus fixture.

#### COL-04 · Holes are not recorded anywhere: windows older than the backfill horizon only increment an in-memory counter, and a window for which Prometheus returns no series is committed as collected with zero rows.

**Severity** high · **Kind** reliability · **Confidence** confirmed-live · **Effort** L

**Where.** `src/greenkube/core/collection.py:136-164`; `src/greenkube/core/collection.py:252-258`; `src/greenkube/core/collection.py:305-334`; `src/greenkube/core/collection_telemetry.py:31-36`; `src/greenkube/core/historical_range_processor.py:275-289`

**Mechanism.** plan_collection drops every window before now - COLLECTION_MAX_BACKFILL and returns only a count; run() logs it and increments greenkube_collection_gap_windows_total (a process-local counter that restarts at 0). _collect_window advances the watermark whenever run_range returns, including with an empty list: `written = ... if rows else 0` then `advance(...)`. An empty CPU result is not an error (_collect_cpu only raises on query errors), so Prometheus without data for the window (scrapes stopped, TSDB lost, wrong data source, scrape interval longer than half the rate window, remote-write lag longer than COLLECTION_SAFETY_LAG) is stored as 'nothing ran'. collection_watermarks has only (cluster_uid, source, last_complete_end, updated_at). GET /api/v1/health returns only status and version; no API route or UI element shows coverage.

**Effect on numbers.** Simulation S5 (controller down 72 h, Prometheus data for the last 2 h only): 577 windows abandoned, 24 windows 'collected', 24 of 865 steps have rows, 841 steps are silent holes, watermark = now, error = None. Live: minikube has no row between 2026-11-14 23:54:30 and 2026-11-15 03:32:30 and 6-18 of 120 steps per hour afterwards; kind has 14-32 of 120 steps per hour for 7 hours (host asleep, Prometheus has no scrapes); both watermarks are at 'now'. Any hourly or daily total built from these rows equals the sum of the surviving steps (kind 05:00-06:00: 19 of 120 steps, about 16% of a full hour) and nothing marks it partial; averages over existing rows are biased the other way. Completeness of the newest window is not checked either: on minikube (safety lag 30 s) 7 of 55 pods at 22:30:00 store a memory value that differs from what Prometheus returns now for the same instant (argocd-redis 24,096,768 stored versus 23,834,624), because the last cAdvisor sample was not yet queryable when the window was collected.

**Affected environments.** All. Typical triggers: controller or database downtime longer than 24 h, Prometheus restart without persistent storage, retention shorter than the backfill horizon, remote-write backends (Thanos receive, Mimir, AMP, GMP) with ingestion delay, laptops and edge clusters that sleep.

**Evidence.** audit-workspace/audit/work/usage-collection/sim_collection.py scenario e output: 'gap_windows=577 windows_planned=24 windows_collected=24 rows_written=48 error=None', 'steps between old and new watermark=865; steps with rows=24; silent holes=841', 'collection_watermarks columns: [cluster_uid, source, last_complete_end, updated_at]'. Live: per-hour distinct timestamps of combined_metrics on both clusters; Prometheus count_over_time comparison for kind 05:00-06:00 (audit-workspace/audit/work/usage-collection/kind_raw_count.json, kind_db_steps.txt); live.sh api api/v1/health -> {status: ok, version: 0.3.1}; minikube cross-check of stored values against the fallback queries at 22:30:00 (audit-workspace/audit/work/usage-collection/mk_db_T.txt, mk_mem_T.json).

**Fix.** 1) New table collection_windows (cluster_uid, source, window_start, window_end, status collected|empty|gap|failed, expected_steps, steps_with_data, pod_rows, cpu_series, reason, collected_at), written in the same transaction as the rows; abandoned ranges get status gap. 2) Distinguish 'no workload' from 'no data': before committing an empty or thin window, run cheap probes on the same range (count(up{job=~"kubelet|cadvisor.*"}), count(kube_pod_info), max(timestamp(container_cpu_usage_seconds_total)) >= window_end - scrape interval). If the scrape targets have no samples the window is failed and retried until it leaves the backfill horizon, then recorded as gap with the reason. 3) Measure the real scrape interval once per hour (count_over_time(up{job="kubelet"}[10m])) and report a configuration error when the rate window holds fewer than 2 scrapes. 4) Expose coverage: GET /api/v1/collection/coverage?start=&end=, fields in /api/v1/health (watermark, lag, last gap), a gauge greenkube_collection_coverage_ratio, and a UI banner on every period that contains gap or empty windows. 5) Hourly and daily rollups store coverage = steps_with_data / expected_steps so consumers can flag or scale partial periods explicitly. 6) Backfill collection_windows for existing data from the distinct timestamps of combined_metrics, marking the rest unknown.

**Acceptance.** a) Scenario S5 produces 577 gap rows and 22 failed/gap rows with reason no_scrape_data instead of 22 empty successes, and the API coverage for the 72 h is 24/865. b) A window whose Prometheus answer is empty while up{job=kubelet} has samples and no pod runs is stored as empty. c) /api/v1/health shows the watermark lag and the last gap. d) An hourly rollup built from 19 of 120 steps carries coverage 0.158. e) The counter survives a controller restart because the API reads the table.

#### COL-05 · On clusters whose cAdvisor exports only pod-level series (cri-dockerd: Minikube Docker driver, Docker Desktop, Mirantis), container rows are Metrics API snapshots stored with usage_source=prometheus, and catch-up windows get no container rows at all.

**Severity** high · **Kind** data-integrity · **Confidence** confirmed-live · **Effort** M

**Where.** `src/greenkube/core/historical_range_processor.py:880-919`; `src/greenkube/core/metric_assembler.py:275-279`; `src/greenkube/core/metric_assembler.py:303-309`

**Mechanism.** When Prometheus has no container-level series, run_range falls back to the pod cgroup for pod rows and fills container usage from MetricsServerCollector for the latest sample of a current window. The assembler labels a container row metrics_server only when the key is in resource_maps.container_timestamp_map, but run_range builds PodResourceMaps with container_cpu_usage_map, container_memory_usage_map and container_sample_count_map only, so that branch is dead and the row inherits the pod row's usage_source 'prometheus'. The value is the Metrics API rate over metrics-server's own window (about 15-30 s), read at collection time (after the step ended), stored as the usage of the whole step; when it exceeds the pod cgroup by more than 5% it is scaled down (reason container_usage_exceeds_pod). During catch-up only the last sample of the current window is filled, so older windows have pod rows without container rows.

**Effect on numbers.** Live minikube: Prometheus returns 0 series for container_cpu_usage_seconds_total{container!=""} (196 series, none with a container label) yet container_metrics holds 19,453 rows, all usage_source='prometheus'; 9,833+ of them were rescaled. Hour 21:00 has 91 pod steps but 33 container steps; hour 04:00 has 8 versus 2. Simulation S7: Metrics API says 900m / 260Mi, the stored container row says 300m / 200Mi with usage_source=prometheus. With the default 5 min step each container value is one 15 s snapshot standing for 300 s (5% time coverage), so p95/max used for rightsizing are instantaneous readings presented as measured step averages, and gates that trust usage_source=prometheus accept them.

**Affected environments.** Every cluster where kubelet cAdvisor has no container labels: cri-dockerd runtimes (Minikube with the Docker driver, Docker Desktop Kubernetes, Mirantis Container Runtime), and pods in sandboxed runtimes. Not affected: containerd/CRI-O clusters (kind live: container series present).

**Evidence.** live.sh prom 'count(container_cpu_usage_seconds_total) by (container, job, metrics_path)' on minikube -> one group without container label (196). live.sh sql on minikube: 'select usage_source, ... from container_metrics' -> only 'prometheus'; per-hour step counts of combined_metrics versus container_metrics. Simulation audit-workspace/audit/work/usage-collection/sim_collection.py scenario f (sim_f.out): 'pod=dockerd-pod container=app cpu_m=300 mem_Mi=200 usage_source=prometheus flags=[container_usage_exceeds_pod]'.

**Fix.** 1) Carry the source per filled container: add container_usage_source_map to PodResourceMaps (or populate container_timestamp_map without overriding the row timestamp) and store usage_source='metrics_server' plus the Metrics API window in a new column sample_window_seconds; when CPU comes from Prometheus and memory from the Metrics API store both sources (cpu_usage_source, memory_usage_source). 2) Consumers (rightsizing statistics, verification gates, evidence) must treat metrics_server rows as point samples: lower confidence, never as step averages; show it in the recommendation evidence. 3) Detect the capability once per run (container-level series present or not) and expose it in /api/v1/health and the UI: 'container usage is sampled from the Metrics API because cAdvisor exports pod-level series only'. 4) Offer the measured alternative: when kubelet /metrics/resource is scraped (ServiceMonitor resource endpoint) its container_cpu_usage_seconds_total has container labels; document enabling it for cri-dockerd and prefer it. 5) Catch-up windows on such clusters must be marked pod_level_usage_only in the window table instead of silently lacking container rows. 6) Migration: on clusters detected as pod-level-only, set usage_source='metrics_server' on existing container rows.

**Acceptance.** Scenario S7 stores usage_source='metrics_server' for the container row and 'prometheus' for the pod row. A cluster with container series is unchanged. The health endpoint reports container_usage_source=metrics_server on the Minikube environment. A verification gate requiring Prometheus usage rejects or downgrades these rows in a unit test.

#### COL-06 · A single failed instant query makes PrometheusCollector replace the configured Prometheus URL with a discovered one for the rest of the process, and discovery probes send the configured bearer token or basic credentials to every candidate service.

**Severity** medium · **Kind** security · **Confidence** confirmed-sim · **Effort** S

**Where.** `src/greenkube/collectors/prometheus_collector.py:94-142`; `src/greenkube/collectors/prometheus_collector.py:182-219`; `src/greenkube/collectors/discovery/prometheus.py:52-80`

**Mechanism.** _query_run_loop (used by collect_pod_storage in every run_range) tries three URL shapes; when all fail (timeout, 5xx, 4xx) it calls _discover_and_update_url, which takes the first discovered service that answers `up`, then `_update_url` sets self.base_url and `self.settings.PROMETHEUS_URL = url` on the shared Config singleton and disables TLS verification for http URLs. Later range queries of the same run and every later PrometheusCollector(cfg) use the new endpoint. PrometheusDiscovery._probe_prometheus_endpoint and _probe_url attach `Authorization: Bearer <PROMETHEUS_BEARER_TOKEN>` or basic auth to each probe.

**Effect on numbers.** After one slow or failing instant query, all usage is read from another Prometheus (for example an Istio or Linkerd add-on Prometheus, an agent, a second tenant) that may have no cAdvisor series, other relabeling or short retention: windows become empty or different and are committed (see COL-04). The switch is logged at INFO only and survives until the pod restarts.

**Affected environments.** Installs with an explicit PROMETHEUS_URL (external Mimir/Thanos/managed Prometheus, or one of several in-cluster Prometheus servers) when another service matching the discovery heuristics exists. Single kube-prometheus installs switch to the same server under another DNS name (harmless).

**Evidence.** audit-workspace/audit/work/usage-collection/sim_url_switch.py output: 'before: collector.base_url = https://mimir.example.com/prometheus', 'after : collector.base_url = http://prometheus.istio-system.svc.cluster.local:9090 | cfg.PROMETHEUS_URL = http://prometheus.istio-system.svc.cluster.local:9090 | verify = False', 'range query after the switch went to: http://prometheus.istio-system...', 'hosts that received the bearer token: [mimir.example.com, prometheus.istio-system.svc.cluster.local:9090]', 'a NEW collector built from the same Config now uses: http://prometheus.istio-system...'.

**Fix.** 1) Discovery runs only when PROMETHEUS_URL is empty. With a configured URL a failure is returned to the caller (instant queries return an error state, range queries raise PrometheusQueryError). 2) Never mutate Config from a collector; keep a discovered URL in the collector and record it in the health endpoint with its origin (configured | discovered). 3) Never send configured credentials to a discovered endpoint; credentials are bound to the configured URL host. 4) A discovered endpoint must pass a data check before use (count(container_cpu_usage_seconds_total) > 0) and the choice is logged at WARNING and exported as an info metric. 5) Keep the TLS verification setting of the configuration.

**Acceptance.** The simulation shows base_url and cfg.PROMETHEUS_URL unchanged after the failure, the instant query reports an error, and only mimir.example.com received the Authorization header. With PROMETHEUS_URL empty, discovery still works and sends no credentials.

#### COL-07 · A failed Kubernetes pod or node list is swallowed (collectors return an empty result), and the window is stored with request 0, owner NULL, no container rows and default zone/PUE, then committed.

**Severity** medium · **Kind** reliability · **Confidence** confirmed-sim · **Effort** S

**Where.** `src/greenkube/collectors/pod_collector.py:140-142`; `src/greenkube/collectors/node_collector.py:110-115`; `src/greenkube/core/historical_range_processor.py:503-539`

**Mechanism.** PodCollector.collect catches every exception and returns [] ('Return empty list on failure'); NodeCollector.collect and collect_instance_types return {}. run_range cannot tell an API error from an empty cluster, builds empty request/owner maps and node info, and writes the rows; MetricsCollectionJob advances the watermark, so the window is never repaired. Nothing in estimation_reasons mentions the missing pod metadata.

**Effect on numbers.** Simulation S4: the stored row has cpu_request=0, memory_request=0, owner=None, no container row, report.error=None and the watermark moved to 10:05. One API timeout (K8S_REQUEST_TIMEOUT 30 s) during a tick gives a full step of every pod without requests, owners and container rows; the allocation of that step uses usage only, and zone/PUE fall back to defaults for every node.

**Affected environments.** All; more frequent on large or loaded API servers, during control-plane upgrades and with network policies or RBAC changes.

**Evidence.** audit-workspace/audit/work/usage-collection/sim_collection.py scenario d output: 'stored row pod=alive container=None cpu_request=0 memory_request=0 owner=None is_estimated=True', 'report.error=None windows_collected=1 watermark=2026-11-07 10:05:00+00:00'.

**Fix.** PodCollector and NodeCollector raise a typed error (or return a result object with ok=False) on API failure. run_range raises RangeCollectionError when the pod list or node list failed and no snapshot newer than one step is available (see COL-01 snapshot table); with a recent snapshot it proceeds and flags the rows metadata_source=snapshot. An empty but successful list remains valid. Add a counter greenkube_collection_k8s_list_errors_total.

**Acceptance.** Scenario S4 with a raising pod collector: report.error is set, no row is written and the watermark stays at 10:00; the next tick with a working API stores cpu_request=500 and the owner.

#### COL-08 · When the carbon-intensity lookup raises, every pod row of the (step, node) group is skipped without log, metric or error and the window is committed.

**Severity** medium · **Kind** reliability · **Confidence** confirmed-sim · **Effort** S

**Where.** `src/greenkube/core/historical_range_processor.py:743-751`; `src/greenkube/core/historical_range_processor.py:599`

**Mechanism.** `carbon_result = await calculator.calculate_emissions(...)` is wrapped in `except Exception: carbon_result = None`, followed by `skipped_carbon += len(pod_keys); continue`. skipped_carbon is never read again. Usage, cost and energy rows of the group are lost although only the CO2e factor was unavailable.

**Effect on numbers.** Simulation S10: rows stored=0, report.error=None, windows_collected=1, watermark advanced. A transient error on the intensity table or cache during one tick removes one step of all pods of the affected nodes (the whole cluster when all nodes share a zone).

**Affected environments.** All; triggered by database errors or bugs on the carbon-intensity read path.

**Evidence.** audit-workspace/audit/work/usage-collection/sim_collection.py scenario h (sim_h.out): 'rows stored=0 report.error=None windows_collected=1 rows_written=0 watermark=2026-11-07 10:05:00+00:00'.

**Fix.** Do not drop rows: on an exception raise RangeCollectionError so the window is retried; if the error persists for more than N attempts store the rows with co2e NULL, intensity_source='unavailable' and a reason, to be filled by the existing intensity recompute job. Log at ERROR and count greenkube_collection_rows_skipped_total{reason}. Remove the dead counter.

**Acceptance.** Scenario S10: report.error is set and the watermark does not move; after the lookup works again the window has its rows. Unit test for the degraded mode: rows present with co2e NULL and intensity_source='unavailable'.

#### COL-09 · The pod-cgroup fallback is used only when the container query returns nothing for the whole cluster, so in a cluster mixing both kinds of nodes or runtimes every pod that has pod-level series only disappears, together with the idle rows of its node.

**Severity** medium · **Kind** bug · **Confidence** confirmed-sim · **Effort** S

**Where.** `src/greenkube/core/historical_range_processor.py:275-300`; `src/greenkube/collectors/prometheus_collector.py:333-339`; `src/greenkube/collectors/promql.py:120-144`

**Mechanism.** _collect_cpu: `results = await self._query_range(chunk, container_cpu_usage()); if results: return results; return await self._query_range(chunk, pod_cgroup_cpu_usage())`; _collect_memory and PrometheusCollector.collect_range do the same. The container query filters container!="", so pods with only a pod cgroup series are absent as soon as one container-level series exists anywhere. The parser already supports mixed input (`elif key not in named_pods`), but never receives it.

**Effect on numbers.** Simulation S2: pod on the pod-level-only node has 0/12 rows, cost 0.000 of 2.400, 0 J, and its node has 0 idle rows (the node is invisible), while the other pod is complete. Cluster energy, cost and CO2e are understated by the share of those pods and nodes.

**Affected environments.** Clusters mixing containerd/CRI-O nodes with cri-dockerd nodes (migrations, Mirantis), and clusters running sandboxed pods (gVisor/GKE Sandbox, Kata) next to runc pods, since cAdvisor only sees the sandbox cgroup of those pods.

**Evidence.** audit-workspace/audit/work/usage-collection/sim_collection.py scenario b output: 'pod on-cri-dockerd pod_rows= 0/12 cpu_m=[] cost=0.000 joules=0.0', 'idle rows per node: {n1: 12, n2: 0}'. The same effect appeared unplanned in scenario f when both pod kinds were in one fixture.

**Fix.** Query both levels in one expression and choose per pod: CPU `max by (namespace,pod,container,node) (rate(container_cpu_usage_seconds_total{pod!="",container!="POD"}[w]))` (container="" is the pod cgroup), same for memory; drop the empty-result fallbacks in _collect_cpu, _collect_memory and PrometheusCollector.collect_range. Keep the existing per-pod rule (containers when present, else pod cgroup). Use the pod cgroup as an invariant for pods with containers: when the container sum is below the pod cgroup by more than a tolerance, flag container_series_incomplete.

**Acceptance.** Scenario S2: on-cri-dockerd has 12 rows with 700 millicores, n2 has 12 idle rows, and on-containerd still has 500 millicores (no double count; the A1 regression tests stay green).

#### COL-10 · In multi-step (catch-up or backfill) windows each row of a pod gets OpenCost cost x step / running time, so pods that ran only part of the window are over-costed.

**Severity** medium · **Kind** bug · **Confidence** confirmed-sim · **Effort** M

**Where.** `src/greenkube/collectors/opencost_collector.py:319-336`; `src/greenkube/core/historical_range_processor.py:573-577`; `src/greenkube/core/historical_range_processor.py:771-776`

**Mechanism.** run_range asks OpenCost once for the whole window and gives every row of the pod `min(1, step / (minutes * 60))` of the window cost. The number of rows is the number of steps with a CPU sample, which exceeds running_time / step whenever the pod covers partial steps. Scheduled single-step windows are exact (share 1); the error appears in the 1-hour chunks used after downtime and for the first-install backfill.

**Effect on numbers.** Simulation S9: a pod that ran 7 minutes across two steps, OpenCost cost 0.70: two rows of 0.50 each = 1.00 stored (+43%). General factor: rows x step / running time, for example 31 minutes over 7 steps = +13%. Short-lived pods in every backfilled hour are over-costed, while pods without rows are dropped (COL-03), so the backfilled total can be wrong in both directions.

**Affected environments.** All installs with OpenCost, for windows collected in catch-up mode (after controller downtime, first install, --last).

**Evidence.** audit-workspace/audit/work/usage-collection/sim_collection.py scenario g output: 'pod job-7min rows= 2 stored cost=1.0000 per-row=0.5000', 'OpenCost says job-7min cost 0.7000 for the window'.

**Fix.** Query OpenCost with the collection step for multi-step windows (window=start,end with step=<step> and accumulate=false) and use each step's own allocation; if the step is finer than OpenCost's resolution, split the window allocation by the pod's running seconds inside each step (overlap of the allocation start/end with the step), normalised so that the shares of one pod sum to 1, and put the share of steps without a row into the unattributed row of COL-03.

**Acceptance.** Scenario S9 stores 0.70 +/- 1% for job-7min (0.30 and 0.40 by running seconds) and 1.20 for the steady pod; a property test checks that the per-pod stored cost of a window equals the OpenCost allocation.

#### COL-11 · CPU is aggregated with max over all series of a container, so consecutive container instances inside one rate window (restart, crash loop, pod recreated under the same name) are not added and CPU is under-counted.

**Severity** medium · **Kind** bug · **Confidence** confirmed-live · **Effort** M

**Where.** `src/greenkube/collectors/promql.py:120-125`; `src/greenkube/core/historical_range_processor.py:177-200`

**Mechanism.** container_cpu_usage() is `max(rate(container_cpu_usage_seconds_total{...}[w])) by (namespace,pod,container,node)`. The comment lists 'the old and new cgroup of a restarted container' as duplicates, but they are consecutive in time: each series' rate covers its own part of the window and the true usage is their sum. max keeps only the larger one.

**Effect on numbers.** Live kind, pod gk-upgrade/gkup-greenkube-postgres-0 (container replaced at 22:05:24 -> 22:05:54): max versus sum of the two cgroup series at 22:06:00, 22:06:30 and 22:07:00 = 4.3 / 6.3, 6.0 / 7.5 and 6.8 / 7.5 millicores; stored 4, 6, 7: -20% over the handover. For a container that restarts every minute with the default 5-minute window, up to five instances share a window and only the largest is counted (up to -80%); CPU rightsizing of unstable containers therefore sees too little usage.

**Affected environments.** All container-level clusters; visible on workloads with restarts, OOM kills, crash loops and StatefulSet pod recreation.

**Evidence.** live.sh promrange on kind with max(...) and sum(...) for that pod between 22:03 and 22:10, the two raw series by id, and the stored cpu_usage_millicores of the same steps (2,2,2,2,2,2,4,6,7,7,3,...).

**Fix.** Deduplicate per container instance, then add instances: `sum by (namespace,pod,container,node) (max by (namespace,pod,container,node,name) (rate(container_cpu_usage_seconds_total{container!="",container!="POD",name!=""}[w])))` for cAdvisor series (name is the runtime container id: equal for duplicate cgroup paths and scrape jobs, different for consecutive instances), `or` the existing max expression restricted to name="" for sources without that label (kubelet /metrics/resource). Keep the Python-side max between the two sources. Memory keeps max. Bump CALCULATION_VERSION.

**Acceptance.** FakePrometheus (extended to evaluate the nested aggregation) or a recorded Prometheus fixture: two series of one container with different name and rates 0.3 and 0.2 in one window give 500 millicores; two series with the same name and different id give the max; a /metrics/resource duplicate does not double. The live handover example stores 6, 8, 8.

#### COL-12 · Memory is stored as one instant value per step (the working set at the end of the step), so peaks between two evaluation points are invisible to rightsizing.

**Severity** medium · **Kind** design · **Confidence** confirmed-live · **Effort** M

**Where.** `src/greenkube/collectors/promql.py:132-144`; `src/greenkube/core/metric_assembler.py:290-291`

**Mechanism.** container_memory_working_set() is `max(container_memory_working_set_bytes{...}) by (...)` evaluated at each step boundary; with the default 5-minute step one scrape out of ten is kept. memory_usage_max_bytes of raw container rows is always None, and later maxima are maxima of these point samples.

**Effect on numbers.** Using the live kind Prometheus data with the default 5-minute step over 90 minutes: container oversized-worker peaks at 3.7Mi in the scrapes but 0.3Mi in the 5-minute points (ratio 0.09); greenkube-renderer 61.3Mi versus 53.2Mi (0.87); 2 of 35 containers lose more than 10% of their peak. A memory request derived from the sampled maximum can be below the real peak, which is the OOM risk the product must avoid.

**Affected environments.** All with a step larger than the scrape interval (default 5m versus 30s).

**Evidence.** live.sh promrange/prom on kind: range query at step 300 versus max_over_time(...[90m]) (audit-workspace/audit/work/usage-collection/kind_mem_5m.json, kind_mem_max.json) and the comparison printed by the audit script.

**Fix.** Collect two memory figures per step: the average for allocation, `max by (...) (avg_over_time(container_memory_working_set_bytes{...}[step]))`, stored in memory_usage_bytes, and the peak, `max by (...) (max_over_time(container_memory_working_set_bytes{...}[step]))`, stored in memory_usage_max_bytes on pod and container rows; rollups take MAX of the peak. Rightsizing and risk checks must use the peak. Do the same for CPU peaks if finer than the step is wanted (max_over_time of a sub-query rate). Old rows keep NULL peak and are treated as lower confidence.

**Acceptance.** Fixture with a container at 100Mi except one scrape at 400Mi inside a step: memory_usage_max_bytes = 400Mi and memory_usage_bytes about 130Mi for that step; the memory recommendation never goes below 400Mi plus headroom.

#### COL-13 · Persistent-volume request and usage per pod are joined by PVC name without the namespace, so same-named claims in different namespaces overwrite each other.

**Severity** medium · **Kind** bug · **Confidence** confirmed-sim · **Effort** S

**Where.** `src/greenkube/collectors/prometheus_collector.py:243-270`

**Mechanism.** _pvc_values builds `values[pvc] = float(value)` keyed by the persistentvolumeclaim label only, for kube_persistentvolumeclaim_resource_requests_storage_bytes and kubelet_volume_stats_used_bytes; the pod mapping then reads `request_by_pvc.get(pvc)`. The last series returned wins. The values are also the current ones, applied to every historical sample of the window.

**Effect on numbers.** Simulation: prod/postgres-0 (500Gi requested, 420Gi used) and staging/postgres-0 (10Gi, 1Gi) both get request 10Gi and usage 1Gi. StatefulSet claims (data-<name>-0) repeat across namespaces in most multi-environment clusters, so storage_request_bytes and storage_usage_bytes are wrong by arbitrary factors for those pods.

**Affected environments.** All clusters with kube-state-metrics where a PVC name exists in more than one namespace.

**Evidence.** audit-workspace/audit/work/usage-collection/sim_pvc.py output: "('prod', 'postgres-0') {'request': '10Gi', 'usage': '1Gi'}", "('staging', 'postgres-0') {'request': '10Gi', 'usage': '1Gi'}".

**Fix.** Key both value maps by (namespace, persistentvolumeclaim) and look them up with the namespace of the pod mapping series. Aggregate duplicates with max. For historical windows use range queries on the chunk instead of the instant value.

**Acceptance.** The simulation returns 500Gi/420Gi for prod and 10Gi/1Gi for staging.

#### COL-14 · Network figures of hostNetwork pods are the node's traffic, repeated for every hostNetwork pod of the node, and the stored values are bytes per second in columns and metrics named *_bytes.

**Severity** medium · **Kind** bug · **Confidence** confirmed-live · **Effort** M

**Where.** `src/greenkube/collectors/promql.py:168-178`; `src/greenkube/core/historical_range_processor.py:821-824`; `src/greenkube/api/metrics_endpoint.py:109-126`

**Mechanism.** pod_network_receive/transmit are `sum(rate(container_network_*_bytes_total[w])) by (namespace,pod,node)` without interface filter. For a pod in the host network namespace cAdvisor reports the node's interfaces (nine per pod on kind: eth0, tunnels...), so each such pod carries the whole node traffic. The result is a rate, stored in network_receive_bytes / network_transmit_bytes / disk_read_bytes / disk_write_bytes and exported as greenkube_pod_network_receive_bytes.

**Effect on numbers.** Live kind at 22:30:00, control-plane node: etcd 13,676, kube-apiserver 13,668, cilium 13,608, cilium-envoy 13,590, kube-controller-manager 13,520, kube-scheduler 13,438 B/s received: the same node traffic counted six times (about 81 kB/s reported for 13.6 kB/s real); three times on worker2. Namespace and cluster network totals are inflated by the number of hostNetwork pods per node (kube-proxy, CNI agents, node-exporter exist on every cluster). A reader of the column name takes a rate for a byte count.

**Affected environments.** All container-level clusters. On cri-dockerd (Minikube) network and disk are NULL for every pod (0 of 22,669 rows).

**Evidence.** live.sh sql on kind: network_receive_bytes of kube-system pods at 2026-11-14 22:30:00; live.sh prom count by (pod, interface) of container_network_receive_bytes_total; kubectl custom-columns HOSTNET.

**Fix.** 1) Read host-network status per pod (kube_pod_info{host_network="true"} or the pod spec in the snapshot of COL-01) and store NULL network values with reason host_network_shared for those pods; optionally store the node traffic once on the node idle row. 2) Exclude non-physical interfaces for the others (interface!~"lo|veth.*|cali.*|lxc.*|cilium.*|docker.*|flannel.*|cni.*|tun.*|gre.*|sit.*|ip6.*|erspan.*"). 3) Rename the fields to *_bytes_per_second in the model, API and exported metrics (keep the old metric names as deprecated aliases for one release) or convert to bytes per step (rate x step) consistently; document the unit in docs and UI.

**Acceptance.** Fixture with two hostNetwork pods and one normal pod on a node: hostNetwork pods have NULL network with the reason, the normal pod has its eth0 rate, and the namespace sum equals the normal pod. API field names carry the unit.

#### COL-15 · Cloud provider detection classifies GKE nodes in us-* regions, and Linode, Oracle, IBM and Alibaba nodes, as AWS, and misses AWS nodes without EKS labels outside five region prefixes.

**Severity** medium · **Kind** portability · **Confidence** confirmed-sim · **Effort** S

**Where.** `src/greenkube/collectors/node_collector.py:158-197`

**Mechanism.** _detect_cloud_provider tests, before the GCP check, `'node.kubernetes.io/instance-type' in labels and region.startswith(('us-','eu-','ap-','ca-','sa-'))` and returns 'aws'. GKE regions us-central1, us-east1, us-east4, us-west1... match, as do Linode us-east, OCI eu-frankfurt-1, IBM eu-de, Alibaba ap-southeast-1. AWS regions me-*, af-*, il-*, mx-*, cn-* without eks.amazonaws.com labels (kOps, self-managed, some Karpenter nodes) return 'unknown'.

**Effect on numbers.** Simulation: GKE us-central1 and us-east4 -> provider aws, PUE 1.15 instead of GCP 1.09 (+5.5% on energy and CO2e, not flagged as estimated because the provider is 'known'); instance power and embodied profiles are looked up in the AWS catalogue with GCP machine types and fall back. Linode, OCI, IBM, Alibaba -> aws PUE 1.15 instead of a flagged default. AWS me-south-1 / af-south-1 -> unknown, PUE 1.3 instead of 1.15 (+13%).

**Affected environments.** GKE in US regions; Linode LKE, Oracle OKE, IBM IKS, Alibaba ACK and any provider whose region names start with us-/eu-/ap-/ca-/sa-; AWS non-EKS nodes in other partitions.

**Evidence.** audit-workspace/audit/work/usage-collection/sim_provider.py output: 'GKE us-central1 -> provider=aws emaps_zone=US-MIDW-MISO pue=1.15', 'GKE europe-west1 -> provider=gcp ... pue=1.09', 'Linode LKE us-east -> provider=aws', 'OCI OKE eu-frankfurt-1 -> provider=aws', 'EKS me-south-1 (karpenter, no eks label) -> provider=unknown pue=1.3'.

**Fix.** Detect the provider from node.spec.providerID first (aws://, gce://, azure://, ovh / openstack://, scaleway://, linode://, oci / ocid1., ibm://, alicloud...), then from provider-specific label prefixes (cloud.google.com/, eks.amazonaws.com/, karpenter.k8s.aws/, kubernetes.azure.com/, k8s.ovh.net/, node.k8s.ovh/, k8s.scaleway.com/, lke.linode.com/, oci.oraclecloud.com/, ibm-cloud.kubernetes.io/), and remove the region-prefix heuristic. Let CLOUD_PROVIDER in the configuration override detection. Unknown stays unknown and flagged. Recompute stored rows of affected clusters (provider, PUE, power source) under a new CALCULATION_VERSION.

**Acceptance.** The nine label sets of sim_provider.py give gcp, gcp, gcp, linode/unknown, oci/unknown, ibm/unknown, alibaba/unknown, aws, aws; a node with providerID gce://... and no cloud.google.com label is gcp.

#### COL-16 · Workload owners are derived by cutting the last dash-separated token of the ReplicaSet name and calling the result a Deployment, and Job pods keep the Job as owner, so Argo Rollouts and bare ReplicaSets get a non-existent Deployment and every CronJob run is a separate workload.

**Severity** medium · **Kind** bug · **Confidence** confirmed-code · **Effort** M

**Where.** `src/greenkube/collectors/pod_collector.py:110-127`

**Mechanism.** `if owner_kind == 'ReplicaSet': parts = owner_name.rsplit('-', 1); owner_kind = 'Deployment'; owner_name = parts[0]`. The ReplicaSet's own ownerReferences are never read. A ReplicaSet owned by an Argo Rollout `web` (web-6d5f7c9b8) becomes Deployment/web; a bare ReplicaSet `my-rs` becomes Deployment/my. A pod owned by Job `report-28912345` (created by CronJob report) is stored as Job/report-28912345. The automation layer does support Rollout targets (automation/actions/workloads.py), so the usage rows and the targets disagree.

**Effect on numbers.** Usage, cost and recommendations of Rollout-managed pods are attributed to a Deployment that does not exist in the cluster or in Git: in-house rightsizing cannot be delivered and signals for Rollout/web find no usage evidence. CronJob usage is split into one workload per run (unbounded workload cardinality, no history across runs, no recommendation for the CronJob template).

**Affected environments.** Clusters using Argo Rollouts, bare ReplicaSets or other ReplicaSet-owning controllers, and CronJobs.

**Evidence.** Code reading of pod_collector.py:113-127: the owner stored on usage rows comes only from this function, which never reads the ownerReferences of the ReplicaSet or the Job.

**Fix.** Resolve the real controller chain: list ReplicaSets and Jobs once per tick (apps/v1 and batch/v1, paginated) and map pod -> ReplicaSet -> its controller ownerReference (Deployment, Rollout, or none: keep ReplicaSet), pod -> Job -> CronJob when the Job has a CronJob controller owner. With kube-state-metrics, kube_replicaset_owner and kube_job_owner give the same for history. Store owner_api_version too. Migration: re-map stored rows whose owner is Deployment/<x> when no such Deployment exists but a Rollout <x> does, and rows with Job/<cronjob>-<suffix> to CronJob/<cronjob>.

**Acceptance.** Unit tests with fake pods: Rollout-owned ReplicaSet -> ('Rollout','web'); bare ReplicaSet 'my-rs' -> ('ReplicaSet','my-rs'); Deployment-owned -> ('Deployment','web'); CronJob Job -> ('CronJob','report'); standalone Job -> ('Job', name).

#### COL-17 · Container rows are all-or-nothing per pod: when one container of the pod spec has no usage sample at the step, no container row is written for any container of that pod.

**Severity** medium · **Kind** bug · **Confidence** confirmed-sim · **Effort** S

**Where.** `src/greenkube/core/metric_assembler.py:214-221`

**Mechanism.** `has_container_usage = all(container_key in cpu_map or container_key in memory_map for container_key in container_keys)`; `if not has_container_usage: continue`. container_keys are all steady-state containers of the current pod spec (including native sidecars). A container that is waiting (ImagePullBackOff, CrashLoopBackOff back-off), finished, or just restarted has no series for the step.

**Effect on numbers.** Simulation S8: pod with containers app (series present, 250m) and sidecar (no series): 0 container rows. The healthy container loses its usage history for every such step, so its rightsizing statistics have holes or the workload gets no recommendation; pod-level totals are unaffected.

**Affected environments.** All container-level clusters; pods with a failing or intermittently running sidecar or secondary container.

**Evidence.** audit-workspace/audit/work/usage-collection/sim_collection.py scenario f with SIM_F_VARIANT=containers (sim_f_containers.out; same line in the first full run, sim_collection.out): 'container rows of two-ctr: 0 (spec has app + sidecar; only app has series)'.

**Fix.** Write a container row for every container that has a sample; containers without a sample get no row (or a row with NULL usage and reason no_sample). Compute cost and energy shares over the containers that have samples plus the request-based share of the others, so that the container rows still sum to the pod row.

**Acceptance.** Scenario S8 yields one container row for app with 250 millicores and the sum of container joules and cost equals the pod row within rounding.

#### COL-18 · Sum-based queries (network, disk, restarts, ephemeral storage) double when the same series is visible twice, and partial responses of Thanos/Mimir are accepted as complete.

**Severity** low · **Kind** portability · **Confidence** confirmed-code · **Effort** S

**Where.** `src/greenkube/collectors/promql.py:146-190`; `src/greenkube/collectors/prometheus_collector.py:320-339`

**Mechanism.** CPU and memory use max and are safe, but pod_network_*, pod_disk_*, pod_ephemeral_storage_usage and pod_restarts use sum(...) by (namespace,pod[,node]) and therefore add replicas when the backend exposes both copies (Thanos Query without replica-label deduplication, Mimir or VictoriaMetrics without HA dedup, two kube-state-metrics replicas, kubelet scraped by two jobs). collect_range only checks status == 'success' and ignores the 'warnings' field, so a partial answer from a store that is down is stored and committed.

**Effect on numbers.** restart_count, network and disk rates are multiplied by the number of visible copies (usually x2); with a partial response an arbitrary subset of pods is missing for the window, permanently.

**Affected environments.** HA Prometheus behind Thanos/Mimir/VictoriaMetrics without deduplication; duplicated exporters. Not reproduced live (both environments have a single Prometheus).

**Evidence.** Code reading of the PromQL catalogue and collect_range. Live kind shows no duplicate series (count by (namespace,pod,container) > 1 is empty).

**Fix.** Wrap the sum queries with an inner max over the identity labels of one source series: network `sum by (namespace,pod,node) (max by (namespace,pod,node,interface) (rate(...)))`, disk and ephemeral `sum by (...) (max by (namespace,pod,node,container,device) (...))`, restarts `sum by (namespace,pod) (max by (namespace,pod,container,uid) (kube_pod_container_status_restarts_total))`. Send dedup=true and partial_response=false to Thanos-compatible endpoints (harmless for Prometheus) and treat a response carrying warnings as a failed window. Add PROMETHEUS_EXTRA_MATCHERS to the configuration (the catalogue already supports extra_matchers) so multi-cluster backends can be filtered by cluster label.

**Acceptance.** FakePrometheus with every series duplicated under a replica label returns the same stored values as without duplicates; a response with status success and warnings raises PrometheusQueryError.

#### COL-19 · Ephemeral-storage usage is never collected on CRI runtimes, and network and disk are absent on cri-dockerd; the product does not tell the user that these dimensions are unavailable.

**Severity** low · **Kind** portability · **Confidence** confirmed-live · **Effort** M

**Where.** `src/greenkube/collectors/promql.py:146-162`

**Mechanism.** pod_ephemeral_storage_usage reads container_fs_usage_bytes{container!=""}; with containerd/CRI-O cAdvisor has no per-container filesystem series (kind: the metric does not exist at all), and on cri-dockerd no series has a container label. Network series on Minikube exist only for the root cgroup.

**Effect on numbers.** ephemeral_storage_usage_bytes is NULL in 14,422 of 14,422 kind rows and 22,669 of 22,669 minikube rows; network and disk are NULL in all minikube rows. The values are honest NULLs, but any feature built on them (ephemeral storage rightsizing, traffic normalisation) silently has no input.

**Affected environments.** containerd and CRI-O clusters for ephemeral storage (most clusters); cri-dockerd for network and disk.

**Evidence.** live.sh sql count(column) on both clusters; live.sh prom 'count(container_fs_usage_bytes) by (container)' on kind -> empty.

**Fix.** Read ephemeral storage from the kubelet volume/pod stats available in Prometheus (kubelet_volume_stats_* covers PVCs only; for ephemeral usage use the kubelet Summary API, /api/v1/nodes/<node>/proxy/stats/summary, which reports per-pod ephemeral-storage usedBytes and needs the nodes/proxy get permission in the chart RBAC). Add a capability report (per metric family: available / missing, with the reason) to /api/v1/health and the UI, computed from one probe query per family at each run.

**Acceptance.** On kind the health capability report says ephemeral_storage_usage: missing (no container_fs_usage_bytes series); with the Summary API source enabled the column is populated for a pod writing to an emptyDir.

#### COL-20 · restart_count stores the lifetime restart counter of the pod, and consumers read it as restarts of the observed period.

**Severity** low · **Kind** design · **Confidence** confirmed-code · **Effort** S

**Where.** `src/greenkube/collectors/promql.py:184-185`; `src/greenkube/core/optimization/evidence.py:300`; `src/greenkube/core/optimization/risks.py:209-213`; `src/greenkube/core/sustainability_score.py:444-451`

**Mechanism.** pod_restarts() is `sum(kube_pod_container_status_restarts_total) by (namespace,pod)`: a cumulative value since pod creation, stored on every row. Rollups correctly use MAX, but evidence takes max(restart_count) of the series and risks.py returns HIGH 'restart_history' for a memory decrease as soon as it is non-zero; the stability score averages the cumulative values.

**Effect on numbers.** A pod restarted once months ago (node reboot) is rated HIGH risk for every memory reduction until it is recreated; live minikube rows carry values up to 216. The stability score penalises long-lived pods for old restarts. Restarts that happened inside the window cannot be distinguished from old ones.

**Affected environments.** All with kube-state-metrics.

**Evidence.** Code reading; live minikube: e2e-oom pods rows with restart_count 3..5, max 216 in the table.

**Fix.** Store the per-step delta next to the cumulative value: `sum by (namespace,pod) (increase(kube_pod_container_status_restarts_total[step]))` in a new column restarts_in_step (rollups SUM it), keep restart_count as cumulative with MAX. Evidence and risk use the sum of restarts_in_step over the lookback window, per container when available, with the termination reason from kube_pod_container_status_last_terminated_reason.

**Acceptance.** A pod with a constant counter of 5 over the window has 0 restarts in evidence and no restart_history risk; a counter going 5 -> 7 yields 2.

#### COL-21 · Tests cannot detect PromQL semantic errors: the fake Prometheus takes pre-computed rates and the alternative label names handled in _series_identity can never occur.

**Severity** low · **Kind** test-gap · **Confidence** confirmed-code · **Effort** M

**Where.** `tests/core/fake_prometheus.py:1-60`; `src/greenkube/core/historical_range_processor.py:156-163`

**Mechanism.** FakePrometheus receives 'already-computed per-second rates', evaluates one aggregation level and ignores range windows, staleness, counter resets and series lifetimes; tests therefore pass whatever the rate window, the max/sum choice across restarts or the behaviour of short-lived series. _series_identity accepts kubernetes_namespace, namespace_name, pod_name, kubernetes_pod_name, container_name and kubernetes_node, but every query aggregates `by (namespace,pod,container,node)`, so those labels are removed before the parser sees them (dead code that suggests support for setups that would in fact yield no data).

**Effect on numbers.** None directly; this is why COL-03, COL-09, COL-11 and COL-12 passed the test suite. A cluster whose series use other label names gets empty windows committed as success (COL-04).

**Affected environments.** Development and CI.

**Evidence.** Reading tests/core/fake_prometheus.py and the catalogue; live comparison of max versus sum on the kind Prometheus.

**Fix.** Add an integration test tier that runs the catalogue against a real Prometheus (promtool tsdb create-blocks-from openmetrics with recorded cAdvisor/kube-state-metrics fixtures: container restart, short-lived pod, duplicate scrape job, pod-level-only node, hostNetwork pod, HA duplicate) and asserts stored core-seconds against the counter deltas. Remove the dead label alternatives or implement label-name configuration (PROMETHEUS_LABEL_NAMESPACE/POD/CONTAINER/NODE) applied in the catalogue's by() clauses and selectors.

**Acceptance.** The integration suite fails on the current max-across-restarts query and on a 40-second pod, and passes after COL-03 and COL-11 are fixed; configuring a custom node label name returns rows grouped by that node.

### 5.2 History reconstruction (experiment)

**Coverage.** Ran (all scripts and temporary SQLite databases are in audit-workspace/audit/work/reconstruction-experiment): E1 real range collection for 2026-11-14 23:10-23:30 against both Prometheus replicas of the Minikube cluster and OpenCost, compared field by field with the live Postgres rows (2204 pod rows, 40 idle rows, 2764 container rows); E2 same window twice into one database; E3 one 20-minute run versus 4 x 5 minutes versus 40 x 30 seconds, and at the production step one 1-hour chunk versus 12 x 5 minutes; E4 steps 30 s, 60 s and 300 s on a steady and a churning window; E5 window 21:40-22:00 containing 15 pods that no longer exist, plus two extra simulations (node absent from the API, re-collection over rows collected live); E6 the real MetricsCollectionJob with an injected clock and a watermark older than COLLECTION_MAX_BACKFILL, and with Prometheus unreachable; E7 the real MetricsCompressor on 40 h of synthetic 5-minute rows written through the real SQLite repository (conservation, second run, late rows, reads with aggregate_summary, aggregate_timeseries and aggregate_by_namespace at three clock positions, prune at now + 8 days); E8 collection with the OpenCost URL on a closed port and a later re-collection. Read in full: core/historical_range_processor.py, core/collection.py, core/metrics_compressor.py, collectors/opencost_collector.py, collectors/promql.py, cli/utils.py; in part: cli/start.py (loops, initial tasks), core/factory.py, core/processor.py, core/config.py (collection settings, cluster name detection), core/calculator.py, core/metric_assembler.py (container assembly), storage/sqlite/repository.py (writes, aggregate reads), storage/postgres/repository.py (cutoff expressions and upserts only), collectors/prometheus_collector.py. Live read-only queries on both clusters (Postgres, Prometheus, OpenCost, controller logs, two API GETs). Context that limits the evidence: the live Minikube data is contiguous only for 2026-11-14 21:14:30-23:54:30 UTC (the host slept afterwards), so all live comparisons use that period; both live images were built just before commit b0cbff2f (cluster name detection), all other collection code equals HEAD. NOT covered: CO2e with real Electricity Maps intensities (no token was used, both sides ran on the FR zone default, so provisional versus consolidated intensity was not compared); the PostgreSQL versions of the compressor SQL and of the aggregate reads were read, not executed (no hourly rows exist yet in the live databases); no local collection against the kind cluster (only live SQL); E4 only on 20-minute windows; windows completed with partial Prometheus data were reasoned about, not reproduced; the workload usage rollup, summary refresher caches, leader fencing and multi-replica controllers were not examined; the repository test suite was not run.

**Earlier defects not fully fixed.**

- BUG-03 (partially_fixed): Half-open windows, watermark and catch-up work (no skipped or duplicated step, --last re-collects); downtime longer than COLLECTION_MAX_BACKFILL is still a permanent, unrecorded gap (RECON-04).
- BUG-06 (partially_fixed): The intensity recompute now loops over combined_metrics and container_metrics (code read, not run); node_idle_metrics is not included (RECON-13).
- BUG-08 (partially_fixed): Power profile fallback is flagged and derived from capacity or snapshots; zone, PUE and embodied profile of a node absent from the API still fall back to defaults (RECON-05). Missing node label case not tested.
