- BUG-41 (partially_fixed): The trailing 1-day cost divided by steps is gone (exact window); new pods still get a trusted zero cost from zero-duration allocations (RECON-11) and catch-up chunks over-count partial pods (RECON-08).

#### RECON-01 · Backfilled or re-collected rows take pod requests, owner and PVC data from the Kubernetes API as it is at collection time: rows of pods that no longer exist lose their owner, carry request 0 and 73% less energy, and a re-collection overwrites correct live rows with these degraded values.

**Severity** high · **Kind** data-integrity · **Confidence** confirmed-live · **Effort** L

**Where.** `src/greenkube/core/historical_range_processor.py:508-531`; `src/greenkube/core/historical_range_processor.py:581-586`; `src/greenkube/core/historical_range_processor.py:789-795`; `src/greenkube/core/historical_range_processor.py:808-809`; `src/greenkube/core/historical_range_processor.py:825-831`; `src/greenkube/storage/sqlite/repository.py:335-378`; `src/greenkube/storage/postgres/repository.py:274`

**Mechanism.** run_range builds pod_request_map, pod_mem_map and pod_owner_map once per call from pod_collector.collect(), i.e. the pods listed by the Kubernetes API now, and applies them to every historical step: `cpu_request=pod_request_map.get(pod_key, 0)`, `memory_request=pod_mem_map.get(pod_key, 0)`, `owner = pod_owner_map.get(pod_key)`. A pod that existed during the step but is gone at collection time therefore gets owner NULL and request 0 (a real zero, not unknown). The Impact Model then allocates node energy and embodied carbon by reservation, so the row gets almost nothing and the difference moves to the node __idle__ row. The same holds for PVC data (collect_pod_storage() is an instant query at collection time stamped on all steps). Both write paths upsert with ON CONFLICT ... DO UPDATE SET every column, so re-collecting a window (greenkube start --last, a lost watermark, catch-up) replaces rows that were correct when collected live. Prometheus still holds the truth: kube_pod_owner, kube_replicaset_owner and kube_pod_container_resource_requests returned Deployment oversized-web and 0.4 core / 402653184 bytes for a deleted pod at the historical timestamp, but run_range never queries them. In live mode the same defect hits the last step of every deleted pod (the pod is gone when its last window is collected), and with step < 4 x scrape interval the 4-scrape rate window adds trailing rows after deletion (3 rows at 30 s step) that are also ownerless.

**Effect on numbers.** Window 2026-11-14 21:40-22:00 on Minikube (pods rolled), backfill versus rows collected live, 140 rows of 15 deleted pods: sum cpu_request 16090 -> 0 mCPU-steps, memory_request 2.3e10 -> 0, owner -> NULL, joules 665.0 -> 179.9 (-73%), CO2e -73%, embodied CO2e 0.0638 -> 0.0024 g (-96%), cost unchanged (x0.975, OpenCost knows deleted pods). Example oversized-web-685f69cd4f-vbk97 (request 400m/384Mi): 13.122 J -> 0.067 J per 30 s step, embodied 1.59e-3 -> 0. Namespace/workload totals of the window: pod joules -5.2% (9073 -> 8601), embodied -11.9%, sum of requests -13%; node idle row +9.5% joules and +13.9% unreserved CPU (node total conserved within 0.1%). Ownerless rows are rolled up as stand-alone Pod workloads, so a Deployment rolled during a reconstructed period loses that usage history. Default settings backfill 24 h at first install: every pod deleted in the previous 24 h (rollouts, finished Jobs that were garbage collected) is stored this way.

**Affected environments.** All environments and databases. Triggered by the first-install backfill (COLLECTION_MAX_BACKFILL, 24 h by default), catch-up after any controller downtime, greenkube start --last, any watermark reset, and in live mode the last step of each deleted pod.

**Evidence.** Simulation with the real processor: audit-workspace/audit/work/reconstruction-experiment/run_collect.py into e5_r1.db compared with live rows (compare.py live_e5_combined.jsonl e5_r1.db): owner_kind/owner_name/cpu_request/memory_request differ on 140 rows, joules ratio 0.2705 for those rows. audit-workspace/audit/work/reconstruction-experiment/e5_overwrite.py: loading the live rows then re-collecting the window drops rows_with_owner 2043 -> 1903, sum cpu_request 123420 -> 107330, joules 9073.4 -> 8600.8. Live Minikube Postgres: the first-run backfill rows (21:14:30-21:35:00) of e2e-oom-68fd4946d7-8h4jd, oversized-web-685f69cd4f-cl2np, oversized-worker-c7bb6894f-ggxgn, prod-kust-6cd9d9845-dcm7t and prod-lb-backend-79f7795b6d-r8ptc all have owner NULL and cpu_request 0 (41-42 rows each). Live kind Postgres: pod greenkube-controller-d85c4df5f-zvrqt at 21:40:00 has a pod row with owner NULL, request 0, 6.788 J while its container row (written earlier, live) has owner greenkube-controller, request 100m, 9.034 J: the pod row was overwritten by the 30-minute re-backfill logged at 21:49:44 (saved 1716 records). Prometheus query at 21:43 for the deleted pod returns its owner and requests.

**Fix.** 1) Historical spec source: in HistoricalRangeProcessor.run_range add range queries per chunk at the collection step: max by (namespace,pod,container,resource) (kube_pod_container_resource_requests{resource=~'cpu|memory'}), the same for kube_pod_container_resource_limits, kube_pod_owner{owner_is_controller='true'} joined with kube_replicaset_owner (ReplicaSet -> Deployment) and kube_job_owner (Job -> CronJob); add them to PromQLCatalog. Build per-sample maps and use them for every non-current sample; keep the Kubernetes API as the source for the current window and as fallback only when the pod's creationTimestamp is <= the sample time. 2) Carry-forward: persist a pod_spec_history table (namespace, pod, uid, owner_kind, owner_name, container, cpu_request, memory_request, cpu_limit, memory_limit, first_seen, last_seen), written at each live collection (reuse workload_spec_observations if it already holds this), and read it when neither kube-state-metrics nor the API knows the pod. 3) Unknown is not zero: make cpu_request/memory_request nullable in CombinedMetric and in combined_metrics/container_metrics (migration), store NULL plus an estimation reason pod_spec_unknown and a new column spec_source (kubernetes_api | kube_state_metrics | carried_forward | unknown). MetricRowAllocator must not treat an unknown request as a zero reservation: allocate such rows on the carried-forward value, else on usage, and flag them. 4) No-downgrade upsert in write_combined_metrics and write_container_metrics (SQLite and PostgreSQL): owner_kind, owner_name, cpu_request, memory_request, storage_* use COALESCE(EXCLUDED.x, table.x) when the incoming spec_source is unknown; when a re-collection produces no container rows for a pod-step, delete the stale container rows of that pod-step in the same transaction so pod and container rows stay consistent. 5) Replace the instant collect_pod_storage() by per-step range queries on kube_persistentvolumeclaim_resource_requests_storage_bytes, kubelet_volume_stats_used_bytes and kube_pod_spec_volumes_persistentvolumeclaims_info. 6) Repair of stored rows: a command (greenkube repair-spec --last N) that fills owner/requests of ownerless rows from kube-state-metrics history still in Prometheus, then re-runs the allocation (greenkube recompute) for the affected node-steps and refreshes workload_usage_hourly; bump CALCULATION_VERSION. 7) Document the spec provenance in docs/methodology/impact-model.md and expose spec_source in the API metric rows.

**Acceptance.** a) Fake Prometheus with CPU series for pod P during 10 steps plus kube_pod_owner/kube_replicaset_owner/kube_pod_container_resource_requests (0.4 core, 384Mi), fake Kubernetes API that does not list P: run_range returns 10 rows with owner Deployment/<name>, cpu_request 400, memory_request 402653184, spec_source kube_state_metrics, and joules equal (1e-9) to a run where the API lists P. b) Same without kube-state-metrics series: rows carry cpu_request NULL, estimation reason pod_spec_unknown, spec_source unknown, never 0. c) Write rows with owner and requests, then write rows for the same keys with unknown spec: stored owner and requests are unchanged in SQLite and PostgreSQL. d) Re-running audit-workspace/audit/work/reconstruction-experiment/run_collect.py for 2026-11-14T21:40-22:00 against the Minikube Prometheus gives 0 rows whose owner differs from the live rows and a pod-joules sum within 0.5% of the live sum.

#### RECON-02 · Raw rows written into an already compressed hour (late backfill, greenkube start --last, greenkube recompute, repaired cost) are never re-aggregated, the API never reads them, and they are deleted at raw retention; the collection-watermark guard of the compressor is not wired in production.

**Severity** high · **Kind** data-integrity · **Confidence** confirmed-sim · **Effort** M

**Where.** `src/greenkube/core/metrics_compressor.py:329-340`; `src/greenkube/core/metrics_compressor.py:365-381`; `src/greenkube/cli/start.py:251`; `src/greenkube/cli/start.py:1008-1013`; `src/greenkube/impact/recompute.py:12-15`; `src/greenkube/core/config.py:264-265`; `src/greenkube/core/config.py:291`

**Mechanism.** MetricsCompressor keeps one watermark per table and `_compression_lower_bound` resumes at that watermark: an hour below it is never read again. Any raw row that reaches such an hour later is ignored: (a) greenkube start --last 7d re-collects 7 days but only the last 24 h are still uncompressed; (b) greenkube recompute rewrites raw rows up to 7 days old and its docstring says hourly aggregates are never touched; (c) a manual re-collection that repairs an OpenCost outage (RECON-09); (d) a backfill with COLLECTION_MAX_BACKFILL > METRICS_COMPRESSION_AGE_HOURS, or a catch-up that is still running when its oldest hour turns 24 h old. The module docstring promises that a bucket is eligible only when its end is <= min(now - age, collection_watermark), but compress_metrics() builds `MetricsCompressor(get_db_manager())` without collection_watermark_provider; only tests/core/test_metrics_compressor_lossless.py passes one, so the green test does not cover the production wiring. No config validation links COLLECTION_MAX_BACKFILL and METRICS_COMPRESSION_AGE_HOURS. The read paths serve hours older than 24 h from the hourly table only (RECON-03), so the repaired or late raw rows are invisible, and _prune_raw deletes them after METRICS_RAW_RETENTION_DAYS.

**Effect on numbers.** Simulation: 40 h of synthetic rows, hour H = now-30h already compressed. Writing 12 rows of a pod missed at first collection (cost 0.06, 600 J) and re-collecting pod steady-a with a repaired cost (0.012 -> 0.036) leaves the hourly rows of H unchanged (steady-a 0.012, late pod absent). aggregate_summary keeps reporting the old figures (-4.18% of the 40 h total) and after the retention prune the hourly total is 1.927 against 2.011 actually collected: the late data is lost for good. In production: a 7-day re-collection after a pricing or OpenCost fix changes nothing the dashboards show beyond 24 h; after a model upgrade plus recompute the API mixes new-model values (last 24 h) with old-model hourly values.

**Affected environments.** All environments and both databases. Deterministic for --last, recompute and any setting with COLLECTION_MAX_BACKFILL > METRICS_COMPRESSION_AGE_HOURS; a race with default settings when an outage is close to 24 h.

**Evidence.** audit-workspace/audit/work/reconstruction-experiment/e7_compress.py (real MetricsCompressor, real SQLite repository): output 'late rows into compressed hour ... compress #3 = hours_compressed 0', hourly before == hourly after while raw now holds late-d 0.06 and steady-a 0.036; after run(now+8d): raw rows left 0, hourly cost total 1.927 versus raw truth 2.011, late hour still [partial-c, steady-a 0.012, varying-b]. grep shows MetricsCompressor( is constructed only in cli/start.py:251 and demo/runner.py:180, both without the provider.

**Fix.** 1) Dirty-hour tracking: add table metrics_dirty_hours(table_name, hour_bucket, marked_at). write_combined_metrics, write_container_metrics, the carbon recompute and impact/recompute.py insert (ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING) the hour of every row they write whose hour is below the table's compression watermark (one cheap watermark read per write batch). 2) MetricsCompressor._compress_to_hourly first re-aggregates every dirty hour from its complete raw set with the existing compress_sql (full replace of that hour: delete the hourly rows of the hour that no longer have raw rows, upsert the rest) and clears the mark, in one transaction per hour; then continues from the watermark as today. Skip a dirty hour whose raw rows were already pruned and log a warning with a counter greenkube_compression_unrepairable_hours_total. 3) _prune_raw must not delete raw rows of an hour that is still marked dirty. 4) Wire the guard: in cli/start.py compress_metrics() pass collection_watermark_provider (read collection_watermarks for the cluster and METRICS_WATERMARK_SOURCE) so hours being backfilled stay raw; add a test that builds the compressor through compress_metrics(). 5) Config validation: warn when COLLECTION_MAX_BACKFILL exceeds METRICS_COMPRESSION_AGE_HOURS unless dirty-hour tracking is active; document in docs/configuration.md. 6) After a recompute or --last, also refresh workload_usage_hourly and the summary caches for the dirty hours. Backfill for existing installs: one-off job that re-aggregates every hour whose raw rows still exist (raw retention window) and whose hourly sums differ from the raw sums.

**Acceptance.** a) e7_compress.py scenario as a unit test: after late rows are written into a compressed hour and the compressor runs, combined_metrics_hourly for that hour has the late pod with sample_count 12 and cost 0.06 and steady-a 0.036; SUM(total_cost) of hourly == SUM(total_cost) of raw for all compressed hours; a second run changes nothing. b) Compressor created through cli.start.compress_metrics() with a collection watermark at now-30h compresses nothing newer than that watermark. c) Running with now+8d prunes raw rows and the hourly total equals the raw total measured before the prune (2.011 in the scenario). d) greenkube recompute on rows 2 days old changes the value returned by aggregate_summary for that day.

#### RECON-03 · Every aggregate that spans the raw/hourly boundary uses `now - METRICS_COMPRESSION_AGE_HOURS` instead of the compression watermark: between 0 and about 2 hours of data (1 hour on average) are missing from summaries, time series, namespace and top-pod totals, and the result changes with the minute of the query.

**Severity** high · **Kind** bug · **Confidence** confirmed-sim · **Effort** M

**Where.** `src/greenkube/storage/sqlite/repository.py:708-732`; `src/greenkube/storage/sqlite/repository.py:536-553`; `src/greenkube/storage/sqlite/repository.py:908-932`; `src/greenkube/storage/sqlite/repository.py:1433-1454`; `src/greenkube/storage/sqlite/repository.py:1502-1523`; `src/greenkube/storage/postgres/repository.py:723-751`; `src/greenkube/storage/postgres/repository.py:934-962`; `src/greenkube/storage/postgres/repository.py:1056-1081`; `src/greenkube/storage/postgres/repository.py:1132-1157`; `src/greenkube/storage/postgres/repository.py:1418-1461`; `src/greenkube/core/metrics_compressor.py:329-340`

**Mechanism.** aggregate_summary, aggregate_timeseries, aggregate_by_namespace, aggregate_top_pods, aggregate_grouped_row_count, count_*_smart and read_combined_metrics_cursor (SQLite and PostgreSQL, nine methods each) all compute `cutoff = now - timedelta(hours=METRICS_COMPRESSION_AGE_HOURS)` and read raw rows with `timestamp >= cutoff - 1 minute` and hourly rows with `hour_bucket < cutoff`. The compressor however only produces hourly rows for hours that end at or before floor_hour(last compressor run - age), and it runs once per hour. Raw rows between that compression watermark and cutoff - 1 minute are in neither branch: the hole is (now - last compressor run) + (minutes past the hour of that run) - 1 minute, i.e. 0 to about 2 h. The hourly branch also uses `hour_bucket >= start`, which drops the partial first hour of a range that starts inside hourly data. The raw rows exist (raw retention is 7 days); they are simply not read.

**Effect on numbers.** Simulation with 40 h of constant synthetic data (truth 2.011 over the range, no late rows): aggregate_summary, aggregate_timeseries and aggregate_by_namespace return -0.35% when queried 5 minutes past the hour, -2.4% at minute 58 and -3.9% thirty minutes into the next hour before the hourly compressor runs again (0.007, 0.048 and 0.078 missing). The hourly chart shows a dip in the bucket 24 h ago (0.041 instead of 0.048, down to 0 later in the hour). For the dashboards: a 48 h range loses 2% on average (up to 4%), a 7-day range 0.6% (up to 1.2%), a 30-day range 0.14%; cost, energy, CO2e and embodied are all affected, always downwards, and the same fixed range gives different totals from one minute to the next. The pre-computed metrics_summary and timeseries cache are built with the same methods (core/summary_refresher.py:156,187).

**Affected environments.** All environments, both databases, default settings, as soon as an install is older than METRICS_COMPRESSION_AGE_HOURS (24 h). Not yet visible on the live test clusters (combined_metrics_hourly is still empty).

**Evidence.** audit-workspace/audit/work/reconstruction-experiment/e7_compress.py reads with the repository methods the API uses, with datetime.now patched in the repository module: 'read at real now: aggregate_summary cost=1.920000 (-4.53%)', 'minute 58: 1.879000 (-6.56%)', 'minute 30 of next hour: 1.849000 (-8.06%)' against raw truth 2.011; 4.18 points of each figure come from the late rows of RECON-02, the rest (0.35, 2.39, 3.88 points) is the boundary hole. Time series output shows bucket 2026-11-14T07:00:00Z = 0.041 versus 0.048 for its neighbours. The PostgreSQL methods contain the identical cutoff expressions (read, not run).

**Fix.** 1) Add one helper used by every dual-table read, e.g. storage/metrics_boundary.py: `async def raw_hourly_boundary(conn, table) -> datetime` returning metrics_compression_watermarks.compressed_until for the table (None when nothing is compressed), cached for a few seconds. 2) In all nine methods of both repositories replace the cutoff logic by: hourly branch = hour buckets with hour_bucket >= floor_hour(start) and hour_bucket < boundary; raw branch = rows with timestamp >= max(start, boundary) and < end. When start is not hour-aligned and falls below the boundary, either read that first partial hour from raw rows when they still exist (timestamp >= start and < start hour + 1 h, excluded from the hourly branch) or pro-rate the hourly bucket by duration; pick one rule and apply it everywhere. 3) Remove the `- 1 minute` fudge. 4) Add an invariant test and a debug endpoint or metric: for any range, the dual-table total must equal the raw-only total while raw rows cover the range (reconciliation check greenkube_read_boundary_mismatch). 5) Summary and timeseries caches need no migration: they are rebuilt hourly by the refresher.

**Acceptance.** a) Property test on SQLite and PostgreSQL: rows every 5 minutes for 40 h with total_cost 0.001, compressor run at an arbitrary minute m1, query at an arbitrary later minute m2 (same hour and next hour): aggregate_summary(start, end).total_cost equals the raw sum for every (m1, m2), and every hourly bucket of aggregate_timeseries equals 0.012 per full-presence pod. b) The same query repeated every minute for two hours returns one constant value. c) e7_compress.py prints 0.00% for the three read times once RECON-02 is also fixed.

#### RECON-04 · Windows older than COLLECTION_MAX_BACKFILL are abandoned although Prometheus still holds them, and the hole is recorded nowhere: no table, no API field, no UI flag; totals over a period with holes are silently low.

**Severity** high · **Kind** reliability · **Confidence** confirmed-live · **Effort** M

**Where.** `src/greenkube/core/collection.py:151-160`; `src/greenkube/core/collection.py:252-258`; `src/greenkube/core/collection.py:318`; `src/greenkube/core/collection_telemetry.py:31-36`; `src/greenkube/core/config.py:265`; `src/greenkube/api/routers/metrics.py:132`

**Mechanism.** plan_collection moves the start to now - COLLECTION_MAX_BACKFILL when the watermark is older: `gap_windows = int((horizon_start - start) / step); start = horizon_start`. MetricsCollectionJob.run then logs one warning and increments the in-process counter greenkube_collection_gap_windows_total, collects the horizon and advances the watermark past the hole. The next run reports gap_windows = 0. Nothing persistent remembers which interval is missing, the counter restarts at 0 with the controller, and with a missing watermark row (first run, RECON-10) no gap is counted at all. A window for which Prometheus answers with no series is also committed as complete (`written = ... if rows else 0`, then advance). The summary, time-series and report endpoints sum whatever rows exist and return no coverage information, so an operator cannot tell a cheap day from a day with 6 hours of missing data, and nothing offers to re-collect the hole while Prometheus retention (15 days by default) still has it.

**Effect on numbers.** Live Minikube: controller log 'Collection watermark for combined_metrics is older than COLLECTION_MAX_BACKFILL; 373 window(s) left as a gap' at 03:31:52, then 1 and 1 window; stored steps have holes 23:54:30 -> 03:32:30 and eight more; GET /api/v1/metrics/summary?last=12h returns total_cost 0.4455 and 84519 J with no completeness field while only about 3 of the 12 hours have rows. Simulation: watermark 22:00:00, restart at 23:40:10, COLLECTION_MAX_BACKFILL 30m: 1 window (59 steps) re-collected, 140 steps (70 minutes that Prometheus has) permanently lost, i.e. 70% of the outage; the second run reports gap_windows 0. With defaults an outage of 36 h loses 12 h of cost, energy and CO2e in every later report (-33% for that period), unflagged; change measurements over the hole fall back to coverage rules, dashboards do not.

**Affected environments.** All. The loss needs a controller or database outage longer than COLLECTION_MAX_BACKFILL (24 h by default, 30 m on the test clusters) or a first install; the missing coverage information affects every install.

**Evidence.** Live: live.sh logs controller --since=12h | grep 'left as a gap' (3 lines, 373 + 1 + 1 windows); live.sh sql gap query on combined_metrics; live.sh api api/v1/health returns only status and version; live.sh api 'api/v1/metrics/summary?last=12h'. Simulation: audit-workspace/audit/work/reconstruction-experiment/e6_gap.py drives the real MetricsCollectionJob with an injected clock: run#1 windows_planned 1, gap_windows 140, watermark 23:39:30; run#2 gap_windows 0; holes in stored timestamps [(21:59:30, 23:10:00)]; only collection_watermarks exists (one row).

**Fix.** 1) Persist gaps: new table collection_gaps(cluster_uid, source, gap_start, gap_end, reason [beyond_backfill | empty_prometheus | collection_error], detected_at, resolved_at, resolution [recollected | data_expired | acknowledged]). MetricsCollectionJob.run inserts a row for [old watermark, horizon_start) before advancing; also when the watermark row is missing but stored rows exist (use MAX(timestamp)+step as the old watermark). 2) Empty answers: when a window returns zero pod rows while the previous window had rows, record a gap with reason empty_prometheus instead of silently completing. 3) Repair: `greenkube collect --from --to` (and POST /api/v1/collection/gaps/{id}/recollect, admin) re-collects a gap in chunks through the normal job with forced=True, marks it resolved, and relies on RECON-02 for compressed hours; a periodic task marks gaps older than the Prometheus retention (probe: the oldest sample of container_cpu_usage_seconds_total) as data_expired. Consider raising the default COLLECTION_MAX_BACKFILL to the Prometheus retention once RECON-01 and RECON-02 are fixed. 4) Expose: GET /api/v1/collection/status (watermark, lag, open gaps), a coverage block {expected_seconds, covered_seconds, coverage_ratio, gaps[]} in /metrics/summary, /metrics/timeseries (per bucket) and reports, computed from distinct collected steps (count(distinct timestamp) x step) against the range; the UI shows a banner and hatches incomplete buckets; exports carry the ratio. 5) Keep greenkube_collection_gap_windows_total and add a gauge greenkube_collection_open_gap_seconds read from the table so it survives restarts; document an alert in docs. Stored data: a one-off scan creates gap rows for existing holes larger than one step in combined_metrics.

**Acceptance.** a) e6_gap.py scenario: after run#1 collection_gaps holds one row (22:00:00, 23:10:00, beyond_backfill); GET /api/v1/collection/status lists it; /metrics/summary over 21:50-23:40 returns coverage_ratio 0.36 (40 of 110 minutes). b) Re-collecting the gap while Prometheus has the data fills the 140 steps, sets resolved_at and coverage_ratio 1.0. c) A job run with no watermark row but stored rows ending 2 h earlier records a gap instead of gap_windows 0. d) A Prometheus that answers 'success' with an empty result for a window after non-empty windows creates a gap row with reason empty_prometheus.

#### RECON-05 · When a node is no longer in the Kubernetes API at collection time, its historical rows fall back to DEFAULT_ZONE, the default PUE and no embodied profile although node_snapshots_scd holds the node's history: CO2e was 19 times too high and embodied carbon zero in the simulation.

**Severity** high · **Kind** data-integrity · **Confidence** confirmed-sim · **Effort** M

**Where.** `src/greenkube/core/historical_range_processor.py:536-539`; `src/greenkube/core/historical_range_processor.py:563`; `src/greenkube/core/historical_range_processor.py:566-570`; `src/greenkube/core/historical_range_processor.py:738-742`; `src/greenkube/core/historical_range_processor.py:811-813`; `src/greenkube/core/historical_range_processor.py:394-396`

**Mechanism.** run_range reads nodes_info from node_collector.collect() (nodes that exist now) and derives from it the zone map (`node_contexts = await self.zone_mapper.map_nodes(nodes_info)`), the provider and PUE (`_ni = nodes_info.get(node_name); provider = _ni.cloud_provider if _ni else None`), node_instance_type, node_zone and the Boavizta cache (`prepare_embodied_data(nodes_info)`). The historical snapshot returned by get_node_info_at() is used only for the power profile. For a node that was scaled down, replaced or reclaimed since the step, the rows get `zone = DEFAULT_ZONE`, the default PUE, NULL node_instance_type and node_zone, and `_node_spec` finds no entry in boavizta_cache for the snapshot's (provider, instance type), so embodied carbon is 0. The rows are flagged (reason 'not found in zone map', intensity_source global_default) but the numbers are wrong while the correct zone, provider and instance type sit in node_snapshots_scd.

**Effect on numbers.** Simulation on the 23:10-23:30 window with the node absent from the API and a snapshot stored for it: joules identical (8000.57 J, power profile comes from the snapshot), emaps_zone FR -> unknown, grid intensity 26 -> 500 gCO2e/kWh, pod CO2e 0.0751 -> 1.4445 g (x19.2), idle CO2e x19.2, embodied CO2e 0.475 -> 0 g (-100%), node_zone and node_instance_type NULL. With an Electricity Maps token and a multi-region or non-default-zone cluster the error is the ratio between the real zone intensity and the DEFAULT_ZONE intensity; the provider PUE profile (DATACENTER_PUE_PROFILES) is replaced by the configured DEFAULT_PUE. On an autoscaled cluster a 24 h backfill hits every node replaced during that day.

**Affected environments.** Clusters with node churn (cluster autoscaler, Karpenter, spot or preemptible nodes, node upgrades, bare-metal node replacement) on first-install backfill, catch-up after downtime and --last re-collection (which overwrites correct live rows); in live mode the last step of pods on a node that just left. Not triggered on static clusters.

**Evidence.** audit-workspace/audit/work/reconstruction-experiment/e5_node_gone.py: real processor, node collector replaced by a stub returning no node, snapshot of the real node saved with timestamp 20:00 through the real node repository. Output: 'node gone (2204, unknown, None, None, 500.0, 1.3, global_default, node_capacity_derived, 8000.57, 1.4445, 0.0, 0.04108)' versus 'node present (2204, FR, FR, cpu-6, 26.0, 1.3, zone_default, node_capacity_derived, 8000.57, 0.0751, 0.4750, 0.04108)'.

**Fix.** In HistoricalRangeProcessor.run_range resolve every node attribute per (node, sample) with one function `node_at(node_name, ts)` = get_node_info_at(node_name, ts) (snapshot valid at ts) or, failing that, the earliest snapshot after ts, or the current NodeInfo; use it for zone mapping (NodeZoneMapper must accept a NodeInfo and map its zone/region to the Electricity Maps zone without calling the API), provider and PUE, node_instance_type, node_zone, the power profile (already done) and the embodied profile. Build boavizta_cache from the union of current nodes and all snapshots that intersect the window (node_timeline) instead of nodes_info only. Add a node_source value to the row provenance (current_api | snapshot | unknown) and keep the existing fallback reasons only when no snapshot exists. Make analyze_nodes run before the first collection and at least once per collection step for clusters with node churn (NODE_ANALYSIS_INTERVAL <= step), or save the node inventory inside the collection job, so a node that lives shorter than the analysis interval still gets a snapshot. Repair: `greenkube recompute` should accept --fix-node-context to rewrite zone, PUE, intensity, CO2e and embodied carbon of rows flagged 'not found in zone map' from the snapshots.

**Acceptance.** a) e5_node_gone.py after the fix prints for the node-gone run the same emaps_zone (FR), grid_intensity (26.0), pue, node_instance_type (cpu-6), CO2e (0.0751 g) and embodied (0.475 g) as the node-present run. b) Unit test: snapshot A (zone eu-west-3, provider aws, m5.large) valid 10:00-12:00 and no current node: rows at 11:00 carry emaps_zone FR, the AWS PUE and a non-zero embodied share. c) With no snapshot at all the row keeps the fallback values, is flagged, and node_source = unknown.

#### RECON-06 · Container rows are produced only for pods that still exist at collection time and, on runtimes without container-level cAdvisor series (cri-dockerd), only for the latest step of a live window: backfilled, caught-up and deleted-pod steps have no container usage at all.

**Severity** high · **Kind** data-integrity · **Confidence** confirmed-live · **Effort** L

**Where.** `src/greenkube/core/historical_range_processor.py:452-458`; `src/greenkube/core/historical_range_processor.py:275-300`; `src/greenkube/core/historical_range_processor.py:880-910`; `src/greenkube/core/metric_assembler.py:196-221`; `src/greenkube/collectors/promql.py:120-144`

**Mechanism.** Two mechanisms. (1) MetricAssembler.assemble_container_metrics builds containers_by_pod from pod_metrics, the pods listed by the Kubernetes API now, and skips a pod aggregate when `containers` is empty: a pod that is gone at collection time gets no container row even when Prometheus has its container series (containerd). (2) On the Minikube cluster (Docker through cri-dockerd) Prometheus has no series with container != '' at all, so _collect_cpu and _collect_memory use the pod cgroup fallback and container usage comes only from the Metrics API fill, which run_range applies only when `current_window` is true and only to `sample_key == latest_sample_ts`. Every step collected in catch-up mode (first-install backfill, restart after downtime, a cycle that covers more than one step, --last) is stored without container rows. Rightsizing evidence, container-scoped change measurement and the workload usage rollup are built on container rows, so reconstructed periods contribute nothing to them, and a re-collection leaves the old container rows in place next to rewritten pod rows (RECON-01).

**Effect on numbers.** Minikube: the live deployment stored 2764 container rows for 23:10-23:30; the reconstruction of the same window stores 0. Live Postgres: 0 container rows before 21:43:30 (the first-run backfill: 3213 pod rows over 58 steps) and container rows for all 262 later live steps. With default settings the 24 h backfilled at install has no container usage on such a runtime, so container recommendations start from zero history, and every outage leaves a container-usage hole as long as the outage. kind (containerd): 8707 of 8707 pod rows with an owner have container rows, but 54 of 75 ownerless pod rows (pods gone at collection time) have none; the 21 others are stale rows left by an earlier live collection.

**Affected environments.** (1) all runtimes for pods deleted before their window is collected; (2) clusters whose cAdvisor exports no per-container series: Docker Engine through cri-dockerd (Minikube docker driver, Docker Desktop, Rancher Desktop with dockerd, kubeadm with cri-dockerd).

**Evidence.** Local run audit-workspace/audit/work/reconstruction-experiment/run_collect.py --db e1_r1.db (23:10-23:30): container_rows 0 in every window, versus live_e1_container.jsonl 2764 rows. Prometheus: count(container_cpu_usage_seconds_total{container!=''}) returns no series over the whole history, count(container_cpu_usage_seconds_total) one series group. live.sh sql: 'select count(*) from container_metrics where timestamp < 21:43' = 0; join of pod rows and container rows per step on both clusters (kind: ownerless 75 pod rows, 21 with container rows).

**Fix.** 1) Deleted pods: build the container list per sample from history instead of the current API: kube_pod_container_info and kube_pod_container_resource_requests/limits range queries (same source as RECON-01), with the Kubernetes API as the current-window source; assemble_container_metrics then receives per-sample container specs. Exclude init containers through kube_pod_init_container_info. 2) Runtimes without container series: (a) detect the situation once per chunk (container query empty, pod cgroup query non-empty) and record it in a gauge greenkube_container_series_missing and in /api/v1/collection/status; (b) query kubelet /metrics/resource series when present (container_cpu_usage_seconds_total and container_memory_working_set_bytes scraped from metrics_path /metrics/resource carry the container label on every runtime) and document the ServiceMonitor change in docs; (c) when neither exists: for single-container pods use the pod cgroup value as the container usage (usage_source prometheus_pod_cgroup; the pod value is the container plus the pause container), for multi-container pods write container rows with usage NULL and reason container_usage_unavailable; (d) keep the Metrics API fill for the latest live step but label it (RECON-07). 3) Consumers (rightsizing, verifier, rollup) must treat steps without container usage as missing coverage, not as zero. 4) A re-collection that yields no container rows for a pod-step deletes the stale container rows of that pod-step. Stored rows: no backfill possible on cri-dockerd for the past; on containerd `greenkube collect --from --to` after the fix restores container rows of deleted pods while Prometheus has the data.

**Acceptance.** a) Fake Prometheus with container series for a pod absent from the fake Kubernetes API plus kube_pod_container_info: run_range returns container rows with requests and limits from kube-state-metrics. b) Fake Prometheus with pod cgroup series only, a 20-step non-current window and single-container pods: 20 container rows per pod with usage equal to the pod value and usage_source prometheus_pod_cgroup; multi-container pods get rows flagged container_usage_unavailable. c) Reconstruction of 23:10-23:30 on Minikube yields a container row for every single-container pod-step. d) After a re-collection no container row exists whose pod row has a different owner or lower joules.

#### RECON-07 · Container usage filled from the Metrics API is stored with usage_source = prometheus and is clamped one-sidedly to the pod's averaged value, so on runtimes without container cAdvisor series container CPU is biased low (-8.8% overall, -31% to -38% for bursty pods) and carries the wrong provenance.

**Severity** high · **Kind** bug · **Confidence** confirmed-live · **Effort** M

**Where.** `src/greenkube/core/historical_range_processor.py:880-917`; `src/greenkube/core/historical_range_processor.py:227-265`; `src/greenkube/core/metric_assembler.py:275-279`; `src/greenkube/core/prometheus_resource_mapper.py:20`

**Mechanism.** run_range fills missing container usage of the latest live sample from MetricsServerCollector and builds `PodResourceMaps(container_cpu_usage_map, container_memory_usage_map, container_sample_count_map)` without container_timestamp_map. assemble_container_metrics sets usage_source to metrics_server only `if container_key in resource_maps.container_timestamp_map`, otherwise it copies the pod row's value, so every such container row is labelled prometheus. Consumers that trust usage_source = prometheus (the verifier's usage gate, roadmap C0.3) accept one instantaneous Metrics API sample per step as if it were a Prometheus rate. In addition _fit_to_pod_cgroup scales the filled containers down when their sum exceeds the pod value by more than 5% and never scales them up. The pod CPU value is a rate over max(step, 4 x scrape) (120 s here) while the Metrics API value is a short-window sample taken between the step start and the collection time, so for bursty pods the sample is above the average about as often as below; clamping only the high side yields min(sample, average), which is biased low. 62% of the pod-steps of the test window carry the container_usage_exceeds_pod flag, so the flag no longer separates the page-cache case it was written for.

**Effect on numbers.** Live Minikube, 23:10-23:30, 2204 pod-steps: sum of container CPU = 8051 against 8824 for the pod rows (-8.8%; -11.1% for pod-steps above 20 mCPU); container below pod in 299 pod-steps, above in 56. Per pod: greenkube-review mean 10.5 versus 16.9 mCPU (-38%) and max 79 versus 114 (-31%); argocd-application-controller-0 mean 6.8 versus 9.8 (-31%). Rightsizing computes container requests from this usage, so CPU recommendations on such clusters are too low by the same proportion before headroom. All 2764 container rows of the window (19453 in the database) are labelled prometheus although Prometheus has no container series on this cluster.

**Affected environments.** Clusters without per-container cAdvisor series (Docker Engine through cri-dockerd: Minikube docker driver, Docker Desktop, Rancher Desktop with dockerd, kubeadm with cri-dockerd); on other runtimes only containers missing from Prometheus at the latest step.

**Evidence.** Live export live_e1_container.jsonl: Counter(usage_source) = {prometheus: 2764}; 1362 pod-steps with reason container_usage_exceeds_pod; ratio computed from live_e1_combined.jsonl and live_e1_container.jsonl (script output: 'containers/pod = 0.912', 'pod usage >= 20m: 0.889', per-pod means above). Prometheus: no series for container_cpu_usage_seconds_total{container!=''}. Code: grep container_timestamp_map shows it is read in metric_assembler.py:277,303 and never set by historical_range_processor.py.

**Fix.** 1) Provenance: in run_range record for every filled key the sample time and pass container_timestamp_map (or a new container_usage_source_map) to PodResourceMaps so the row gets usage_source = metrics_server; also store the per-resource source when CPU comes from Prometheus and memory from the Metrics API (usage_source 'mixed' or two columns usage_source_cpu / usage_source_memory). Keep the row timestamp of the step (BUG-05 must not come back): use the map only for provenance. 2) Bias: replace the one-sided clamp for CPU by a split of the pod's Prometheus value: when the pod value is the pod cgroup series, set container CPU = pod CPU x (container sample / sum of container samples) for multi-container pods and = pod CPU for single-container pods, and mark the row with reason container_split_from_pod_cgroup; keep the clamp for memory only (page-cache case) and apply it symmetrically for single-container pods (container memory = pod working set). 3) The verifier and ranking must not treat metrics_server rows as Prometheus-grade evidence: require usage_source in {prometheus, prometheus_pod_cgroup} or lower the confidence. 4) Stored rows: the source of existing container rows cannot be derived from the data, so do not rewrite them; bump CALCULATION_VERSION and state in the changelog that container rows written before the fix on clusters without container cAdvisor series are Metrics API samples labelled prometheus.

**Acceptance.** a) Unit test: pod cgroup series only, Metrics API usage for the pod's single container 30 mCPU while the pod rate is 20 mCPU, and 10 mCPU in another step where the pod rate is 20: container rows carry 20 and 20 mCPU (not 20 and 10), usage_source metrics_server or prometheus_pod_cgroup as specified, never prometheus. b) Over 100 random steps with unbiased noise the mean container CPU equals the mean pod CPU within 1%. c) On the live Minikube cluster after the fix, sum(container cpu) / sum(pod cpu) over 20 minutes is within 1% for single-container pods and no container row filled from the Metrics API has usage_source prometheus.

#### RECON-08 · The OpenCost cost of a pod is spread over its Prometheus rows with `step / (minutes x 60)`, so the stored cost depends on how the window was chunked: catch-up chunks over-count pods that start or stop inside the chunk (x1.17 to x1.5 at a 5-minute step, +2.8% for the whole cluster in a busy hour) and cost_source flips between opencost and opencost_derived.

**Severity** medium · **Kind** bug · **Confidence** confirmed-sim · **Effort** M

**Where.** `src/greenkube/collectors/opencost_collector.py:319-336`; `src/greenkube/collectors/opencost_collector.py:680-697`; `src/greenkube/core/historical_range_processor.py:573-579`; `src/greenkube/core/historical_range_processor.py:771-776`; `src/greenkube/core/collection.py:167-169`

**Mechanism.** run_range asks OpenCost for one accumulated allocation over the whole call window and gives each pod row `total x min(1, step / (minutes x 60))`. The number of rows a pod has in the window comes from Prometheus (every step with a CPU rate), not from OpenCost: rows cover the partial first and last steps of the pod's life and, when the rate window is longer than the step, extra trailing steps. OpenCost's `minutes` is the pod's running time as OpenCost sees it (quantised: 5, 10, 15 minutes on this cluster). So rows x step > minutes x 60 and the sum over the pod's rows exceeds the OpenCost total. In live mode a window is one step, the share is 1 and the stored cost is OpenCost's figure for that step; the scheduler uses chunks of up to one hour whenever it catches up (first install, restart, late cycle, --last), so the same history gets a different cost depending on how it was collected. The per-row source also changes with the window length because a longer window lifts the figure above PRECISION_FLOOR (opencost) while a one-step window is rebuilt from unit prices (opencost_derived). OpenCost itself is not additive across window lengths (20-minute accumulate: 0.00119 and 5 minutes for a pod that the two 5-minute sets price at 0.00119 each), which the single accumulated query inherits.

**Effect on numbers.** Step 5 m, hour 21:30-22:30 on Minikube, one 1-hour chunk versus twelve 5-minute windows: cluster pod cost 0.131005 versus 0.127442 (+2.8%); the 35 pods with partial presence +20.5%; pods started at 21:57 (OpenCost minutes 30, 7 rows) x1.17; greenkube-renderer-655bb6b54-jz6m7 x1.25; oversized-web-685f69cd4f-vbk97 x1.5 (3.570e-3 versus 2.380e-3; OpenCost's own 1-hour figure is 1.190e-3). Step 30 s, window 21:40-22:00: stored cost of partial-presence pods ranges from x0.2 to x1.7 across one 20-minute run, four 5-minute runs and forty 30-second runs (e2e-oom-69dc8cb77b-27nks: 4.46e-5, 1.91e-5, 9.0e-6); against OpenCost's allocation for the same 20 minutes the stored total is +5.1% and +50.7% for partial-presence pods. Usage, energy and CO2e are identical across chunkings. cost_source differs on 37% to 40% of rows between a live and a reconstructed window (880 of 2204).

**Affected environments.** All installs with OpenCost, whenever a collection call covers more than one step: first-install backfill, catch-up after downtime or a late cycle, --last. Steady live collection (one step per call) is exact.

**Evidence.** audit-workspace/audit/work/reconstruction-experiment/run_collect.py with --splits 1, 4, 40 (e5_r1.db, e3_split4.db, e3_split40.db) and at step 300s (e3p_1h.db, e3p_12x5m.db); per-pod tables printed by the inline comparison scripts; audit-workspace/audit/work/reconstruction-experiment/cost_conservation.py e5_r1.db: 'TOTAL greenkube=0.041044 opencost=0.039040 ratio=1.0513', 'PARTIAL-presence pods only ratio=1.5073'. OpenCost probes on port 39003 for 20 m, 5 m and 30 s windows and with step=5m (four allocation sets returned).

**Fix.** 1) Query OpenCost per step instead of one accumulated window: when step >= 1 m call /allocation/compute with window=start,end, step=<collection step>, accumulate=false, aggregate=namespace,pod, includeIdle=false and map each allocation set to its sample start; the row of step T gets the allocation of [T, T+step) with share 1, exactly as in live mode, so the result no longer depends on chunking. Keep the chunk at one hour so a call returns at most 12 sets at 5 m (60 at 1 m). 2) When step < 1 m (OpenCost resolution) or the step query fails, split the accumulated allocation over the pod's rows in the window by presence so that the sum is conserved: row cost = total x w_row / sum(w_rows), with w = max(request, usage) core-seconds and byte-seconds for the CPU and RAM components; never use minutes as the denominator. 3) Do not create cost for trailing rows after a pod's last memory sample (rows with memory_usage_bytes NULL and no running container): give them cost 0 with cost_source none and reason pod_terminated, or better stop producing such rows by setting rate_window = step when step >= 2 x scrape interval. 4) Provenance: derive cost_source from the allocation trust, not from the window length: store opencost when the figure is OpenCost's and add a boolean cost_rebuilt (or keep opencost_derived but compute it on the per-step set so live and backfill agree). 5) Add a reconciliation metric per collected window: sum of stored opencost-sourced cost versus the OpenCost total of the same window (greenkube_cost_reconciliation_ratio) and warn outside +/-2%. Stored rows: `greenkube collect --from --to` over the raw retention re-prices catch-up periods; hourly rows follow through RECON-02.

**Acceptance.** a) run_collect.py for 2026-11-14T21:30-22:30 at step 300s with --splits 1 and --splits 12 produces identical total_cost, cost_cpu, cost_ram and cost_source for every row (compare.py reports 0 differing rows at tolerance 1e-9). b) Same for step 30s with --splits 1, 4 and 40 on 21:40-22:00. c) cost_conservation.py reports a ratio within 1.00 +/- 0.01 for all pods and for partial-presence pods when compared with the per-step OpenCost sets. d) Unit test with a fake OpenCost: pod present 7 of 12 steps, allocation sets with cost 1.0 per present step: stored sum = 7.0 for any chunking.

#### RECON-09 · An OpenCost outage or lag is stored as modelled custom_rate cost (+10.8% in the simulation), the window is marked complete, and nothing re-collects it when OpenCost is back.

**Severity** medium · **Kind** reliability · **Confidence** confirmed-sim · **Effort** M

**Where.** `src/greenkube/collectors/opencost_collector.py:408-443`; `src/greenkube/core/historical_range_processor.py:573-579`; `src/greenkube/core/collection.py:316-334`

**Mechanism.** OpenCostCollector.collect catches HTTP errors and returns an empty list ('OpenCost API returned no data' warning); run_range also wraps the call in `except Exception: cost_map = {}`. Every row of the window then gets the Impact Model fallback (cost_source custom_rate, reason 'No observed cost for pod ...; cost allocated by reservation at custom_rate unit prices'), the write succeeds and the watermark advances. A Prometheus error fails the window (RangeCollectionError) and is retried; an OpenCost error is not, although OpenCost can answer for a past window later. The same happens per pod when OpenCost has no allocation yet for a new pod at collection time (safety lag 30 s to 1 m): the live row is custom_rate while a later collection of the same step gets the OpenCost figure. Rows are flagged individually, but the mix of observed and modelled cost inside one series is not corrected and cost-gated consumers (the verifier requires an OpenCost-class cost_source) lose those windows.

**Effect on numbers.** Simulation: collecting 23:10-23:30 with the OpenCost URL on a closed port stores 2204 rows, all custom_rate, total cost 0.045496 against 0.041078 with OpenCost (+10.8%; cost_ram +38%, cost_cpu +1.9%), 1644 rows change source. A manual re-collection of the window with OpenCost reachable restores every row exactly (0 differing rows at 1e-9), which shows the repair is possible but never triggered. Live versus reconstruction of 21:40-22:00: 37 of 2205 rows were custom_rate when collected live and are opencost or opencost_derived when re-collected (new pods and pods just deleted), e.g. oversized-web-685f69cd4f-vbk97 at 21:47:30: live cost 0, reconstruction 1.19e-4.

**Affected environments.** All installs with OpenCost; OpenCost restarts, upgrades, network policies, Prometheus slowness behind OpenCost.

**Evidence.** audit-workspace/audit/work/reconstruction-experiment/run_collect.py --db e8.db --opencost http://127.0.0.1:9/allocation/compute, then compare.py e1_r1.db e8_outage.db (cost_source differs on 1644 rows, total_cost B/A 1.1076) and the second run into e8.db followed by compare.py e1_r1.db e8.db --tol 1e-9 (no differing field). compare.py live_e5_combined.jsonl e5_r1.db for the 37 rows.

**Fix.** 1) Distinguish 'OpenCost not configured' from 'OpenCost configured but failed': OpenCostCollector.collect must raise OpenCostUnavailable on transport errors, non-2xx answers and undecodable bodies when a URL is configured or was discovered before; an empty allocation set stays an empty result. 2) Cost repair queue: new table cost_repair_windows(cluster_uid, window_start, window_end, reason [opencost_unavailable | allocation_missing], attempts, next_attempt_at, resolved_at). run_range reports through a new attribute (like last_idle_metrics) whether cost was unavailable and which pods with an OpenCost history had no allocation; MetricsCollectionJob._collect_window still writes the rows with the modelled cost (so usage and energy are not delayed) and inserts the window in the queue. 3) A controller loop (every step, bounded work per run) re-collects cost only for queued windows once OpenCost answers: update total_cost, cost_cpu, cost_ram, cost_storage, cost_network, cost_lb, cost_source and the cost reason of pod and container rows, re-run the idle-row cost allocation for the node-step, and mark hours dirty for RECON-02. Give up after the Prometheus retention and keep the flag. 4) Expose greenkube_cost_repair_pending_windows and the queue in /api/v1/collection/status. 5) For new pods: schedule one cost re-read of each live window after a delay of two OpenCost resolutions (10 minutes by default) when the window contained pods without an allocation.

**Acceptance.** a) Integration test with a fake OpenCost that fails for windows W1-W3 then recovers: after recovery and one repair loop, rows of W1-W3 are identical (1e-9) to rows collected with OpenCost always up, cost_source opencost or opencost_derived, and the queue is empty. b) During the outage rows are written with custom_rate and the watermark advances (usage is not delayed). c) A pod created 20 s before a window end has custom_rate at first collection and the OpenCost figure after the delayed re-read. d) With no OpenCost URL configured and none discovered no queue row is created.

#### RECON-10 · The collection watermark is keyed by the auto-detected cluster name, and a missing watermark row is treated as a first run: any name change triggers a full re-backfill that overwrites live rows with reconstructed ones and counts no gap.

**Severity** medium · **Kind** design · **Confidence** confirmed-live · **Effort** M

**Where.** `src/greenkube/core/collection.py:221`; `src/greenkube/core/collection.py:153-155`; `src/greenkube/cli/utils.py:52-57`; `src/greenkube/core/config.py:1140-1182`

**Mechanism.** MetricsCollectionJob uses `self.cluster_uid = cluster_uid or getattr(config, 'CLUSTER_NAME', '') or 'default'` and write_combined_metrics_to_database passes no cluster_uid, so the watermark key is the cluster name, not the stable kube-system UID that cluster_identities already stores. The name comes from _auto_detect_cluster_name: the node name on a single-node cluster, a provider label, 'default' on other multi-node clusters, and the pod's own K8S_NODE_NAME when the node list cannot be read at import time (5 s timeout, RBAC). The name therefore changes when a single-node cluster gets a second node or a new node name, when the node listing fails at one start and succeeds at another, when an operator sets clusterName for a readable name (the log message suggests it), or after an upgrade that changes the detection (commit b0cbff2f did). With no watermark row for the new key plan_collection takes `start = horizon_start` and gap_windows stays 0: the whole COLLECTION_MAX_BACKFILL horizon is collected again and upserted over the rows collected live, with the degradations of RECON-01, RECON-05, RECON-06 and RECON-08, and an outage longer than the horizon is not even reported.

**Effect on numbers.** Live kind cluster (image built before b0cbff2f): collection_watermarks holds two keys, gk-cilium-worker (stopped at 21:49:00) and gk-cilium-worker2 (current); cluster_identities holds both names for the single cluster UID 97d1af52-...; the new controller logged 'Collected 1/1 window(s), saved 1716 record(s)' at 21:49:44, a 30-minute re-backfill, after which the pod rows of greenkube-controller-d85c4df5f-zvrqt (25 rows) have owner NULL, request 0 and 25% less energy than their own container rows (6.788 J versus 9.034 J at 21:40:00). With default settings the re-backfill is 24 h (288 steps at 5 m) and every pod or node that disappeared during that day is degraded.

**Affected environments.** At HEAD: single-node clusters that grow or change node name, installs where the node list is unreadable at controller start (restricted RBAC, API or CNI not ready within 5 s), operators who set or change clusterName, upgrades from images older than b0cbff2f on multi-node clusters. Observed live on kind with the pre-fix image.

**Evidence.** live.sh (GK_ENV=kind) sql 'select * from collection_watermarks' (two rows), 'select * from cluster_identities' (two names, one uid), controller log line at 2026-11-14T21:49:44, pod row versus container row query for greenkube-controller-d85c4df5f-zvrqt. git log: b0cbff2f 'fix: auto-detect one cluster name for every pod of a multi-node cluster' committed 22:04:45 UTC, after the kind controller pod started (21:49:37 UTC). Code path at HEAD read end to end (config.py:1140-1182, collection.py:147-160,221).

**Fix.** 1) Key the watermark (and every other per-cluster cursor: metrics_compression_watermarks is global today, accrual and ledger watermarks) by the stable cluster UID from cluster_identities (kube-system namespace UID); resolve it once at controller start, fail closed (do not collect) when it cannot be read rather than inventing a key. Migration: rewrite collection_watermarks.cluster_uid to the UID, keeping the row with the greatest last_complete_end per source. 2) Missing watermark with existing data is not a first run: when no row exists for the key but combined_metrics has rows, initialise the watermark to MAX(timestamp) + step (per source), record a gap for anything older than the horizon (RECON-04) and log the adoption. 3) A scheduled catch-up must never overwrite rows that already exist with richer provenance: rely on the no-downgrade upsert of RECON-01 and skip windows that are fully stored unless the run is an explicit --last. 4) Cluster name stays a display label: changing it must not change any storage key; add a test that changes CLUSTER_NAME between two job runs.

**Acceptance.** a) Two MetricsCollectionJob runs on the same database with CLUSTER_NAME 'a' then 'b' and the same cluster UID: the second run plans only the windows after the first run's watermark (windows_planned 1 for one elapsed step), rows_written equals one step of rows, and no stored row changes. b) Database with rows up to T and no watermark row: the first run starts at T + step and records a gap when T is older than the horizon. c) Migration test: two watermark rows keyed by names for one UID collapse into one row with the later last_complete_end.

#### RECON-11 · OpenCost allocations with zero duration (minutes = 0, start = end) are stored as a trusted OpenCost cost of 0 for pods that run with non-zero requests.

**Severity** medium · **Kind** bug · **Confidence** confirmed-live · **Effort** S

**Where.** `src/greenkube/collectors/opencost_collector.py:128-152`; `src/greenkube/collectors/opencost_collector.py:296-300`; `src/greenkube/collectors/opencost_collector.py:193-202`

**Mechanism.** For a pod that OpenCost has just discovered, the allocation of the window is a placeholder: minutes 0, cpuCoreHours 0, totalCost 0 and start = end = window end. untrusted_cost_reason only distrusts a zero total when `_ran_with_requests(item)` is true, which requires minutes > 0, and needs_node_prices also requires minutes > 0, so the placeholder is neither rebuilt nor distrusted: _allocation_to_cost_metric returns source opencost with total 0. The row is then indistinguishable from a measured free pod, and consumers that require an OpenCost cost_source accept it. In a multi-step catch-up window every row of the pod gets cost 0 (the share is applied to a zero total); in live mode it hits the steps that end on an OpenCost resolution boundary.

**Effect on numbers.** Live Minikube: 13 rows with cost_source opencost, total_cost 0 and cpu_request > 0 (for example greenkube-api-68b49b945d-j4cbk, request 100 m, at 21:59:30; three greenkube-soak pods at 23:29:30). Reconstruction of 21:40-22:00 in one 20-minute window: the three pods started at 21:57 get cost 0 on all their rows, 4 to 5 each (per-step collection stores 1.28e-4, 1.41e-4 and 2.97e-5). At step 5 m, twelve 5-minute windows store cost 0 with source opencost for e2e-oom-69dc8cb77b-27nks where the 1-hour chunk stores 6.37e-5. For a change measurement the first steps of the new pods after a rollout are under-costed with a trusted flag, which inflates the measured saving of the after-window (the residue of BUG-41).

**Affected environments.** All installs with OpenCost; every new pod, more rows in catch-up windows.

**Evidence.** live.sh sql: cost_source = 'opencost' and total_cost = 0 and cpu_request > 0 -> 13 rows listed with pod names and timestamps. OpenCost probe (port 39003): window 21:59:30-22:00:00 returns greenkube-api-68b49b945d-j4cbk with minutes=0, cpuCoreHours=0, total=0, start=end=22:00:00; the 20-minute and step=5m queries return the same placeholder. Comparison table of e5_r1.db, e3_split4.db, e3_split40.db and e3p_12x5m.db.

**Fix.** In collectors/opencost_collector.py treat a zero-duration allocation as 'no allocation': add `def is_placeholder(item)` = minutes is None or minutes <= 0, or start == end, and in _allocation_to_cost_metric return None for it (the pod then takes the modelled fallback with cost_source custom_rate and its reason, and is queued for the delayed cost re-read of RECON-09). Extend untrusted_cost_reason: a zero total is also untrusted when the Kubernetes or kube-state-metrics spec of the pod has non-zero requests and the pod has usage rows in the window, whatever minutes says; pass the request information from run_range (cost is joined there) rather than relying on OpenCost's request averages. Add a counter greenkube_opencost_placeholder_allocations_total. Stored rows: migration that sets cost_source = 'none' (or re-prices through the repair queue) on rows where cost_source = 'opencost' and total_cost = 0 and (cpu_request > 0 or memory_request > 0).

**Acceptance.** a) Unit test: allocation {minutes: 0, start == end, totalCost: 0, cpuCoreRequestAverage: 0} for a pod with request 100 m -> _allocation_to_cost_metric returns None and the stored row has cost_source custom_rate with a positive modelled cost. b) Allocation {minutes: 5, totalCost: 0, cpuCoreRequestAverage: 0.1} stays untrusted (cost_source none) as today. c) A pod with no requests and minutes 5, total 0 stays opencost with cost 0. d) On the live cluster the query for opencost rows with total_cost 0 and cpu_request > 0 returns no row collected after the fix.

#### RECON-12 · Rows can be dropped without any trace while the window is committed: a failed intensity lookup skips every pod of a node-step (the skipped_carbon counter is never reported), and the SQLite writer swallows per-row errors.

**Severity** medium · **Kind** reliability · **Confidence** confirmed-code · **Effort** S

**Where.** `src/greenkube/core/historical_range_processor.py:599`; `src/greenkube/core/historical_range_processor.py:743-751`; `src/greenkube/storage/sqlite/repository.py:293-436`; `src/greenkube/core/collection.py:316-334`

**Mechanism.** In run_range `carbon_result = await calculator.calculate_emissions(...)` is wrapped in `except Exception: carbon_result = None`; when it is None the code does `skipped_carbon += len(pod_keys); continue`: no pod row, no container row and no idle row for that (sample, node), and skipped_carbon is never logged, exported or returned. calculate_emissions reads the intensity repository, so a database error or statement timeout (30 s by default) during the lookup is enough. The caller sees a normal, smaller row list, writes it and advances the watermark, so the node-step is lost for good. Separately SQLiteCombinedMetricsRepository.write_combined_metrics catches sqlite3.Error and Exception per row, logs and continues, then commits and returns: a row that fails a constraint or a type conversion disappears while the window is complete. Neither path creates a gap record or a metric.

**Effect on numbers.** Each occurrence removes all pods of one node for one step (cost, energy, CO2e, usage) from every later total, or single rows on SQLite; no figure indicates it. Not observed in the live data of this audit (the intensity lookups did not fail).

**Affected environments.** All (first path); SQLite installs and greenkube demo (second path).

**Evidence.** Code read end to end: historical_range_processor.py lines 599 and 750 are the only references to skipped_carbon (grep); calculator.calculate_emissions awaits repository lookups; sqlite/repository.py write loop has `except sqlite3.Error as e: logging.error('Could not save combined metric ...')` inside the for loop followed by commit; collection.py advances after any non-raising run_range and write. Not reproduced by simulation.

**Fix.** 1) run_range: do not catch a failed intensity resolution silently. Either let the exception propagate as RangeCollectionError (the window is retried, as for Prometheus errors) or, when the calculator itself failed after its own fallbacks, build the rows with the zone or global default intensity and intensity_source global_default plus reason intensity_lookup_failed; never skip the rows. Remove skipped_carbon or export it as greenkube_collection_skipped_rows_total{reason}. 2) SQLite writer: run the batch in one transaction and re-raise on the first error (QueryError), like the PostgreSQL path, so the job does not advance the watermark; keep the per-row log. 3) MetricsCollectionJob._collect_window: compare `written` with the number of pod and container rows returned and raise when fewer rows were stored. 4) Tests for both paths.

**Acceptance.** a) Calculator stub raising on the second node of a two-node sample: MetricsCollectionJob.run reports an error, the watermark is unchanged and the next run stores both nodes; or, with the default-intensity variant, both nodes are stored and the second is flagged intensity_lookup_failed. b) SQLite repository with one invalid row in a batch of 10: write_combined_metrics raises, no row of the batch is committed, the watermark does not move. c) A writer that returns 9 for 10 rows makes _collect_window raise.

#### RECON-13 · Node idle rows have no hourly aggregate and are deleted at raw retention, and the carbon-intensity recompute skips them: node totals (pods + idle) cannot be rebuilt beyond 7 days and idle CO2e keeps provisional intensities.

**Severity** low · **Kind** design · **Confidence** confirmed-sim · **Effort** M

**Where.** `src/greenkube/core/metrics_compressor.py:196-207`; `src/greenkube/core/metrics_compressor.py:531-586`; `src/greenkube/storage/sqlite/repository.py:1294`; `src/greenkube/impact/rows.py:61-67`

**Mechanism.** node_idle_metrics holds one __idle__ row per node and step; impact/rows.py states that node totals stay reconstructible as the sum of pod rows plus the idle row. TABLES in metrics_compressor.py contains only combined_metrics and container_metrics, and _prune_raw calls _delete_idle_before with the pod cutoff, so idle rows vanish after METRICS_RAW_RETENTION_DAYS with nothing kept. recompute_carbon_with_latest_intensities loops over ('combined_metrics', 'container_metrics') only, so idle CO2e is never updated when consolidated intensities arrive. Today only greenkube recompute reads idle rows, so no API figure is wrong yet, but any node-level or cluster-level total built on them (idle share, allocation coverage, reconciliation with OpenCost node cost) is limited to 7 days and mixes provisional and consolidated intensities.

**Effect on numbers.** Simulation: after the compressor runs with now + 8 days, node_idle_metrics has 0 rows while the hourly pod table keeps its totals; the idle share of the simulated node is no longer available for that period. On the live Minikube data idle is about 40% of node energy (5317 J idle versus 8001 J pods in 20 minutes).

**Affected environments.** All.

**Evidence.** audit-workspace/audit/work/reconstruction-experiment/e7_compress.py: 'compress at NOW+8d: raw_rows_pruned 2538 ... idle rows left: 0'; sqlite_master lists no hourly idle table. Code: metrics_compressor.py TABLES and _delete_idle_before; sqlite/repository.py:1294 table loop (read, PostgreSQL equivalent not opened).

**Fix.** Add node_idle_metrics_hourly(node, scope, hour_bucket, sample_count, duration_seconds, joules, co2e_grams, embodied_co2e_grams, total_cost, cost_cpu, cost_ram, cost_source, cpu_unreserved_core_seconds, memory_unreserved_byte_seconds, pue avg, grid_intensity avg, emaps_zone, power_source, intensity_source, model_version) with migrations for both backends; add it to the compressor as a third _MetricsTable-like entry (identity node, scope; sums for extensive columns, duration-weighted integrals for unreserved capacity) under the same watermark and dirty-hour logic as RECON-02; prune raw idle rows only after their hour is compressed. Include node_idle_metrics in recompute_carbon_with_latest_intensities (same intensity lookup by emaps_zone and hour). Provide read methods read_idle_hourly and a dual-table aggregate using the boundary helper of RECON-03. Existing installs: compress the idle rows still within raw retention at the first run after the migration.

**Acceptance.** a) e7_compress.py after the fix: SUM(joules), SUM(total_cost), SUM(co2e_grams) of node_idle_metrics_hourly equal the raw idle sums for compressed hours, and remain after the now + 8 days run. b) After an intensity update for a zone, idle rows of the last 24 h carry the new grid_intensity and CO2e. c) For any hour, hourly pod rows + hourly idle rows of a node reproduce the node total within 1e-6.

#### RECON-14 · aggregate_timeseries returns CPU and memory as sums of samples: the raw part of a bucket is steps-per-bucket times larger than the hourly part (4800 versus 700 in the simulation); the values are not exposed by the API today.

**Severity** low · **Kind** bug · **Confidence** confirmed-sim · **Effort** S

**Where.** `src/greenkube/storage/sqlite/repository.py:936`; `src/greenkube/storage/sqlite/repository.py:956-957`; `src/greenkube/storage/postgres/repository.py:968`; `src/greenkube/storage/postgres/repository.py:990-991`

**Mechanism.** The method unions raw rows (cpu_usage_millicores, memory_usage_bytes per step) with hourly rows (`cpu_usage_avg AS cpu_usage_millicores`, one row per pod-hour) and returns `SUM(cpu_usage_millicores)` and `SUM(memory_usage_bytes)` per bucket. For raw rows the sum runs over every step of the bucket, for hourly rows over pods only, and a pod present 15 minutes counts with its full average. Neither figure is a usage: the correct bucket value is the duration-weighted mean over the bucket (or core-seconds). api/routers/metrics.py and core/summary_refresher.py currently ignore the two keys, so no user-facing number is wrong yet; the defect is a trap for the next consumer.

**Effect on numbers.** Simulation with three pods (100 m steady, 200 m average, 400 m during 15 minutes per hour) at a 5-minute step: hourly-part buckets return 700, raw-part buckets 4800, the true time-weighted mean is 400 mCPU; memory 734 MB versus 5033 MB.

**Affected environments.** All, both databases; latent.

**Evidence.** audit-workspace/audit/work/reconstruction-experiment/e7_compress.py output 'timeseries around the raw/hourly boundary': 2026-11-14T06:00:00Z 0.048 700 734003200 and 2026-11-14T08:00:00Z 0.048 4800 5033164800.

**Fix.** Return duration-weighted figures from both branches: add `duration_seconds` to both selects (raw: COALESCE(duration_seconds, step); hourly: duration_seconds) and compute cpu_core_seconds = SUM(cpu_usage_millicores x duration_seconds) / 1000 and memory_byte_seconds = SUM(memory_usage_bytes x duration_seconds); expose avg_cpu_millicores = cpu_core_seconds x 1000 / bucket_seconds and avg_memory_bytes = memory_byte_seconds / bucket_seconds, and rename the keys so old callers fail loudly. Same change in the PostgreSQL method. Add them to TimeseriesPoint only with these definitions.

**Acceptance.** For the e7_compress.py data every hour bucket, raw or hourly, returns avg_cpu_millicores 400 and the same memory average on SQLite and PostgreSQL; a bucket that mixes raw and hourly rows (after RECON-03) returns the same value.

#### RECON-15 · Against a non-deduplicated HA Prometheus pair the stored usage depends on which replica answers: row values differ by up to 100% between replicas and short-lived pods exist on one replica only, so a re-collection through another replica does not reproduce history.

**Severity** low · **Kind** portability · **Confidence** confirmed-live · **Effort** S

**Where.** `src/greenkube/collectors/prometheus_collector.py:273-340`; `src/greenkube/core/historical_range_processor.py:267-273`

**Mechanism.** kube-prometheus runs two Prometheus replicas behind one Service (session affinity ClientIP on this cluster, none by default in the kube-prometheus-stack chart). Each replica scrapes at its own phase (replica 0 at :22 and :52, replica 1 at :20 and :50 for the kubelet), so `rate(...[120s])` and the instant memory value at the same evaluation time differ. GreenKube sends about ten independent range queries per chunk to one URL and has no notion of replica: with affinity the answer changes when the affinity expires or the controller moves; without affinity the CPU, memory and placement queries of one window can come from different replicas, and a replica that restarted returns fewer samples for the affected steps while the other replica has them. Totals are conserved (the CPU seconds move between neighbouring steps), per-row values and peaks are not reproducible.

**Effect on numbers.** 23:10-23:30 on Minikube, same GreenKube code, replica 0 versus the rows stored live through replica 1: 168 of 2204 rows differ in CPU (5 versus 13 mCPU at 23:10:00 for argocd-application-controller-0, relative difference up to 100%), 98 in memory (up to 12.5%), 182 in joules and CO2e (up to 19%); sums within 0.07% (CPU) and 0.01% (energy). Through replica 1 all 2204 rows are identical to the live rows. In 21:40-22:00 a pod that lived under one minute (e2e-oom-argo-696f74958b-85f49, 3 rows) exists in the live rows and not in the reconstruction.

**Affected environments.** Prometheus HA pairs without a deduplicating query layer (kube-prometheus default with 2 replicas, prometheus-operator installs with replicas >= 2); not Thanos Query, Mimir, Cortex or VictoriaMetrics with deduplication.

**Evidence.** compare.py live_e1_combined.jsonl e1_single.db (port-forward to the Service, replica 0) versus compare.py live_e1_combined.jsonl e1_r1.db (port-forward to pod prometheus-k8s-1): 168 versus 0 differing cpu rows. Raw samples of container_memory_working_set_bytes for the pod at 23:10: replica 0 [.., 23:09:52 558788608, 23:10:22 600453120], replica 1 [.., 23:09:50 558788608, 23:10:20 558792704]; the live row holds 558792704. kubectl get svc prometheus-k8s: sessionAffinity ClientIP, two endpoints.

**Fix.** 1) Documentation (docs/configuration.md, installation guide): with more than one Prometheus replica point PROMETHEUS_URL at a deduplicating endpoint (Thanos Query, Mimir, VictoriaMetrics) or at one replica's pod DNS name, and explain the effect. 2) Detection: at controller start and hourly, query `count(count by (prometheus_replica) (up))` or compare `prometheus_build_info` instances; when more than one replica answers behind the configured URL, log a warning, set a gauge greenkube_prometheus_replicas and show it in /api/v1/collection/status. 3) Optional hardening: when replicas are detected and their pod addresses are discoverable (endpoints of the Service), query each replica and merge samples with max per (series, timestamp) for gauges and take the replica with the most samples per series for rates, or pin all queries of a window to one replica and store its name in a row provenance field so a re-collection can use the same one.

**Acceptance.** a) With two fake Prometheus endpoints behind one URL returning different sample phases, the controller logs the warning and exposes greenkube_prometheus_replicas 2. b) With the optional merge enabled, collecting the same window through either entry point produces identical rows. c) The docs page states the limitation and the recommended endpoints.

### 5.3 Cost

**Coverage.** READ IN FULL: collectors/opencost_collector.py, impact/pricing.py, impact/allocation.py, impact/rows.py, impact/model.py, impact/types.py, models/provenance.py, core/metric_assembler.py, core/historical_range_processor.py, core/collection.py (plan + MetricsCollectionJob), scripts/generate_pricing_catalog.py, data/pricing/*.json (structure, metadata, coverage, spot-checked prices), the cost/pricing/currency settings of core/config.py, docs/methodology/impact-model.md (stored rows, OpenCost precision, cost fallback chain, limitations). READ IN PART: impact/projector.py (sample_from_metric, baseline, container_resources), impact/recompute.py (header and container re-split), storage/postgres/repository.py and storage/sqlite/repository.py (combined_metrics upsert), collectors/discovery/opencost.py, utils/http_client.py, analyzers/orphaned_pv.py and orphaned_lb.py, verify/deletion.py (header), core/optimization/verifier.py (cost trust), core/metrics_compressor.py and storage/workload_usage_sql.py (cost aggregation columns only), frontend currency formatting, helm secret projection. RAN: (1) live comparisons on both clusters (minikube and kind) of stored combined_metrics/container_metrics/node_idle_metrics rows against OpenCost responses for the same windows (hourly and 30 s windows), OpenCost PV/LB/asset endpoints, GreenKube API /health, /config, /metrics/summary, the recommendation_history unit prices, the greenkube_collection_source_requests_total counter; scripts and raw outputs in audit/work/cost (cmp.py, oc_*.json, gk_mk_22.txt). (2) a simulation driving the real HistoricalRangeProcessor, MetricsCollectionJob, OpenCostCollector (httpx MockTransport), MetricAssembler, Impact Model and projector: audit/work/cost/sim_cost.py, output in sim_cost.out (7 scenarios, rc=0). NOT COVERED: any real cloud cluster (AWS/GCP/Azure/OVH/Scaleway pricing was checked statically against the bundled catalogs only); Kubecost as a cost source for metric rows (no collector produces cost_source=kubecost); hourly compression on aged data (no combined_metrics_hourly rows exist yet in the live installs, so rollup provenance was checked by reading code only); the greenkube recompute command was not executed; change measurement internals beyond the cost-trust helpers; GPU cost paths (no GPU in the live clusters); OpenCost cloud-bill reconciliation adjustments; SvelteKit pages beyond currency formatting and the absence of cost provenance. Elapsed about 40 minutes of the 55-minute budget when the report was finalised. Cluster identity (why the node listing failed on kind) was observed through its effect on collection only; its root cause is not established.

**Earlier defects not fully fixed.**

- BUG-15 (partially_fixed): Projections price only the released reservation, but the per-container price comes from a usage-based split of the pod cost (COST-02: x0.55 and x5.5 errors) and the bound 'never more than the observed cost' is not enforced when a row's recorded request is below the current request (COST-07: x75 in simulation).
- BUG-19 (partially_fixed): Pod rows now exclude idle for cost and energy and node_idle_metrics stores the remainder, but idle cost is always a modeled price, does not reconcile with OpenCost pod costs and is read by no API or UI (COST-13).

#### COST-01 · An OpenCost outage, empty answer or rejected request silently switches the window to list-price or default-rate cost, the watermark still advances, the window is never re-priced, and a re-collection overwrites good OpenCost cost.

**Severity** high · **Kind** data-integrity · **Confidence** confirmed-live · **Effort** L

**Where.** `src/greenkube/collectors/opencost_collector.py:400-443`; `src/greenkube/core/historical_range_processor.py:572-579`; `src/greenkube/core/metric_assembler.py:38-55`; `src/greenkube/impact/rows.py:288-319`; `src/greenkube/core/collection.py:305-334`; `src/greenkube/storage/postgres/repository.py:274-320`; `src/greenkube/storage/sqlite/repository.py:335-373`; `src/greenkube/models/provenance.py:67-69`; `src/greenkube/core/optimization/verifier.py:167-183`; `src/greenkube/impact/recompute.py:1-16`

**Mechanism.** Step by step. (1) OpenCostCollector.collect() never raises for a failed call: an unresolved URL, any httpx.HTTPError (timeout, 401, 503), invalid JSON and an empty data list all end in 'return []' (lines 400-403, 412-415, 419-424, 438-443); only a Prometheus counter records outcome error or empty.
