(2) run_range builds 'cost_map = {(c.namespace, c.pod_name): c for c in cost_metrics}' inside 'try ... except Exception: cost_map = {}' (572-579), so an outage is indistinguishable from a cluster without OpenCost. (3) Every row then goes through cost_fields(None, ...) which yields cost_source none, and MetricRowAllocator._apply replaces it by the modeled cost labelled pricing_catalog or custom_rate (rows.py 297-318). (4) MetricsCollectionJob._collect_window writes the rows and advances the watermark (collection.py 316-334); report.error stays None. The window is collected exactly once, and nothing re-reads OpenCost later (impact/recompute.py: 'Nothing is re-read from Prometheus or OpenCost'). (5) pricing_catalog is in TRUSTED_COST_SOURCES, and the verifier trusts each row on its own (cost_is_trusted), so rows priced by OpenCost (spot, discounts, custom pricing) and rows priced at on-demand list price are compared in one measurement. (6) The upsert is unconditional: 'ON CONFLICT (pod_name, namespace, timestamp) DO UPDATE SET total_cost = EXCLUDED.total_cost, ... cost_source = EXCLUDED.cost_source', so 'greenkube collect --last' (or any re-run of a window) during an outage replaces stored OpenCost cost by the fallback.

**Effect on numbers.** Per-step cost of every pod, hourly rollups (the hour becomes mixed and is lost to measurement after compression; before compression raw rows stay individually trusted), measurement baselines and after-windows, dashboard totals. Direction and size depend on the gap between OpenCost prices and the fallback tier. Worked example (simulation A, m5.4xlarge spot node priced 0.30/h by OpenCost, catalog on-demand 0.896/h in eu-west-3, pod 8 cores / 16 GiB): healthy step 0.010320, outage step 0.030818, ratio 2.99, both rows pass is_cost_trusted. A 2-hour outage inside a 24-hour baseline raises the baseline mean by (22 + 2 x 2.99) / 24 - 1 = +16.6%: a fabricated saving of 16.6% of the workload cost; the same outage in the after-window hides a real saving of that size. On-prem with OpenCost custom pricing, the fallback is the OpenCost default rate (0.031611 per vCPU-hour) unless IMPACT_PRICING_* is set, so the step cost changes by default-rate/custom-rate. Live frequency: kind 10 of 448 collected steps had no OpenCost row at all and 39 of 367 OpenCost requests were counted empty; minikube 3 of 392 steps.

**Affected environments.** All installs that use OpenCost. Numerically worst on clouds where the catalog has the instance type (AWS, Azure: fallback is trusted pricing_catalog at on-demand price) and wherever OpenCost prices differ from list price (spot, reserved, savings plans, custom on-prem pricing). Both databases.

**Evidence.** Live: 'select timestamp ... having count(*) filter (where cost_source like 'opencost%') = 0' returns 3 steps on minikube (11-15 04:41:30, 06:43:30, 06:44:00) and 10 on kind; in these steps all 59 (34) pods are custom_rate although the neighbouring steps are opencost_derived; Prometheus query sum by (source,outcome)(greenkube_collection_source_requests_total) on kind: opencost success 328, empty 39; collection_watermarks kept advancing. Simulation audit/work/cost/sim_cost.py scenario A (real OpenCostCollector with an HTTP 503, real HistoricalRangeProcessor and MetricsCollectionJob): 'cycle 2 (OpenCost 503) error=None watermark=10:10 succeeded=True', row 10:05 cost_source=pricing_catalog total_cost=0.030818 trusted=True against 10:00 cost_source=opencost total_cost=0.010320; 'cycle 3 (forced re-collect ...)' rewrites the 10:00 row to pricing_catalog 0.030818. Re-collection is not hypothetical: on kind a change of the watermark key re-collected 853 pod rows (COST-04).

**Fix.** 1. Collector contract: make collect()/collect_range() return a typed result (status = ok | empty | unavailable | not_configured, plus metrics) or raise OpenCostUnavailableError for transport errors, non-2xx, invalid JSON and unresolved URL after OpenCost was configured or discovered once. Never return [] for an error. 2. run_range: keep the status of the window. When OpenCost is configured/was reachable before (persist last successful allocation time, e.g. a collection_watermarks row with source opencost_cost) and the status is unavailable or empty, still write usage rows but mark their cost as pending: modeled cost with a distinct provenance (new cost_source value such as pending_opencost, or a cost_pending boolean column) that is never trusted, and enqueue the window in a new table cost_backfill_queue(cluster, window_start, window_end, attempts, last_error). 3. New controller job 'cost reconciliation' (every few minutes, leader only): for each queued window still inside Prometheus/OpenCost retention, call collect_range for the exact window and UPDATE only total_cost, cost_cpu, cost_ram, cost_storage, cost_network, cost_lb, cost_source, is_estimated and estimation_reasons of pod rows, then re-split container rows; drop the queue entry on success; expose greenkube_cost_backfill_pending_windows and the age of the oldest entry. 4. Upsert guard in write_combined_metrics and write_container_metrics (Postgres and SQLite): when the stored cost_source is opencost, opencost_derived or kubecost and EXCLUDED.cost_source is not, keep the stored cost columns (CASE expressions). 5. Measurement: require one price basis per measurement; rows whose cost_source family differs from the dominant family of the baseline are excluded (or the verdict is inconclusive with reason mixed_price_basis). 6. Health: GET /health/services must test the allocation endpoint itself and report the last successful allocation; the UI shows a banner while windows are pending. 7. Backfill of stored rows: one-off 'greenkube cost backfill --since <raw retention>' that re-queries OpenCost for raw rows with cost_source custom_rate or pricing_catalog in periods where the same cluster has opencost rows. Document the behaviour in docs/methodology/impact-model.md and docs/configuration.md.

**Acceptance.** (a) Range harness with a fake OpenCost answering 503 for window 2: rows of window 2 carry the pending provenance, is_cost_trusted is False for them, cost_backfill_queue has one entry, the usage watermark advances. (b) After the fake recovers, one reconciliation run sets those rows to cost_source opencost with total_cost equal to the allocation (0.010320 in the simulation data) and empties the queue. (c) A forced re-collection of window 1 while OpenCost is down leaves its stored opencost cost unchanged (both databases). (d) Verifier test: baseline of opencost rows and an after-window containing pricing_catalog rows never produces a realized saving from the price switch (rows excluded or verdict inconclusive). (e) A cluster that never had OpenCost produces modeled rows and no queue entry. (f) HTTP 401 and invalid JSON behave like 503.

#### COST-02 · The pod cost is split between containers by CPU usage share instead of reservation, so container costs and the unit prices of rightsizing projections are wrong for every multi-container pod.

**Severity** high · **Kind** bug · **Confidence** confirmed-live · **Effort** M

**Where.** `src/greenkube/core/metric_assembler.py:223-264`; `src/greenkube/impact/recompute.py:105-127`; `src/greenkube/collectors/opencost_collector.py:390-393`; `src/greenkube/impact/model.py:144-202`; `src/greenkube/core/optimization/analyzers/rightsizing_cpu.py:57-63`

**Mechanism.** OpenCost is queried per pod only ('aggregate': 'namespace,pod'). assemble_container_metrics then splits the pod row: 'cpu_weights = [max(resource_maps.container_cpu_usage_map[key], 0) ...]' (CPU usage, then memory usage, then requests only when usage is missing or zero), 'cost_cpu = (aggregate.cost_cpu or 0.0) * share', 'cost_ram = (aggregate.cost_ram or 0.0) * memory_share' with memory usage weights. OpenCost and the Impact Model both charge the reservation max(request, usage), so a container with a large request and little usage (the target of rightsizing) receives too little cost and a busy sidecar too much. The projection anchors each container sample to this cost: sample_prices divides the container cost_cpu by the container's reserved core-hours, so the unit price itself is distorted and stored in the recommendation (assumptions.unit_prices). The same split is used for modeled rows and by greenkube recompute.

**Effect on numbers.** Container-level cost_cpu and cost_ram, per-container dashboards, projected annual savings of CPU and memory rightsizing for multi-container workloads (service-mesh sidecars, log shippers), and the projected/measured ratio used by the cost gate. Worked example (simulation C): pod with app (request 1000m, usage 50m) and proxy (request 100m, usage 50m), OpenCost CPU cost 0.034772/h. Stored: app 0.017385 (50%), proxy 0.017385 (50%); by reservation: 0.031611 (91%) and 0.003161 (9%). Projection app 1.0 -> 0.1 core: 0.015646/h against 0.028450/h true (x0.55); proxy 0.10 -> 0.05 core: 0.008693/h against 0.001581/h true (x5.50). Live: kind pod gk-upgrade/gkup-greenkube-fbcdccb44-2dztk, containers greenkube (request 100m, usage 6.1m) and greenkube-api (request 50m, usage 7.7m): stored cost_cpu 0.0002758 and 0.0004353 (39% / 61%, by reservation 67% / 33%); the active recommendations for these containers carry cpu_core_hour 0.017419 and 0.054986 while every single-container workload shows about 0.0315. minikube pod greenkube-review-6b4d77d89d-wbsgf (two containers, 10m each): 88% / 12% instead of 50% / 50%, unit prices 0.0507 and 0.0074 per core-hour.

**Affected environments.** All environments and both cost paths (OpenCost and modeled). Single-container pods are not affected.

**Evidence.** Live SQL on container_metrics (sum(cost_cpu) and sum(cost_cpu)/sum(reserved core-hours) per container) for the two pods above, and recommendation_history.impact->assumptions->unit_prices of RIGHTSIZING_CPU rows on both clusters. Simulation audit/work/cost/sim_cost.py scenario C (real run_range and real ReservationAwareImpactModel.project): output lines 'container app ... stored cost_cpu=0.017385 (50% of pod) | by reservation: 0.031611 (91%)' and 'rightsize proxy cpu 0.10 -> 0.05 core: projected CPU saving 0.008693/h vs OpenCost-true 0.001581/h (x5.50)'.

**Fix.** 1. Preferred: collect OpenCost at container granularity. In OpenCostCollector.collect use aggregate=namespace,pod,container (one call), build the pod CostMetric as the sum of its containers (so pod totals stay identical to today) and a container cost map keyed (namespace, pod, container) carrying cpu/ram/gpu and the sub-precision rebuild per container. Pass it to assemble_container_metrics and use the container's own cost when present; non-container components (pv, network, lb, shared) stay on the pod row and are split by reservation. 2. Fallback split (no container allocation, modeled rows, recompute): CPU cost by CPU reservation share max(cpu_request, cpu_usage) per container, RAM cost by memory reservation share max(memory_request, memory_usage); never by usage alone; equal split only when both are zero. Apply the same function in impact/recompute.py so collection and recompute agree. 3. Bump CALCULATION_VERSION (range.4) and document the rule in docs/methodology/impact-model.md (stored rows section). 4. Stored data: container rows inside raw retention are re-split by 'greenkube recompute --all' (pod rows are unchanged); hourly container aggregates and workload_usage_hourly cannot be re-split, so recommendations built on windows older than the fix must be flagged (calculation_version below range.4) or the rollup rebuilt from raw rows where they still exist. 5. Re-run the recommendation scan after the recompute so stored projections are replaced.

**Acceptance.** (a) Unit test of assemble_container_metrics: pod cost_cpu C, containers app (1000m request, 50m usage) and proxy (100m, 50m): app cost_cpu = 0.909 C, proxy = 0.091 C within 1%; sum of container rows equals the pod row for total_cost, cost_cpu and cost_ram. (b) With a container-level OpenCost fixture, each container row equals OpenCost's container cpuCost and ramCost within rounding. (c) Projection test: app 1.0 -> 0.1 core projects 0.9 x unit price x hours and proxy 0.10 -> 0.05 projects 0.05 x unit price x hours, both within 2% of the OpenCost figure. (d) On the live e2e clusters the unit prices stored in recommendations of multi-container workloads are within 3% of the single-container ones. (e) recompute produces the same container rows as collection for the same pod row.

#### COST-03 · collect_pv_costs and collect_lb_costs never return a cost with a real OpenCost (wrong query parameter and response fields), so orphan PV and LoadBalancer savings always use the flat defaults and their deletion can never be measured.

**Severity** high · **Kind** bug · **Confidence** confirmed-live · **Effort** M

**Where.** `src/greenkube/collectors/opencost_collector.py:506-587`; `src/greenkube/collectors/opencost_collector.py:589-678`; `src/greenkube/collectors/pv_collector.py:283-290`; `src/greenkube/collectors/lb_collector.py:540-546`; `src/greenkube/core/optimization/analyzers/orphaned_pv.py:59-73`; `src/greenkube/core/optimization/analyzers/orphaned_lb.py:65-103`; `src/greenkube/core/config.py:416-421`; `src/greenkube/verify/deletion.py:9-17`

**Mechanism.** Both functions send 'params = {"aggregateBy": "persistentvolume" | "service", "window": "7d"}' to three candidate paths. On OpenCost the first two paths (/api/v1/allocation/aggregate, /allocation/aggregate) do not exist (404) and /allocation ignores the unknown aggregateBy parameter (the real one is aggregate), returning un-aggregated allocations keyed cluster/node/namespace/pod/container. The parser then looks for fields OpenCost does not have: 'allocations.get(name)', 'properties.persistentVolume', 'entry.get("pvCosts")', 'entry.get("storageCost")' and 'entry.get("loadBalancerCosts")'. OpenCost reports PV cost in each allocation's 'pvs' map (key 'cluster=<id>:name=<pv>', fields byteHours, cost, adjustment) and LoadBalancer cost in 'lbAllocations' (key '<cluster>/<namespace>/<service>', fields service, cost, private, ip, hours). Both functions therefore always return {}. Consequences: orphaned_pv always falls to 'annual_cost = capacity_gib * cfg.STORAGE_COST_PER_GIB_MONTH * 12' with cost_source custom_rate, orphaned_lb to LOAD_BALANCER_COST_PER_MONTH * 12 for any LoadBalancer with an address that is not a recognised bare-metal allocator; verify/deletion.py refuses to book a custom_rate rate, so every deletion ends inconclusive. In addition 'if window_cost:' drops an observed zero, and the annualization 'window_cost * (365.0 / window_days)' assumes the asset existed for the whole 7 days.

**Effect on numbers.** Projected annual saving of every orphan PersistentVolume and orphan LoadBalancer recommendation, and the realized savings of deletions in the ledger (always missing). Live example (kind, local storage): PV gk-waste-csi-released, 5 GiB, phase Released: OpenCost reports cost 0.001418 for 5.18 h in the __unmounted__ allocation, i.e. 2.40 per year; GreenKube shows potential_savings_cost 6.00 with cost_source custom_rate / STORAGE_COST_PER_GIB_MONTH (x2.5). A 2 TiB orphan volume on an on-prem NAS is shown at 2048 x 0.10 x 12 = 2,457.60 per year whatever the storage really costs. On a cloud with OpenCost, a gp3 volume (0.08 per GiB-month) is over-stated by 25%, a provisioned-IOPS or premium SSD volume under-stated by 2 to 10 times, and a LoadBalancer is always 216 per year.

**Affected environments.** All environments with OpenCost for the lookup failure; on-prem and local clusters additionally get a cloud-like flat default (0.10 per GiB-month, 18 per LoadBalancer-month) although they pay nothing per GiB or per LoadBalancer.

**Evidence.** Live: 'live.sh opencost "api/v1/allocation/aggregate?aggregateBy=persistentvolume&window=7d"' and 'allocation/aggregate?...' return NotFound; 'allocation?aggregateBy=persistentvolume&window=7d' and 'allocation?aggregateBy=service&window=7d' return the same un-aggregated 154 entries, none with pvCosts, storageCost or loadBalancerCosts; kind 'allocation/compute?window=6h&aggregate=namespace&accumulate=true' shows pvs['cluster=default-cluster:name=gk-waste-csi-released'] = {byteHours 27786224475, cost 0.001418} and lbAllocations['default-cluster/gk-waste/echo-lb'] = {service gk-waste/echo-lb, cost 0, private true, hours 5.18}; recommendation_history on kind: ORPHANED_PERSISTENT_VOLUME gk-waste-csi-released potential_savings_cost 6.00, cost_source custom_rate. Simulation sim_cost.py scenario E feeds the recorded live payload (with pvs cost 1.25 and an lbAllocations cost 0.42) to the real functions: 'collect_pv_costs -> {}' and 'collect_lb_costs -> {}'.

**Fix.** 1. Rewrite collect_pv_costs: call the resolved allocation endpoint (/allocation/compute, the path already used by collect) with window=<start>,<end>, aggregate=namespace, accumulate=true, includeIdle=false; iterate every allocation including __unmounted__ and sum pv['cost'] + pv['adjustment'] per PV name parsed from the 'cluster=<id>:name=<pv>' key; also return byteHours so the caller can annualize by the hours the volume existed (cost / (byteHours / capacity_bytes) x 8760) instead of 365 / window_days. Alternative source to evaluate: OpenCost /assets with filter assetType:"disk" (it lists the same volume with bytes, byteHours and cost). 2. Rewrite collect_lb_costs from lbAllocations (key '<cluster>/<namespace>/<service>', field cost and hours) with the same annualization; match on namespace/name exactly, never on the service name suffix alone (today a suffix match could attach another namespace's cost). 3. Keep observed zeros: return 0.0 for an asset OpenCost knows at zero cost, and let the analyzers treat it as an observed rate (cost_source opencost, saving 0) instead of replacing it by the flat default. 4. Flat defaults: apply STORAGE_COST_PER_GIB_MONTH and LOAD_BALANCER_COST_PER_MONTH only when the node provider is a known cloud; on unknown/on-prem providers project no cost unless the operator set the values explicitly (make the settings Optional, default None, and document them in docs/configuration.md and the Helm values). 5. Use the configured currency in the recommendation text instead of a literal dollar sign. 6. Add contract tests from payloads recorded on a real OpenCost (the files in audit/work/cost/oc_pv_live.json are a starting point) and an e2e assertion on the kind cluster. 7. Existing recommendations are recomputed by the next scan; no data migration.

**Acceptance.** (a) Recorded kind payload: collect_pv_costs(['gk-waste-csi-released']) returns 0.001418 (+-1%) for the 5.18 h window and the recommendation shows about 2.40 per year with cost_source opencost. (b) lbAllocations fixture with cost 0.42 over 7 days for shop/web-lb: collect_lb_costs(['shop/web-lb']) returns 0.42 and annual_cost 21.9; a Service other/web-lb is not matched. (c) An asset known to OpenCost at cost 0 yields a projection of 0 with cost_source opencost, not the flat default. (d) Unknown provider and no explicit default: orphan PV recommendation has no cost saving and says so. (e) After apply detection confirms the PV is absent, the deletion measurement of an OpenCost-priced PV is realized with rate source allocated_rate_at_decision. (f) The 404 answers of non-existing paths are not requested any more.

#### COST-04 · The collection watermark is keyed by the cluster name, which on the kind install fell back to the controller's node name; when the name changed, the controller re-collected a range it had already collected and overwrote stored cost, requests and provenance.

**Severity** high · **Kind** data-integrity · **Confidence** confirmed-live · **Effort** M

**Where.** `src/greenkube/core/collection.py:221`; `src/greenkube/core/collection.py:145-164`; `src/greenkube/core/config.py:1004-1006`; `src/greenkube/core/config.py:1105-1184`; `helm-chart/templates/components.yaml:153-155`; `helm-chart/values.yaml:404`; `src/greenkube/storage/postgres/repository.py:274-317`

**Mechanism.** MetricsCollectionJob keys its watermark with 'self.cluster_uid = cluster_uid or getattr(config, "CLUSTER_NAME", "") or "default"'. CLUSTER_NAME is auto-detected when the Helm value config.clusterName is empty (the default): the only node's name, then a provider cluster-name label, then 'default' for other multi-node clusters, and, when the startup node listing fails ('None out of cluster or on any error', logged at debug level only), the Downward-API K8S_NODE_NAME, i.e. the node the pod runs on. On the kind install (3 nodes, the controller service account may list nodes) detection ended in that last branch: the name was gk-cilium-worker for the first controller pod and gk-cilium-worker2 after it was rescheduled. plan_collection treats an unknown key as a first run ('if watermark is None: start = horizon_start') and collects the whole COLLECTION_MAX_BACKFILL again (24 h by default). The unconditional upsert then replaces the stored pod rows by values computed at re-collection time: OpenCost queried per 1-hour chunk instead of per step, requests read from the live API (0 for pods that are gone, see COST-07), fallback prices if OpenCost happens to be unavailable (COST-01). Container rows of pods that no longer exist are not produced again, so the two tables disagree. A stable identity exists (cluster_identities maps both names to the same kube-system UID, and the ledger uses that UID), but collection does not use it. The collection is described as an idempotent upsert (cli/utils.py); it is not.

**Effect on numbers.** Every pod row of the last COLLECTION_MAX_BACKFILL (24 h by default) is rewritten at each change of the detected name; rows of pods replaced in that period lose their requests, and their modeled cost with them. Live kind: collection_watermarks holds 'gk-cilium-worker | 2026-11-14 21:49:00' and 'gk-cilium-worker2 | 2026-11-15 07:17:30'; the 853 pod rows between 21:33:30 and 21:48:30 were collected twice. For pod greenkube-controller-d85c4df5f-zvrqt the 21 pod rows now read total_cost 0.00003500, cpu_request 0, while the container rows of the same steps still read 0.00004000 (and 0.00003517 custom_rate, 0.00000000 opencost) with cpu_request 100: one step, two costs (-12.5%) and two provenances; sum of container rows 0.308989 against pod rows 0.308934 on the cluster.

**Affected environments.** Multi-node clusters without a provider cluster-name node label (kubeadm, k3s, kind, bare metal, OVHcloud, Scaleway) when the node listing at start-up fails, and any install whose operator changes config.clusterName. Single-node clusters and EKS, GKE, AKS or Cluster API clusters keep a stable name.

**Evidence.** Live SQL on kind: 'select * from collection_watermarks' (two rows, node names); 'select * from cluster_identities' (gk-cilium-worker and gk-cilium-worker2 registered at 21:38:40 and 21:49:43 with the same UID 97d1af52-8d4f-4434-b68c-500edc400f79); join of container_metrics and combined_metrics on (namespace, pod_name, timestamp) showing 21 pod-steps whose pod row has cpu_request 0 and another cost than its container row; 'kubectl auth can-i list nodes --as=system:serviceaccount:greenkube:greenkube-controller' = yes; controller log records carry cluster = gk-cilium-worker2. minikube (single node) has one watermark row and no pod/container mismatch (0 of 18,733 pod-steps).

**Fix.** 1. Key collection_watermarks by the stable cluster UID (the kube-system namespace UID already stored in cluster_identities); migration: for each UID keep the greatest last_complete_end among its names and delete the others. Apply the same key to the other name-keyed tables (accrual watermarks, bindings) in the same change or a follow-up. 2. Identity detection: remove the K8S_NODE_NAME fallback for multi-node clusters; when the node listing fails, retry with backoff during start-up and keep the controller not-ready (no collection) until the cluster UID is known; never derive the name from a node unless the cluster has exactly one node. Log the result and the branch at info level and show it in GET /health. 3. Guard in plan_collection: when no watermark exists but rows already exist for the cluster inside the backfill horizon, start at the newest stored timestamp; a wider re-collection must be requested explicitly (--last or a new --force flag). 4. Make re-collection quality-preserving: the upsert guards of COST-01 (never downgrade cost provenance) and COST-07 (never overwrite known requests with 0), and delete or rewrite the container rows of a re-collected pod-step in the same transaction. 5. Chart and docs: state that config.clusterName must be set on multi-node clusters without provider labels; add a Helm NOTES warning when it is empty. 6. Repair: on affected installs, collapse the duplicate watermarks; rows already overwritten cannot be restored, but pod-steps whose pod row disagrees with its container rows can be listed with the SQL above and flagged.

**Acceptance.** (a) Two MetricsCollectionJob runs for the same cluster UID with CLUSTER_NAME 'a' then 'b': the second run plans no window that ends before the first watermark, and no stored row changes. (b) Config test: with a failing node listing, K8S_NODE_NAME set and three nodes, the detected name is never the node name and collection does not start until the UID is resolved. (c) Migration test: two watermark rows for one UID collapse into one with the later end. (d) After a controller reschedule on the kind e2e install, collection_watermarks has one row per cluster and the query comparing pod rows with the sum of their container rows returns 0 mismatches. (e) A re-collection requested with --last rewrites pod and container rows of a step together.

#### COST-05 · OpenCost allocations that carry no usable cost (minutes = 0, or sub-precision figures that cannot be rebuilt) are stored with the trusted label opencost, as an exact zero or a rounded value.

**Severity** medium · **Kind** data-integrity · **Confidence** confirmed-live · **Effort** S

**Where.** `src/greenkube/collectors/opencost_collector.py:128-152`; `src/greenkube/collectors/opencost_collector.py:193-202`; `src/greenkube/collectors/opencost_collector.py:291-300`; `src/greenkube/impact/rows.py:120-125`; `src/greenkube/models/provenance.py:111-136`; `src/greenkube/models/provenance.py:187-209`

**Mechanism.** Case A, zero minutes. After a gap in OpenCost's own data, or in the first window of a new pod, OpenCost answers with allocations where minutes = 0, start = end, cpuCoreHours = 0 and totalCost = 0 while cpuCoreRequestAverage is set. needs_node_prices returns False ('if minutes is None or minutes <= 0: return False'), so nothing is rebuilt; untrusted_cost_reason needs _ran_with_requests, which requires 'minutes > 0', so the zero is not flagged; the CostMetric gets source opencost and total 0. _is_observed_cost is True for it, so the modeled fallback is not applied either. The documented rule (a zero for a pod with requests is a missing price, fail closed) is bypassed. is_cost_trusted rejects such a row at read time only when the row has a request; pods without requests pass as trusted zeros, and hourly rollups sum the zero and keep the trusted label. Case B, failed rebuild. When rebuilt_cost returns None for a non-zero sub-precision allocation (node price not yet known, agreement check failed), the 5-decimal OpenCost figures are kept with source opencost and are trusted although their rounding error is unbounded relative to the value.

**Effect on numbers.** Case A: the cost of every pod is 0 for the affected step. kind: at 11-15 06:14:30 and 06:44:30 all 34 pod rows are opencost with total_cost 0 (normal step total 0.00070); 240 zero opencost rows in total (1.7% of rows), 187 of them on pods without requests that pass is_cost_trusted. minikube: 58 zero rows, at 06:44:30 45 of 59 pods. With the default 5-minute step each event removes 5 minutes of cost: an hour containing one zero step is under-stated by 8.3% and still labelled opencost_derived (trusted), which reads as an 8.3% saving if it falls in an after-window. Case B: kind, 759 rows of 21 pods written during the first 25 minutes after installation carry rounded figures; per pod the rounded/rebuilt ratio ranges from 0.607 to 1.272 (-39% to +27%), e.g. 0.00003000 instead of 0.00003174 with the RAM cost rounded to 0.

**Affected environments.** All installs with OpenCost. Case A happens at every pod start and after every OpenCost or Prometheus gap; Case B mainly on fresh installs and whenever the node price query fails. The relative error grows as the step shrinks.

**Evidence.** Live: kind 'select timestamp, cost_source, count(*), sum(total_cost) ...' shows '11-15 06:14:30 | opencost | 34 | 0.0000000' and '11-15 06:44:30 | opencost | 34 | 0.0000000'; re-querying OpenCost for [06:44:30, 06:45:00) returns e.g. greenkube/greenkube-controller-555fc5795b-622tr with minutes 0, start = end = 06:45:00Z, cpuCoreRequestAverage 0.1, totalCost 0 (saved in audit/work/cost/oc_kind_0644.json). Simulation sim_cost.py scenario B passes these live items to the real _allocation_to_cost_metric, cost_fields and is_cost_trusted: 'CostMetric.source=opencost total=0.0' and 'is_cost_trusted(row)=True (row cpu_request=0m)' for the pod without requests. Case B: SQL comparing avg(total_cost) of opencost and opencost_derived rows per pod on kind (ratio 0.607 to 1.272, 759 rows).

**Fix.** 1. In _allocation_to_cost_metric treat an allocation with minutes <= 0 (or start == end, or cpuCoreHours == 0 and ramByteHours == 0 while a request or usage average is positive) as carrying no cost: source none, whatever the requests. The row then receives the modeled cost with its own label (or, with COST-01, the pending provenance and a later re-pricing). 2. For a non-zero allocation below PRECISION_FLOOR whose rebuild fails, do not keep the trusted label: use source none (fail closed, as the methodology states) or a new untrusted label opencost_rounded; count these in a metric greenkube_opencost_unrebuilt_allocations_total. 3. Move the zero rule out of the read path: is_cost_trusted must not accept a zero total from an OpenCost-family source for any row whose usage or request is positive; keep the current check as defence in depth. 4. Stored rows: migration or one-off command that sets cost_source to none for raw rows with cost_source opencost, total_cost = 0 and (cpu_request > 0 or memory_request > 0 or cpu_usage_millicores > 0), then 'greenkube recompute' to give them the modeled cost; hourly rows containing such steps cannot be repaired and should be marked mixed. 5. Update the OpenCost precision section of docs/methodology/impact-model.md.

**Acceptance.** (a) Unit test with the recorded live item (minutes 0, start = end, request 0.1 core, totalCost 0): CostMetric.source is none; for the pod without requests but with cpuCoreUsageAverage > 0 as well. (b) run_range with such an allocation stores a row whose cost_source is not opencost and whose total_cost is the modeled reservation cost, never 0 for a pod with a request. (c) A sub-precision allocation with cpuCost 0.00003 and no node price is not trusted. (d) is_cost_trusted({cost_source: opencost, total_cost: 0, cpu_usage_millicores: 6}) is False. (e) On the e2e clusters no row has cost_source like 'opencost%' and total_cost = 0 with a positive request.

#### COST-06 · The OpenCost credentials (OPENCOST_BEARER_TOKEN, OPENCOST_USERNAME, OPENCOST_PASSWORD) are sent only by the discovery health probe, never by the allocation requests, so an authenticated OpenCost or Kubecost silently yields no cost.

**Severity** medium · **Kind** bug · **Confidence** confirmed-sim · **Effort** S

**Where.** `src/greenkube/collectors/opencost_collector.py:354-358`; `src/greenkube/collectors/opencost_collector.py:408-415`; `src/greenkube/collectors/opencost_collector.py:490-493`; `src/greenkube/collectors/discovery/opencost.py:50-62`; `src/greenkube/core/config.py:204-206`; `helm-chart/templates/_helpers.tpl:240-243`; `src/greenkube/core/health.py:111-131`

**Mechanism.** config.py declares the three secrets and the Helm chart projects them into the API and controller pods. The only code that reads them is OpenCostDiscovery._probe_opencost_endpoint, which adds 'Authorization: Bearer ...' or basic auth to its /healthz probe. OpenCostCollector._get_client builds 'get_async_http_client(verify=verify)' (User-Agent only) and every request is 'client.get(u, params=params)' without headers or auth: collect, _fetch_node_prices, collect_pv_costs, collect_lb_costs and _probe. Behind an authenticating proxy the allocation call gets 401/403, collect() returns [] (see COST-01) and all cost becomes modeled, while discovery and the service health check, which do authenticate or only test /healthz, report OpenCost as reachable.

**Effect on numbers.** All cost figures of the install: OpenCost prices are replaced by list prices or default rates for every row, permanently, with cost_source pricing_catalog or custom_rate. Example: a cluster whose Kubecost reports negotiated prices 40% below list shows costs 67% too high on catalog-covered nodes, or generic 0.031611 per vCPU-hour rates elsewhere; no measured saving is possible on custom_rate rows.

**Affected environments.** Installs where OpenCost or Kubecost is exposed through an ingress, gateway or sidecar that requires a bearer token or basic auth (typical for Kubecost enterprise, multi-tenant platforms, cross-cluster access). In-cluster unauthenticated OpenCost is not affected.

**Evidence.** Code path above; grep shows the three settings referenced only in config.py and collectors/discovery/opencost.py. Simulation sim_cost.py scenario D with OPENCOST_BEARER_TOKEN set: 'get_async_http_client kwargs: {'verify': True}', 'Authorization header seen by OpenCost: None', 'collect_range result: []'. utils/http_client.py (SafeAsyncClient) adds no credentials.

**Fix.** 1. In OpenCostCollector._get_client build the client with the credentials: headers Authorization Bearer when OPENCOST_BEARER_TOKEN is set, httpx.BasicAuth when OPENCOST_USERNAME and OPENCOST_PASSWORD are set (same precedence as the Prometheus collector; reuse its helper if one exists). Recreate the client when the settings change. 2. Use the same client factory in discovery and in core/health.py, and make the health check call the allocation endpoint with a one-step window, so a 401 on /allocation is reported as unhealthy with the status code. 3. Treat 401/403 as a configuration error: log at error level once per run, set a gauge greenkube_opencost_auth_failed, and report it in GET /health/services. 4. Never forward the credentials on cross-host redirects (SafeAsyncClient already strips them). 5. Document the settings in docs/configuration.md (they are projected by the chart but not described). No data migration; windows collected while auth failed are re-priced by the reconciliation of COST-01.

**Acceptance.** (a) Unit test with httpx.MockTransport: with OPENCOST_BEARER_TOKEN=abc every request of collect, _fetch_node_prices, collect_pv_costs and collect_lb_costs carries 'Authorization: Bearer abc'; with username/password it carries the basic header; with neither, no Authorization header. (b) A 401 answer makes the OpenCost health entry unhealthy and increments the auth-failure metric; the collection does not label rows as if OpenCost were absent (see COST-01). (c) Helm unit test: the three keys are still projected to the controller and API.

#### COST-07 · Historical windows are priced with the requests and nodes read from the live API at collection time: rows of pods that no longer exist get request 0, which collapses their modeled cost and makes OpenCost cost inconsistent with the recorded reservation.

**Severity** medium · **Kind** data-integrity · **Confidence** confirmed-live · **Effort** L

**Where.** `src/greenkube/core/historical_range_processor.py:508-531`; `src/greenkube/core/historical_range_processor.py:536-539`; `src/greenkube/core/historical_range_processor.py:808-809`; `src/greenkube/core/historical_range_processor.py:844-856`; `src/greenkube/impact/rows.py:183-184`; `src/greenkube/impact/pricing.py:387-413`; `src/greenkube/impact/model.py:410-430`; `src/greenkube/storage/postgres/repository.py:280-281`

**Mechanism.** run_range calls pod_collector.collect() once ('pod_request_map_agg[key] += p.cpu_request') and uses the result for every step of the range: 'cpu_request=pod_request_map.get(pod_key, 0)'. A pod deleted before the collection (the last one or two steps of every terminated pod in normal operation, every replaced pod in a catch-up after controller downtime, and the whole first-install backfill of COLLECTION_MAX_BACKFILL = 24h) is stored with cpu_request 0 and memory_request 0; a pod whose requests changed is stored with the new requests for the old steps. Effects on cost: (1) a row without OpenCost cost is modeled from max(request, usage) = usage only; (2) a row with OpenCost cost keeps OpenCost's figure, which was computed from the real request, next to a recorded reservation of almost nothing, so every consumer that divides cost by reservation gets an inflated unit price (observed_rates_from_rows per node, sample_prices per sample); (3) _project_container_resources computes the released quantity from the change ('_released(current, proposed, sample.cpu_usage_cores)') and multiplies it by that price without capping it at the sample's own reservation, so the bound 'never more than the observed cost' (BUG-15) does not hold on such rows; pod-level rows are used by the projection when a target has only pod-level usage (context.container_rows with container None); (4) nodes are handled the same way: a node absent from the snapshots and from the live API is priced as 'cores = spec.cpu_capacity_cores or spec.vcores or 1.0' with 0 GiB of memory, so RAM cost is 0 and CPU cost is capped at one core for all its pods; (5) a re-collection overwrites correct stored requests ('cpu_request = EXCLUDED.cpu_request').

**Effect on numbers.** Modeled cost of rows of deleted pods (down by request/usage, often 10 to 100 times), node-level observed prices (up), per-sample unit prices and projections built on pod-level rows (up). Simulation G: pod with 2 cores / 4 GiB requested and 20m used, cluster without OpenCost, 1 h: 0.080170 when the pod still exists at collection time, 0.001046 when it was deleted before the catch-up (-98.7%). With OpenCost the row keeps cost_cpu 0.005268 per step but cpu_request 0: sample CPU price 3.161 per core-hour instead of 0.0316 (x100) and a projection 2.0 -> 0.5 core claims 4.7415/h while the whole observed CPU cost is 0.06322/h (x75). Live: minikube 46 rows and kind 35 rows have cpu_request 0 for pods that requested CPU at other steps; e.g. greenkube-api-76ff9d698c-cgmxn at 21:56:30 has cost_cpu 0.00002634 (the cost of 100m) with cpu_request 0 and usage 3m (implied price x33); the 41 backfill rows of oversized-web-685f69cd4f-cl2np (400m / 384Mi requested) are all stored with requests 0.

**Affected environments.** All. Largest on installs without OpenCost (all cost is modeled) after controller downtime or at first install, and on clusters with high pod or node churn (CI, batch, autoscaled pools).

**Evidence.** Live SQL: rows with cpu_request = 0 joined to the max request of the same pod, on both clusters (listing in the audit notes). Simulation sim_cost.py scenario G with the real run_range, sample_prices and model.project: 'pod deleted before the catch-up rows=12 cpu_request=0 cost_source=custom_rate 1h cost=0.001046 (reservation at default rates: 0.080170)' and 'projection 2.0 -> 0.5 core on these rows: CPU saving 4.741500/h, while the whole observed CPU cost is 0.063220/h (x75.0)'. Live kind: the 21 pod rows of greenkube-controller-d85c4df5f-zvrqt were overwritten with cpu_request 0 by a re-collection while their container rows still show 100m (COST-04).

**Fix.** 1. Requests must come from a time series, not from the live API: query kube-state-metrics per step (kube_pod_container_resource_requests for cpu and memory, by namespace, pod, container) with the same range queries as usage, and use the live pod list only as a fallback for the current step. Fail the window (RangeCollectionError) rather than write requests of 0 when neither source knows a pod that has usage. 2. Owner references of deleted pods: take them from kube_pod_owner in the same way. 3. Nodes: when a node has no snapshot and is not in the live API, read its capacity for the step from kube_node_status_capacity; never price against the 1-core / 0-GiB default: skip the modeled cost for that group (cost_source none, estimation reason) instead. 4. Distinguish 'request unknown' from 'request 0' in the row model (nullable column or a flag) so consumers can exclude unknown rows. 5. Hardening in the model: in _project_container_resources cap the released CPU and memory of a sample at sample.cpu_reserved_cores and sample.mem_reserved_gib, and in sample_prices ignore samples whose observed cost implies a unit price more than N times the node unit price (log and count them). 6. Upsert: do not overwrite stored non-zero requests with 0 on a re-collection. 7. Stored rows: mark raw rows with request 0 and an OpenCost cost that implies a request (cost_cpu / price x 1000 above usage) as request-unknown; rows inside Prometheus retention can be repaired by re-collecting with the new request source.

**Acceptance.** (a) Range test where the pod list is empty but kube-state-metrics range data holds requests 2000m / 4 GiB: rows carry cpu_request 2000 and the modeled 1 h cost is 0.080170. (b) Neither source knows the pod: no row with request 0 and a non-zero modeled cost is written; the row is flagged request-unknown and untrusted. (c) Model test: a baseline of rows with request 0, usage 20m and observed cost C never projects more than C for a change 2.0 -> 0.5 core. (d) A catch-up window on a node missing from snapshots and API uses kube_node_status_capacity, or writes cost_source none. (e) Re-collecting a window after the pod was deleted leaves cpu_request and memory_request unchanged.

#### COST-08 · Cost provenance stops at the database: the API and UI show one cost figure that mixes OpenCost costs, list prices, generic default rates and zero rows, with no indication of the share that is estimated, and the OpenCost health check does not test the allocation call.

**Severity** medium · **Kind** ux · **Confidence** confirmed-live · **Effort** M

**Where.** `src/greenkube/api/schemas.py:385`; `src/greenkube/api/routers/config.py:79`; `src/greenkube/api/metrics_endpoint.py:929-934`; `src/greenkube/core/health.py:111-131`; `src/greenkube/impact/pricing.py:61-73`; `src/greenkube/data/pricing/opencost_default.json`; `frontend/src/lib/utils/format.js:56-70`; `frontend/src/lib/charts.js:106`

**Mechanism.** Every stored row has cost_source, but 'grep -r cost_source src/greenkube/api frontend/src' finds nothing: no router or schema returns it, the cache tables metrics_summary and metrics_timeseries_cache hold total_cost only, and the UI renders 'Cost' in the configured currency. On a cluster without OpenCost and without IMPACT_PRICING_* (the default on-prem install) every amount is the reservation priced at OpenCost's default custom pricing, described by OpenCost as GCP us-central1 prices (0.031611 per vCPU-hour, 0.004237 per GiB-hour, USD): the figure has no relation to the cost of the hardware, and nothing in the API or UI says so. With OpenCost the total silently mixes observed rows, modeled rows for pods OpenCost does not know, fallback rows (COST-01) and zero rows (COST-05). GET /health/services only probes /healthz, so it stays green while allocation calls fail or return nothing.

**Effect on numbers.** Dashboard and API totals (metrics/summary, timeseries, namespace and workload views): not wrong by a fixed factor, but of unknown quality to the reader. Live minikube: 5,317 of 20,250 pod rows (26%, 5.9% of the cost) are custom_rate default-rate costs inside a total presented like the rest; GET /api/v1/metrics/summary?last=1h returns {total_cost: 0.0502, ...} with no provenance field. On a bare-metal cluster a 64-core / 256 GiB node is shown at 64 x 0.031611 + 256 x 0.004237 = 3.11 per hour (27,200 per year) by default, whatever its real cost (typically several times lower on owned hardware).

**Affected environments.** All; most misleading on-prem and on providers without catalog coverage (GCP, see COST-09) where 100% of the cost is the generic default.

**Evidence.** Live: 'live.sh api api/v1/health' -> {status ok}; 'api/v1/config' exposes only currency; 'api/v1/metrics/summary?last=1h' has no provenance; information_schema shows metrics_summary and metrics_timeseries_cache without any source column; SQL group by cost_source on both clusters. Code: no occurrence of cost_source under src/greenkube/api or frontend/src.

**Fix.** 1. Storage: add to the hourly/summary/timeseries cache tables the cost split by provenance family: cost_observed (opencost, opencost_derived, kubecost), cost_list_price (pricing_catalog), cost_default_rate (custom_rate with detail opencost_default), cost_custom_rate (configured), cost_unpriced_rows (count of rows with none or zero). Fill them in summary_refresher and the compressor. 2. API: return these fields plus cost_observed_share on /metrics/summary, /metrics/timeseries and the namespace/workload endpoints, and return cost_source on row-level endpoints. Add GET /api/v1/cost/pricing describing the active tiers: OpenCost reachable and last successful allocation, catalog provider/region coverage for the current nodes, custom rates and whether they are the built-in defaults, currency. 3. UI: a provenance badge next to every cost total (Observed / List price / Default rates) with the observed share, and a blocking banner when more than a threshold (for example 20%) of the cost in the selected window comes from default rates: 'Costs use generic default rates (GCP us-central1 list prices). Set impact.pricing.* or install OpenCost.' 4. Setup: on an unknown or on-prem provider without OpenCost and without configured rates, the setup status and Helm NOTES must ask for rates. 5. Health: see COST-06 item 2. 6. Backfill: the new summary columns are derived from existing rows by one refresh; no row migration.

**Acceptance.** (a) API test: a window with 3 opencost rows (cost 3), 1 pricing_catalog row (1) and 1 custom_rate default row (1) returns total_cost 5, cost_observed 3, cost_list_price 1, cost_default_rate 1, cost_observed_share 0.6. (b) Frontend test: the dashboard shows the Default rates badge and the banner when cost_default_rate / total_cost > 0.2. (c) GET /api/v1/cost/pricing on a cluster without OpenCost and without IMPACT_PRICING_* reports default_rates true with the source text of opencost_default.json. (d) The OpenCost health entry turns unhealthy when the allocation endpoint answers 401 or an empty set for a window with running pods.

#### COST-09 · The bundled price catalogs cover a small part of the fleets (GCP empty, AWS 35 families, Azure 100 sizes, missing the default AKS sizes), ignore spot and discounts, have no age check, and yet pricing_catalog is trusted to book measured savings.

**Severity** medium · **Kind** design · **Confidence** confirmed-code · **Effort** L

**Where.** `scripts/generate_pricing_catalog.py:70-71`; `scripts/generate_pricing_catalog.py:376-379`; `scripts/generate_pricing_catalog.py:391-392`; `src/greenkube/data/pricing/gcp.json:11`; `src/greenkube/data/pricing/aws.json`; `src/greenkube/data/pricing/azure.json`; `src/greenkube/impact/pricing.py:174-191`; `src/greenkube/impact/pricing.py:360-384`; `src/greenkube/models/provenance.py:67-69`; `src/greenkube/models/node.py:28-36`

**Mechanism.** (1) Coverage. The generator keeps for AWS and Azure only the instance types that have a power profile ('keep = known_instance_types(provider) if filtered'). aws.json has 257 types in us-east-1 from 35 families (m5, m6g, m6i, m7g, m7i, m8g, r5-r8g, t3, t4g, x1, x2, z1d): c5, c6i, c6a, c6g, c7g, c7i, m5a, m6a, m7a, t3a, i3 and all GPU families are absent. azure.json has 97 sizes per region: Standard_DS2_v2, Standard_D2s_v3 and Standard_D4s_v3 (common AKS defaults), Dasv5, Ddsv5 and the v6 series are absent. gcp.json has 'regions': {}. A node that is not found falls to custom_rate at the OpenCost default rates. One cluster can therefore mix trusted list prices (m5 nodes) and untrusted generic rates (c5 nodes). (2) Capacity type. NodeInfo carries no capacity type and no code reads karpenter.sh/capacity-type, eks.amazonaws.com/capacityType, cloud.google.com/gke-spot or kubernetes.azure.com/scalesetpriority, so spot and preemptible nodes are priced on-demand; reserved instances, savings plans and committed-use discounts are ignored as documented. (3) Trust. TRUSTED_COST_SOURCES contains pricing_catalog, so a saving measured on these rows is booked in the ledger at list price. (4) Age. generated_at (2026-11-07) is not read at runtime: the catalog ages with the installed release and nothing warns about it.

**Effect on numbers.** Without OpenCost: cost of all nodes not in a catalog (every GCP node; AWS compute-optimised, AMD, burstable-a and GPU nodes; common Azure sizes) is the generic rate: t3a.medium 0.080/h against 0.0376 list (+113%), m6a.large 0.097 against 0.0864 (+12%), c5.large 0.080 against 0.085 (-6%), g4dn.xlarge 0.194 against 0.526 (-63%, GPU not priced), GCP e2 machines about +45% (e2 is 0.0218 per vCPU-hour and 0.0029 per GiB-hour in us-central1). Spot nodes covered by a catalog are over-stated by the spot discount (typically 2 to 4 times) and the measured savings booked from them by the same factor.

**Affected environments.** Clouds without OpenCost: GCP (all nodes), AWS and Azure (uncovered types and all spot or discounted capacity). OVHcloud and Scaleway catalogs are complete but only used when IMPACT_PRICING_CURRENCY is EUR (see COST-11). Not relevant when OpenCost prices every pod.

**Evidence.** Static inspection of the catalog files (python: families in regions['us-east-1'], lookups of m6a.large, c5.large, c6i.large, c7g.large, t3a.medium, g4dn.xlarge = absent; westeurope Standard_DS2_v2, Standard_D2s_v3, Standard_D4s_v3, Standard_D2as_v5 = absent; gcp regions = {}), the generator filter, grep for spot/capacity-type in src/greenkube (only the Karpenter recommendation provider), provenance.py. Spot-checked present prices are correct (see verified_correct).

**Fix.** 1. Decouple the catalog from the power-profile list: generate AWS and Azure with --all-instance-types for current-generation Linux types (compressed or per-region files loaded lazily if size is a concern; the index is already lazy per provider), and add a CI check that the 50 most common EKS and AKS node types resolve in the main regions. 2. GCP: ship per-family per-region vCPU and GiB prices (generated offline with an API key from the Cloud Billing Catalog API, or from the public pricing pages) and compute a node price from the machine type's family, vCPUs and memory; until then label GCP nodes explicitly (detail gcp_default_rates). 3. Capacity type: add capacity_type (on_demand, spot, unknown) to NodeInfo from the node labels listed above; for spot nodes do not use the on-demand catalog price as a trusted cost: either apply a documented spot estimate labelled untrusted (new cost_source pricing_catalog_spot_estimate) or fall to custom rates. 4. Trust: keep pricing_catalog trusted only for on-demand nodes; add an install-level switch impact.pricing.discounted (reserved, savings plans, negotiated) that makes list-price savings untrusted or scales them by a configured discount factor. 5. Age: expose generated_at in GET /api/v1/cost/pricing (COST-08), log a warning and show a UI notice when the catalog is older than 180 days; document the regeneration in the release checklist. 6. No stored-row migration; affected rows keep their label, and 'greenkube recompute' re-prices raw rows after a catalog update.

**Acceptance.** (a) PricingCatalog.lookup resolves c5.large, c6i.large, c7g.large, m6a.large, t3a.medium in us-east-1 and eu-west-1 and Standard_DS2_v2, Standard_D2s_v3, Standard_D4s_v3 in westeurope and eastus. (b) A GCP e2-standard-4 node in europe-west1 gets a price within 3% of the public list price, or is labelled gcp_default_rates. (c) A node labelled karpenter.sh/capacity-type=spot never yields a row that is_cost_trusted accepts from the on-demand catalog. (d) With a catalog whose generated_at is 200 days old, the pricing endpoint reports stale true. (e) CI fails when a catalog file has an empty regions map for a provider listed in CATALOG_PROVIDERS without the explicit default-rate label.

#### COST-10 · The OpenCost query has no cluster filter and aggregates by namespace and pod only, so a multi-cluster OpenCost or Kubecost merges same-named pods of different clusters into one cost.

**Severity** medium · **Kind** bug · **Confidence** likely · **Effort** S

**Where.** `src/greenkube/collectors/opencost_collector.py:390-393`; `src/greenkube/collectors/opencost_collector.py:265-277`; `src/greenkube/collectors/opencost_collector.py:483-488`; `src/greenkube/core/historical_range_processor.py:576-577`

**Mechanism.** collect() sends 'params = {"window": window, "aggregate": "namespace,pod"}' plus accumulate and includeIdle, and reads only properties.namespace and properties.pod; properties.cluster (present in every allocation, 'default-cluster' in the live installs) is ignored and CLUSTER_NAME is never sent as a filter. When the queried endpoint serves several clusters (Kubecost with federated storage, or OpenCost on a shared Thanos or Mimir), allocations of pods with the same namespace and name are summed by the aggregation: StatefulSet pods (postgres-0, prometheus-k8s-0), static pods on equally named nodes and any fixed-name pod. The node unit prices used for the sub-precision rebuild (aggregate=node) are merged the same way for equally named nodes. cost_map is keyed (namespace, pod), so the merged figure is attributed to the local pod.

**Effect on numbers.** Cost of same-named pods: multiplied by the number of clusters that have such a pod (2 clusters with monitoring/prometheus-k8s-0: x2), labelled opencost and trusted. Pods with generated suffixes are not affected.

**Affected environments.** Multi-cluster Kubecost or OpenCost backed by a shared metrics store. Single-cluster OpenCost (the live installs) is not affected.

**Evidence.** Code reading end to end; the live OpenCost answers show properties.cluster on every allocation and no filter in the request. Not reproduced: no multi-cluster OpenCost was available, and the behaviour of the aggregation across clusters is taken from the OpenCost allocation API semantics (aggregation keys define identity).

**Fix.** 1. Add an optional setting OPENCOST_CLUSTER_ID (Helm config.opencost.clusterId; default: auto). 2. Query with aggregate=cluster,namespace,pod and, when a cluster id is configured, add the OpenCost filter (filter=cluster:"<id>"). 3. In _allocation_to_cost_metric keep properties.cluster on CostMetric; in collect_range, when the answer contains more than one cluster and no id is configured, do not guess: log an error, set a gauge greenkube_opencost_ambiguous_cluster and return the allocations of no cluster (status unavailable under COST-01), so no merged cost is stored. 4. Apply the same filter to _fetch_node_prices (aggregate=cluster,node) and to the PV/LB lookups. 5. Document the setting.

**Acceptance.** (a) Fixture with two clusters holding monitoring/prometheus-k8s-0 at cost 0.01 and 0.02: with OPENCOST_CLUSTER_ID=a the stored cost is 0.01; without an id nothing is stored as opencost and the ambiguity metric is 1. (b) Single-cluster fixture: results identical to today. (c) Node price rebuild uses only the configured cluster's node.

#### COST-11 · Currencies are labels, not checked values: the USD default rates are relabelled with IMPACT_PRICING_CURRENCY, OpenCost's currency is never read, and recommendation texts print a dollar sign.

**Severity** low · **Kind** design · **Confidence** confirmed-sim · **Effort** S

**Where.** `src/greenkube/impact/pricing.py:249-262`; `src/greenkube/impact/pricing.py:360-369`; `src/greenkube/core/config.py:431-433`; `src/greenkube/api/routers/config.py:79`; `src/greenkube/core/optimization/analyzers/orphaned_pv.py:66-73`; `src/greenkube/core/optimization/analyzers/orphaned_lb.py:71-103`

**Mechanism.** CustomRates.from_config takes the OpenCost default figures (USD) when no rate is configured and stamps them with the configured currency: 'currency = ... IMPACT_PRICING_CURRENCY', 'cpu_core_hour = cpu if cpu is not None else defaults.cpu_core_hour'. The documentation tells OVHcloud and Scaleway users to set EUR to enable their catalogs; every node that is not in the catalog (and every flat PV/LB default) is then a USD figure shown as EUR. Observed OpenCost costs are also stamped with the configured currency ('Observed costs are labeled with IMPACT_PRICING_CURRENCY'), without reading OpenCost's own currency, so a mismatch is undetectable. Because the default currency is USD, the EUR catalogs are skipped silently (debug log only) on OVHcloud and Scaleway. The PV and LoadBalancer recommendation texts contain a literal '$'.

**Effect on numbers.** Amounts are off by the exchange rate (about 5 to 15% between USD and EUR) for default-rate rows and flat defaults when the currency is EUR; sums can mix EUR catalog prices with USD OpenCost defaults in one total labelled EUR. On OVHcloud or Scaleway with the default USD, all costs are the generic USD default instead of the provider's EUR list price (for example Scaleway DEV1-L 0.04284 EUR/h list against 4 x 0.031611 + 8 x 0.004237 = 0.160 USD/h default, x3.7).

**Affected environments.** Installs that set a non-USD currency, and OVHcloud or Scaleway installs that keep the default USD.

**Evidence.** Simulation sim_cost.py scenario F: 'IMPACT_PRICING_CURRENCY=EUR, no custom rates -> {cpu_core_hour: 0.031611, ram_gib_hour: 0.004237, currency: EUR, source: custom_rate, detail: opencost_default}'. Code reading of the resolver currency check and of the analyzers' description strings.

**Fix.** 1. Refuse to relabel: when IMPACT_PRICING_CURRENCY differs from the currency of the default rates and no custom rate is configured, either fail configuration validation with a clear message or keep the default rates unpriced (cost_source none) for affected nodes; never emit USD figures under another code. Same rule for STORAGE_COST_PER_GIB_MONTH and LOAD_BALANCER_COST_PER_MONTH. 2. Read OpenCost's currency once per run (its configuration endpoint exposes currencyCode for custom pricing; cloud pricing is USD) and expose a mismatch as a warning in GET /api/v1/cost/pricing, a log line and a metric. 3. On a node whose provider has a catalog in another currency than the configured one, log at warning level once and report it in the pricing endpoint ('catalog available in EUR; set impact.pricing.currency'). 4. Replace the literal '$' in orphaned_pv.py and orphaned_lb.py by the configured currency code. 5. Docs: one table of which source produces which currency.

**Acceptance.** (a) Config with IMPACT_PRICING_CURRENCY=EUR and no custom rates: validation error, or modeled rows on uncatalogued nodes carry cost_source none; no UnitPrices object has currency EUR with the value 0.031611. (b) Scaleway node DEV1-L with currency USD: the pricing endpoint lists the skipped EUR catalog. (c) A fake OpenCost reporting currencyCode EUR while the setting is USD raises the mismatch warning. (d) Recommendation descriptions contain the configured currency code and no '$' when the currency is EUR.

#### COST-12 · Projections read every stored positive cost as an observed cost: default-rate rows get confidence 0.7 instead of 0.5 and mixed hourly aggregates are presented as OpenCost with confidence 0.9.

**Severity** low · **Kind** bug · **Confidence** confirmed-sim · **Effort** S

**Where.** `src/greenkube/impact/projector.py:175-181`; `src/greenkube/impact/model.py:43-53`; `src/greenkube/impact/model.py:163-202`; `src/greenkube/impact/types.py:38-54`

**Mechanism.** sample_from_metric sets 'observed_cost_total=total_cost if total_cost is not None and total_cost > 0 else None' without looking at cost_source. Since rows without OpenCost now store a modeled cost, sample_prices takes the observed branch for them: 'source, raw_source = parse_cost_source(sample.observed_cost_source)', 'confidence = PRICE_CONFIDENCE.get(source, ...)' gives 0.7 for custom_rate whatever its detail, while the node-level path (price_confidence) gives 0.5 for the built-in default rates; the detail becomes observed_components and the rates are listed under observed prices. parse_cost_source maps mixed (an hourly aggregate that contains fallback or zero rows) and unknown labels to opencost, so such a sample is priced as an OpenCost observation with confidence 0.9.

**Effect on numbers.** Projection confidence and the provenance printed with each recommendation and pull request (assumptions.unit_prices, observed_cost_sources): 0.7 instead of 0.5 for clusters on default rates (+40%), 0.9 for mixed aggregates. Ranking scores that use confidence move accordingly. The projected amounts themselves are unchanged for modeled rows.

**Affected environments.** All installs without OpenCost (every projection) and installs with OpenCost for hours that contain fallback rows.

**Evidence.** Simulation sim_cost.py scenario F with the real sample_from_metric and sample_prices: 'stored custom_rate(opencost_default) row -> sample_prices: observed=True source=custom_rate detail=observed_components confidence=0.7 (node-level default-rate confidence is 0.5)' and 'stored mixed hourly row -> sample_prices: observed=True source=opencost confidence=0.9 raw_source=mixed'.

**Fix.** 1. In sample_from_metric set the observed_* fields only when the row's cost_source is in ALLOCATED_COST_SOURCES (opencost, opencost_derived, kubecost) or is empty on a legacy row with a positive cost (same rule as rows._is_observed_cost; share one helper). Modeled rows then use the node unit prices and price_confidence, which already distinguishes configured rates from defaults. 2. For a mixed aggregate keep the amount (it is the stored cost) but set the source to mixed with DEFAULT_RATE_CONFIDENCE, and do not report it as opencost in assumptions; add CostSource.MIXED handling to PRICE_CONFIDENCE. 3. Store the rate detail (configured or opencost_default) with modeled rows, for example in estimation_reasons or a new cost_detail column, so recompute and projections can tell them apart. 4. Re-run the recommendation scan after the change; no row migration.

**Acceptance.** (a) sample_prices on a row with cost_source custom_rate returns observed False and confidence 0.5 with default rates, 0.7 with configured rates. (b) A row with cost_source mixed returns source mixed and confidence 0.5, and assumptions.observed_cost_sources does not list opencost. (c) A row with cost_source opencost_derived is unchanged (observed True, 0.9). (d) The projected amount of a modeled row is identical before and after the change.

#### COST-13 · Idle cost is always priced with the model, does not reconcile with OpenCost pod costs (pods + idle differ from the node cost) and is read by no API, UI or report.

**Severity** low · **Kind** design · **Confidence** confirmed-live · **Effort** M

**Where.** `src/greenkube/impact/rows.py:321-367`; `src/greenkube/collectors/opencost_collector.py:393`; `src/greenkube/collectors/opencost_collector.py:467-504`; `src/greenkube/storage/postgres/repository.py:560-597`

**Mechanism.** _idle_row prices the unreserved capacity at modeled (catalog or custom) prices: 'cost_cpu = _rescale(impact.cost_allocated.cpu, node.prices.cpu_core_hour, modeled.cpu_core_hour)', 'cost_source=modeled.source.value', even on clusters where OpenCost prices are known (the collector already fetches OpenCost node unit prices for the sub-precision rebuild). The unreserved quantity is capacity minus GreenKube's own reservation max(request, usage), which differs from the basis OpenCost charged. The two parts therefore do not add up to the node cost, and when OpenCost prices differ from the modeled tier (spot, discounts, custom pricing) the idle part is on another price basis than the pods. The methodology documents the model-only idle cost, but node_idle_metrics is only written, compressed and recomputed: no router, summary or UI reads it, so the cost GreenKube displays is the allocated workload cost, not the cluster cost, without saying so.

**Effect on numbers.** Idle cost and any node or cluster total built from pods + idle. Live minikube 2026-11-14 22:00-23:00 (6 cores, 15.6 GiB at default rates = 0.25578/h): pods 0.123128 + idle 0.119796 = 0.242924 (-5.0%); RAM alone 0.030079 + 0.024316 = 0.054395 against 0.066106 (-17.7%) because OpenCost charged 5.78 GiB-hours while GreenKube subtracted 9.86 GiB of reservation from idle. The dashboard total for that hour (0.123) is 48% of the node cost. With spot nodes the idle part would be priced about 3 times higher than the pods.

**Affected environments.** All installs with OpenCost; the exposure gap applies to all installs.

**Evidence.** Live SQL on node_idle_metrics and combined_metrics for the hour, node capacity from kubectl, OpenCost aggregate=node for the same hour (cpuCoreHours 2.928, ramByteHours 5.78 GiB-h); grep shows node_idle_metrics read only by impact/recompute.py and the compressor.

**Fix.** 1. Price idle on the pods' basis: when the node has OpenCost unit prices (the per-node aggregate already queried by _fetch_node_prices; query it for every window rather than only for sub-precision pods), compute idle = node cost at OpenCost prices minus the sum of OpenCost pod costs on the node (never negative), label it opencost_derived; fall back to the modeled tier otherwise. 2. Add an invariant check per node and step: abs(pods + idle - node cost) / node cost below 2% when one price basis is used; count violations in a metric and log the worst node. 3. Expose idle: add idle cost, idle energy and their provenance to the summary and timeseries endpoints and show 'allocated to workloads' and 'idle capacity' as two parts of the cluster cost in the UI. 4. Document the definition of the displayed cost. 5. Stored idle rows inside raw retention are rewritten by 'greenkube recompute' once the new rule exists.

**Acceptance.** (a) Range test with OpenCost node prices and three pods: idle row total equals node cost minus pod costs within 1% and carries an OpenCost-family label. (b) The invariant metric is 0 for that fixture and non-zero when a pod row is priced on another basis. (c) GET /metrics/summary returns idle_cost and the UI total equals allocated + idle. (d) On the live minikube hour the reconstructed node cost is within 2% of 0.2558.

### 5.4 Energy and carbon

**Coverage.** Read in full: energy/estimator.py, energy/node_power.py, impact/power.py, impact/model.py, impact/allocation.py, impact/rows.py, impact/types.py, impact/recompute.py, core/calculator.py, core/embodied_service.py, core/node_zone_mapper.py, core/historical_range_processor.py (all 965 lines), core/metric_assembler.py, collectors/electricity_maps_collector.py, wattnet_collector.py, base_electricity_provider.py, boavizta_collector.py, dcgm_collector.py, models/boavizta.py, utils/mapping_translator.py, data/instance_profiles.py, region_mapping.py, datacenter_pue_profiles.py, electricity_maps_regions_grid_intensity_default.py and the data files (defaults CSV parsed and cross-checked against the region mapping; instance/provider/GPU tables). Read the relevant parts of: core/config.py (energy/carbon settings, PUE resolution), core/collection.py (watermark), core/metrics_compressor.py (aggregation columns, idle pruning), cli/start.py (intensity job, recompute, node analysis, loop cadence), storage/base_repository.py + storage/postgres/repository.py (intensity lookups, save_history, recompute, aggregate_summary), storage/*/node_repository.py (SCD compare columns), impact/projector.py (node profile and sample conversion), api/routers/report.py, the Footprint page, docs/methodology/impact-model.md, docs/power_estimation_methodology.md, docs/wattnet.md (skimmed), the defect register of docs/roadmap.md. Ran (all scripts and outputs under audit-workspace/audit/work/energy-carbon): recompute_live.py (independent hand recompute of two 30 s steps, 2026-11-15 06:49:00Z and 2026-11-14 22:30:00Z, on minikube and on the three kind nodes: per-row energy matches to 1e-7, conservation holds); sim_boavizta.py; sim_range.py a/b/c; sim_recompute.py; sim_zone.py; sim_k8s_blip.py (real GreenKube code on temporary SQLite databases with fakes); one GET on the public Boavizta API to capture the real response shape; read-only live SQL / PromQL / kubectl / API / log queries on both environments (provenance distribution, intensity history, idle vs pod totals, node snapshot versions, request flips, node-level CPU, node bootIDs, float4 SUM behaviour with a computed query, one recommendation impact payload). The two existing Boavizta/embodied test files were run (19 passed) to show they pass against a mocked, outdated payload. NOT covered: any real cloud cluster (cloud paths by code and simulation only); Wattnet and Electricity Maps live payloads (no credentials used) including Wattnet timestamp/null formats; real GPUs/DCGM; SQLite variants of the intensity lookups beyond the recompute; SummaryRefresher / timeseries cache internals and the workload_usage_hourly energy columns; how the frontend displays provenance flags beyond the Footprint totals; the CO2e path inside verify/ and the ledger (only the projection side was checked); the demo data generator; the raw/hourly split of aggregate_summary by wall-clock cutoff (noticed: hours between the compression watermark and now-24h can be missed or partially counted — storage dimension, not analysed); energy behaviour when Prometheus has gaps (no idle row is written for steps without samples, so node energy is absent for those steps; live data has multi-hour gaps from host sleep — not analysed further). Wall-clock used: about 30 of 55 minutes.

**Earlier defects not fully fixed.**

- BUG-06 (partially_fixed): Container rows are now recomputed with pod rows (verified by simulation), but the same defect exists for the newer node_idle_metrics table (NRG-09).
- BUG-08 (partially_fixed): Collection uses capacity-derived profiles with provenance and resolves nodes via kube_pod_info; the projector still uses 1 W/10 W per core for unlisted instance types (NRG-05).
- BUG-18 (partially_fixed): Memory power and DCGM GPU power are modelled; GPU nodes without DCGM are undetected and unflagged (NRG-13); storage and network energy are still excluded (documented, not flagged in API/UI).
- BUG-19 (partially_fixed): Cost and energy now share the reservation boundary with an explicit __idle__ row, but idle rows are never exposed, rolled up or kept beyond raw retention (NRG-02).

#### NRG-01 · The Boavizta response model does not match the real API (gwp.embedded/use are objects), so every lookup fails and every node gets the flat DEFAULT_EMBODIED_EMISSIONS_KG=100 kg fallback, unflagged and re-queried every cycle.

**Severity** critical · **Kind** bug · **Confidence** confirmed-sim · **Effort** M

**Where.** `src/greenkube/models/boavizta.py:13-25`; `src/greenkube/collectors/boavizta_collector.py:80-98`; `src/greenkube/core/embodied_service.py:62-88`; `src/greenkube/core/embodied_service.py:101-117`; `src/greenkube/core/historical_range_processor.py:394-408`; `src/greenkube/core/metric_assembler.py:126-180`; `tests/collectors/test_boavizta_collector.py:21`

**Mechanism.** `BoaviztaGwp` declares `manufacture: Optional[float]` and `use: Optional[float]`, and `BoaviztaResponse.verbose: Optional[bool]`. The public API (v1.x; the repo itself ships api.boavizta.org-openapi.json version 1.3.11) answers `{'impacts':{'gwp':{'unit':'kgCO2eq','embedded':{'value':24.0,'min':13.92,'max':40.05,...},'use':{'value':200.0,...}}}` and, with verbose=true (what `prepare_embodied_data` sends), a `verbose` object. `BoaviztaResponse(**response.json())` therefore raises a pydantic ValidationError on `impacts.gwp.use`; `_get_cloud_instance_impact` catches it in `except Exception` ('Unexpected error calling Boavizta'), returns None, and `prepare_embodied_data` injects `{'gwp_manufacture': DEFAULT_EMBODIED_EMISSIONS_KG (100.0), 'lifespan_hours': 35040, 'is_fallback': True}`. Even if parsing succeeded, the code reads `gwp.manufacture`, a field the API no longer returns (the value is `gwp.embedded.value`). The fallback is not persisted, so the lookup (one HTTPS call per distinct provider/instance type) is repeated on every collection cycle forever. On-prem nodes (`cloud_provider='unknown'`, instance type 'cpu-N', both truthy) skip the HTTP call and get the same 100 kg. The `is_fallback` marker goes nowhere: `EmbodiedEmissionsService.is_embodied_fallback` has no caller, `build_estimation_flags` has no embodied reason, and there is no `embodied_source` provenance column, so rows carry a made-up constant with no flag. The existing tests pass because they mock the pre-v1 shape `{'gwp': {'manufacture': 1500.0}}`.

**Effect on numbers.** `embodied_co2e_grams` of every pod row, every idle row, `total_embodied_co2e_grams` / 'Scope 3 CO₂ (hardware)' in the UI, the embodied component of projections (`co2e_breakdown_annual_g.embodied`) and of change measurements. Every node is charged 100 kg / 35,040 h = 2.854 g/h whatever its size. Real Boavizta value for aws m5.large is 24 kg (fetched 2026-11-15) -> GreenKube over-states it 4.2×; a 96-vCPU m5.24xlarge is of the order of 1 t -> under-stated ~10×; an on-prem 2-socket server (typically 1–2 t) -> under-stated 10–20×. Cluster embodied totals are simply 2.854 g/h × node count. Example from live recommendation id 95 (minikube): embodied 37.45 g/yr = 78.74 core-h × (2.854 g/h / 6 cores).

**Affected environments.** All: every cloud (AWS/GCP/Azure/OVH/Scaleway) and on-prem. Air-gapped installs additionally log a failed HTTPS call per instance type per cycle.

**Evidence.** audit-workspace/audit/work/energy-carbon/boavizta_m5_large.json (GET https://api.boavizta.org/v1/cloud/instance?provider=aws&instance_type=m5.large, HTTP 200). audit-workspace/audit/work/energy-carbon/sim_boavizta.py (real BoaviztaCollector with an httpx MockTransport returning that payload + real EmbodiedEmissionsService): 'model parse: FAILS -> ValidationError ... impacts.gwp.use Input should be a valid number', 'collector.get_server_impact -> None', cache = 100.0 kg / is_fallback for aws/m5.large, aws/m5.24xlarge and unknown/cpu-64 (2.8539 g/h each), 'cycle 1: HTTP calls=3, cycle 2: HTTP calls=5, save_profile calls=0'. Live: both controllers log 'Fetching 1 missing Boavizta profiles from API...' and 'Boavizta profile unavailable for unknown/cpu-N. Using fallback embodied emissions' every 30 s cycle; `select count(*) filter (where estimation_reasons ilike '%embodied%' or estimation_reasons ilike '%boavizta%'), count(*) from combined_metrics` -> 0 | 22138 (minikube), 0 | 14116 (kind); hand recompute: pods + idle embodied per node-step = 0.023782 g = 100 kg/35040 h × 30 s. `select count(*) from instance_carbon_profiles` -> 0 on minikube (nothing is ever cached). tests/collectors/test_boavizta_collector.py + tests/core/test_embodied_fallback.py: 19 passed (mocked old format).

**Fix.** 1) models/boavizta.py: model the v1 schema — `class BoaviztaImpactValue(BaseModel): value: float|None; min: float|None; max: float|None; warnings: list[str]|None` ; `class BoaviztaGwp: embedded: BoaviztaImpactValue|float|None; use: BoaviztaImpactValue|float|None; manufacture: float|None (legacy); unit: str|None`; `verbose: Any`; `model_config = ConfigDict(extra='ignore')`. Add a helper `embedded_kg()` returning `embedded.value` (or legacy `manufacture`) and checking `unit == 'kgCO2eq'`. Send `verbose=false` (the verbose tree is unused) and pass no `duration` so the value is the lifetime total (API doc: 'If no duration is given, the life_duration of the asset is used'); store Boavizta's `hours_life_time` if read, otherwise keep DEFAULT_HARDWARE_LIFESPAN_YEARS. 2) embodied_service.prepare_embodied_data: read `impact.impacts.gwp.embedded_kg()`. Persist a negative-cache entry for failures (table instance_carbon_profiles, today empty on both live clusters: add `source` = boavizta | fallback | configured, `fetched_at`, `error`) and retry at most every 24 h instead of every cycle. Do not write `embodied_emissions_kg` on NodeInfo snapshots here (see NRG-07). 3) Fallback scaling: replace the flat 100 kg by a capacity-proportional fallback (`DEFAULT_EMBODIED_KG_PER_VCPU`, default ≈ 12 kg/vCPU, the Boavizta m5 family ratio, plus optional `DEFAULT_EMBODIED_KG_PER_GIB`), and allow an operator override per node (annotation `greenkube.io/embodied-kg`, and a Helm map by instance type / node label) for on-prem hardware. 4) Provenance: add column `embodied_source` (boavizta | configured | fallback_per_vcpu | none) to combined_metrics, container_metrics, node_idle_metrics and their hourly tables (aggregate with `_source_aggregate`); when the source is a fallback append an estimation reason and expose it in API rows, summaries (`embodied_sources` breakdown) and in projection assumptions. 5) Backfill: bump IMPACT_MODEL_VERSION (embodied numbers change), document that `greenkube recompute --since <retention>` re-allocates raw rows; hourly aggregates keep the old values and must be reported as `embodied_source=legacy_fallback`. 6) Tests: replace the mocked fixtures by a recorded v1 payload (commit boavizta_m5_large.json-like fixtures for aws, azure, gcp, scaleway) and add a contract test that validates the model against the bundled OpenAPI example.

**Acceptance.** a) Feeding the recorded v1 payload for aws/m5.large to BoaviztaCollector.get_server_impact returns embedded 24.0 kg; prepare_embodied_data caches gwp 24.0 without is_fallback and calls save_profile once; a second call performs 0 HTTP requests. b) With the API unreachable, a 96-vCPU node gets fallback 96 × DEFAULT_EMBODIED_KG_PER_VCPU, rows carry embodied_source=fallback_per_vcpu and an estimation reason, and only one HTTP attempt is made per 24 h. c) On-prem node annotated greenkube.io/embodied-kg=1500 -> rows allocate 1500 kg/35040 h and embodied_source=configured. d) /metrics/summary exposes the embodied source mix. e) The live kind controller no longer logs 'Fetching 1 missing Boavizta profiles' every cycle.

#### NRG-02 · Cluster totals (API, UI Footprint page, yearly report) silently exclude the node __idle__ share; idle rows are write-only, never rolled up and deleted after raw retention.

**Severity** high · **Kind** data-integrity · **Confidence** confirmed-live · **Effort** L

**Where.** `src/greenkube/storage/postgres/repository.py:709-790`; `src/greenkube/api/routers/report.py:146-183`; `frontend/src/routes/footprint/+page.svelte:172-197`; `src/greenkube/core/metrics_compressor.py:195-207`; `src/greenkube/core/metrics_compressor.py:572-586`; `src/greenkube/storage/base_repository.py:272-282`; `docs/methodology/impact-model.md:131-140`

**Mechanism.** Since Impact Model 1.1.0 a node's energy, operational CO2e, embodied CO2e and modeled cost are split between pod rows (by reservation/usage) and one `__idle__` row per node and step in `node_idle_metrics` (impact/rows.py `_idle_row`). The idle row holds the unreserved idle power, unreserved memory power, unattributed GPU power and the unreserved share of embodied emissions.
