Nothing ever reads that table except `greenkube recompute` (`read_idle_metrics` has a single caller, impact/recompute.py:218): `aggregate_summary`, the timeseries, by-namespace, top-pods and report queries select only `combined_metrics` / `combined_metrics_hourly`; `SummaryRefresher`, the Prometheus gauges (api/metrics_endpoint.py) and the frontend have no notion of idle. The Footprint page nevertheless labels the pod-only sums 'Total CO₂ (Scope 2+3)', 'Total Energy', 'Scope 2 CO₂ (electricity)', 'Scope 3 CO₂ (hardware)', and `/report/summary` + `/report/export` produce the yearly report from the same sums. In addition `MetricsCompressor` has no hourly rollup for idle rows (`TABLES` lists only combined_metrics and container_metrics) and `_delete_idle_before` deletes them with the raw pod rows after METRICS_RAW_RETENTION_DAYS (7 days), so after one week the node totals that docs/methodology/impact-model.md promises ('node totals stay reconstructible as Σ pod rows + idle row') cannot be rebuilt at all. Finally rows written before model 1.1.0 (and every hourly aggregate, never recomputed) put 100% of node power on pods, so any time series or yearly total that spans the upgrade mixes two boundaries and shows a step drop that is not a real reduction; no summary, timeseries or report response exposes the `model_version` mix or an idle/unallocated figure.

**Effect on numbers.** Every headline number of the Footprint page and of the report (total CO2e, energy, embodied, and modeled cost without OpenCost) is the allocated share only. Live, last 24 h: kind API total_energy_joules=47,190 J while node_idle_metrics holds 923,040 J for the same window (API shows 4.9% of the modelled node energy; CO2e 0.443 g shown vs 8.67 g idle; embodied 1.37 g shown vs 28.5 g idle). minikube: 75,763 J shown vs 47,972 J idle (61% shown). On a production cluster whose CPU requests cover 40% of capacity roughly 60% of idle power and of embodied emissions are invisible; a user who reports the 'Total CO₂ (Scope 2+3)' figure under-reports the cluster footprint by that share. Direction: always under. After 7 days the missing share is unrecoverable.

**Affected environments.** All environments and databases. Worst on clusters with low request coverage (dev clusters, kind/minikube, over-provisioned node pools).

**Evidence.** Live: `live.sh api 'api/v1/metrics/summary?last=24h'` -> kind total_energy_joules=47189.9, total_co2e_grams=0.443, total_embodied=1.3745; `select sum(joules::float8), sum(co2e_grams::float8), sum(embodied_co2e_grams::float8) from combined_metrics where timestamp >= now()-interval '24 hours'` -> 47189.36 | 0.44306 | 1.3744 (identical: pods only); `select sum(joules), sum(co2e_grams), sum(embodied_co2e_grams) from node_idle_metrics ...` -> 923040.5 | 8.6663 | 28.5438. minikube: API 75762.6 J vs pods 75763.2 J vs idle 47971.8 J. Code: grep for read_idle_metrics/node_idle_metrics shows no reader outside impact/recompute.py, metrics_compressor.py (delete) and cli/db.py; frontend has no 'idle' reference outside recommendations.

**Fix.** 1) Storage: add `node_idle_metrics_hourly` (node, hour_bucket, sample_count, duration_seconds, joules, co2e_grams, embodied_co2e_grams, total_cost, cost_cpu, cost_ram, avg cpu_unreserved_cores / memory_unreserved_bytes, gpu energy, provenance columns aggregated like `_source_aggregate`) and register it in `MetricsCompressor.TABLES` so idle rows are compressed under the same watermark/transaction as combined_metrics and pruned with METRICS_AGGREGATED_RETENTION_DAYS, never just deleted. 2) Repository: add `aggregate_idle_summary(start, end)` and `aggregate_idle_timeseries(start, end, granularity)` (raw + hourly, same split rule as pod rows) for PostgreSQL and SQLite. 3) API: extend MetricsSummaryResponse, DashboardSummaryResponse, TimeseriesPoint and ReportSummaryResponse with `idle_co2e_grams`, `idle_embodied_co2e_grams`, `idle_energy_joules`, `idle_cost`, and explicit `cluster_total_*` = allocated + idle (cluster-wide scope only; when a namespace filter is set return allocated only and `idle_*: null`). Add `model_versions` (distinct versions and row share) to summary/report responses so a mixed-boundary range is visible. `/report/export` gains one `__idle__` line per node and period (namespace empty, scope=idle). Add gauges `greenkube_node_idle_co2e_grams_total` etc. 4) UI: Footprint page shows 'Allocated to workloads' and 'Unallocated (idle capacity)' with the cluster total = sum; rename the current cards accordingly; show a banner when the range contains rows of a model_version < 1.1.0. 5) Summary refresher: store the idle totals in `metrics_summary`. 6) Backfill: run the new compression over idle rows still in raw retention; older periods have no idle data — return `idle_*: null` with `idle_coverage_start` instead of 0. 7) Docs: state in impact-model.md and power_estimation_methodology.md which API fields are allocated vs cluster totals.

**Acceptance.** a) Fixture: 1 node, 2 cores, 1 pod reserving 0.5 core for 1 h; GET /metrics/summary returns allocated energy = pod joules, idle_energy_joules = idle row joules, cluster_total_energy_joules = node power × 3600 within 1e-6. b) After compression + raw pruning of that hour the same request returns the same three numbers. c) Namespace-filtered summary returns idle_* = null. d) On the kind e2e cluster cluster_total_energy_joules ≈ Σ nodes (idle W + dynamic W + memory W) × covered seconds (±1%). e) /report/export contains one scope=idle row per node/period and its totals equal /report/summary cluster totals. f) A range mixing model_version NULL and 2.0.0 rows returns both versions in `model_versions`.

#### NRG-03 · The hourly carbon-intensity job never fetches DEFAULT_ZONE: clusters whose nodes carry no mappable zone/region label (on-prem, kind, k3s, kubeadm) never get live intensity even with a valid token, and the job returns before the recompute.

**Severity** high · **Kind** bug · **Confidence** confirmed-live · **Effort** S

**Where.** `src/greenkube/cli/start.py:64-91`; `src/greenkube/core/node_zone_mapper.py:104-110`; `src/greenkube/core/historical_range_processor.py:738-739`

**Mechanism.** Rows of a node whose zone and region cannot be mapped are computed for `DEFAULT_ZONE` (NodeZoneMapper.map_nodes -> `mapped = self._config.DEFAULT_ZONE`; run_range: `zone = node_context.emaps_zone if node_context else self._config.DEFAULT_ZONE`). `collect_carbon_intensity_for_all_zones` builds its zone list differently: `unique_zone_region_providers = {(zone, region, provider) for nodes if node_info.zone or node_info.region}` then `get_emaps_zone_from_cloud_zone`; DEFAULT_ZONE is never added. When no node has a label the set is empty and the function logs 'No mappable Electricity Maps zones found based on node discovery.' and returns — before any provider call and before `_recompute_combined_metrics`. So `carbon_intensity_history` stays empty for the very zone every row uses, and all rows are computed with the static zone default (`intensity_source=zone_default`) forever. In a mixed cluster (some labelled nodes, some not) the unlabelled nodes' DEFAULT_ZONE is equally never fetched. The same job also returns early (`No node zones discovered`) when the node list is empty, skipping the recompute of the previous 24 h.

**Effect on numbers.** grid_intensity and co2e_grams of every row on affected clusters use the bundled static value instead of the hourly measured one. For DE the bundled value is 224 g/kWh while the hourly value typically ranges 150–600 (annual mean ≈ 350): CO2e wrong by −60%…+50% hour by hour and ≈ −35% on average; for a zone without a bundled default (NL, ES, CH, AT, SE… see NRG-04) the value is the 500 g/kWh global default although the operator configured both a zone and a token. Carbon-aware features (hour-of-day intensity) are impossible on-prem.

**Affected environments.** On-prem / bare metal / kind / k3s / kubeadm / any cluster without topology.kubernetes.io/zone|region labels, with Electricity Maps or Wattnet credentials configured. Clouds with standard labels are not affected.

**Evidence.** Live kind (DEFAULT_ZONE=FR, nodes zone=None): `live.sh logs controller --since=12h` -> 3× '--- Starting hourly carbon intensity collection task ---' each followed by 'No mappable Electricity Maps zones found based on node discovery.' and no 'Recomputed … combined metrics'; zone mapper logs 'Could not map cloud zone 'None' or region 'None' … Using default: 'FR''; `select zone, count(*) from carbon_intensity_history group by 1` -> no rows; all 12,994 rows emaps_zone=FR, intensity_source=zone_default. Simulation audit-workspace/audit/work/energy-carbon/sim_zone.py (real collect_carbon_intensity_for_all_zones, DEFAULT_ZONE=FR, a token, 3 nodes without labels, fake provider): 'zone used for the rows of every node: ['FR']', 'zones fetched from the electricity provider: [] | save_history calls: 0 | recompute calls: 0'.

**Fix.** In `collect_carbon_intensity_for_all_zones` derive the zones from the same resolver the rows use: `contexts = await NodeZoneMapper(node_collector, cfg).map_nodes(nodes_info)`; `emaps_zones = {c.emaps_zone for c in contexts.values()}`; additionally add `cfg.DEFAULT_ZONE` when it is not 'unknown' (rows of unresolved nodes and of nodes that left the cluster use it), and add the distinct `emaps_zone` values of combined_metrics rows of the last 24 h (so a zone whose nodes just left is still consolidated). Drop 'unknown'. Never return before `_recompute_combined_metrics`: run it in a `finally`-like step whenever at least one zone was refreshed or history exists. When the node list is empty, still fetch DEFAULT_ZONE and the recent row zones. Expose `greenkube_intensity_zones{zone, status}` (fetched / skipped_no_token / failed) and include the list in the health/config API so 'no live intensity' is visible. No data migration; after the fix the 24 h recompute corrects raw rows of the last day (older rows keep zone_default and stay labelled as such).

**Acceptance.** a) sim_zone.py scenario: provider.collect is called exactly once with 'FR', save_history once, recompute once. b) Mixed cluster (node A zone eu-west-3a, node B no label, DEFAULT_ZONE=DE): zones fetched == {'FR','DE'}. c) DEFAULT_ZONE='unknown' and no labels: no provider call, one warning, recompute still runs. d) On the live kind cluster with a token, carbon_intensity_history gets FR rows within one hour and rows switch to intensity_source realtime/consolidated.

#### NRG-04 · The bundled zone-default intensity table is a one-day snapshot with 251 of 349 zones empty and malformed rows (ES, SE, NO, DK, AU): 19 of the 51 zones cloud regions map to fall back to 500 gCO2e/kWh.

**Severity** high · **Kind** data-integrity · **Confidence** confirmed-code · **Effort** M

**Where.** `src/greenkube/data/electricity_maps_default.csv:87`; `src/greenkube/data/electricity_maps_default.csv:146`; `src/greenkube/data/electricity_maps_default.csv:210-211`; `src/greenkube/data/electricity_maps_default.csv:249`; `src/greenkube/data/electricity_maps_regions_grid_intensity_default.py:8-12`; `src/greenkube/data/electricity_maps_regions_grid_intensity_default.py:24-32`; `src/greenkube/core/calculator.py:164-192`; `src/greenkube/data/cloud_region_electricity_maps_mapping.csv:18-23`

**Mechanism.** `DEFAULT_GRID_INTENSITY_BY_ZONE` is what every install without an Electricity Maps/Wattnet credential uses (the default install), and what any install uses when the provider is down for more than INTENSITY_MAX_AGE. The CSV has 349 zone rows but only 98 carry a 'Default Grid Intensity'. Five rows are column-shifted — e.g. `ES;Spain;Spain;89;`, `SE;Sweden;Sweden;22;`, `NO;Norway;Norway;35;` (also DK, AU): the number sits in the 'Tier' column, so `_load_defaults` (which reads `row.get('Default Grid Intensity')`) finds nothing. `IT;Italy;Italy;` has neither tier nor value; NL, CH, AT, CZ, CN, ZA, NZ, AU-NSW, DK-DK1, US-CENT-SWPP, US-SW-AZPS… have a tier but no value. Cross-checking the region mapping: of the 51 distinct zones that cloud_region_electricity_maps_mapping.csv can produce, 19 have no default: AE, AT, AU-NSW, AU-SA, BH, CH, CN, ES, ID, IL, IT, MY-WM, NL, NZ, QA, SE, US-CENT-SWPP, US-SW-AZPS, ZA. These include very common regions: Azure westeurope and GCP europe-west4 (NL), AWS eu-north-1 (SE), AWS eu-south-1/-2, GCP europe-west8/europe-southwest1, Azure italynorth/spaincentral (IT/ES), GCP europe-west6 and Azure switzerlandnorth (CH), Scaleway AMS (NL). For them `_apply_defaults` falls through to `DEFAULT_INTENSITY` = 500 (`intensity_source=global_default`). Separately, the module docstring states the values are 'the values registered the 23/11/2025': a single-day reading, not an annual average (GB 67, DE 224, IE 178, BE 82 look like a windy autumn day; public annual averages are roughly GB ≈ 200, DE ≈ 350, IE ≈ 290), yet it is applied to every hour of every year. The table has no vintage/unit/source column and no test checks its completeness against the region mapping.

**Effect on numbers.** grid_intensity / co2e_grams of all rows without a live intensity. Missing default -> 500 g/kWh: Sweden (real ≈ 20–40) over-stated ≈ 12–25×, Switzerland (≈ 40–80) ≈ 6–12×, Spain (≈ 150) ≈ 3.3×, Italy (≈ 300) ≈ 1.7×, Netherlands (≈ 270) ≈ 1.9×, Australia NSW / South Africa (≈ 650–700) under-stated ≈ 25–30%. Snapshot bias where a default exists: GB ≈ −65%, DE ≈ −35%, IE ≈ −40% versus annual means (approximate public figures; direction and order of magnitude are the point). Worked example: a 100-node Azure westeurope cluster drawing 20 kW IT, PUE 1.18, no token: reported 20 kW × 8760 h × 1.18 × 500 g = 103 t CO2e/yr instead of ≈ 56 t with the NL annual mean.

**Affected environments.** Every install without a token (default), and any install during provider outages; clouds in the 19 zones above get 500 g/kWh; on-prem with DEFAULT_ZONE set to one of the 251 empty zones likewise.

**Evidence.** Parsed src/greenkube/data/electricity_maps_default.csv: 349 rows, 98 with a default; field counts {5: 243, 6: 99, 4: 8}; rows with a number in the Tier column and no default: ['AU','DK','ES','NO','SE']; mapped cloud zones 51, without default: the 19 listed. Raw lines 87 `ES;Spain;Spain;89;`, 146 `IT;Italy;Italy;`, 210 `NL;Netherlands;Netherlands;A;`, 211 `NO;Norway;Norway;35;`, 249 `SE;Sweden;Sweden;22;`, 74 `DE;Germany;Germany;A;224;`. Region mapping lines: 'Google Cloud Platform,europe-west4,NL', 'Amazon Web Services,eu-north-1,SE', 'Microsoft Azure,westeurope,NL', 'Microsoft Azure,switzerlandnorth,CH'. Code path calculator._apply_defaults read end to end; live clusters show the mechanism for FR (26, zone_default).

**Fix.** 1) Regenerate the table from a documented source of ANNUAL averages (Electricity Maps yearly CSV exports or Ember yearly data), last complete calendar year, lifecycle factors, with columns `zone;country;name;intensity_g_per_kwh;year;source;emission_factor_type`; ship it as a proper comma CSV parsed with csv.DictReader and validated at load (pydantic row model; fail the build on a malformed row). 2) Fallback chain in `_apply_defaults`: zone value -> parent zone (e.g. 'US-CAL-CISO' -> 'US', 'IT-NO' -> 'IT', 'SE-SE3' -> 'SE') or, the other way round, the demand-weighted mean of sub-zones for a country code -> global default; record the fallback level in intensity_source (`zone_default`, `country_default`, `global_default`) and in estimation reasons. 3) Add a CI test: every zone produced by cloud_region_electricity_maps_mapping.csv and every Wattnet zone has a default; every value is within 5–1200; no row has a numeric tier. 4) Expose the table vintage (`intensity_defaults_year`) in the config/health API and show a UI badge when a range is computed with defaults. 5) Stored rows: rows with intensity_source in (zone_default, global_default) within raw retention can be re-priced by a `greenkube recompute --intensity-defaults` pass (co2e = joules/3.6e6 × pue × new default, also for node_idle_metrics); hourly aggregates need a one-off migration that rescales co2e_grams by new/old default when intensity_source is a default and emaps_zone is known, or must be flagged legacy.

**Acceptance.** a) Loader returns a value for all 51 mapped zones and for ES, SE, NO, DK, IT, NL, CH, AT. b) calculate_emissions(zone='NL', no history) returns the NL annual mean with intensity_source=zone_default, not 500/global_default. c) zone 'US-XYZ-UNKNOWN' falls back to the US country value with intensity_source=country_default. d) CI test fails when a mapped zone loses its default or a row is column-shifted. e) GB default is within ±15% of the published annual mean of the stated year.

#### NRG-05 · Projections use a different node power model from collection: for every node without a bundled instance profile the projector assumes 1 W idle / 10 W max per core (DEFAULT_INSTANCE_*), while stored rows use the CCF per-vCPU coefficients (0.6–0.8 / 2.8–4.3 W).

**Severity** high · **Kind** bug · **Confidence** confirmed-live · **Effort** S

**Where.** `src/greenkube/impact/projector.py:100-132`; `src/greenkube/energy/node_power.py:62-80`; `src/greenkube/energy/node_power.py:93-130`; `src/greenkube/impact/recompute.py:147-151`; `docs/methodology/impact-model.md:61-67`

**Mechanism.** Collection resolves node power with `resolve_node_power_profile` (instance profile -> capacity × provider CCF coefficients, cross-provider average 0.726/3.564 W per vCPU when the provider is unknown -> default). The projector has its own copy, `_power_profile(config, instance_type, capacity_cores)` ('Mirrors BasicEstimator profile selection'): instance profile, else `per_core_min = DEFAULT_INSTANCE_MIN_WATTS / DEFAULT_INSTANCE_VCORES` (1.0 W) and `per_core_max = DEFAULT_INSTANCE_MAX_WATTS / DEFAULT_INSTANCE_VCORES` (10.0 W) times capacity, labelled `node_capacity_derived` exactly like the collection profile. It ignores the node's provider. The two models only agree for the 740 bundled instance types (277 AWS, 307 GCP, 100 Azure, 44 OVH, 12 Scaleway — e.g. c7i.*, Standard_D4ds_v5, DEV1-M are absent). So the projected CO2e of a rightsizing (idle share released = Δcores × P_min/A) and of deletions/schedules (dynamic share (P_max−P_min)/cores) is computed with other watts than the rows that change measurement later compares it with, breaking the roadmap principle 'same model for estimate and measurement'. docs/methodology/impact-model.md documents the projector variant, docs/power_estimation_methodology.md the collection variant.

**Effect on numbers.** `co2e_breakdown_annual_g.idle_operational`, `co2e_measurable_expected_annual_g`, `value_annual` (carbon-priced) of recommendations and PR bodies. Idle share over-stated by 1.0/0.726 = +38% on on-prem/unknown provider, +35% AWS (0.74), +41% GCP (0.71), +28% Azure (0.78), +25% OVH (0.80), +67% Scaleway (0.60) for unlisted instance types; the dynamic share of deletion/schedule projections by (10−1)/(3.564−0.726) = 3.2×. Live example (minikube, recommendation 95): capacity released 78.74 core-h/yr, projected idle_operational 2.6614 g = 78.74 × 1.0 W/1000 × 1.3 × 26; with the 0.726 W/core used by the stored rows it is 1.932 g. A perfectly realized change therefore measures ≈ 27% less operational CO2e than projected.

**Affected environments.** All on-prem clusters (minikube, kind, kubeadm, k3s, bare metal) and every cloud node whose instance type is not among the 740 bundled profiles (new AWS/GCP/Azure families, most OVH/Scaleway types).

**Evidence.** Live minikube: `select impact from recommendation_history where id=95` -> capacity_released.cpu_core_hours=78.7397, co2e_breakdown_annual_g.idle_operational=2.66140 (= 78.7397×1.0/1000×1.3×26 exactly), while the hand recompute of stored rows for the same node (recompute_live.py) reproduces idle power = 0.726 W × 6 cores = 4.36 W to 1e-7. Code: projector.py:114-126 vs node_power.py:62-80.

**Fix.** Delete `_power_profile` from impact/projector.py and call `greenkube.energy.node_power.resolve_node_power_profile(node_name, instance_type, cpu_capacity_cores, provider, INSTANCE_PROFILES, default_profile)` in `_node_profile` (pass the node's cloud_provider; for nodes known only from rows pass provider=None as today). Map `NodePowerProfile.source` to PowerSource and `is_fallback`. Do the same wherever a default profile dict is rebuilt (impact/recompute.py already uses the shared resolver). Add a parity test that builds, for a table of (instance_type, provider, capacity) cases (known type, unknown type per provider, unknown provider, cpu-N, nothing), the collection NodeSpec and the projector NodeImpactProfile and asserts identical idle_watts/max_watts/power_source. Bump IMPACT_MODEL_VERSION (projection numbers change) and let the next scan re-project active recommendations; note in CHANGELOG that projected CO2e of open recommendations drops by up to 40%. Fix docs/methodology/impact-model.md 'Power profile' to describe the CCF coefficients.

**Acceptance.** a) Parity test passes for all cases. b) For a 6-core node with provider unknown and a 0.1-core request cut over a full year, projected idle_operational == 0.1 × 8760 × 0.726/1000 × PUE × intensity (±1e-9). c) On the minikube e2e cluster the projected idle_operational of recommendation 95 becomes ≈ 1.93 g for the same capacity released. d) grep finds a single implementation of profile selection.

#### NRG-06 · Energy, CO2e and cost are stored as float4 (REAL) in PostgreSQL and summed with SUM(real): totals drift by percents beyond ~1M rows and stop growing beyond ~17M rows.

**Severity** high · **Kind** data-integrity · **Confidence** confirmed-live · **Effort** M

**Where.** `src/greenkube/core/migrations/scripts/postgres/0001_baseline_migrations.sql:13`; `src/greenkube/core/migrations/scripts/postgres/0002_hourly_aggregation_and_node_scd2.sql:20`; `src/greenkube/core/migrations/scripts/postgres/0033_container_metrics.sql:12`; `src/greenkube/storage/postgres/repository.py:779-786`; `src/greenkube/storage/postgres/repository.py:986-989`; `src/greenkube/storage/postgres/repository.py:1103-1106`; `src/greenkube/storage/postgres/repository.py:1180-1183`

**Mechanism.** combined_metrics, combined_metrics_hourly, container_metrics(_hourly) declare joules, co2e_grams, embodied_co2e_grams, total_cost, pue, grid_intensity as REAL (4-byte float). PostgreSQL's `sum(real)` accumulates in float4 and returns real, and the repository sums these columns without a cast (aggregate_summary, timeseries, by-namespace, top-pods, report). A float4 accumulator has 24 bits: once the running sum is ≥ 2^21 times a typical addend each addition is rounded to a coarse grid (systematic drift), and once it is ≥ 2^24 times the addend the addend is lost entirely, so the total saturates. The result also depends on the plan (parallel partial aggregates sum fewer rows each), so the same request can return different totals. node_idle_metrics, metrics_summary and workload_usage_hourly use double precision; SQLite REAL is 8 bytes and is not affected.

**Effect on numbers.** All PostgreSQL totals computed over many rows: total_co2e_grams, total_embodied_co2e_grams, total_energy_joules, total_cost in /metrics/summary, /metrics/timeseries, /metrics/by-namespace, /metrics/top-pods, /report/summary, dashboard summary. Measured on the live PostgreSQL with identical addends of 1e-4: 0.5M rows −0.53%, 2M rows +1.97%, 5M rows −2.79%, 10M rows +8.77%, 30M rows -> 2048 instead of 3000 (−32%, saturated). Row counts: a yearly report reads pods × 8760 hourly rows — 230 pods ≈ 2M rows (±2%), 570 pods ≈ 5M (±3%), 2000 pods ≈ 17.5M rows (total stops growing: the last months add nothing). Small clusters (< 100 pods) stay below 0.5%.

**Affected environments.** PostgreSQL (the production database). Grows with cluster size and range length; SQLite unaffected.

**Evidence.** Live read-only queries on the kind PostgreSQL: `select pg_typeof(sum(joules)) from combined_metrics` -> real; information_schema: combined_metrics(_hourly).joules/co2e_grams/embodied_co2e_grams/total_cost = real; `select n, sum(v::real), n*0.0001 from (values (500000),(2000000),(5000000),(10000000)) t(n), lateral generate_series(1,n) ...` -> 49.736 / 203.933 / 486.030 / 1087.724 (true 50 / 200 / 500 / 1000); `select sum(v::real), sum(v::float8) from (select 0.0001 v from generate_series(1,30000000))` -> 2048 | 3000.0000015. Already visible at 20k rows: API total_energy_joules 75762.63 vs float8 sum 75763.24.

**Fix.** 1) Migration (PostgreSQL): `ALTER TABLE combined_metrics ALTER COLUMN joules TYPE double precision, ALTER COLUMN co2e_grams TYPE double precision, ALTER COLUMN embodied_co2e_grams TYPE double precision, ALTER COLUMN total_cost TYPE double precision, ALTER COLUMN pue TYPE double precision, ALTER COLUMN grid_intensity TYPE double precision` and the same for combined_metrics_hourly, container_metrics, container_metrics_hourly (table rewrite: run in the controller migration with a lock timeout, batch per table; document the duration for large tables). Existing values keep their 7 significant digits (acceptable per row). 2) Until/independently of the migration, cast in every aggregate: `SUM(joules::double precision)` etc. in storage/postgres/repository.py (all SUM/AVG over these columns, including metrics_compressor SQL: `SUM(co2e_grams::double precision)`). 3) Add a repository test on real PostgreSQL that inserts 3M rows of 1e-4 (COPY) and asserts the summary equals 300 ± 1e-6 relative; add a schema test that no numeric measure column of the metrics tables is `real`. 4) Hourly rows already compressed were summed in float4 per (pod, hour) over ≤ 12 raw rows — error negligible, no backfill needed; cached summaries are recomputed by the next SummaryRefresher run.

**Acceptance.** a) After migration `select data_type from information_schema.columns where table_name like '%metrics%' and column_name in ('joules','co2e_grams','embodied_co2e_grams','total_cost')` returns only 'double precision'. b) 30M-row synthetic sum test returns 3000 ± 0.001. c) /metrics/summary on the live kind DB equals the float8 SQL sum to 1e-9 relative. d) Repeating /report/summary 5 times returns identical totals.

#### NRG-07 · Two writers flip node_snapshots_scd.embodied_emissions_kg between NULL and the value every cycle: the SCD table grows by ~2 versions per node per cycle, and `greenkube recompute` zeroes embodied emissions when the latest version is the NULL one.

**Severity** high · **Kind** data-integrity · **Confidence** confirmed-live · **Effort** M

**Where.** `src/greenkube/core/embodied_service.py:119-132`; `src/greenkube/cli/start.py:163-175`; `src/greenkube/storage/postgres/node_repository.py:20-33`; `src/greenkube/storage/sqlite/node_repository.py:26-37`; `src/greenkube/impact/recompute.py:163-169`; `src/greenkube/impact/recompute.py:181-185`; `src/greenkube/core/historical_range_processor.py:485-501`

**Mechanism.** `save_nodes` creates a new SCD-2 version whenever one of `_SCD_COMPARE_COLS` changes, and `embodied_emissions_kg` is one of them. Two jobs save the same nodes with different values: `analyze_nodes()` (node-analysis loop) saves fresh `NodeCollector.collect()` objects whose `embodied_emissions_kg` is None, and `EmbodiedEmissionsService.prepare_embodied_data()` — called by every `run_range`, i.e. every collection cycle — sets `info.embodied_emissions_kg = profile['gwp_manufacture']` and calls `node_repository.save_nodes` again. Each alternation closes the current version and opens a new one, for nodes that did not change at all. Consequences: (1) unbounded growth of node_snapshots_scd and of the per-run node timeline (`get_snapshots(start, end)` + `get_latest_snapshots_before`), slowing every collection and every recommendation context build over months; (2) `MetricRecomputer._nodes_at(end)` takes the latest snapshot before the chunk end and `_spec` computes `embodied_g_per_hour` from `info.embodied_emissions_kg`; when that version is a NULL one the whole chunk is re-allocated with embodied = 0 for pods, containers and idle rows; (3) the node history is useless as an audit trail (hundreds of identical versions); (4) once the flapping is fixed, `get_node_info_at` will hit its 'snapshot too old' branch (age measured from the version start, NODE_DATA_MAX_AGE_DAYS=30) for every stable node at every step.

**Effect on numbers.** Row growth: live, 305 versions for one unchanged minikube node and 314 per kind node after ~3 h of effective running (accelerated 30 s cycle). With production defaults (5-minute step and 5-minute node analysis) up to 2 versions per node per 5 minutes = 576/day/node: 100 nodes -> 57,600 rows/day, 21 M rows/year. `greenkube recompute`: embodied_co2e_grams of the recomputed range becomes 0 for every chunk whose latest snapshot is NULL (live sample: 11 of 13 ten-minute boundaries), i.e. 'Scope 3' totals of recomputed periods drop by ~85–100% and projections/measurements that read them follow.

**Affected environments.** All environments and both databases; visible within minutes of install.

**Evidence.** Live: `select node_name, count(*) from node_snapshots_scd group by 1` -> minikube 305; kind 314 per node (942 rows, 3 current); consecutive versions differ only by embodied_emissions_kg NULL/100 with valid_from 20–40 s apart; `select h, (select embodied_emissions_kg from node_snapshots_scd s where s.valid_from <= h order by s.valid_from desc limit 1) from generate_series('2026-11-14 21:50+00','2026-11-14 23:50+00', interval '10 minutes') h` -> NULL ×11, 100 ×2. Simulation audit-workspace/audit/work/energy-carbon/sim_recompute.py (real SQLiteNodeRepository, real MetricRecomputer, production interleaving of the two writers): 'node_snapshots_scd versions for one unchanged node over 2 h: 48'; before recompute embodied pods 0.7135 g/h + idle 2.1404 g/h; after `MetricRecomputer.run(only_outdated=False)`: pods 0.0, idle 0.0 (joules unchanged).

**Fix.** 1) Remove embodied emissions from node identity: drop `embodied_emissions_kg` from `_SCD_COMPARE_COLS` (both backends) and stop calling `node_repository.save_nodes` from `prepare_embodied_data` (delete embodied_service.py:119-132; node snapshots are owned by the node-analysis loop and by nothing else). 2) Keep embodied data in `instance_carbon_profiles` keyed by (provider, instance_type) [+ node override], with source and fetched_at (see NRG-01), and make every consumer read it from there: `MetricRecomputer._spec`, `ImpactProjector._build_pool`, the nodes API. `MetricRecomputer` must use the embodied repository (or the profile recorded at collection time) rather than the snapshot column, and must refuse to lower a stored non-zero embodied value to 0 because a lookup returned nothing (keep the row's value and count it in the report). 3) Migration: collapse consecutive SCD versions that are identical on all remaining compare columns (keep the earliest valid_from and the latest valid_to), in batches; add a unique partial index `(node_name) WHERE is_current`. 4) `get_node_info_at`: measure staleness against the version's `valid_to`/`is_current` (a current version is never 'too old'); add `get_latest_snapshots_before(..., include_inactive=True)` for recompute so removed nodes keep their capacity. 5) Add gauge `greenkube_node_snapshot_versions_total` and a test that runs analyze_nodes + run_range alternately 50 times and asserts one version per unchanged node.

**Acceptance.** a) 50 alternating analyze_nodes()/run_range() cycles on an unchanged cluster leave exactly 1 row per node in node_snapshots_scd. b) sim_recompute.py scenario: embodied totals identical before and after `greenkube recompute --all`. c) Migration on the live minikube DB reduces 305 versions to 1 (or to the number of real changes). d) A node unchanged for 45 days still resolves its snapshot (no 'too old' warning) while it is current.

#### NRG-08 · Lookup and Kubernetes API errors are swallowed inside run_range: an intensity lookup error drops every pod of the node-step, a failed node/pod list writes rows with zero requests, default zone, default PUE and no embodied — and the watermark advances, so the window is never repaired.

**Severity** medium · **Kind** reliability · **Confidence** confirmed-sim · **Effort** M

**Where.** `src/greenkube/core/historical_range_processor.py:743-751`; `src/greenkube/core/historical_range_processor.py:599`; `src/greenkube/core/historical_range_processor.py:503-506`; `src/greenkube/core/historical_range_processor.py:509-531`; `src/greenkube/core/historical_range_processor.py:536-539`; `src/greenkube/core/historical_range_processor.py:566-570`; `src/greenkube/core/collection.py:305-334`

**Mechanism.** Prometheus failures raise RangeCollectionError and fail the window (correct). Everything else degrades silently: (a) `carbon_result = await calculator.calculate_emissions(...)` is wrapped in `except Exception: carbon_result = None`, then `skipped_carbon += len(pod_keys); continue` — the pod rows AND the idle row of that node and step are not produced. `calculate_emissions` no longer returns None by itself, but `_lookup_intensity` -> `repository.get_intensity_point` raises QueryError on any DB error (pool timeout, statement timeout, connection reset). `skipped_carbon` is never logged, exported or returned. (b) `node_collector.collect()` / `collect_instance_types()` failures become `nodes_info = {}` / `current_node_map = {}`: every node then has no zone context (-> DEFAULT_ZONE, 'unknown' by default -> 500 g/kWh), no provider (-> default PUE 1.3), no embodied profile (-> 0). (c) `pod_collector.collect()` failure becomes empty request maps: every pod row gets cpu_request=0 / memory_request=0, so the reservation-aware allocation moves the pods' idle and embodied share to `__idle__`. (d) `prepare_embodied_data` failure -> embodied 0 for the window. In all cases `MetricsCollectionJob._collect_window` writes what it got and advances the watermark; rows are upserted once and never revisited (the 24 h intensity recompute only touches rows whose zone has a fresh point, and never requests/PUE/embodied).

**Effect on numbers.** (a) Missing steps: energy/CO2e/cost of all pods of the node for the affected hour(s) are absent from every total (under-count), with no gap marker. Simulation: one failing lookup hour -> 12 of 24 steps missing, report.error=None, watermark moved past them. (b)+(c) Simulation of one API-server blip on an AWS eu-west-3 node: the 09:05 row has cpu_request 0 (real 1000m), joules 210.0 instead of 387.6 (−46%), co2e 0.03792 g instead of 0.00322 g (×11.8), embodied 0 instead of 0.1189 g, PUE 1.3 instead of 1.15, grid_intensity 500 global_default instead of 26 — while the rows before and after are correct. In a backfill the whole chunk (up to one hour of steps for every pod) is affected.

**Affected environments.** All. Likelihood grows with API-server throttling/rolling upgrades, DB pool pressure (DB_POOL_MAX_SIZE=10, statement timeout 30 s) and long backfills.

**Evidence.** audit-workspace/audit/work/energy-carbon/sim_range.py a (real MetricsCollectionJob + HistoricalRangeProcessor + SQLite, intensity repository raising for hour 10): 'report: windows planned/collected = 2 2 error = None watermark = 2026-11-07 11:00:00+00:00', 'pod rows per hour: [(2026-11-07T09, 12, 24)]', idle rows only for hour 09. audit-workspace/audit/work/energy-carbon/sim_k8s_blip.py (node+pod list raise during one cycle): errors None/None/None, watermark 09:15; pod row 09:05 = (request 0, 200m, 210.0 J, 0.03792 g, embodied 0.0, pue 1.3, 500.0, global_default, zone unknown) vs 09:00/09:10 = (1000, 200m, 387.6 J, 0.00322 g, 0.1189, 1.15, 26.0, zone_default, FR).

**Fix.** Treat every input that changes stored numbers as required for the window: 1) Intensity: resolve intensities once per (zone, normalized hour) before the allocation loop (replace the per-(step,node) `get_for_zone_at_time` prefetch, which issues steps × nodes sequential queries, by one range query per zone); on a lookup exception raise RangeCollectionError (window retried next cycle) — never `continue`. Remove `skipped_carbon`. 2) Node inventory: if `node_collector.collect()` raises, fall back to the node snapshots valid at the window (zone/provider/capacity from `get_node_info_at`), and if no snapshot covers a node that has samples raise RangeCollectionError; an empty node list with non-empty samples is an error, not a degraded mode. 3) Pod requests: if `pod_collector.collect()` raises, read requests from kube-state-metrics for the window (see NRG-11) or raise RangeCollectionError. 4) Embodied: on `prepare_embodied_data` failure use the persisted profiles; only when none exists mark rows `embodied_source=none`. 5) Bound retries: after N consecutive failures of the same window (config COLLECTION_MAX_WINDOW_RETRIES, default 12) write the window in degraded mode with explicit provenance (`usage_source`, `intensity_source`, new `requests_source=unknown`) and record it in a `collection_gaps`/quality table surfaced by the health API and a gauge `greenkube_collection_degraded_windows_total{reason}`. 6) Repair: add `greenkube recollect --since/--until` that re-runs run_range for degraded windows still inside Prometheus retention (idempotent upsert).

**Acceptance.** a) sim_range.py a: the job reports an error, the watermark stays at 10:00, and the next healthy run writes 12 steps for hour 10. b) sim_k8s_blip.py: the 09:05 window is not written during the blip and is collected correctly by the next cycle (requests 1000, zone FR, PUE 1.15). c) After COLLECTION_MAX_WINDOW_RETRIES failures the window is written with requests_source=unknown and counted in greenkube_collection_degraded_windows_total. d) grep shows no bare `except Exception: ... = {}` on the node, pod or intensity inputs of run_range.

#### NRG-09 · The intensity consolidation recompute updates pod and container rows but not node_idle_metrics, so idle rows keep the provisional or default intensity and node totals stop conserving.

**Severity** medium · **Kind** bug · **Confidence** confirmed-sim · **Effort** S

**Where.** `src/greenkube/storage/postgres/repository.py:1567-1660`; `src/greenkube/storage/sqlite/repository.py:1269-1300`; `src/greenkube/storage/base_repository.py:609-700`; `src/greenkube/cli/start.py:120-150`; `src/greenkube/impact/rows.py:347-367`

**Mechanism.** `recompute_carbon_with_latest_intensities` loops `for table in ('combined_metrics', 'container_metrics')` (PostgreSQL and SQLite; the Python base implementation does the same). `node_idle_metrics` has the same `grid_intensity`, `co2e_grams`, `intensity_source`, `pue`, `emaps_zone` columns (written by `_idle_row`) and is skipped. This is the BUG-06 pattern again on the table added later by B2. In normal operation with a provider the intensity of the current hour is not published yet when a step is collected, so rows are first computed with the previous hour's value (or with the zone default right after a restart/backfill, when collection runs before the first intensity fetch) and corrected by the hourly recompute — pods only. Idle rows therefore systematically carry the lagged/provisional/default intensity.

**Effect on numbers.** co2e_grams / grid_intensity / intensity_source of every idle row (typically the larger share of node energy, see NRG-02). Simulation: provisional 60 g/kWh consolidated to 30: pod rows 0.06362 -> 0.03181 g (consolidated), idle rows stay 0.29297 g at 60 (realtime); the node's implied intensity becomes 54.65 g/kWh instead of 30 (+82%). After a restart with a token, up to 24 h of backfilled idle rows keep the static zone default. Today the error is latent (idle rows are not displayed); it becomes a displayed error as soon as NRG-02 is fixed, and it already breaks the documented conservation 'pod rows plus the idle row equal the node totals for operational CO2e'.

**Affected environments.** All installs with an Electricity Maps token or Wattnet credentials (both databases).

**Evidence.** audit-workspace/audit/work/energy-carbon/sim_range.py b (real run_range, SQLite repositories, save_history 60 estimated then 30 final, real recompute): 'recompute_carbon_with_latest_intensities updated rows: 24' (12 pod + 12 container); 'after consolidation: pod rows … intensity=30.0-30.0 (consolidated) | idle rows … intensity=60.0-60.0 (realtime)'; 'implied intensity = 54.65 g/kWh'. Code: the table tuple at postgres/repository.py (for table in ('combined_metrics', 'container_metrics')) and sqlite/repository.py:1294.

**Fix.** Add `node_idle_metrics` to the recompute in all three implementations, in the same transaction: same lateral join on (emaps_zone, normalized timestamp), same max-age rule, `co2e_grams = joules / 3.6e6 × COALESCE(pue, DEFAULT_PUE) × intensity`, `grid_intensity`, `intensity_source` (the table has no estimation_reasons/namespace: skip those parts; when a namespace filter is passed, still refresh idle rows of the nodes hosting that namespace or document that idle rows are only refreshed by unfiltered runs). Return the idle count separately in the log line. Add a parametrised test over ALL tables that have a grid_intensity column (information_schema / PRAGMA) so a future table cannot be forgotten. One-off repair: run the recompute over the raw-retention window (7 days) with the stored history at upgrade; older idle rows no longer exist.

**Acceptance.** a) sim_range.py b: after the recompute idle rows show intensity 30 / consolidated and (Σ pods + idle co2e) == node kWh × PUE × 30 within 1e-9. b) Test on PostgreSQL and SQLite: a pod row, its container row and the idle row of the same step always end with the same grid_intensity and intensity_source. c) The schema-driven test fails if a new table with grid_intensity is not handled.

#### NRG-10 · Zone and PUE are taken from the live node list only: pods of a node that left the cluster before its window is collected (autoscaler scale-down, spot reclaim, backfill) are computed with DEFAULT_ZONE / the 500 g global default and the default PUE although the node snapshot knows zone and provider.

**Severity** medium · **Kind** bug · **Confidence** confirmed-sim · **Effort** M

**Where.** `src/greenkube/core/historical_range_processor.py:561-563`; `src/greenkube/core/historical_range_processor.py:738-742`; `src/greenkube/core/historical_range_processor.py:811-813`; `src/greenkube/core/historical_range_processor.py:844-853`

**Mechanism.** run_range resolves the power profile historically (`profile_for_node` -> `get_node_info_at(node, ts)` from node_snapshots_scd), but zone, provider and PUE come from the node list read at run time: `node_contexts = await self.zone_mapper.map_nodes(nodes_info)`, `zone = node_context.emaps_zone if node_context else self._config.DEFAULT_ZONE`, `_ni = nodes_info.get(node_name)`, `provider = _ni.cloud_provider if _ni else None`, `pue = self._config.get_pue_for_provider(provider)`; `node_zone` / `node_instance_type` on the row likewise. A window is collected at least one safety lag after it closes, and up to 24 h later during catch-up, so a node present during the window may be gone. Its pods and its idle row then get zone DEFAULT_ZONE ('unknown' unless configured -> `global_default` 500 g/kWh), PUE = raw DEFAULT_PUE (1.3) and node_zone NULL. The embodied profile cache is built from live nodes only, so a removed node also gets embodied 0 whenever no live node shares its (provider, instance type).

**Effect on numbers.** co2e_grams, grid_intensity, pue, emaps_zone, embodied of the last step(s) of every removed node, and of whole backfilled windows. Simulation (AWS eu-west-3, DEFAULT_ZONE unset): pod on the removed node: zone 'unknown', 500 g/kWh, PUE 1.3, co2e 0.04994 g; identical pod on the surviving node: zone FR, 26 g/kWh, PUE 1.15, co2e 0.00230 g — 21.7× more CO2e for the same joules. With Karpenter/spot churn where nodes live ~2 h, 1 step in 24 (≈ 4%) of node-steps is affected: in a low-carbon zone that alone inflates the cluster CO2e by ≈ +80% (0.04 × 21.7); in a ≈ 500 g zone the bias is only the PUE (+13% on those steps). After a controller outage the whole backfill is affected for every node replaced meanwhile.

**Affected environments.** Clusters with node churn (cluster-autoscaler, Karpenter, spot/preemptible pools, node upgrades), all clouds; strongest where DEFAULT_ZONE is unset and the real zone is far from 500 g/kWh.

**Evidence.** audit-workspace/audit/work/energy-carbon/sim_range.py c (real run_range; node 'spot-gone' present in node snapshots with zone eu-west-3a / provider aws but absent from the live node list): 'pod a node=n1 emaps_zone=FR intensity=26.0 (zone_default) pue=1.15 … co2e=0.00230 g' vs 'pod b node=spot-gone emaps_zone=unknown intensity=500.0 (global_default) pue=1.3 power_source=instance_profile joules=276.6 co2e=0.04994 g'.

**Fix.** Resolve all node attributes per (node, step) from one function `node_at(node_name, ts)` = snapshot valid at ts (including inactive versions) or the live NodeInfo: build `node_contexts` with `zone_mapper.map_nodes({name: info})` for the union of live nodes and snapshot nodes seen in the chunk (cache by (zone, region, provider)); use that info for provider/PUE, `node_zone`, `node_instance_type`, the NodeSpec and the embodied profile key (`prepare_embodied_data` must receive the union too). Only when neither a snapshot nor a live node exists use DEFAULT_ZONE/default PUE and say so in estimation reasons (`Node 'x' unknown at <ts>`). Ensure node-analysis saves a snapshot at least once per collection step (or have run_range persist the live list first) so every node that produced samples has a snapshot. Stored rows: provide `greenkube recompute --zones` for raw rows whose emaps_zone differs from the snapshot zone at their timestamp (re-price co2e with stored joules); hourly rows cannot be repaired.

**Acceptance.** a) sim_range.py c: both pods get zone FR, intensity 26, PUE 1.15 and a non-zero embodied share. b) Backfill test: 3 nodes replaced during a simulated 2 h outage -> all backfilled rows carry the zone/PUE of the snapshot valid at their timestamp. c) A node with neither snapshot nor live info yields rows flagged with the explicit reason and counted in a gauge.

#### NRG-11 · Reservation shares use the requests of pods alive at collection time: rows of terminated pods (last steps after a rollout, finished jobs, any backfill) get cpu_request=0 / memory_request=0, shifting their idle energy, CO2e and embodied share to __idle__.

**Severity** medium · **Kind** bug · **Confidence** confirmed-live · **Effort** M

**Where.** `src/greenkube/core/historical_range_processor.py:508-531`; `src/greenkube/core/historical_range_processor.py:808-809`; `src/greenkube/impact/types.py:366-372`; `src/greenkube/impact/allocation.py:136-142`

**Mechanism.** The Impact Model allocates idle CPU power, memory power and embodied emissions by reservation `max(request, usage)`. Usage comes from Prometheus per step, but requests come from `pod_collector.collect()` executed when the window is processed: `pod_request_map_agg[key] += p.cpu_request` … `cpu_request=pod_request_map.get(pod_key, 0)`. A pod that no longer exists at that moment (deleted during a rollout, evicted, completed and garbage-collected, or simply older than the backfill) gets request 0, so its reservation collapses to its usage. The samples themselves still exist (Prometheus keeps the series; `rate()` even yields values for up to one rate window after the last scrape). kube-state-metrics exposes the historical truth (`kube_pod_container_resource_requests`), already scraped by the same Prometheus, but it is not queried.

**Effect on numbers.** joules, co2e_grams, embodied_co2e_grams (and modeled cost without OpenCost) of the affected pod rows, under-stated; the difference lands in the hidden idle rows (NRG-02), so allocated totals drop. Live: 15 of 88 pods (minikube) and 9 of 48 (kind) have rows where cpu_request flips from its real value to 0 for their last 3–4 steps (e.g. oversized-web-685f69cd4f-vbk97: 400m for 16 rows, then 0 for 3 rows; greenkube-api-…: 100m then 0). For such a row a 400m request on a 6-core node means 0.4/6 × 4.36 W = 0.29 W of idle power and 6.7% of the node's embodied emissions that the row loses (≈ −90% of its energy when usage is ~1m). After a controller outage with a deployment rollout in between, 100% of the backfilled rows of the replaced pods are affected; rightsizing evidence (`cpu_request` on rows) is also wrong for those steps.

**Affected environments.** All. Proportional to pod churn (CI/batch namespaces, frequent rollouts, HPA scale-in, spot) and to backfill length.

**Evidence.** Live SQL: `with p as (select namespace, pod_name, max(cpu_request) mx, min(cpu_request) mn, count(*) filter (where cpu_request=0) z … from combined_metrics group by 1,2) select count(*), count(*) filter (where mx>0 and mn=0), sum(z) filter (where mx>0 and mn=0) from p` -> minikube 88 | 15 | 46; kind 48 | 9 | 35; per-pod listing shows last_nonzero immediately followed by first_zero and 3–4 trailing zero rows. Simulation sim_k8s_blip.py shows the energy effect of request 0 (387.6 J -> 210.0 J). Code path read end to end.

**Fix.** Collect requests (and limits) per step from Prometheus in the same chunk as usage: add PromQLCatalog queries `max by (namespace, pod, container) (kube_pod_container_resource_requests{resource='cpu'})` and `{resource='memory'}` (range, step-aligned, `max_over_time(...[step])` semantics so a pod that died mid-step still has its request), build `pod_request_by_sample[sample_start][(ns, pod)]` and per-container maps, and use them for the row of that sample. Fallback order: step value from kube-state-metrics -> last known value of the same pod earlier in the chunk or in storage (latest stored row of that pod) -> live pod list -> 0 with `requests_source=unknown` and an estimation reason. Record `requests_source` (kube_state_metrics | pod_list | carried_forward | unknown) as provenance. Same for owner (kube_pod_owner) so terminated pods keep their workload identity. Backfill of stored rows: within raw retention, a recompute pass can restore requests of zero-request tail rows from the pod's previous non-zero row and re-allocate the node-step (`greenkube recompute --fix-requests`).

**Acceptance.** a) Range test: pod deleted at t+7 min, window [t, t+15 min) collected at t+20 min with an empty live pod list -> all its rows carry the real request and requests_source=kube_state_metrics. b) Live query above returns 0 pods with requests flipping to zero after one day. c) Backfill test over a rollout: rows of the old ReplicaSet's pods keep their requests. d) Without kube-state-metrics the row is flagged requests_source=pod_list/unknown.

#### NRG-12 · Node CPU utilisation is the sum of pod CPU only: kubelet, container runtime, kernel and other non-pod CPU are ignored, so dynamic node power is under-estimated (live: pods account for 36% of the node's CPU activity on minikube).

**Severity** medium · **Kind** design · **Confidence** confirmed-live · **Effort** M

**Where.** `src/greenkube/impact/allocation.py:97-103`; `src/greenkube/core/historical_range_processor.py:659-690`; `docs/power_estimation_methodology.md:10-11`

**Mechanism.** `node_totals` computes `utilization = min(Σ sample.cpu_usage_cores × hours / (capacity × hours), 1)` where the samples are the pods of the node (container cgroup series from cAdvisor). CPU spent outside pod cgroups — kubelet, containerd/dockerd, systemd, kernel threads, static daemons, host processes on shared machines — never enters the utilisation, so `dynamic_kwh = utilization × (P_max − P_min) × H` misses it. The methodology formula `node_utilization = total_node_cpu_cores / node_vcores` reads as whole-node utilisation, and the conservation claim ('pods + idle = node power × time') holds only against this reduced definition of node power. No provenance says the node utilisation was derived from pods.

**Effect on numbers.** Dynamic energy and its CO2e, per node and in totals: under-stated by the non-pod share of CPU. Live minikube at 06:49:30Z: root cgroup 0.556 cores (node-exporter non-idle 0.563) vs Σ pods 0.204 cores stored -> dynamic power 0.58 W modelled vs 1.58 W with the node's real utilisation (−63%); kind host: 0.39 cores vs 0.305 from pods (−22%). On production nodes system overhead is typically 3–10% of capacity: with the CCF slope (≈ 2.8 W/vCPU) that is ≈ 0.1–0.3 W per vCPU, i.e. roughly 5–15% of total node power on lightly loaded nodes.

**Affected environments.** All; largest on small/lightly loaded nodes, control-plane nodes, nodes running host daemons, and dev clusters.

**Evidence.** Live Prometheus (minikube, t=1794725370): `sum by (node) (rate(container_cpu_usage_seconds_total{id='/'}[2m]))` -> 0.5558; `sum by (instance) (rate(node_cpu_seconds_total{mode!='idle',mode!='iowait'}[2m]))` -> 0.5629; `select sum(cpu_usage_millicores)/1000.0 from combined_metrics where timestamp='2026-11-15 06:49:00+00'` -> 0.2040; recompute_live.py reproduces util=0.0340 and dynamic 0.58 W from the stored rows. kind: node-exporter 0.39 vs pods 0.146+0.090+0.069.

**Fix.** Measure node utilisation at node level and keep pods as the allocation key: add a PromQLCatalog query per node and step with a source chain (1) root cgroup `sum by (node) (rate(container_cpu_usage_seconds_total{id='/'}[w]))`, (2) node-exporter `sum by (instance) (rate(node_cpu_seconds_total{mode!~'idle|iowait|steal'}[w]))` joined to node names through `kube_node_info` (internal_ip/node labels), (3) fallback Σ pods. Pass `node_cpu_used_cores` in NodeSpec/NodeImpactProfile; `node_totals` uses `max(node value, Σ pods)` for utilisation; dynamic energy is allocated to pods by their usage share of the NODE usage (pod_i / node_used) and the remainder (system share) goes to a dedicated `__system__` component of the node idle row (new columns `system_joules`, `system_co2e_grams`) so conservation is against real node utilisation. Store `node_utilization_source` (root_cgroup | node_exporter | pod_sum) on the idle row and add it to estimation reasons when it is pod_sum. Bump the model minor version; document in both methodology files.

**Acceptance.** a) Fixture: node 4 cores, pods use 1.0 core, root cgroup reports 1.4 -> utilisation 0.35, pods receive 1.0/1.4 of the dynamic energy, the idle row's system component 0.4/1.4, and Σ = node power × time. b) Without node-level series the result equals today's and node_utilization_source=pod_sum is recorded. c) On live minikube the modelled dynamic power at the sampled step is ≈ 1.58 W.

#### NRG-13 · GPU energy is counted only when DCGM series exist; GPU nodes without the DCGM exporter are neither detected nor flagged, so their dominant power draw is silently missing.

**Severity** medium · **Kind** design · **Confidence** confirmed-code · **Effort** M

**Where.** `src/greenkube/core/historical_range_processor.py:302-320`; `src/greenkube/collectors/dcgm_collector.py:17-18`; `src/greenkube/impact/power.py:136-155`; `src/greenkube/collectors/node_collector.py:43-105`; `docs/methodology/impact-model.md:429`

**Mechanism.** `_collect_gpu` returns `{}` when DCGM is absent, unreachable or errors (warning only), and then no GPU power exists anywhere. Nothing in the pipeline knows that a node has GPUs: NodeCollector does not read `status.capacity['nvidia.com/gpu']` (or amd.com/gpu, gpu.intel.com/*) nor the `nvidia.com/gpu.product` / `nvidia.com/gpu.count` labels, and PodCollector does not read GPU requests. `GpuPowerModel.profile_for` is documented as matching 'a DCGM modelName or a nvidia.com/gpu.product label' but the label is never passed to it. A GPU node is therefore modelled as a CPU-only node (CCF per-vCPU watts) with `power_source=instance_profile|node_capacity_derived` and no estimation reason; the limitation is in the docs only. A DCGM query error for one window equally drops GPU energy for that window without failing or flagging it (`gpu_power_watts` NULL is indistinguishable from 'no GPU').

**Effect on numbers.** joules / co2e_grams of GPU workloads and nodes: under-stated by the whole GPU draw. Example: AWS p4d.24xlarge (96 vCPU, 8 × A100 SXM 400 W): modelled 96 × 0.74–3.5 W = 71–336 W, real GPU boards add up to 3.2 kW (idle ≈ 480 W at 15%) -> node energy under-stated ≈ 5–10×. Embodied emissions of GPUs are likewise absent. Direction: always under; no flag.

**Affected environments.** Any cluster with GPU nodes where the DCGM exporter is not installed, not scraped by the configured Prometheus, or temporarily failing; AMD/Intel GPUs always.

**Evidence.** Code: grep -i 'nvidia|gpu.product|gpu_count' over src/greenkube finds only dcgm_collector.py, models/metrics.py and the unused docstring in impact/power.py:137; run_range passes GPU data only from `_collect_gpu`; no GPU hardware in the two live clusters (gpu_power_watts NULL everywhere).

**Fix.** 1) NodeCollector: read extended-resource capacity (`nvidia.com/gpu`, `amd.com/gpu`, `gpu.intel.com/*`) into `NodeInfo.gpu_count` and labels `nvidia.com/gpu.product`, `nvidia.com/gpu.memory` into `NodeInfo.gpu_model` (new SCD columns). PodCollector: read GPU requests per container. 2) run_range: for a node with gpu_count > 0 and no DCGM sample at a step, estimate with the TDP table: per GPU `GpuPowerModel.profile_for(gpu_model).watts(None)` (idle floor) for unrequested GPUs and a configurable assumed utilisation (default 50%) for GPUs requested by pods, attributed to the requesting pods; set a new provenance `gpu_source` on pod rows (dcgm | gpu_tdp_estimate | none) and add the reason 'GPU power estimated from TDP: DCGM exporter not available'. If the model is unknown, flag `gpu_source=unmeasured` and count the node in `greenkube_gpu_nodes_unmeasured`. 3) A DCGM query error on a cluster that previously returned GPU series must fail the window (RangeCollectionError) instead of dropping GPU power. 4) Surface `gpu_source` in the API rows and a UI badge on the node/namespace views; document.

**Acceptance.** a) Node with capacity nvidia.com/gpu=4, label gpu.product=Tesla-T4, no DCGM: rows of the pod requesting 1 GPU carry gpu_power_watts = 10.5 + 0.5 × (70 − 10.5) W, gpu_source=gpu_tdp_estimate and a reason; the idle row carries 3 × 10.5 W. b) Unknown GPU model: gauge greenkube_gpu_nodes_unmeasured = 1 and rows flagged. c) With DCGM present nothing changes (gpu_source=dcgm). d) DCGM outage on a cluster with known GPU series fails the window.

#### NRG-14 · On-prem and bare-metal nodes are modelled with hyperscaler per-vCPU averages and the operator cannot supply real server power: DEFAULT_INSTANCE_* is dead configuration for any node whose capacity is known, and architecture is ignored.

**Severity** medium · **Kind** design · **Confidence** confirmed-code · **Effort** L

**Where.** `src/greenkube/energy/node_power.py:62-80`; `src/greenkube/energy/node_power.py:108-123`; `src/greenkube/data/provider_power_estimates.csv:1-6`; `src/greenkube/data/instance_profiles.py:83-92`; `helm-chart/values.yaml:530-536`; `src/greenkube/core/config.py:277-279`

**Mechanism.** For a node without a bundled instance profile the power profile is `cores × per_core_watts(provider)`; with provider 'unknown' this is the arithmetic mean of the five provider rows of provider_power_estimates.csv (0.726 W idle, 3.564 W max per vCPU), labelled 'cross-provider average CCF per-vCPU coefficients' although the OVH and Scaleway rows are GreenKube's own estimates, not CCF data. These coefficients describe one hardware thread of a dense, shared hyperscale server. A dedicated on-prem server is charged the same per-thread figure although it carries its whole chassis (PSU losses, fans, disks, BMC, NICs). There is no way to correct it: `config.defaults.instance` (DEFAULT_INSTANCE_VCORES/MIN_WATTS/MAX_WATTS) is only used when neither instance type nor CPU capacity is known, which never happens for a real node (the kubelet always reports capacity); no per-node annotation, node-label selector or per-instance-type override exists, and measured power (IPMI/Redfish/Kepler/Scaphandre exporters) cannot be plugged in. `NodeInfo.architecture` (arm64/amd64) is collected but unused, and the 740 'instance profiles' are the same provider average × vCPUs, so `power_source=instance_profile` (not flagged as estimated) is no more specific than `node_capacity_derived` (flagged).

**Effect on numbers.** joules and co2e_grams of every row on such nodes. Example: 2-socket server with 64 threads: modelled idle 46 W / max 228 W; typical measured values are ≈ 120–200 W idle and 400–550 W at full load -> energy under-stated ≈ 2–3×. ARM nodes (Graviton, Ampere) are charged the provider's x86-dominated average (likely over-stated: CCF publishes separate, lower coefficients for Graviton). The live kind/minikube nodes run on arm64 and are modelled at 0.726–3.564 W per core.

**Affected environments.** On-prem, bare metal, private cloud, edge, kind/minikube/k3s; cloud ARM instance families.

**Evidence.** Code read end to end (resolve_node_power_profile, per_core_watts, load_instance_profiles); live rows: estimation reason 'Power profile for node 'minikube' derived from 6 vCPU capacity and cross-provider average CCF per-vCPU coefficients', architecture=arm64 in node_snapshots_scd; hand recompute confirms 0.726/3.564 W per core.

**Fix.** Add an explicit resolution tier above the bundled data, `power_source=configured`: (1) node annotations `greenkube.io/power-idle-watts` and `greenkube.io/power-max-watts` (whole node) ; (2) Helm `config.power.nodeProfiles: [{matchLabels|instanceType|nodeNameRegex, idleWatts, maxWatts}]` ; (3) Helm `config.power.perVcpu: {idleWatts, maxWatts}` as the cluster-wide default for unknown providers, replacing the cross-provider average when set (and replacing the dead DEFAULT_INSTANCE_* semantics — keep those names as deprecated aliases of perVcpu). Add tier `power_source=measured` for a node-level power series (configurable PromQL, e.g. Redfish/IPMI exporter or Kepler node power) used as the node total when present. Use architecture: ship per-architecture coefficients (CCF per-microarchitecture table, at least an arm64 row per provider) and select by `NodeInfo.architecture`. Rename the reason to 'average of bundled provider coefficients'. Mark `instance_profile` rows as estimated=false only if the profile is instance-specific; otherwise expose `power_profile_detail=provider_average`. Document a calibration procedure (measure idle/max with a wattmeter or BMC). Bump the model minor version when coefficients change.

**Acceptance.** a) Node annotated idle=150/max=450 W: idle row + pods sum to 150 W × step at 0% utilisation and power_source=configured. b) nodeProfiles entry matching label hardware=r740 applies to matching nodes only. c) perVcpu {1.5, 6} on a 64-core unknown-provider node yields 96/384 W. d) arm64 AWS node uses the arm64 coefficient. e) projector and collection agree for all four cases (shared resolver, see NRG-05).

#### NRG-15 · Nodes that share one physical host (kind, k3d, minikube multi-node, nested clusters) are each charged the full host capacity: idle power, memory power and embodied emissions are multiplied by the node count.

**Severity** medium · **Kind** portability · **Confidence** confirmed-live · **Effort** M

**Where.** `src/greenkube/collectors/node_collector.py:81-93`; `src/greenkube/energy/node_power.py:108-123`; `src/greenkube/impact/allocation.py:97-118`

**Mechanism.** Container-based 'nodes' report the host's CPU count and memory as their own capacity. GreenKube derives power, memory power (0.392 W/GiB × capacity) and embodied emissions per Kubernetes node, with no notion that several nodes are one machine. On the kind e2e cluster all three nodes report cpu=14, memory≈39 GiB and the same `status.nodeInfo.bootID` (same kernel), with `spec.providerID=kind://…`. The model therefore attributes 3 × (10.2 W idle + 15.3 W memory) and 3 × 100 kg embodied to a single 14-core host.

**Effect on numbers.** Idle energy, memory energy, embodied emissions and modeled cost of the cluster ×N (N = nodes per host): kind e2e cluster 77 W modelled baseline instead of ≈ 25.5 W for the one host (×3). This is the first number an evaluator sees in a kind/k3d quickstart. Dynamic power is not multiplied (it follows pod usage).

**Affected environments.** kind, k3d, minikube --nodes>1 with container drivers, vcluster-on-host style labs. Real VMs/bare-metal nodes are not affected.

**Evidence.** Live kind: `kubectl get nodes -o jsonpath` -> the three nodes share bootID=f58cc2d1-81de-4919-87c6-1dbd303023c2, each cpu=14, memory=41005524Ki, providerID kind://docker/gk-cilium/…; node_snapshots_scd: cpu_capacity_cores=14, memory_capacity_bytes=41989656576 for each; recompute_live.py: per node idle=10.16 W, mem=15.33 W; node-exporter on the three nodes reports the same host CPU (0.39 cores).

**Fix.** Collect `status.nodeInfo.bootID`, `machineID`, `systemUUID` and `spec.providerID` in NodeCollector. Group nodes by bootID (fallback: providerID scheme `kind://`, `k3d://`); when a group has more than one node, treat the group as one physical host: host capacity = the (identical) reported capacity, each node's share = 1/N (or proportional to its kubelet allocatable if they differ), and scale idle watts, max watts, memory watts and embodied g/h of each node by that share before allocation. Record `power_source` detail `shared_host` and an estimation reason ('3 nodes share one host (bootID …): host power split 1/3'). Add gauge greenkube_shared_host_groups. Document the behaviour and an opt-out (`config.power.sharedHostDetection=false`). Mirror the scaling in the projector.

**Acceptance.** a) Three NodeInfo with the same bootID and 14 cores each: Σ over nodes of (pod + idle joules) per step with no load == 14 × 0.726 W × step + memory power of 39 GiB once. b) Nodes with distinct bootIDs are unchanged. c) On the live kind cluster the modelled baseline power drops from ≈ 77 W to ≈ 25.5 W and rows carry the shared_host reason.

#### NRG-16 · Wattnet's 15-minute intensities are stored but only the :00 point of each hour is ever used (default hour normalisation); sub-zone proxies are unflagged and one null value aborts a zone's collection.

**Severity** low · **Kind** design · **Confidence** confirmed-code · **Effort** M

**Where.** `src/greenkube/core/calculator.py:94-101`; `src/greenkube/storage/postgres/repository.py:1586-1590`; `src/greenkube/collectors/wattnet_collector.py:104-133`; `src/greenkube/collectors/wattnet_collector.py:376-389`; `docs/wattnet.md:20`

**Mechanism.** `CarbonCalculator._normalize` truncates the lookup time to the hour (NORMALIZATION_GRANULARITY=hour, the Helm default) and both the calculator and the recompute take 'the latest point at or before the hour start'. With Wattnet's 15-minute series the :15, :30 and :45 points are saved by `save_history` and never read: an hour is represented by its first quarter, not by its mean, while docs/wattnet.md advertises '15-minute granularity (finer than Electricity Maps' hourly data)'. `_flatten_footprints` does `float(value)` outside any try: a null in one series raises and `process_zone` drops the whole zone for that hour. Country codes are silently proxied to one bidding zone (IT->IT_NORTH, SE->SE3, NO->NO2, DK->DK1) and stored as `wattnet_complete` / later `consolidated` with no proxy marker.

**Effect on numbers.** grid_intensity / co2e of Wattnet users: per-hour error equal to the intra-hour variation (a few % typically, 10–20% during solar ramps), unbiased on average; SE/NO/IT/DK country-level users get one bidding zone's intensity (SE3 vs SE1 or IT_NORTH vs IT_SICILY differ by tens of %) labelled as measured.

**Affected environments.** Installs with ELECTRICITY_PROVIDER=wattnet (EU).

**Evidence.** Code read: calculator._normalize + recompute lookup_expr date_trunc('hour'); wattnet_collector.EM_TO_WATTNET_ZONE and _flatten_footprints. No Wattnet credentials were available, so the payload shape (null values, timestamp format) was not exercised live.

**Fix.** Resolve intensity as the duration-weighted mean of the provider points inside each row's own interval [timestamp, timestamp+duration) (fall back to the latest point within INTENSITY_MAX_AGE); for hourly providers this equals today's behaviour, for 15-minute providers it uses all points. Implement it identically in the calculator and in the set-based recompute (lateral AVG over points in the interval). Alternatively set NORMALIZATION_GRANULARITY=none automatically for Wattnet and document it. Skip null/non-finite values per point in `_flatten_footprints` (count them) instead of raising. Record proxies: `estimationMethod='wattnet_proxy:<zone>'`, `isEstimated=true`, and map them to a new intensity_source `proxy_zone`. Fix the doc sentence.

**Acceptance.** a) History 100/200/300/400 at :00/:15/:30/:45 and a 5-minute row at 10:35 -> intensity 300 (interval mean), an hourly aggregate of the 12 rows ≈ 250. b) A null value in the payload stores the other points. c) Zone 'SE' rows carry intensity_source=proxy_zone.

#### NRG-17 · Methodology docs and code disagree, and dead legacy estimator code with a different model is still shipped.

**Severity** low · **Kind** docs · **Confidence** confirmed-code · **Effort** S

**Where.** `docs/methodology/impact-model.md:61-67`; `docs/methodology/impact-model.md:131-140`; `src/greenkube/energy/estimator.py:73-287`; `src/greenkube/core/embodied_service.py:136-215`; `src/greenkube/data/datacenter_pue_profiles.py:20`; `src/greenkube/data/datacenter_pue_profiles.csv:47`; `src/greenkube/core/metrics_compressor.py:115-116`

**Mechanism.** (1) impact-model.md 'Power profile' says `node_capacity_derived` uses DEFAULT_INSTANCE_MIN/MAX_WATTS per DEFAULT_INSTANCE_VCORES × capacity; collection uses provider CCF coefficients (power_estimation_methodology.md is right; the projector still implements the old text, NRG-05). (2) impact-model.md states node totals 'stay reconstructible as Σ pod rows + idle row' without saying idle rows are deleted after raw retention and never exposed (NRG-02). (3) `BasicEstimator.estimate`, `calculate_node_energy`, `_create_cpu_profile`, `EmbodiedEmissionsService.calculate_pod_embodied` and `is_embodied_fallback` have no production caller but implement the pre-1.1.0 usage-based model (1–10 W per core) and are still unit-tested, which keeps misleading tests green. (4) PUE data: datacenter_pue_profiles.py has default_azure 1.18, the CSV next to it 1.16; the CSV (59 per-site rows) and region_electricity_maps.csv are not loaded by any code. (5) Hourly aggregates store AVG(grid_intensity) and AVG(pue) unweighted, so hourly co2e ≠ hourly kWh × PUE × stored intensity whenever energy varies within the hour (display only: co2e itself is summed). (6) 'Scope 2' / 'Scope 3' UI labels cover a lifecycle emission factor and exclude the idle share. (7) An operator cannot override the PUE of a recognised provider: `get_pue_for_provider` returns the bundled provider value before DEFAULT_PUE. (8) Storage and network energy are excluded from every figure; only the docs and the asset-deletion projection note say so, the Footprint totals and API summaries carry no such notice.

**Effect on numbers.** No stored number changes by itself; the mismatches hide NRG-02 and NRG-05, and a user recomputing CO2e from hourly intensity × energy gets a different figure than the stored one (typically < 5%).

**Affected environments.** All.

**Evidence.** Docs and code read side by side; grep for callers of BasicEstimator.estimate / calculate_pod_embodied / is_embodied_fallback returns none outside their own modules and tests; csv/py values compared.

**Fix.** Rewrite the 'Power profile' section from energy/node_power.py; add a 'What the totals contain' section (allocated vs idle vs cluster) and the retention of idle rows. Delete the dead estimator/embodied methods and their tests (keep BasicEstimator only as the holder of instance_profiles/default profile, or move those to energy/node_power.py). Make the PUE table single-sourced (load the CSV or delete it), align default_azure, and add `config.pueOverrides: {aws: 1.1}` taking precedence over the bundled value with `pue_source` recorded. Store energy-weighted intensity in hourly rows (`SUM(co2e)/NULLIF(SUM(joules)/3.6e6 × pue,0)`) or drop the column from hourly API responses. Rename UI labels to 'Operational (electricity, location-based, life-cycle factor)' and 'Embodied (hardware)', and add a 'Not included: storage, network, GPU without DCGM' note to the Footprint page and a `not_modeled` list to the summary responses.

**Acceptance.** a) A docs test extracts the coefficients table from power_estimation_methodology.md and compares it with provider_power_estimates.csv. b) grep for calculate_node_energy and calculate_pod_embodied under src returns nothing. c) hourly row: co2e_grams == joules/3.6e6 × pue × grid_intensity within 1e-6. d) pueOverrides test.

### 5.5 Storage write path

**Coverage.** {'read_in_full': ['src/greenkube/storage/postgres/repository.py (write paths, read_combined_metrics, hourly reads, aggregate_summary, recompute; other aggregates skimmed)', 'src/greenkube/storage/sqlite/repository.py (write paths, reads, recompute; other aggregates skimmed)', 'src/greenkube/storage/postgres/node_repository.py, src/greenkube/storage/sqlite/node_repository.py', 'src/greenkube/storage/postgres/collection_watermark_repository.py, sqlite counterpart', 'src/greenkube/storage/unit_of_work.py, src/greenkube/core/db.py, src/greenkube/core/migrations/runner.py', 'src/greenkube/core/collection.py, the collection/compression/recompute/node jobs of cli/start.py, cli/utils.py write_combined_metrics_to_database', 'models/metrics.py CombinedMetric', 'migrations 0000 (combined_metrics), 0002 (hourly, SCD), 0003, 0033, 0038, 0039, 0040, 0050, 0064 in both dialects; v0.3.1 schema and writer through git show', 'core/metrics_compressor.py column mapping and limit logic'], 'ran': ['audit-workspace/audit/work/storage-write/sim_storage.py (SQLite swallow, degraded overwrite, fractional ISO bounds)', 'audit-workspace/audit/work/storage-write/sim_compress.py (compression overtaking collection)', 'read-only live SQL on minikube and kind: schema, indexes, row counts versus sequences, watermarks, SCD versions, co2e formula consistency, request-zero shares, float4/float8 comparison semantics'], 'not_covered': ['Remaining aggregate queries of both repositories (timeseries, by_namespace, top_pods, paging) were only skimmed: dialect differences there are not established.', 'metrics_summary, timeseries cache, workload_usage_hourly and recommendation/ledger tables (other dimensions).', '`greenkube db copy` was read for table selection and value conversion only, not executed.', 'Retention deletes (one DELETE per table under the 30 s statement timeout) were not measured.', 'No PostgreSQL simulation (only read-only statements on the live databases); the migration batch was not timed on a large database; statement timeout behaviour of very large executemany batches was not measured.', 'The upgrade path from 0.3.1 was checked by reading migrations and the old writer, not by running hack/upgrade/upgrade-test.sh.']}

**Earlier defects not fully fixed.**

- BUG-02 (partially_fixed): Compression watermark exists and compression/prune are transactional, but the collection watermark bound is not wired in production (STO-02).
- BUG-06 (partially_fixed): Container rows are recomputed with pod rows in one transaction; idle rows and hourly rows are not (NRG-09), and the float4 comparison rewrites every row (STO-11).
- BUG-13 (partially_fixed): A leader fence is checked before the row write and before the watermark advance, but the four commits are not one transaction (STO-03).
- BUG-01 (partially_fixed): New rows are corrected; legacy raw and hourly rows stay inflated and unflagged (STO-07).

#### STO-01 · Every metrics upsert overwrites all columns unconditionally and never removes rows the new pass did not produce, so any re-collection of a stored window replaces good values with degraded ones and leaves a mix of two collection generations.

**Severity** high · **Kind** data-integrity · **Confidence** confirmed-sim · **Effort** L

**Where.** `src/greenkube/storage/postgres/repository.py:274-317`; `src/greenkube/storage/postgres/repository.py:480-512`; `src/greenkube/storage/postgres/repository.py:566-572`; `src/greenkube/storage/sqlite/repository.py:335-380`; `src/greenkube/storage/sqlite/repository.py:1088-1120`; `src/greenkube/core/collection.py:305-334`

**Mechanism.** combined_metrics, container_metrics and node_idle_metrics are written with `INSERT ... ON CONFLICT (pod_name, namespace, timestamp) DO UPDATE SET <every column> = EXCLUDED.<column>` (container key: namespace, pod_name, container_name, timestamp; idle key: node, timestamp). There is no comparison of provenance or quality between the stored row and the incoming row, and no delete of the rows of the window that the new pass did not return. A window is re-collected whenever (a) the watermark row is missing or forked (cluster name change, see COST-04/COL-02/RECON-10: seen live on kind), (b) an operator runs `greenkube start --last ...`, (c) a crash happens between the row commits and the watermark commit (STO-03), (d) the first run after an upgrade re-collects 24 h (CHANGELOG, data note of A1). The second pass is computed from the Kubernetes API and OpenCost as they are at that later time (RECON-01, COST-01): pods that were deleted meanwhile get cpu_request=0, memory_request=0 and owner NULL, an OpenCost outage gives cost_source=custom_rate. These values silently replace the opencost cost, the real requests and the owner that were stored by the first pass. Rows that only the first pass produced stay, rows that only the second pass produced are added, so the window total is the sum of rows from two different allocations (each pass allocates node energy and cost over the pods it saw).

**Effect on numbers.** Per re-collected row: cost switches from OpenCost to the modelled rate (simulation: 0.05 -> 0.011), cpu_request/memory_request drop to 0 and owner to NULL, which removes the pod from workload rightsizing and changes its reservation share of energy and cost. Per window: simulation total cost 0.10 (2 rows) became 0.065 over 3 rows, none of the two passes' own totals (0.10 and 0.015). Live kind install: max(id)=27277 for 26424 combined_metrics rows and 28041 for 27178 container rows, i.e. about 850 pod rows and 860 container rows were upserted twice after the watermark fork.

**Affected environments.** All databases and environments. Triggered by controller restarts with an unstable cluster name, `greenkube start --last`, upgrades, crashes during a write.

**Evidence.** Simulation audit-workspace/audit/work/storage-write/sim_storage.py (SIM B, real MetricsCollectionJob + SQLite repository; the PostgreSQL statement has the same SET list): after re-collection the row api-1 is (0.011, custom_rate, request 0, owner None), api-2 keeps its first-pass values, api-9 is added; SUM(total_cost)=0.065 over 3 rows. Live: `select count(*), max(id) from combined_metrics` on kind (26424 vs 27277).

**Fix.** 1. Give every raw row a write generation: add `collected_at TIMESTAMPTZ` and `collection_run_id` (UUID of the MetricsCollectionJob window pass) to combined_metrics, container_metrics and node_idle_metrics (new migration, both dialects; NULL on existing rows). 2. Make the window write a replace, not a per-row upsert: in one transaction (see STO-03) upsert the rows of the pass, then `DELETE FROM <table> WHERE timestamp >= window.start AND timestamp < window.end AND collection_run_id IS DISTINCT FROM $run` so that a window always holds exactly one generation. 3. Add a quality guard so that a later pass cannot degrade a stored row.
