Then refresh metrics_summary / metrics_timeseries_cache. 2) Add model_version to both hourly tables and to workload_usage_hourly (migration both dialects): single value when all samples agree, 'mixed' otherwise (same rule as _source_aggregate); backfill from raw for hours still in raw, NULL (=unknown/legacy) for older ones. 3) Expose in API/UI the model version (or 'mixed/legacy') of a window and a banner when a window mixes versions. 4) The CLI summary must state how many hours were re-aggregated and that hours older than raw retention cannot be recomputed. 5) Recompute idle rows' carbon in recompute_carbon_with_latest_intensities as well (node_idle_metrics is skipped today).

**Acceptance.** 1) Temporary DB with 3 days of rows at model v-old, compress, run recompute for 3 days with a model that doubles joules: SUM(joules) in combined_metrics_hourly and workload_usage_hourly doubles for all 3 days, on SQLite and PostgreSQL. 2) Hourly rows carry model_version = new version; an hour with both versions carries 'mixed'. 3) Running recompute twice changes nothing (idempotent). 4) aggregate_summary before/after equals the raw sums.

#### ROL-07 · node_idle_metrics has no hourly aggregate and is deleted with raw retention, so node totals (pods + idle) cannot be reconstructed after 7 days and idle carbon is never consolidated.

**Severity** medium · **Kind** data-integrity · **Confidence** confirmed-sim · **Effort** M

**Where.** `src/greenkube/core/metrics_compressor.py:206-207`; `src/greenkube/core/metrics_compressor.py:538-541`; `src/greenkube/core/metrics_compressor.py:575-587`; `src/greenkube/storage/postgres/repository.py:1602`; `src/greenkube/impact/rows.py:59-66`

**Mechanism.** The Impact Model stores the unreserved share of each node per step in node_idle_metrics so that 'sum of pod rows + idle row = node total'. The compressor only prunes this table (`_delete_idle_before`, same cutoff as combined_metrics); TABLES contains no idle entry, so nothing is aggregated. The carbon-intensity recompute loops over ('combined_metrics', 'container_metrics') only, so idle rows keep provisional/default intensity. The only reader of idle rows is `greenkube recompute`.

**Effect on numbers.** On the live minikube cluster idle cost is 0.709 against 0.837 of pod cost over the same 13 h (about 46% of node cost). After 7 days this half of the node cost/energy/CO2e no longer exists anywhere: cluster totals older than a week can never be reconciled with the node bill or with OpenCost node totals, and showback of idle cost over a month or a year is impossible. Simulation: 872 idle rows (cost 1744) -> 0 rows after the retention run, no hourly equivalent.

**Affected environments.** all

**Evidence.** audit-workspace/audit/work/rollup-retention/sim1.py step H ('idle rows left: 0', no idle hourly table exists). Live: `select round(sum(total_cost)::numeric,6) from node_idle_metrics` = 0.708851 vs `select sum(total_cost) from combined_metrics` = 0.837197 (minikube).

**Fix.** Add node_idle_metrics_hourly (node, hour_bucket, sample_count, duration_seconds, SUM of joules, co2e_grams, embodied_co2e_grams, total_cost, cost_cpu, cost_ram, duration-weighted averages of cpu_unreserved_cores, memory_unreserved_bytes, gpu_power_watts, provenance aggregated like pod rows, model_version) with migrations for both dialects; add it to MetricsCompressor (same watermark and transaction as combined_metrics so pod and idle hours stay consistent), to _prune_hourly, to cli/db.py METRIC_TABLES and to the VACUUM list. Include node_idle_metrics in recompute_carbon_with_latest_intensities. Provide a reader (raw + hourly, boundary = watermark) and expose idle in summaries as a separate line so totals reconcile. Backfill: aggregate the idle rows still in raw at migration time.

**Acceptance.** 1) sim1.py: after compression, SUM(total_cost) of node_idle_metrics_hourly equals the raw idle sum for compressed hours; after the 8-day run the hourly idle total is still 1744. 2) For every compressed hour and node: pod hourly sums + idle hourly sums == the same sums computed on raw rows. 3) Intensity recompute changes idle co2e_grams consistently with pod rows.

#### ROL-08 · Day, week and month buckets are computed with date_trunc in the PostgreSQL session time zone, which the connection pool never pins to UTC, so on a non-UTC database buckets are cut at local midnight and labelled with the wrong UTC day.

**Severity** medium · **Kind** portability · **Confidence** confirmed-code · **Effort** S

**Where.** `src/greenkube/storage/postgres/repository.py:985-1012`; `src/greenkube/storage/postgres/repository.py:677-683`; `src/greenkube/core/db.py:119-128`

**Mechanism.** aggregate_timeseries runs `date_trunc('day'|'week'|'month', ts)` on timestamptz values; PostgreSQL truncates in the session TimeZone. asyncpg.create_pool sets only search_path and statement_timeout in server_settings. The compressor and the rollup use `AT TIME ZONE 'UTC'` explicitly, this reader does not. With TimeZone=Europe/Paris, date_trunc('day') returns local midnight (23:00Z of the previous day); asyncpg returns it as a UTC datetime and the code formats it with '%Y-%m-%dT00:00:00Z', so the bucket holding local 15 November is labelled '2026-11-14T00:00:00Z'. list_metric_years (EXTRACT(YEAR ...)) has the same dependency. SQLite uses UTC strings, so the two backends disagree.

**Effect on numbers.** Daily/weekly/monthly cost, CO2e and energy series (metrics_timeseries_cache for 7d/30d/1y/ytd and /metrics/timeseries) are shifted by the UTC offset and every label is one day (or one month at month start) early for zones east of UTC; e.g. 1/24..2/24 of each day's cost moves to the neighbouring bar and the bar is dated the previous day. Totals are unchanged. Both live clusters run with TimeZone=UTC (bundled Postgres), so they do not show it.

**Affected environments.** external / managed PostgreSQL whose database, role or server default TimeZone is not UTC (common on self-managed on-prem servers)

**Evidence.** Code read end to end; live `show timezone` = UTC on both clusters (default of the bundled image only).

**Fix.** Pin the session: add 'timezone': 'UTC' to server_settings in DatabaseManager (core/db.py) and assert it at startup (SELECT current_setting('TimeZone')). Additionally make the SQL independent of the session: use `date_trunc(unit, ts AT TIME ZONE 'UTC') AT TIME ZONE 'UTC'` (or date_trunc(unit, ts, 'UTC') on PG >= 12) in aggregate_timeseries and EXTRACT(YEAR FROM ts AT TIME ZONE 'UTC') in list_metric_years; grep the Postgres repositories for other date_trunc/EXTRACT/::date uses. No data migration; the caches are rebuilt at the next refresh.

**Acceptance.** Integration test on PostgreSQL with `ALTER ROLE ... SET timezone='Asia/Tokyo'`: rows at 2026-11-14T23:30Z and 2026-11-15T00:30Z produce day buckets '2026-11-14T00:00:00Z' and '2026-11-15T00:00:00Z' with the right sums, identical to the SQLite result.

#### ROL-09 · With METRICS_AGGREGATED_RETENTION_DAYS = -1 (default) three per-hour tables grow forever, including workload_usage_hourly which is only read for the recommendation lookback, with no size guard against the default 10Gi volume.

**Severity** medium · **Kind** reliability · **Confidence** likely · **Effort** L (split: rollup retention + validators S; gauges S; daily tier L)

**Where.** `src/greenkube/core/config.py:291-295`; `src/greenkube/core/metrics_compressor.py:519-525`; `src/greenkube/core/metrics_compressor.py:589-615`; `helm-chart/values.yaml:431-437`; `helm-chart/values.yaml:1022-1024`

**Mechanism.** _prune_hourly and _prune_usage_rollup return immediately when the retention is negative. combined_metrics_hourly (one row per pod-hour), container_metrics_hourly (one per container-hour) and workload_usage_hourly (one per target x container x node x hour, about 1 KB per row on the live database: 1152 rows = 1144 kB) are then never pruned. The rollup is read only over RECOMMENDATION_LOOKBACK_DAYS (14) and baseline windows, yet shares the 'forever' retention. There is no validator for the opposite misconfiguration either: a non-negative aggregated retention smaller than the raw retention deletes hourly rows that readers still rely on for everything older than 24 h (hole between the two retentions while raw rows exist).

**Effect on numbers.** Estimate from live row sizes: a 1000-pod cluster writes about 8.8 M pod-hour rows, about 11 M container-hour rows and about 17 M rollup rows per year, roughly 25-30 GB per year, against a default PostgreSQL volume of 10Gi: the database fills in about 4-5 months, after which collection writes fail and every number stops. A 60-pod cluster grows about 1.8 GB per year. Pod churn (CronJobs, rollouts) multiplies the per-pod tables because every pod name is a new series.

**Affected environments.** all installs with default retention; fastest on large or high-churn clusters; SQLite installs on small PVCs

**Evidence.** Config/Helm defaults read; live sizes: combined_metrics 43.9k rows = 40 MB, workload_usage_hourly 1152 rows = 1144 kB, 118 rollup rows per hour for 59 pods (minikube). Growth figure is an extrapolation, not measured.

**Fix.** 1) Give workload_usage_hourly its own retention (default max(RECOMMENDATION_LOOKBACK_DAYS, CHANGE_MEASUREMENT_PRE_WINDOW_DAYS + POST) + margin, e.g. 35 days) independent of the hourly metric retention. 2) Add a daily tier: after N days (default 90) roll pod-hour rows into per-workload (owner) daily rows, which removes the pod-name cardinality, and prune pod-hour rows; keep additive columns exact. 3) Change the default aggregated retention to a finite documented value (e.g. 400 days) or keep -1 only with the daily tier. 4) Validator: when >= 0, METRICS_AGGREGATED_RETENTION_DAYS must be >= METRICS_RAW_RETENTION_DAYS and >= the lookback/measurement needs. 5) Export database size and per-table row counts as Prometheus gauges, alert at 80% of the PVC, and document sizing (rows per pod per year) in docs/configuration.md and Helm notes.

**Acceptance.** 1) With rollup retention 35 d, rows of workload_usage_hourly older than 35 d are pruned while combined_metrics_hourly is kept. 2) Config(METRICS_AGGREGATED_RETENTION_DAYS=3, METRICS_RAW_RETENTION_DAYS=7) is rejected. 3) Daily tier: SUM(cost, joules, co2e) per workload and day equals the hourly sums before pruning. 4) /prometheus/metrics exposes table sizes.

#### ROL-10 · Report/export aggregation averages CPU and memory usage with one vote per row, so a compressed hourly row weighs like one raw 5-minute row, and namespace grouping reports the maximum pod request and the mean pod usage instead of namespace totals.

**Severity** medium · **Kind** bug · **Confidence** confirmed-sim · **Effort** S

**Where.** `src/greenkube/core/aggregator.py:91-92`; `src/greenkube/core/aggregator.py:139-144`; `src/greenkube/api/routers/report.py:284-289`

**Mechanism.** aggregate_metrics receives the output of read_combined_metrics_smart (hourly rows with sample_count/duration 3600 for history older than 24 h, raw rows for the last day) and computes `agg_cpu_usage = round(sum(cpu_usages) / len(cpu_usages))` and the same for memory: an unweighted mean over rows, ignoring sample_count and duration_seconds (which it does use for PUE and grid intensity). With group_by='namespace' all pods of the namespace fall in one group: cpu_request/memory_request = max over pods, usage = mean over pods and samples, so the namespace row shows per-pod values under a namespace label. calculation_version is that of the last row, and provenance columns (cost_source, usage_source, cost breakdown) are dropped from the aggregate.

**Effect on numbers.** Simulation: 6 compressed days at 100 m plus the last raw day at 900 m: the 7-day report shows cpu avg 633 m; the time-weighted value is 214 m (3x too high, biased toward the last 24 h). For a namespace with 10 pods requesting 500 m each, the namespace row shows cpu_request 500 m instead of 5000 m.

**Affected environments.** all (report and export endpoints, CLI report)

**Evidence.** audit-workspace/audit/work/rollup-retention/sim3.py part (c): 'aggregator 7d cpu avg: 633 time-weighted truth: 214.3'. Namespace grouping read in code (max / mean over the group).

**Fix.** In aggregate_metrics weight usage means by duration_seconds (fallback sample_count, then 1); for group_by='namespace' first aggregate per timestamp bucket the SUM over pods (requests and usage), then take the time-weighted mean of those sums (and the max of the sums for peak); expose cpu_usage_max. Propagate provenance with the same rule as the compressor ('mixed' when sources differ) and the cost breakdown sums. Better: build reports from workload_usage_hourly / SQL aggregations with the watermark boundary of ROL-01 instead of loading rows.

**Acceptance.** 1) sim3.py (c) returns 214. 2) Namespace report for 10 pods x 500 m requests and 100 m usage each over 7 days returns cpu_request 5000 m and cpu usage 1000 m. 3) Report totals equal aggregate_summary for the same window.

#### ROL-11 · A usage-rollup hour rebuilt from compressed rows uses each pod's hourly maximum as its only point, so p50-p99 of that hour all equal the peak.

**Severity** low · **Kind** design · **Confidence** confirmed-sim · **Effort** M (S for the short-term rule)

**Where.** `src/greenkube/core/workload_usage.py:264-272`; `src/greenkube/storage/workload_usage_sql.py:99-115`; `src/greenkube/core/workload_usage.py:838-846`

**Mechanism.** When read_hour_inputs returns rows from combined_metrics_hourly/container_metrics_hourly (first backfill of the lookback beyond raw retention after an upgrade or `greenkube db copy --include-metrics`, or a late-hour re-roll), _HourAccumulator.add takes `point = max(cpu, peak)` with the row's sample_count as weight ('its point is the hour's peak'). All percentiles of the hour therefore equal cpu_usage_max. The row is tagged source='hourly' but summaries and recommendations do not surface it.

**Effect on numbers.** Simulation: hour with eleven samples at 100 m and one at 900 m. Rolled from raw: p50 100, p90 100, p95 900. Rolled from the compressed row: p50 = p90 = p95 = 900 (9x on p50/p90). Affects lookback days 8-14 on the first scan after an upgrade/migration (percentiles and therefore recommended requests too high, savings understated) until those days leave the lookback.

**Affected environments.** upgrades from releases without the rollup, database copies, late-hour re-rolls of hours whose raw rows are pruned

**Evidence.** audit-workspace/audit/work/rollup-retention/sim1.py steps G and H: "('raw', 166.67, 100.0, 100.0, 900.0, 900.0)" vs "('hourly', 166.0, 900.0, 900.0, 900.0, 900.0)".

**Fix.** With the histogram of ROL-04 the compressor should also store the per-pod hourly histogram (or at least p50/p90/p95/p99) in *_hourly so that a rollup built from compressed rows keeps the distribution. Short term: for 'hourly' sources use the hourly average as the point for p50/p90 and the max only for p99/max, and expose `source` (share of lookback hours with source != raw) in the recommendation evidence and lower the confidence of recommendations whose lookback contains such hours.

**Acceptance.** Rolling the same hour from raw rows and from its compressed rows gives p50/p90 within the documented error; recommendation evidence lists the number of lookback hours rebuilt from compressed data.

#### ROL-12 · Hourly compression silently drops or approximates per-row information: estimation reasons, model version, intensity timestamp, ephemeral-storage columns, and it resolves owner/node/zone and rounding differently on SQLite and PostgreSQL.

**Severity** low · **Kind** data-integrity · **Confidence** confirmed-code · **Effort** M

**Where.** `src/greenkube/core/metrics_compressor.py:71-74`; `src/greenkube/core/metrics_compressor.py:104-165`

**Mechanism.** aggregate_columns: estimation_reasons is the literal '[]' while is_estimated is OR-ed, so an hourly row can be flagged estimated with no reason; calculation_version = MAX (lexical) and model_version is not stored; grid_intensity_timestamp, period, ephemeral_storage_request_bytes and ephemeral_storage_usage_bytes exist in combined_metrics but not in the hourly table; pue and grid_intensity are plain AVG over samples (not energy-weighted, so co2e/joules of the hour no longer equals the stored intensity); cpu_request/memory_request/limits are MAX of the hour (not time-weighted); cpu_usage_avg/memory_usage_avg are CAST(AVG AS INTEGER) on SQLite (truncation: 166.67 -> 166 in sim1.py) and AVG()::INTEGER on PostgreSQL (rounding: 167); owner_kind/owner_name/node/node_instance_type/node_zone/emaps_zone are MODE() on PostgreSQL but an arbitrary row's value on SQLite (bare column in GROUP BY); gpu_usage_millicores is MAX while CPU is an average.

**Effect on numbers.** Additive totals are unaffected. Lost: why a row was estimated; which model produced it; per-pod ephemeral storage history after 24 h; exact grid intensity used (displayed intensity of a compressed hour can differ from co2e/kWh by the within-hour spread). SQLite vs PostgreSQL: usage averages differ by up to 1 millicore/byte and, for a pod that changed node or owner inside an hour, the attributed node/owner can differ between backends.

**Affected environments.** all (dialect differences: SQLite vs PostgreSQL)

**Evidence.** Code read in full; live information_schema column lists of combined_metrics vs combined_metrics_hourly; sim1.py hourly row shows cpu_usage_avg 166 for a true 166.67.

**Fix.** Aggregate estimation_reasons as the sorted union of reasons (json array) per hour; add model_version ('mixed' rule); add the missing storage columns (AVG/MAX like storage_*), store grid intensity as SUM(co2e)/SUM(joules*pue) derived or energy-weighted and keep MIN/MAX of grid_intensity_timestamp; use ROUND() on both dialects; implement a deterministic mode on SQLite (subquery ordered by count desc, value) or pick the value of the latest sample on both; store usage averages as REAL. Document every hourly column's aggregation rule in docs/architecture.md.

**Acceptance.** Compression of the same synthetic hour on SQLite and PostgreSQL yields identical rows (all columns); an hour with reasons ['a'] and ['b'] yields ['a','b']; SUM(co2e)/SUM(joules) reproduces the stored hourly intensity.

#### ROL-13 · Dashboard caches can serve stale data without saying so: the time-series cache keeps old buckets when a window becomes empty, is replaced without a transaction, and KPI windows are up to one hour old and end at the refresh time, not at 'now'.

**Severity** low · **Kind** ux · **Confidence** confirmed-code · **Effort** S

**Where.** `src/greenkube/storage/postgres/timeseries_cache_repository.py:22-46`; `src/greenkube/storage/sqlite/timeseries_cache_repository.py:36-52`; `src/greenkube/core/summary_refresher.py:90-146`; `src/greenkube/cli/start.py:796-802`; `src/greenkube/core/metrics_compressor.py:621-648`

**Mechanism.** upsert_points returns immediately when the new point list is empty, so the previous buckets of that (window, namespace) stay in metrics_timeseries_cache forever (collection stopped for more than the window, or a namespace deleted: namespace_cache rows are only ever inserted/updated, never removed, and the refresher keeps iterating over them). The DELETE and the INSERTs run outside a transaction on PostgreSQL, so a concurrent read can see an empty or partial series. The refresher runs every 3600 s; rows carry updated_at but windows are 'refresh time - delta .. refresh time'.

**Effect on numbers.** Live (minikube): metrics_summary refreshed at 09:24:06, read at 10:09: the '1h' KPI describes 08:24-09:24, and the all-time total is 0.734 against 0.837 in the tables (12% behind on a 13 h history). At refresh time the values matched direct SQL except for the last collection step not yet written (0.130048 vs 0.132319 for 1 h, 1.7%). A namespace deleted a month ago still shows its last 1h/6h/24h chart.

**Affected environments.** all

**Evidence.** Live SQL on both clusters (metrics_summary vs SUM over combined_metrics at updated_at and at now()); code read for the empty-list return and missing transaction.

**Fix.** upsert_points must take (window_slug, namespace, points) and delete the existing rows even when points is empty, inside one transaction; prune namespace_cache entries not seen for longer than the longest window and delete their cache rows; end KPI windows at the collection watermark (not wall clock) and store window_start/window_end in metrics_summary; return them and updated_at in the API and show 'as of' in the UI; refresh the 1h/6h/24h windows after each collection cycle (they only touch raw rows) and keep the hourly cadence for long windows.

**Acceptance.** 1) After collection stops for 2 h, the 1h time-series endpoint returns no points (or zeros) instead of the last cached hour. 2) A concurrent reader never observes an empty series during refresh (transactional replace). 3) Summary rows expose window_end equal to the collection watermark and the UI displays it.

#### MEA-01 · A change verdict compares one baseline week with one post week, and the block bootstrap only sees within-week noise, so week-to-week variation yields false 'realized' (and false 'not_realized') verdicts far above the nominal 5%.

**Severity** high · **Kind** design · **Confidence** confirmed-sim · **Effort** L

**Where.** `src/greenkube/verify/statistics.py:66-106`; `src/greenkube/verify/statistics.py:109-122`; `src/greenkube/verify/counterfactual.py:64-98`; `src/greenkube/verify/strategy.py:336-393`; `src/greenkube/verify/regression.py:314-354`

**Mechanism.** With the default windows (7 days before, 7 days after) every hour of the week has exactly one baseline value (HourOfWeekCounterfactual.fit, counterfactual.py:70-73). The hourly saving is baseline(hour of week) - post(hour). moving_block_bootstrap (statistics.py:87-97) resamples 24-hour blocks of those 168 differences: `size = max(1, min(block, n - 1)); starts = n - size + 1` and takes the 5th/95th percentile of the resampled means. This interval only measures how much the difference varies from day to day INSIDE the pair of weeks. Anything that shifts the level of a whole week (traffic or replica level, a spot or reserved price move, usage-driven allocation, grid carbon intensity, a holiday) is a constant offset in all 168 differences, so it has zero bootstrap variance and is read as a certain effect. outcome_rule then returns `realized` as soon as `ci_low > 0` (statistics.py:118). Other weaknesses of the same routine: only 7 independent blocks, a percentile interval (no studentisation or bias correction), non-circular blocks (`rng.randrange(starts)`: the first and last 23 hours are under-sampled), and one test per quantity (allocated_cost, co2e_g, cost_cpu, cost_ram are each tested at 90% with no multiplicity control; the per-recommendation outcome is then taken from a component interval, service.py:1227-1236). The OLS path (regression.py) adds a baseline-residual bootstrap but has the same single-week structure.

**Effect on numbers.** Verdicts and the claimed saving. Simulation with the real moving_block_bootstrap + outcome_rule and a TRUE SAVING OF ZERO (400 trials each, 168 h + 168 h): independent hourly noise -> 11.5% 'realized' (nominal 5%); AR(1) 0.9 -> 14.8%; AR(1) 0.98 -> 32.0%; a week-level shift of 5% (sd) with 2% hourly noise -> 49.8% 'realized' and 50.2% 'not_realized', 0% inconclusive. Worked example: a service whose weekly cost level moves by +-5% between weeks and costs 1.00/h gets, half of the time, a 'verified' saving of about 0.04/h (350/yr) that the change did not produce; the other half a 'not_realized' (verification_status failed) it did not deserve. Workloads with fixed requests on fixed-price nodes are deterministic and are not affected; workloads whose per-replica cost or CO2e moves from week to week are.

**Affected environments.** All. Worst for HPA-scaled or usage-allocated workloads, spot/variable node prices, and every CO2e verdict (grid intensity changes weekly).

**Evidence.** audit-workspace/audit/work/measurement-verdict/sim_bootstrap.py (real greenkube.verify.statistics). Output: 'iid realized=11.5% not_realized=65.8%', 'ar1_0.9 realized=14.8%', 'ar1_0.98 realized=32.0%', 'week_level realized=49.8% not_realized=50.2% inconclusive=0.0%'.

**Fix.** 1) Make the interval reflect between-week uncertainty. Preferred: lengthen the default baseline to 28 days (CHANGE_MEASUREMENT_PRE_WINDOW_DAYS=28, keep 7 as the validated minimum) and estimate the counterfactual of each hour of the week from 4 observations; compute the standard error of the mean saving from the week-level means (a between-week variance term added to the block-bootstrap variance), and when fewer than 2 baseline weeks exist add a configured prior week-to-week coefficient of variation (for example 5% of the counterfactual, stored in counterfactual_params) instead of zero. 2) In statistics.py use a circular block bootstrap (wrap around the series) and a studentised or BCa interval; require at least 5 complete days in the post-window. 3) Verdict rule: `realized` needs ci_low > epsilon (see the noise-floor finding) and the between-week term. 4) Use a control: subtract the same-period change of an untouched reference (cluster-wide unit price, per-replica cost of unchanged workloads in the same namespace/pool) - a difference-in-differences - so a common level shift cancels. 5) Apply a multiplicity rule: the change verdict uses the primary quantity only; component and CO2e claims need their own interval AND the primary realized. 6) Record `interval_method`, `baseline_weeks` and `between_week_cv` in counterfactual_params and bump strategy version. No migration of stored measurements: keep them, flagged with their strategy_version; offer re-measurement for realized changes decided with version <= 2.2 that have a non-deterministic baseline.

**Acceptance.** Monte-Carlo test in the test-suite (seeded, 400 trials, true saving 0): false-realized rate <= 7% for iid, AR(1) 0.9 and a 5% week-level shift; power test: a true 30% saving with 5% week-level noise is realized in >= 90% of trials. A deterministic series (constant before, constant lower after) is still realized with CI width ~0.

#### MEA-02 · Price moves between the two windows are booked as savings (or erase them) whenever the change resized both CPU and memory and the nodes carry no pool label: the measurement values requests at the prices of each window instead of at constant prices.

**Severity** high · **Kind** design · **Confidence** confirmed-sim · **Effort** M

**Where.** `src/greenkube/verify/confounders.py:384-415`; `src/greenkube/verify/service.py:272-295`; `src/greenkube/verify/strategy.py:279-310`; `src/greenkube/verify/service.py:1422-1438`

**Mechanism.** The claimed quantity is the difference of trusted allocated cost (requests x unit price of each window). The only workload-level price control is ConfounderDetector._price_change: it compares the unit price of a resource only when 'the change did not touch' it (`abs(requested_after / requested_before - 1.0) > PRICE_CHANGE_TOLERANCE: continue`, confounders.py:393-400). A rightsizing change normally resizes CPU and memory together, so neither resource is a control. The pool-wide price card needs `node.node_pool` (service.py:286-295: `if not pools: return None`), which on-prem clusters (kubeadm, k3s, kind, minikube, bare metal) do not have. In that case no confounder is raised and the confidence stays 0.9. The baseline snapshot stores a `price_card` (service.py:663, 1422-1438) but nothing reads it.

**Effect on numbers.** allocated_cost saving, claimed_annualized, ledger accrual, projection_accuracy. Simulation (real strategy + detector): true change 1.0->0.5 core and 2->1 GiB at 0.03/core-h and 0.005/GiB-h saves 0.0200/h = 175.32/yr. Same change with node prices -30% after the change: realized, 0.0260/h = 227.92/yr (+30%), no confounder, confidence 0.9. With prices +30%: realized 122.72/yr (-30%). Direction: follows the price move; size: (price change) x (post-change cost).

**Affected environments.** All clusters without node-pool labels (on-prem, kind, minikube, k3s) and any cluster for changes that touch both resources when the pool check cannot run; spot, reserved-instance or OpenCost custom-pricing edits trigger it.

**Evidence.** audit-workspace/audit/work/measurement-verdict/sim_measure.py section C: 'C1 ... outcome=realized saving/h=0.026000 annualized=227.92 confounders=[] confidence=0.9', 'C2 ... annualized=122.72 confounders=[]'; C3 shows the control only fires when requests are (almost) unchanged.

**Fix.** Measure the request effect at constant prices and report the price effect separately. In RequestRightsizingStrategy: (a) compute the saving as (baseline per-replica core_hours - post per-replica core_hours) x baseline cpu unit price + the same for memory, x post replica-hours, using the unit prices of the frozen snapshot price_card (already stored); (b) keep the observed cost delta as a second, reported quantity `allocated_cost_observed`; (c) record `price_effect = observed - constant_price` in counterfactual_params and raise `price_card_change` (moderate above 5%, severe above 20%) from the ratio post unit price / baseline unit price computed on the workload's OWN rows as cost_cpu/core_hours - this works for a resized resource because it is a price per requested unit, unless the allocator charges max(request, usage): in that case use cluster-wide rows. (d) When no pool label exists, build the price control from all pods on the nodes the workload ran on (same code as pool_unit_prices, keyed by node instead of pool) rather than skipping. Docs: savings-measurement.md sections 3 and 6. No backfill; bump strategy version.

**Acceptance.** sim_measure.py C1/C2 after the fix: claimed saving 175.32/yr +-1% in both cases, a price_card_change confounder present with the +-30% deviation, confidence reduced or outcome contaminated above 20%. C0 unchanged.

#### MEA-03 · The measured CO2e saving is the raw difference of two weeks' emissions with no normalisation for grid carbon intensity (or PUE), so a windier or calmer post week is booked as a carbon saving or loss of the change.

**Severity** high · **Kind** design · **Confidence** confirmed-sim · **Effort** M

**Where.** `src/greenkube/verify/strategy.py:271-277`; `src/greenkube/verify/series.py:149-151`; `src/greenkube/verify/series.py:220-226`; `src/greenkube/verify/confounders.py:228-256`; `src/greenkube/verify/service.py:1180-1186`

**Mechanism.** co2e_g per hour is `sum(co2e_grams)` of the rows (series.py:150) = energy x PUE x grid intensity of that hour. The strategy fits the same hour-of-week counterfactual on it (strategy.py:275-276) and claims the difference (`QuantitySpec('co2e_g', 'gCO2e', True, ...)`). No confounder looks at intensity, PUE, emaps zone or intensity source between the windows (ConfounderDetector.detect has price, replica, instance-type and pool checks only), and the snapshot keeps only the dominant `intensity_source` string. The claimed value becomes `measured_co2e_saved_grams` of the recommendation (service.py:1180-1186) and feeds the ledger.

**Effect on numbers.** co2e_g saving, claimed CO2e, ledger gCO2e. Simulation: a real cut from 0.050 to 0.040 kWh/h at 300 g/kWh saves 3.0 g/h (26.3 kg/yr). If the post week averages 200 g/kWh the measurement returns 7.0 g/h (61.4 kg/yr, 2.3x) 'realized'; at 400 g/kWh it returns -1.0 g/h 'not_realized'. Weekly mean intensity commonly moves 20-40% in wind/solar-heavy zones.

**Affected environments.** Every install with measured intensity (Electricity Maps token; realtime/consolidated). Without a token carbon is untrusted and the CO2e verdict is inconclusive.

**Evidence.** audit-workspace/audit/work/measurement-verdict/sim_co2e.py: 'G0 ... saving=3.000 g/h annualized=26.3 kg', 'G1 ... realized saving=7.000 g/h annualized=61.4 kg confounders=[]', 'G2 ... not_realized saving=-1.000 g/h'.

**Fix.** Measure energy, value it at one intensity. (1) Add `energy_kwh` (already in HourBucket) as a measured quantity with its own counterfactual. (2) Define the claimed CO2e saving as sum over post hours of (counterfactual_energy(t) - actual_energy(t)) x PUE(t) x intensity(t) using the POST hour's intensity for both terms (what the grid actually emitted for the avoided energy), plus the embodied delta. This requires storing per-hour `grid_intensity` and `pue` (energy-weighted means) in HourBucket for both windows (add optional fields; keep them out of the snapshot digest when unset, like _OPTIONAL_BUCKET_FIELDS). (3) Add an `intensity_shift` confounder (moderate above 10%, reported with before/after means) and record `co2e_basis: energy_delta_at_post_intensity`. (4) Snapshots frozen before the fix have no intensity: fall back to the current method and flag `co2e_basis: raw_delta` with a moderate confounder. Update savings-measurement.md section 3.

**Acceptance.** sim_co2e.py G1 and G2 return the energy saving valued at the post intensity (0.010 kWh/h x 200 = 2.0 g/h and x 400 = 4.0 g/h), both realized, with an intensity_shift confounder; G0 unchanged at 3.0 g/h.

#### MEA-04 · Hour buckets built from hourly rollup rows (all production data older than 24 h) take the longest single pod as the covered time, so any hour in which pods were replaced gets an overstated cost rate and replica count, or is dropped as under-covered.

**Severity** high · **Kind** bug · **Confidence** confirmed-sim · **Effort** M

**Where.** `src/greenkube/verify/series.py:121-141`; `src/greenkube/verify/series.py:165-181`; `src/greenkube/storage/base_repository.py:299-342`; `src/greenkube/core/metrics_compressor.py:104-126`; `src/greenkube/core/optimization/verifier.py:253-264`

**Mechanism.** ChangeMeasurementService._read_workload calls read_combined_metrics_smart, which serves everything older than now - METRICS_COMPRESSION_AGE_HOURS (24 h) from combined_metrics_hourly (base_repository.py:318-342). A 7-day baseline is therefore 6 days of rollup rows, and a post-window decided at its end is rollup rows for all but the last day. A rollup row is one row per pod and hour with `timestamp = hour_bucket`, `sample_count = COUNT(*)`, `duration_seconds = SUM(...)`. hourly_buckets groups rows by timestamp and assumes one group = one sample time: `coverage = max(_coverage_seconds(row, step) for row in members)` (series.py:124), then `acc.cost += sum(total_cost)`, `acc.cost_seconds += coverage`, `replicas = pod_seconds / covered`. For raw rows that is right (replicas of one instant). For rollup rows the pods of one hour are not simultaneous: pod A for the first 30 minutes and pod B for the last 30 give coverage = 1800 s, cost rate = (costA + costB) / 1800 x 3600 = 2x, replicas = 2, coverage 0.5.

**Effect on numbers.** Hourly cost, cost_cpu, cost_ram, co2e_g, core_hours, gib_hours and replicas of the bucket; hence the workload-total saving, the replica multiplier of the per-replica saving (strategy.py:381-384), coverage and the verdict. Simulation, truth 0.012/h and 1 replica: raw rows -> 0.012, 1.0 replica, coverage 1.0; rollup rows, pod replaced at minute 30 -> 0.024/h, 2.0 replicas, coverage 0.5; replaced at minute 20 -> 0.018/h, 1.5 replicas; six sequential 10-minute pods -> 0.072/h (6x), 6 replicas, coverage 0.167 (< 0.5: the hour is discarded). Stable Deployments are affected only in hours with a rollout, eviction or node drain; workloads with routine pod churn (Jobs/CronJobs, HPA scale events, spot nodes, crash loops) get systematically inflated rates and replica-hours in both windows, so the claimed saving scales with the churn (2x when a pod is replaced every hour), or lose so many hours that the change is inconclusive. The same change measured before and after compression gives different numbers (non-reproducible).

**Affected environments.** All production installs (default compression age 24 h, windows of 7 days). Not visible in the accelerated e2e clusters (no rollup rows yet: combined_metrics_hourly is empty on both).

**Evidence.** audit-workspace/audit/work/measurement-verdict/sim_measure.py section A (real hourly_buckets on CombinedMetric rows shaped as MetricsCompressor.aggregate_columns writes them): A1 raw 0.012/1.0/1.000; A2 0.024/2.0/0.500; A3 0.018/1.5/0.667; A4 0.072/6.0/0.167. Live: `select count(*) from combined_metrics_hourly` = 0 on minikube and kind, so this path has never run end to end.

**Fix.** In hourly_buckets distinguish rollup rows from raw rows (rollup: sample_count > 1 or duration_seconds > step, or better an explicit `period`/`is_rollup` attribute set by read_hourly_metrics). For rollup rows of an hour: covered time = min(3600, the collection coverage of the hour) - take it from the cluster-level collection coverage of that hour when available (number of collected steps x step, e.g. from collection_watermarks or max over ALL pods of the namespace), otherwise min(3600, sum of pod coverage) capped by the longest; cost = sum of pod costs / covered; replicas = sum(pod coverage) / covered; trusted-cost seconds = covered when every pod row is trusted. Simplest robust variant: the compressor also writes a workload-level hourly table (namespace, owner, hour: sum cost, pod_seconds, covered_seconds = COUNT(DISTINCT timestamp) x step, trusted_seconds) computed from raw rows, and the measurement reads that table for old hours. Add a parity test: buckets from raw rows == buckets from their rollup for churn scenarios. Already frozen snapshots and decided measurements cannot be recomputed after raw pruning (7 days): flag measurements with strategy version <= 2.2 whose buckets came from rollup rows and had replicas above the workload's desired replicas.

**Acceptance.** For scenarios A2, A3, A4 of sim_measure.py (and a 3-replica workload with one pod replaced mid-hour) the bucket from rollup rows equals the bucket from the raw rows within 1%: cost 0.012/h, replicas 1.0, coverage 1.0. An end-to-end test runs MetricsCompressor on SQLite, then _build_snapshot, and compares with the snapshot built before compression.

#### MEA-05 · One untrusted cost sample makes the whole pod-hour untrusted once the hour is compressed (cost_source becomes 'mixed'), so measurements read from hourly rows lose every such hour; on the live clusters 24% (kind) and 40% (minikube) of pod-hours contain at least one such sample.

**Severity** high · **Kind** data-integrity · **Confidence** confirmed-sim · **Effort** M

**Where.** `src/greenkube/models/provenance.py:212-222`; `src/greenkube/models/provenance.py:111-136`; `src/greenkube/core/metrics_compressor.py:139`; `src/greenkube/verify/series.py:139-141`; `src/greenkube/verify/strategy.py:388-390`

**Mechanism.** Raw rows: a sample time counts for cost when every replica row at that time is trusted (series.py:139), so one custom_rate sample out of 12 only removes 5 minutes. The compressor aggregates cost_source with cost_source_aggregate_sql: opencost + opencost_derived stays trusted, anything else in the hour gives 'mixed' (provenance.py:216-221), which is_cost_trusted rejects. hourly_buckets then finds `all(cost_is_trusted(row))` false for the single group of that hour: cost = None, cost_coverage = 0, the hour is not in the series. In addition any untrusted pod row (for example the last samples of a terminating pod, stored with custom_rate) makes the whole workload-hour untrusted. Coverage = aligned hours / post hours must reach 0.8, otherwise the change is inconclusive.

**Effect on numbers.** Coverage, confidence and the verdict; also the mean (hours are removed non-randomly: rollout, restart and OpenCost-gap hours). Simulation: 1 custom_rate sample of 12 -> raw bucket cost 0.012/h, cost_coverage 0.917; the rollup row of the same hour -> cost None, cost_coverage 0. Live (read-only SQL): kind 118 of 488 pod-hours contain an untrusted sample although only 270 of 26,594 samples (1.0%) are custom_rate; minikube 271 of 670 pod-hours (104 partially, 167 fully). With 24% of hours lost a 7-day measurement has coverage <= 0.76 < 0.8: inconclusive, whatever the saving.

**Affected environments.** All production installs once data is older than 24 h; worse where OpenCost has gaps or lags (the newest samples of each new pod are custom_rate on both live clusters).

**Evidence.** audit-workspace/audit/work/measurement-verdict/sim_measure.py section B: 'B1 raw ... cost/h=0.012 cost_cov=0.917', 'B2 hourly rollup (mixed) cost/h=None cost_cov=0.000'. Live query: `with h as (select namespace,pod_name,date_trunc('hour',timestamp) hr,count(*) n,count(*) filter (where cost_source not in ('opencost','opencost_derived','kubecost','pricing_catalog')) bad from combined_metrics group by 1,2,3) select count(*), count(*) filter (where bad>0), count(*) filter (where bad=n) from h` -> minikube 670 | 271 | 167, kind 488 | 118 | 5.

**Fix.** Keep the trusted part of an hour through compression. Add to combined_metrics_hourly (and container_metrics_hourly) the columns trusted_cost, trusted_cost_cpu, trusted_cost_ram, trusted_cost_seconds (SUM over raw rows whose cost_source is trusted and whose cost passes is_cost_trusted) next to the totals; map them in read_hourly_metrics; in hourly_buckets use them for rollup rows (cost rate = trusted_cost / trusted_cost_seconds, cost_coverage = trusted seconds / 3600). Migration: new nullable columns; backfill from raw rows for the hours still raw (7 days); older hours keep NULL and fall back to today's rule. Separately, restate custom_rate samples when OpenCost data arrives late (cost auditor's scope) so the share of untrusted samples drops. Expose the per-change share of untrusted hours in the measurement (`untrusted_cost_hours`) so an inconclusive verdict says why.

**Acceptance.** Compress an hour with 11 opencost samples and 1 custom_rate sample: the bucket built from the rollup has cost equal to the raw-row bucket (0.012/h) and cost_coverage 0.917. A 7-day window with one untrusted sample in 30% of its hours keeps coverage >= 0.97.

#### MEA-06 · Without OpenCost or Kubecost no change can ever get a cost verdict: rows priced with configured rates (custom_rate) or the default are never trusted, so every on-prem measurement ends 'inconclusive' with the reason 'no trusted allocated cost in the baseline window'.

**Severity** high · **Kind** portability · **Confidence** confirmed-code · **Effort** M

**Where.** `src/greenkube/core/optimization/verifier.py:103`; `src/greenkube/models/provenance.py:111-124`; `src/greenkube/verify/series.py:139-141`; `src/greenkube/verify/strategy.py:327-335`; `src/greenkube/verify/deletion.py:72-74`

**Mechanism.** TRUSTED_COST_SOURCES = {opencost, opencost_derived, kubecost, pricing_catalog}. A cluster without OpenCost writes cost_source custom_rate (configured rates) or none. hourly_buckets only accumulates cost for trusted groups, so both series are empty and _fit_basis returns inconclusive (strategy.py:327-335). The same rule blocks deletions (is_trusted_rate_source). The Prove step and the ledger therefore never record anything measured on-prem unless OpenCost is installed, even though a request cut valued at the operator's own configured rate is a well-defined allocated saving. The live clusters show the mechanism on single samples: the e2e-oom baseline hour has coverage 0.65 but cost_coverage 0.358 because its custom_rate samples are discarded.

**Effect on numbers.** All measured cost savings and ledger entries: none are produced (verdict inconclusive, confidence 0) in the on-prem-without-OpenCost configuration the product promises to support. requested core-hours/GiB-hours freed are measured but not surfaced as a verdict.

**Affected environments.** minikube, kind, kubeadm, k3s, bare metal and any cloud cluster without OpenCost/Kubecost.

**Evidence.** Code path above; live minikube snapshot of change 4495eb2f...: hour 21:00 coverage 0.65, cost_coverage 0.358 (custom_rate samples dropped); live cost_source distribution minikube: custom_rate 10,905 of 43,692 rows.

**Fix.** Decide the verdict on the physical quantity and value it with a declared price basis. (1) Make requested capacity (core_hours, gib_hours per replica) a first-class, always-available verdict: `realized` when the per-replica request reduction is observed over the post-window. (2) Value it at constant unit prices with an explicit `price_basis`: opencost | kubecost | pricing_catalog | configured_rate. Treat configured rates as trusted for a measurement labelled `price_basis=configured_rate` (new CostSource handling: allowed set extended for measurements, kept out of 'billed' claims), and show the basis in API/UI/ledger (quantity `measured_allocated`, attribute price_basis). (3) Keep `none`/default-priced rows untrusted, and say so in the reason ('no cost source: install OpenCost or configure rates'). Docs: savings-measurement.md section 3 and configuration.md.

**Acceptance.** On a SQLite fixture with 14 days of rows priced only with cost_source=custom_rate and a request cut at day 7, run_due decides `realized` with price_basis=configured_rate, saving = delta requests x configured rates +-1%; with cost_source=none it is inconclusive with the explicit reason.

#### MEA-07 · Health gates of the final decision have no baseline comparison and a zero restart tolerance over the whole 7-day post-window, so one unrelated container restart or pre-existing CPU throttling sends a correctly realized change to rollback_review and stops its savings.

**Severity** high · **Kind** design · **Confidence** confirmed-code · **Effort** M

**Where.** `src/greenkube/core/optimization/verifier.py:1042-1144`; `src/greenkube/core/optimization/verifier.py:362-385`; `src/greenkube/verify/service.py:1022-1027`; `src/greenkube/verify/service.py:1241-1243`; `src/greenkube/core/config.py:498-502`; `src/greenkube/cli/start.py:384-399`

**Mechanism.** _measure evaluates the gates over [post.start, post.end) = 7 days for all pods of the workload (service.py:1023-1027). `restarts`: restart_increase sums the per-pod increases of the restart counter over the window and fails when it exceeds VERIFICATION_MAX_RESTART_DELTA = 0, i.e. any single restart of any replica in a week. `cpu_throttling`: fails above 5% of CFS periods, an absolute level that depends on the CPU limit (which a request change does not touch) and is common before any change. `readiness` < 0.99 likewise. None is compared with the same signal in the baseline window. A failed gate overrides the outcome: `outcome = MeasurementOutcome.ROLLBACK_REVIEW` (service.py:1241-1243), and the lifecycle job then supersedes the recommendation's savings (start.py:384-399). The thresholds were designed for the 72-hour interim verifier and are now applied to a window 2.3x longer.

**Effect on numbers.** Verdict (rollback_review instead of verified), realized totals and ledger accrual (stopped). Example: a 10-replica Deployment whose containers restart once a month each (liveness blips) has P(at least one restart in 7 days) = 1 - exp(-10 x 7/30) = 90%: nine out of ten correct rightsizing changes on it are flagged for rollback. A workload throttled 8% before and after a memory-only change is flagged although nothing changed.

**Affected environments.** All; grows with replica count and window length.

**Evidence.** Code path above. Live measurements show the gates evaluated with threshold 0 for restarts ('restarts ... threshold 0.0') over the whole post-window.

**Fix.** Compare with the baseline. (1) Freeze the same health signals for the pre-window in the baseline snapshot (restart rate per replica-day, OOM kills, throttling ratio, readiness) - WorkloadHealthCollector.collect over plan.pre at snapshot time. (2) Gates fail on a significant worsening, not on an absolute level: restarts per replica-day in the post-window above the baseline rate with a Poisson test (p <= 0.05) and at least max(1, MAX_RESTART_DELTA) more than expected; throttling ratio above baseline + 0.05 absolute; readiness below baseline - 0.01. Keep the absolute rule only when the baseline signal is missing, labelled `baseline_missing`. (3) Scale the restart tolerance with replica-days. (4) Record both values (`value`, `baseline_value`) in HealthGateResult. Update docs section 7 and Helm values descriptions (verification.*).

**Acceptance.** Fixture: baseline 3 restarts/week and 8% throttling, post 3 restarts and 8% -> all gates passed, outcome realized. Baseline 0 restarts, post 6 restarts on 1 replica -> restarts gate failed -> rollback_review. Baseline missing -> current behaviour with detail 'baseline_missing'.

#### MEA-08 · Any pod-template change that leaves the requests untouched (an image deployment, an env change, a rollout restart) contaminates a running measurement and, once the change is realized, closes it and marks its recommendations 'regressed', so savings on actively deployed workloads are never verified or stop accruing at the next release.

**Severity** high · **Kind** design · **Confidence** confirmed-sim · **Effort** M

**Where.** `src/greenkube/verify/digest.py:53-78`; `src/greenkube/verify/confounders.py:321-339`; `src/greenkube/verify/drift.py:259-290`; `src/greenkube/verify/drift.py:362-374`; `src/greenkube/verify/drift.py:492-536`; `src/greenkube/verify/drift.py:659-667`

**Mechanism.** The change is identified by pod_template_digest: SHA-256 of the whole normalised pod template ('an image bump, an environment change or a kubectl rollout restart annotation is a different spec', digest.py:13-14). (1) During the measurement, ConfounderDetector._spec_changes raises `spec_change` with severity severe for the first observation after the rollout whose digest differs from spec_digest_after (confounders.py:321-339): the outcome becomes contaminated and the recommendations inconclusive. (2) After a realized verdict, DriftMonitor._watch calls detect_spec_drift; `_differs` returns 'spec_drift' on any digest difference; two hours later _close_on_spec_drift moves the change realized -> closed with accrual_end at the first differing observation and _end_members marks every verified recommendation regressed ('the measured saving no longer describes what runs'). Neither check looks at whether the resource requests of the measured containers changed, which is the only thing the measured saving depends on (allocated cost = requests x unit price).

**Effect on numbers.** Verdicts, realized totals and ledger accrual. A team that ships an image once a week or more: every 7-day post-window contains a deployment -> contaminated -> never verified. A team that ships every two weeks: a change realized on day 8 is closed at the next release, e.g. day 12: the ledger keeps about 11 days of a saving that actually persists all year (97% of the annual saving is not booked) and the recommendation shows 'regressed'. Direction: realized savings understated, regression counts overstated.

**Affected environments.** All; every workload under continuous delivery (the workloads most worth rightsizing).

**Evidence.** audit-workspace/audit/work/measurement-verdict/sim_specdrift.py (real digest, detector and detect_spec_drift): 'same requests, image 1.4.0 vs 1.4.1 -> digests equal? False'; 'image deployed on day 3 of the post-window -> [(spec_change, severe)] contaminated: True'; 'image deployed on day 12 -> spec_drift | the pod template no longer matches the measured change | accrual_end 2026-11-21T10:00'.

**Fix.** Track what the measurement depends on. (1) Add a second digest to WorkloadSpecObservation and ChangeEvent: `resources_digest` = SHA-256 of the per-container (and init/sidecar) requests and limits, plus replicas-relevant fields if desired (migration: nullable column on workload_spec_observations and change_events; computed by the applied detector next to spec_digest). (2) Confounders: `spec_change` is severe only when resources_digest differs from the change's; a template change with identical resources is `template_change` with severity mild (or moderate when the image changed, because usage may move). (3) Drift: detect_spec_drift closes the change only when resources_digest differs (requests reverted or changed) or the workload UID changed AND the new object's resources differ; an image-only change records an informational event. (4) For observations stored before the migration (resources_digest NULL) read the live requests from the workload snapshot before closing. (5) Re-open changes closed with event spec_drift whose current live requests still equal the applied ones (one-off repair job with a ledger restatement entry). Update savings-measurement.md sections 6 and 9.

**Acceptance.** sim_specdrift.py after the fix: day-3 image deployment -> confounder template_change (mild), not contaminated; day-12 image deployment -> no spec drift, change stays realized and keeps accruing; a deployment that restores the old CPU request -> spec_drift, closed, recommendations regressed.

#### MEA-09 · A saving is declared 'realized' as soon as its interval is above zero, without having to exceed the noise floor, while 'not_realized' uses the floor: a 0.3% drift below the 2% floor is a verified saving.

**Severity** medium · **Kind** bug · **Confidence** confirmed-sim · **Effort** S

**Where.** `src/greenkube/verify/statistics.py:109-122`; `src/greenkube/verify/strategy.py:387-393`

**Mechanism.** outcome_rule: `if ci_low > 0: return REALIZED; if ci_high <= epsilon: return NOT_REALIZED` with epsilon = CHANGE_MEASUREMENT_EPSILON_RATIO (0.02) x |counterfactual|. The REALIZED branch is tested first and ignores epsilon. Allocated cost of a fixed-request workload is deterministic, so the interval has near-zero width and any positive difference, however small (rounding of OpenCost prices, a price refresh, a token request change), is 'realized'. The interim verifier required `delta > MIN_RELATIVE_CHANGE * abs(before)` (verifier.py:1493); the new rule lost it.

**Effect on numbers.** Verdict counts ('verified'), verification_status and the recommendation state; the claimed amount is small. Simulation: requests -0.3% on both resources -> 'realized', saving 0.00012/h (1.05/yr), CI [0.00012, 0.00012], epsilon would be 0.0008/h; +0.3% -> 'not_realized'.

**Affected environments.** All.

**Evidence.** audit-workspace/audit/work/measurement-verdict/sim_measure.py section D: 'D1 ... outcome=realized saving/h=0.000120 annualized=1.05', 'D2 ... outcome=not_realized'.

**Fix.** outcome_rule(ci_low, ci_high, epsilon): REALIZED when ci_low > epsilon; NOT_REALIZED when ci_high <= epsilon; INCONCLUSIVE otherwise (interval straddles epsilon). Update _reason texts ('is above the noise floor') and docs section 5. Add a 'negligible' note when 0 < ci_low <= epsilon. Stored measurements are immutable: no migration.

**Acceptance.** outcome_rule(0.0001, 0.0001, 0.0008) -> not_realized (the whole interval is below the floor); outcome_rule(0.0005, 0.002, 0.0008) -> inconclusive; outcome_rule(0.02, 0.02, 0.0008) -> realized; sim D1 is no longer realized.

#### MEA-10 · The accelerated end-to-end settings can never produce a decided verdict, so the statistics, attribution, drift and ledger-accrual code of the Prove step has never run against a real cluster; all 5 live changes are inconclusive for that reason and the stored reason text misnames the cause.

**Severity** medium · **Kind** test-gap · **Confidence** confirmed-live · **Effort** M

**Where.** `src/greenkube/verify/series.py:41-51`; `src/greenkube/verify/series.py:207`; `src/greenkube/verify/series.py:229-239`; `src/greenkube/verify/counterfactual.py:84-98`; `src/greenkube/verify/strategy.py:327-335`; `src/greenkube/core/config.py:509-526`; `src/greenkube/core/config.py:1030-1045`

**Mechanism.** Buckets are UTC clock hours; an hour counts for a quantity only when >= 50% of it has qualifying data (MIN_HOUR_COVERAGE), and a post hour is aligned only when the baseline holds the same hour of the week. With the live settings (pre 0.25 d, post 0.025-0.05 d, warm-up 0.05 h) the baseline holds six consecutive hours of one day and the post-window the next one or two hours: no hour of the week can match, so the result is inconclusive by construction (the methodology doc admits 'usually inconclusive'). Config validation only bounds the PRE window (config.py:1030-1040); the post-window has no minimum, so 36 minutes is accepted although no clock hour can reach 50% coverage unless the window happens to straddle it by 30 minutes. Hand recomputation of the live measurements: minikube oversized-worker (change 0335c712): the only baseline hour with data, 21:00, has 59 samples x 30 s = 1770 s = 0.4917 coverage (< 0.5) -> excluded -> 'no trusted allocated cost in the baseline window'; recomputed cost (0.0014935 + 0.0001338) / 1770 x 3600 = 0.0033098/h and core_hours 17 x 0.3 / 59 = 0.0864, both equal to the stored snapshot. kind oversized-worker (change 5ee15bd2): baseline hour 21:00 has 28 samples = 840 s = 0.2333 coverage; cost 28 x 8.848e-05 / 840 x 3600 = 0.010618/h vs stored 0.0106114. In both cases OpenCost cost WAS trusted; the hour was dropped for coverage, but the reason says 'no trusted allocated cost' (and even 'no trusted requested CPU cores').

**Effect on numbers.** No wrong amount is produced, but every e2e verdict is inconclusive with confidence 0: the realized/not_realized paths, component attribution, projection_accuracy, drift monitoring and measured ledger accrual are unexercised outside unit tests. By hand the kind change saved 0.0106 - 0.00038 = 0.0102/h.

**Affected environments.** Accelerated/quickstart installs (CHANGE_MEASUREMENT_ACCELERATED) and any install younger than the windows.

**Evidence.** Live SQL on change_events, change_states, baseline_snapshots, change_measurements, combined_metrics of both clusters (dumps in the work directory: live_minikube_*.txt, live_kind_*.txt); recomputation above.

**Fix.** (1) Give accelerated mode its own time grid: when CHANGE_MEASUREMENT_ACCELERATED is on, bucket by the collection step grouped into N-minute slots and use a flat (non-seasonal) counterfactual = baseline mean, labelled `model: flat_mean_accelerated`, so a 30-minute baseline and post-window give a decided outcome and exercise the whole chain; mark such measurements `representative: false` and keep them out of realized totals unless a demo flag allows. (2) Validate CHANGE_MEASUREMENT_POST_WINDOW_DAYS >= 1 (>= 2 clock hours in accelerated mode) in config.py. (3) Make reasons truthful: distinguish 'no rows', 'rows but untrusted cost', 'hours below 50% coverage (n hours, best coverage x%)', 'no post hour matches a baseline hour of the week'; do not say 'trusted' for request quantities. (4) Add a nightly e2e with real 7+7 day data or a time-shifted fixture database replayed through run_due.

**Acceptance.** On the accelerated e2e cluster the oversized-worker change is decided `realized` with a saving within 5% of the hand value 0.0102/h (kind) and a non-zero confidence; an install with POST_WINDOW_DAYS=0.025 without the accelerated flag fails config validation.

#### MEA-11 · The OOM-kill baseline rate of a reliability increase divides kills counted over the whole scan window by an exposure reconstructed from the number of usage samples, which is tiny for a crash-looping container: live rates of 11,527 and 2,058 kills/day come from 28 and 5 kills over 58 minutes divided by 3.5 minutes.

**Severity** medium · **Kind** bug · **Confidence** confirmed-live · **Effort** S

**Where.** `src/greenkube/core/optimization/evidence.py:357-398`; `src/greenkube/verify/strategy.py:731-753`; `src/greenkube/verify/strategy.py:570-629`; `src/greenkube/core/reliability_signals.py:167-195`

**Mechanism.** evidence.oom_events is read over the scan lookback for all pods matched to the target (reliability_signals.py: `increase(...)[window]`, summed per target). first_observed_at is `min(max(end - step_count x step, first_hour), first_hour + 1h)` (evidence.py:380-388): step_count is the number of usage samples, and an OOM-looping container has few (7 samples = 210 s on the live recommendations 80 and 81, whose pods existed since 21:14 in a window 21:00-21:58). _baseline_exposure then uses end - first_observed_at = 210 s as the denominator (strategy.py:741-752). The numerator and denominator do not cover the same time. The error is bounded by one hour (the clamp), so it matters for workloads younger than a few hours, exactly the case of a new deployment that OOMs at once. No minimum exposure or minimum post-window is required before a rate per day and a p-value are published.

**Effect on numbers.** baseline_rate_per_day, p_value, the reliability outcome and the oom_kills gate. Live: e2e-oom 28 kills / 0.0583 h = 11,527/day; over the 43.6 minutes its pods existed in the window (21:14:30-21:58:08) the rate is 916/day (12.6x lower); e2e-oom-argo 5 / 0.0583 h = 2,058/day (164/day over the same 43.6 min). An overstated baseline rate turns a fix that did not work into a confirmed one. Run with the real oom_rate_outcome: 5 kills before, workload 60 minutes old with 6 minutes of usage samples, 120 kills in the 24 h after. Exposure as computed today (360 s): 'realized - OOM-kill rate dropped: 120/day after against 1.2e+03/day before (p=0.000191)', gate passed. True exposure (3600 s): 'not_realized - rate did not drop: 120/day against 120/day', gate failed -> rollback_review.

**Affected environments.** All; workloads observed for less than a few hours before the recommendation, and accelerated installs.

**Evidence.** Live: change_measurements.counterfactual_params->'reliability' on minikube: {baseline_events: 28, baseline_hours: 0.0583, baseline_rate_per_day: 11526.586621, post_hours: 0.6, p_value: 0.0} and {baseline_events: 5, baseline_hours: 0.0583, baseline_rate_per_day: 2058.319039}; recommendation_history 80/81 evidence: sample_count 7, first_observed_at 21:54:38, observation_window 21:00:00-21:58:08; combined_metrics shows e2e-oom pods from 21:14:30.

**Fix.** (1) Exposure = time the workload EXISTED in the evidence window, not time with usage samples: use the earliest of the pods' first row timestamps (any row, with or without usage) or kube_pod_start_time/`kube_pod_created`, bounded by the window; store it as evidence.exposure_seconds with its source. (2) Count kills and exposure over the same pods and the same interval. (3) Require a minimum exposure (for example 6 h before and 24 h after) before publishing a rate per day or a p-value; below it return inconclusive 'exposure too short' and show raw counts ('28 kills in 58 min'). (4) Never extrapolate a count to a daily rate in the UI without the exposure next to it.

**Acceptance.** Fixture reproducing recommendation 80 (28 kills, pods present 21:14-21:58, 7 usage samples): baseline_hours = 0.73 (44 min) and outcome inconclusive 'exposure too short'; with 3 days of exposure and 28 kills -> 9.3/day.

#### MEA-12 · Rows of dead or terminating pods that carry no owner and zero requests are attached to the workload by pod-name inference and enter the baseline, diluting requested capacity and adding phantom replicas (live: baseline CPU request 0.086 cores instead of 0.300).

**Severity** medium · **Kind** data-integrity · **Confidence** confirmed-live · **Effort** M

**Where.** `src/greenkube/verify/service.py:239-256`; `src/greenkube/core/optimization/targeting.py:11-36`; `src/greenkube/verify/series.py:126-130`; `src/greenkube/verify/confounders.py:97-103`

**Mechanism.** _read_workload keeps every row whose target_key equals the change target; target_key falls back to infer_deployment_target(pod_name) when owner_kind/owner_name are NULL. The collection path stores rows with NULL owner, cpu_request = 0 and memory_request = 0 for pods it could not describe: pods that existed only in the back-filled history and the last three samples of each terminating pod (all cost-bearing, often trusted). hourly_buckets adds them: `acc.cpu_request += cpu_request/1000 * seconds` with 0, `pod_seconds += seconds`. Requested core-hours and GiB-hours are averaged down, replicas go up while old and new pods overlap, and the per-replica request used by the price-card control (confounders.py:97-103, 391-398) is wrong. The Deployment name regex also attaches pods of a different workload whose name matches `<name>-<hash>-<5 chars>`.

**Effect on numbers.** core_hours, gib_hours, replicas, the snapshot price_card (cost / requested units) and the price-change control. Live minikube change 0335c712: 42 owner-less request-0 rows (21:14:30-21:35:00) + 17 real rows at 300m -> stored core_hours 0.0864 (true 0.300, 3.5x low), gib_hours 0.072 (true 0.25); live change 4495eb2f: replicas 1.013, 105bc5e6: 1.079 for single-replica workloads. In production the effect is concentrated in the first days after install (back-filled history) and in rollout hours.

**Affected environments.** All; strongest right after installation (history back-fill) and for workloads with pod churn.

**Evidence.** Live SQL: `select pod_name, owner_kind, owner_name, count(*), min(cpu_request), max(cpu_request) ... where namespace='greenkube-e2e-apps'` shows 266 rows with NULL owner and request 0 (e.g. oversized-worker-c7bb6894f-ggxgn 42 rows; oversized-worker-584885964c-s5s4g 3 rows after its termination); baseline_snapshots.hours of change 0335c712 (core_hours 0.08644).

**Fix.** In the measurement: exclude rows without resolved owner AND without requests from request quantities and from replicas (count them in a separate `unresolved_pod_seconds`; when it exceeds 5% of the hour, mark the hour's request quantities missing and add a mild confounder `unresolved_pods`). Prefer matching by workload UID/owner when the row has it; restrict name inference to rows whose ReplicaSet hash belongs to the workload's known ReplicaSets when available. Upstream (collection auditor's scope): fill owner and requests of historical pods from kube_pod_owner/kube_pod_container_resource_requests instead of writing 0, and keep the last known spec for terminating pods.

**Acceptance.** Rebuilding the snapshot of live change 0335c712 from the same rows gives core_hours 0.300 and gib_hours 0.25 for hour 21:00 and reports the 42 unresolved rows; a rollout hour of a 1-replica workload has replicas <= 1.05.

#### MEA-13 · Hours are rated per covered time and hours without rows are dropped, so a workload that runs part of each hour (batch, scale-to-zero) is annualised as if it ran continuously, and the projection it is compared with uses a different annualisation (observed replica-hours x 8760 vs rate x 8766).

**Severity** medium · **Kind** design · **Confidence** confirmed-sim · **Effort** M

**Where.** `src/greenkube/verify/series.py:165-181`; `src/greenkube/verify/series.py:229-239`; `src/greenkube/verify/strategy.py:42`; `src/greenkube/verify/strategy.py:394-412`; `src/greenkube/verify/service.py:1488`; `src/greenkube/verify/attribution.py:63-73`

**Mechanism.** `cost = acc.cost / acc.cost_seconds * 3600` extrapolates the covered part of an hour to the full hour. That is right for a collection gap and wrong when the pod was simply not running (no cost). The code cannot tell the two apart. An hour with >= 50% presence is kept at the full-hour rate; an hour with less, or with no pod, is removed from the series instead of counting as zero cost. The annualised saving is `interval.mean * HOURS_PER_YEAR` with 8766 h. The projection (impact.allocated_cost_annual) is built from observed replica-hours scaled to the year with annualization_factor 8760 and the presence observed in the lookback. projection_accuracy = annualized / projected therefore compares two different bases whenever the workload is not present 100% of the time or is young.

**Effect on numbers.** annualized, claimed_annualized, projection_accuracy. Simulation: a pod that runs 35 minutes of every hour at 0.001 per 5-minute sample costs 0.007 per clock hour; the bucket reports 0.012/h (1.71x). Live kind change 5ee15bd2: projected allocated saving 18.10 + 2.04 = 20.14/yr (capacity_released.replica_hours 1971 = 22.5% presence of a workload first seen 13 minutes before the scan), while the allocated rate difference recomputed by hand is (0.010618 - 0.000382) x 8766 = 89.7/yr: projection_accuracy would be 4.45 for a change that did exactly what was projected. The 8766/8760 mismatch alone is +0.07%.

**Affected environments.** All; batch/CronJob workloads, scale-to-zero (KEDA), HPA to zero, and young workloads.

**Evidence.** audit-workspace/audit/work/measurement-verdict/sim_measure.py section E: 'E1 raw, 7 samples then absent cost/h=0.012 coverage=0.583'. Live kind: recommendation_history ids 16, 18 impact JSON (allocated_cost_annual 18.1046 and 2.0367, replica_hours 1971.0, annualization_factor 8760) vs combined_metrics of oversized-worker.

**Fix.** (1) Separate 'no data' from 'not running': use the collection coverage of the hour (steps actually collected for the cluster, from collection_watermarks or any row of the namespace at that step). Hour cost = sum of cost over collected steps / collected seconds x 3600; steps collected with no pod of the workload count as zero cost and zero replicas. Drop an hour only when collection itself covered < 50%. (2) Use one annualisation constant everywhere (8760 or 365.25 x 24) shared by the Impact Model and verify. (3) Define projection_accuracy on the same basis: compare the measured per-replica-hour saving x the replica-hours assumed by the projection, or re-annualise the projection with the post-window presence; store both `projected_annual` and `projected_annual_at_measured_presence`.

**Acceptance.** sim E1 returns 0.007/h; a workload present 35 min/h with a 50% request cut gets annualized = 0.0035 x hours-per-year; live kind change: projection_accuracy between 0.9 and 1.1 once decided.

#### MEA-14 · A reliability increase that works is still reported as 'not_realized' (recommendation verification 'failed'), because the change outcome is the cost outcome and a request increase always costs more.

**Severity** medium · **Kind** ux · **Confidence** confirmed-code · **Effort** M

**Where.** `src/greenkube/verify/strategy.py:239-247`; `src/greenkube/verify/service.py:1033-1037`; `src/greenkube/verify/service.py:1223`; `src/greenkube/core/optimization/verifier.py:1413-1422`

**Mechanism.** RequestRightsizingStrategy.estimate sets `outcome = primary.outcome` where primary is allocated_cost. For a memory increase the saving is negative, ci_high <= epsilon, so the change is NOT_REALIZED. The reliability outcome (OOM rate dropped) is only appended to the reasons and to counterfactual_params.reliability (service.py:1033-1037). The recommendation is moved with mark_not_realized (verification_status failed). The doc states this on purpose ('The savings outcome of the change is unchanged'), but the user-facing verdict of a successful fix is a failure, and not_realized counters include it.

**Effect on numbers.** Verdict distribution: every applied increase counts as not_realized/failed; realized-rate KPIs are biased down; the negative saving is correct and should stay booked as a cost.

**Affected environments.** All installs that deliver increases (H1).

**Evidence.** Code path; the live increases (minikube changes 4495eb2f, 105bc5e6) show reliability outcome 'realized' next to a change outcome that can only be inconclusive or not_realized.

**Fix.** Add an `objective` to the change (savings | reliability) derived from is_reliability_increase of its members. For reliability changes the change outcome is the reliability outcome (realized when the OOM rate dropped and the gates pass), the cost is reported as `cost_of_fix` (signed, booked in the ledger as a negative measured_allocated entry), and the recommendation moves to verified with verification detail 'reliability'. API/UI: show 'Fix confirmed, costs +X/yr'. Mixed changes (a decrease and an increase): keep the savings objective, show both.

**Acceptance.** Fixture: memory increase with 12 OOM kills in 7 days before and 0 after -> change state realized, objective reliability, cost estimate negative and unclaimed as saving, recommendation verified; same with 12 kills after -> not_realized and rollback_review.

#### MEA-15 · A deletion is 'measured' by restating its projection: the pre-window cost of a Service or PersistentVolume cannot be read from pod metrics, so the realized amount, its zero-width interval and coverage 1.0 are the projected rate, and absence is checked once and never again.

**Severity** medium · **Kind** design · **Confidence** confirmed-code · **Effort** M

**Where.** `src/greenkube/verify/deletion.py:96-181`; `src/greenkube/verify/service.py:239-256`; `src/greenkube/verify/service.py:497-501`; `src/greenkube/verify/drift.py:800-826`

**Mechanism.** The deletion target is WorkloadRef(namespace, kind=Service|PersistentVolume, name). _read_workload filters combined_metrics rows (pods) with target_key(row) == target.key; a pod row resolves to its owner, an inferred Deployment or ('Pod', pod_name), never to a Service or PersistentVolume (and PVs have an empty namespace). pre_costs and observed_after are therefore always empty: the 'pre_window_mean' method and the 'still observed in n post-change hours' check are unreachable. The estimate becomes rate = projected / HOURS_PER_YEAR with ci_low = ci_high = rate, coverage 1.0, aligned_hours = len(post), projection_accuracy 1.0 (deletion.py:155-173). Absence is the detector's single observation (rollout_completed_source == 'resource_absent'); the drift monitor's probe only knows Deployment/StatefulSet/DaemonSet (drift.py:810-817), so a LoadBalancer recreated by GitOps drift or an operator keeps accruing 'measured' savings.

**Effect on numbers.** Deletion savings shown as measured (confidence 0.9, CI width 0, accuracy 100%) equal the projection by construction; if the resource is recreated after detection the accrual continues indefinitely (e.g. 18/month for a cloud LoadBalancer).

**Affected environments.** All clusters where orphan LoadBalancer / PV deletions are delivered (clouds mainly).

**Evidence.** Code path end to end (deletion.py, service.py _read_workload, targeting.target_key, drift.kubernetes_workload_probe). Not exercised live (no deletion change on either cluster).

**Fix.** (1) Label the result honestly: method `allocated_rate_at_decision` is a `projected_released_rate`, not a measurement: set basis/ledger quantity to a distinct kind (e.g. measured_absence_x_projected_rate), no CI, confidence from the rate source. (2) Read the asset's own cost before the change from the source that prices it (OpenCost assets/PV and LB cost API, already used in opencost_collector for PVs) at snapshot time and store it in the snapshot (asset_rate, source, window). (3) Re-verify absence at decision time and daily afterwards (extend the probe to Service and PersistentVolume via CoreV1); a resource present again ends the accrual from its creationTimestamp (state regressed/closed, reason resource_recreated). (4) Remove or fix the unreachable pre/post pod-row logic.

**Acceptance.** Fixture: deleted LoadBalancer with OpenCost asset rate 0.025/h in the baseline -> realized, rate_source observed, 219/yr; the Service recreated on day 3 -> accrual ends at its creationTimestamp and the change is closed with reason resource_recreated.

#### MEA-16 · Two verification systems with different rules write the same 'verified' / 'not_realized' labels: recommendations applied by hand (PATCH /apply) or with CHANGE_MEASUREMENT_ENABLED=false are judged by the interim verifier (72 h before/after means, no counterfactual, no interval, no confounders).

**Severity** medium · **Kind** architecture · **Confidence** confirmed-code · **Effort** M

**Where.** `src/greenkube/cli/start.py:355-379`; `src/greenkube/core/optimization/verifier.py:620-668`; `src/greenkube/core/optimization/verifier.py:680-704`; `src/greenkube/core/optimization/verifier.py:1462-1505`

**Mechanism.** The lifecycle job runs `verifier.verify_due()` and then `changes.run_due()` on every pass (start.py:378-379). verify_due handles every applied record without a change_id (`_change_managed`), i.e. manual applies and all records when change measurement is disabled. Its windows are VERIFICATION_WINDOW_HOURS (72 h) after the apply and the same length before; the outcome is `delta > 0 and delta > 0.02 * before` on two window means (_estimate), with no hour-of-week alignment, no load driver, no replica basis, no confounder detection and no interval. Both paths call apply_stored_measurement, write savings_measurements rows and feed the same ledger and API fields, distinguished only by created_by/strategy_key.

**Effect on numbers.** Verified savings of manually applied recommendations carry none of the safeguards of the change pipeline (a replica drop, a price change or a weekend/weekday window mix is booked as saving) yet are summed with change-measured savings.

**Affected environments.** All installs where users mark recommendations applied by hand or disable change measurement.

**Evidence.** Code path above; docs/methodology/savings-measurement.md line 23 confirms the interim verifier is still in use.

**Fix.** Route manual applies through the change pipeline: PATCH /apply creates a ChangeEvent (application_method manual, rollout_completed_at = declared or detected time, baseline_window_end = that time) and a baseline snapshot, then the record is change-managed. Keep the interim verifier only behind CHANGE_MEASUREMENT_ENABLED=false, mark its measurements `method=interim_before_after` in API/UI/ledger and exclude them from 'measured' totals by default. Remove the parallel call once no unmanaged applied records remain (migration: create change events for applied, undecided records).

**Acceptance.** After PATCH /apply on an active recommendation a change_events row exists and verify_due returns nothing for it; savings API reports interim-method amounts in a separate block.

#### MEA-17 · The baseline snapshot is frozen in the detection pass, before the last baseline samples are written and before provisional values are restated, and the recorded rollout start equals the rollout completion when the GitOps sync is observed late.

**Severity** low · **Kind** bug · **Confidence** confirmed-live · **Effort** S

**Where.** `src/greenkube/verify/service.py:508-513`; `src/greenkube/verify/service.py:551-564`; `src/greenkube/verify/service.py:585`; `src/greenkube/verify/service.py:635-668`

**Mechanism.** Live minikube: pull request merged_at 21:47:36, Deployment rollout completed 21:47:38, sync observed 21:47:51, change recorded 21:48:05 with baseline_window_end = 21:47:38 and rollout_started_source = 'rollout_completed'. The baseline end at completion instead of merge is explained by the image: the clamp to merged_at (service.py:555-557, commit 38b6c884, 2026-11-14 22:11 UTC) is newer than the live controller (created 21:38 UTC), so the clamp itself has not run live. What remains at HEAD: because the GitOps sync is observed by polling after a fast rollout, `synced_at <= completed` is false and rollout_started_at falls back to the completion time (service.py:559-564), so the stored rollout duration is zero. The snapshot is built in the same pass as detection: on kind it holds 28 of the 29 baseline samples (the 21:52:00 sample was written after 21:52:49) and is immutable afterwards (UNIQUE change_id), so late-arriving or later-restated rows (OpenCost lag, carbon-intensity consolidation) never reach the baseline while the post-window uses final values.
