**Effect on numbers.** Seconds to minutes of rollout inside a 7-day baseline (< 0.1%); the last 1-2 samples missing; baseline CO2e of the last 24-48 h frozen with provisional intensity. Small, but systematic and it makes the stored rollout timing wrong for audit.

**Affected environments.** GitOps changes with fast rollouts; all for the snapshot timing.

**Evidence.** Live SQL: recommendation_pull_requests (merged_at 21:47:36, sync_observed_at 21:47:51) vs change_events (baseline_window_end 21:47:38, rollout_started_source rollout_completed); kind snapshot coverage 0.2333 = 28 samples vs 29 raw rows.

**Fix.** Record the candidate decision times in change details (merged_at, approval time, completion) and the chosen `baseline_end_source`, and re-run the e2e on an image that contains commit 38b6c884 to confirm the clamp. Use merged_at as the rollout start when the sync is observed after completion. Freeze the snapshot only once the collection watermark has passed baseline_window_end + one step (and, for CO2e, re-freeze a `final` revision of the snapshot after the intensity consolidation horizon, keeping the first revision and both digests).

**Acceptance.** For the live sequence the change stores baseline_window_end = 21:47:36 with baseline_end_source = pr_merged and rollout_started_at = 21:47:36; the snapshot contains every raw row with timestamp < baseline_window_end.

#### MEA-18 · read_combined_metrics_smart splits raw and hourly data at a cutoff that is not hour-aligned, so each measurement read that spans the cutoff misses up to one hour of rows (the hour not yet compressed and older than the cutoff).

**Severity** low · **Kind** bug · **Confidence** likely · **Effort** S

**Where.** `src/greenkube/storage/base_repository.py:316-342`; `src/greenkube/core/metrics_compressor.py:329-341`

**Mechanism.** The reader uses `compression_cutoff = now - AGE` (to the second): hourly rows with hour_bucket < cutoff plus raw rows with timestamp >= cutoff. The compressor only compresses closed hours before floor_hour(now - AGE) and runs hourly. The raw rows between floor_hour(cutoff) and cutoff are older than the cutoff (not read as raw) and belong to an hour that has no rollup row yet (not read as hourly). When the rollup exists and raw rows of the same hour are also read, the hour is represented twice (rates stay about right, coverage is capped).

**Effect on numbers.** One partial hour per window read (<= 0.6% of a 7-day window), plus the lag of the compressor; shifts which hours pass the 50% coverage rule.

**Affected environments.** All production installs.

**Evidence.** Code reading of both functions; not reproduced (belongs to the storage dimension).

**Fix.** Align the reader on the compression watermark: read hourly rows for hour_bucket < watermark (metrics_compression_watermarks.compressed_until) and raw rows for timestamp >= watermark; never both for one hour.

**Acceptance.** With raw rows every 5 minutes for 48 h and the compressor run at an arbitrary minute, the smart read returns rows whose total covered time equals 48 h +- one step for any `now`.

### 5.8 Recommendation engine

**Coverage.** Wall-clock used: about 20 minutes of the 55-minute budget (the session's tool calls were fast); stopped on effort, not on time. READ IN FULL: core/optimization/statistics.py, thresholds.py, targeting.py, context.py, context_builder.py, evidence.py, scan_service.py, analyzers/rightsizing.py, rightsizing_cpu.py, rightsizing_memory.py, zombie.py, idle_namespace.py, nodes.py, autoscaling.py, off_peak.py, orphaned_lb.py, orphaned_pv.py; core/workload_usage.py; core/reliability_signals.py; core/recommendation_reconciliation.py; models/workload_usage.py; storage/workload_usage_sql.py (summary_sql, node_sql); engine.py (collect, run_scan, _persist, last-known logic); dedup.py (hysteresis part); applied_detector.py (rightsizing detection path, lines 568-990); core/recommendation_realization.py; storage upsert_recommendations (Postgres fully, SQLite relevant parts). READ PARTLY / SKIMMED: providers/vpa.py (collect, object_recommendations, _recommendation), providers/karpenter.py (utilisation and removable-node helpers), lifecycle.py (transition table, expire), phantom_repair.py (docstring and outline only), eligibility.py (outline, codes), risks.py (confidence, HPA/OOM parts), enrich.py (risk profile), docs/recommendation.md (grep only), automation/policy.py sample gate. NOT READ: models/recommendation_v2.py, models/evidence.py, analyzers/carbon_aware.py, registry.py, scoring.py, track_record.py, actions.py, verifier.py, the body of eligibility.py and lifecycle.py (apply/mark_* methods), applied_detector deletion and rollback paths, phantom_repair logic, dedup consensus/materialize, signals/consensus.py, karpenter policy findings, read_baseline_rows SQL, scan memory/query-count measurement on a large cluster (not measured). RAN: four simulations with real code in audit/work/recommendation-engine/ (sim_flap.py, sim_gates.py, sim_applied_swallow.py, sim_ignore.py); read-only live SQL/Prometheus/kubectl on both clusters (minikube and kind); hand recomputation of 6 live recommendations from workload_usage_hourly and of one from raw container_metrics. No pytest run. Live clusters use accelerated settings (1-day lookback, short coverage/unused periods), so findings about 7/14-day behaviour rest on code and simulation. Assumption: REC-01's ratchet (repeated steps) is extrapolated from one observed step (ids 80 -> 88) plus the formula; no second step was applied live.

**Earlier defects not fully fixed.**

- BUG-10 (partially_fixed): Limits are now in the rollup and summaries carry container_kind (container/sidecar/init); collection side not checked here.
- BUG-37 (partially_fixed): ACTIVE->VERIFIED no longer allowed, VERIFIED->REGRESSED and ROLLBACK_REVIEW->APPLIED exist; new holes found: advice swallowed while a row is 'applied' (REC-01) and ignore not honoured by scans (REC-06).

#### REC-01 · Once a rightsizing recommendation of a container is in status 'applied', every later recommendation of the same type for that container is silently discarded by upsert_recommendations (even a different proposal such as an OOM-driven increase after a GreenKube decrease), for as long as the row stays 'applied', which is permanent after an inconclusive or not_realized verdict.

**Severity** high · **Kind** bug · **Confidence** confirmed-sim · **Effort** S

**Where.** `src/greenkube/storage/sqlite/recommendation_repository.py:348-385`; `src/greenkube/storage/postgres/recommendation_repository.py:203-216`; `src/greenkube/storage/postgres/recommendation_repository.py:285-323`; `src/greenkube/core/recommendation_realization.py:12-17`; `src/greenkube/core/recommendation_realization.py:52-80`; `src/greenkube/core/optimization/engine.py:431-448`

**Mechanism.** upsert_recommendations() first looks for an ACTIVE row with the same fingerprint/identity. When there is none it selects the most recent row of the same identity (scope, namespace, pod_name, container_name, target_node, type) whose status is pr_open or in RECORDED_CHANGE_STATUSES (applied, verifying, verified, rollback_review). For verifying/verified/rollback_review it calls absorbs_regenerated_recommendation(), which only absorbs a restatement of the recorded change within ROLLUP_LAG_WINDOW (6 h). But when the row's status is exactly 'applied', the code goes to `if applied_row: refreshed = refresh_applied_recommendation(...); UPDATE ... SET updated_at ...; count += 1; continue` with no comparison at all: refresh_applied_recommendation() starts with `del observed_record`. The incoming recommendation is neither inserted nor queued, whatever its current/proposed values and however old the applied change is. A row stays 'applied' between detection and the start of measurement and, more importantly, returns to 'applied' permanently when the measurement ends inconclusive or not_realized (lifecycle.mark_inconclusive and mark_not_realized both transition to RecommendationStatus.APPLIED, lifecycle.py:855-920; live: all 6 applied rows on minikube are status applied / verification_status inconclusive, 11 hours after their verdict). The engine counts the swallowed record as written (the method returns 1), _persist() returns it in `kept`, and compute_diff() reports it as `new` on every scan because it is never in the active snapshot.

**Effect on numbers.** After the first applied change of a container resource, GreenKube stops producing recommendations for it: no follow-up decrease, and no reliability increase when the applied decrease turns out too aggressive (OOM kills, throttling). Simulation: decrease 1024->308Mi applied; the next scan's 'increase 308->616Mi, containers were OOM-killed' is upserted (return value 1) but the table still holds only the applied row and get_active_recommendations() returns 0 rows, both 1 hour and 3 days after the apply. Scan summaries overstate 'recommendation_count' and 'new' by the number of swallowed records on every run.

**Affected environments.** All, SQLite and PostgreSQL (same branch in both repositories). Hits every workload whose change measurement ends inconclusive or not_realized (the common verdict without trusted cost data, e.g. on-prem without OpenCost) and every workload during the applied->verifying gap.

**Evidence.** audit/work/recommendation-engine/sim_applied_swallow.py with the real SQLiteRecommendationRepository (in-memory): '[applied 1 h ago] upsert of the increase 308->616Mi returned 1 / after scan: [(1, 'applied', 1024, 308)] / active recommendations for the container: 0' and the same for '[applied 3 days ago]'. Live minikube: `select id,status,verification_status,updated_at from recommendation_history where id in (6,7,26,27,80,81)` -> all 'applied' + 'inconclusive'; recommendation_events for id 80: applied 21:58:46, verification_started 21:58:57, inconclusive 23:38:07 (ids 88/89 exist only because they were inserted during the verifying phase, when the 6-hour absorb rule did not match).

**Fix.** In both repositories treat status 'applied' like the other recorded statuses: call absorbs_regenerated_recommendation(recorded, incoming, now) and only when it returns True (same baseline as the recorded change, or proposal equal to the landed value, within ROLLUP_LAG_WINDOW of applied_at) refresh the applied row and skip; otherwise INSERT the incoming record as a new active recommendation (new id, same fingerprint), carrying `previous_change_id`/`previous_recommendation_id` so the UI and the ledger can relate them. Additionally require for absorption that incoming.current_* equals recorded.current_* (pre-change baseline) or incoming.recommended_* equals the landed value; anything whose current_* equals the landed value is new advice by definition. Make upsert_recommendations return the list of (fingerprint, action) so that engine._persist() reports only rows really written and compute_diff() does not report swallowed records as new; log swallowed records at INFO with the reason. Backfill: none needed, the next scan inserts the missing recommendations.

**Acceptance.** sim_applied_swallow.py prints 'active recommendations for the container: 1' in both cases, with current 308Mi and proposed 616Mi. A regenerated pre-change advice (current 1024Mi, proposed 308Mi) within 6 h of applied_at is still absorbed; the same after 7 h is inserted. Repository contract test runs against SQLite and PostgreSQL. Run diff: a swallowed record is not counted in new_count or recommendation_count.

#### REC-02 · OOM kills are counted over the whole lookback regardless of request/limit changes, so after an OOM fix is applied the old kills keep 'proving' that the NEW limit was passed and drive a further unjustified memory increase on every scan.

**Severity** high · **Kind** bug · **Confidence** confirmed-live · **Effort** M

**Where.** `src/greenkube/core/reliability_signals.py:167-202`; `src/greenkube/core/reliability_signals.py:149-164`; `src/greenkube/core/optimization/analyzers/rightsizing.py:393-417`; `src/greenkube/core/optimization/analyzers/rightsizing.py:448-461`; `src/greenkube/core/optimization/analyzers/rightsizing.py:501-520`

**Mechanism.** read_prometheus_signals() counts OOM kills with a PromQL window equal to the full lookback (window_seconds=(end-start), 14 days by default) and _per_container() sums every pod name that maps to the target, including the pods of ReplicaSets that ran with the previous request/limit. apply_signals() stores that sum as summary.oom_kill_count. memory_proposal() then treats any non-zero count as proof about the CURRENT limit: `floor = min(oom_proven_usage(summary) * step, request * max_increase_factor(config))` where oom_proven_usage() returns the summary's latest memory_limit (the value after the fix). The step is uncapped ('OOM kills are proof that the request is too low: no cap on that step') and needs_limit_headroom() also returns 'oom_headroom', so the limit is raised again as well. Nothing relates the kill timestamps to the time the request/limit last changed (the hourly rollup knows the request per hour, change_events knows the apply time). The same stale count also blocks every memory AND CPU decrease for the container for the whole lookback (rightsizing.py:324 and :481); restarts_in_window (in-place container restarts over the lookback) blocks them in the same way and is equally blind to the configuration the restarts happened under.

**Effect on numbers.** Recommended memory request and limit are inflated by about x1.5-1.67 per applied step for as long as the old kills stay inside the lookback (14 days). Worked example (live minikube): e2e-oom had request 32Mi/limit 32Mi and was OOM-killed; recommendation 80 (32->64Mi, limit->71Mi) was applied at 21:58. The new pod has run 12 h with 0 restarts and an observed maximum of 37MiB, yet recommendation 88 (created 22:01, still active) proposes 64->107Mi with the reason 'Sized from the memory limit (71MiB) the OOM kills prove was passed x 1.5'. That statement is false: the kills happened at 32Mi. If 88 is applied the limit becomes about 119Mi and the next scan proposes min(119x1.5, 107x2)=179Mi, then about 299Mi, 500Mi ... one pull request per step until the kills age out: a workload that needs about 40Mi can be ratcheted to several hundred Mi, and the projected cost of each increase is reported as a justified reliability spend.

**Affected environments.** All environments with Prometheus + kube-state-metrics or cAdvisor OOM events (the default). Worst with always-on automation, which opens a PR for each step.

**Evidence.** Live (GK_ENV=minikube): `select id,status,applied_at,current_memory_request_bytes/1048576,recommended_memory_request_bytes/1048576,reason from recommendation_history where owner_name='e2e-oom'` -> id 80 applied 2026-11-14 21:58 (32->64), id 88 active created 22:01 updated 2026-11-15 10:06 (64->107, 'memory limit (71MiB) the OOM kills prove was passed x 1.5 ... observed maximum 37MiB'). `kubectl get pods -n greenkube-e2e-apps`: e2e-oom-5f998b67bf-hp8sh Running, RESTARTS 0, AGE 12h; deployment resources requests.memory=64Mi limits.memory=71Mi; Prometheus kube_pod_container_status_restarts_total for the pod = 0. Same for e2e-oom-argo (ids 81/89). Code path read end to end.

**Fix.** 1) Make OOM evidence relative to the configuration it happened under. In read_prometheus_signals() return kills per (pod, container) with the time of the last kill (e.g. query the subquery as a range, or `max_over_time(timestamp(...))`), and in apply_signals() keep only kills of pods that ran with the summary's current memory limit/request: simplest robust rule: ignore kills older than the last hour in which the rollup's memory_limit or memory_request for that target container differs from the current one (read_usage_snapshot can return `request_changed_at`/`limit_changed_at` = first hour_bucket of the trailing run of identical request+limit), and additionally ignore kills that predate the latest change_events apply time for the target. 2) Expose both numbers on the summary (oom_kill_count = kills under the current limit; oom_kill_count_before_change) and say so in the reason text. 3) Do the same for restarts_in_window: only restarts of pods running the current pod template count against a decrease. 4) After a memory increase is applied, hold further OOM-driven increases for the target until at least one kill is observed under the new limit. 5) Existing active recommendations created from stale kills (reason contains 'OOM kills prove' and no kill since the last change) must be recomputed on the next scan and resolved; no stored-data migration beyond that.

**Acceptance.** Unit: summary with memory_request=64Mi, limit=71Mi, max=37Mi, p99=36Mi, kills=[(t0-2h, under limit 32Mi)] and request change at t0-1h -> memory_proposal() returns None. Same summary with one kill after the change -> increase with basis oom_limit. Integration (fake Prometheus + SQLite): apply a 32->64Mi fix, run two scans with the old kills still in the window -> no new RIGHTSIZING_MEMORY recommendation, and the reason of no active recommendation mentions a limit the kills did not happen at. Decrease test: a container with an OOM kill 10 days ago under a previous limit and 7 clean days under the current one is eligible for a decrease again.

#### REC-03 · The decrease target is not checked against the engine's own increase trigger: a decrease to max(p95,(avg+max)/2)x1.2 lands below p99/0.9 for any workload with an occasional peak, so GreenKube proposes a decrease, then an increase, then the same decrease again, forever, and memory decreases go below the observed peak.

**Severity** high · **Kind** bug · **Confidence** confirmed-sim · **Effort** S

**Where.** `src/greenkube/core/optimization/analyzers/rightsizing.py:330-340`; `src/greenkube/core/optimization/analyzers/rightsizing.py:485-498`; `src/greenkube/core/optimization/statistics.py:105-108`; `src/greenkube/core/optimization/analyzers/rightsizing.py:257-262`; `src/greenkube/core/optimization/analyzers/rightsizing.py:239-254`

**Mechanism.** cpu_proposal()/memory_proposal() size a decrease with balanced_rightsizing_target(): `int(max(p95_usage, (avg_usage + observed_max) / 2.0) * RIGHTSIZING_HEADROOM)`. The increase rule triggers when `stats.p99 > request * RIGHTSIZING_INCREASE_THRESHOLD (0.9)` and sizes to p99 x 1.2. The decrease branch only verifies that the increase trigger is not met for the CURRENT request (`if evidence or _cpu_increase_triggered(config, summary): return None`), never for the request it proposes. Because the lookback p99 is the nearest-rank p99 of the hourly p99s (close to the window maximum, see the percentile finding), any workload whose peak hours are fewer than 5% of hours (p95 stays low) and whose peak exceeds about 1.17x its average gets a decrease target T=0.6x(avg+max) with p99 > 0.9T. After the decrease is applied the next scan proposes an increase to p99x1.2; at that request avg/request is again below 0.3, so the following scan proposes the same decrease. Hysteresis (dedup.py REVISION_HYSTERESIS) only holds proposals against the same current request and direction, and the automation cooldown only delays the cycle. For memory the decrease target is also below the observed maximum working set (only the memory_equals_request policy adds max(raw, max x 1.1)); with the default `keep` policy and no memory limit the pod becomes Burstable below its real peak and is the first eviction candidate under node pressure.

**Effect on numbers.** Recommended requests oscillate between two values; each half-cycle is a pull request, a rollout, a change-measurement window and a ledger entry. Simulation, workload with a one-hour daily peak (avg 112m/112Mi, p95 100, p99=max 400), request 1000m/1024Mi: scan0 decrease to 307m/308Mi (memory 23% BELOW the 400Mi peak), scan1 increase to 480m/480Mi, scan2 decrease to 307m/308Mi, ... indefinitely. Workload with one 5-minute spike per day (avg 52m, max 500m; 152Mi, max 800Mi): 330m<->600m and 572Mi<->960Mi (decrease 28% below the peak). The savings claimed for the decrease (for example 1024->308Mi) are then partly reversed by the following increase, so projected and 'realized' savings in the ledger are overstated by the share that is given back (here (480-308)/(1024-308)=24%).

**Affected environments.** All. Any workload with daily/weekly batch peaks, cron-like load, GC or cache spikes; not triggered by flat workloads or by peaks lasting more than about 5% of hours (p95 then covers them).

**Evidence.** Simulation with the real rollup_rows/summarize/cpu_proposal/memory_proposal: audit/work/recommendation-engine/sim_flap.py. Output: '=== daily 1h peak: cpu avg=112 p50=100 p95=100 p99=400 max=400 ... scan 0: CPU decrease 1000m -> 307m; MEM decrease 1024Mi -> 308Mi observed max 400Mi; scan 1: CPU increase 307m -> 480m; MEM increase 308Mi -> 480Mi; scan 2: CPU decrease 480m -> 307m ...' (6 scans, never stable); '=== 5-min spike per day: 1000m->330m->600m->330m ..., 2048Mi->572Mi->960Mi->572Mi ...'. The business-hours profile (peak 33% of hours) is stable, which confirms the mechanism.

**Fix.** Make the two rules consistent in rightsizing.py: (1) a decrease target must never satisfy the increase trigger: decrease_target = max(balanced target, ceil(p99 / RIGHTSIZING_INCREASE_THRESHOLD) + margin) for CPU, and for memory additionally max(..., round_up_mib(observed_max x MEMORY_MAX_FACTOR)) under every limits policy (memory is incompressible: never request less than the observed peak plus headroom). (2) Add a dead band: propose a decrease only if the resulting request, re-evaluated with the same summary, yields no proposal (call cpu_proposal/memory_proposal recursively on a copy of the summary with the proposed request and assert None; add this as an invariant test too). (3) Symmetric guard for increases: an increase target must not satisfy the decrease threshold (avg/proposed < RIGHTSIZING_*_THRESHOLD) unless OOM-backed; if it does, mark the workload as 'bursty: request cannot satisfy both rules' and emit an autoscaling/limit advice instead of a request change. (4) Document the formula in docs/recommendation.md. (5) Existing active decrease recommendations must be recomputed by the next scan (no migration); already applied decreases that are now followed by an increase should be linked (increase.reason mentions the previous GreenKube change) so the ledger can net them.

**Acceptance.** Property test over random usage distributions (avg, p95, p99, max, any request): applying the proposal and re-running the proposal function with unchanged usage returns None (fixed point in one step, at most two for capped increases). The two profiles of sim_flap.py end with 'stable' at scan 1. Memory decrease: proposed >= round_up_mib(max x 1.1) for every profile (400Mi peak -> at least 440Mi).

#### REC-04 · Rightsizing fails open when reliability signals are unknown: with oom_kill_count/restarts_in_window = None (no kube-state-metrics, Prometheus error or timeout) a crash-looping or OOM-killed container is offered CPU and memory decreases, although the rollup itself holds its restart count.

**Severity** high · **Kind** bug · **Confidence** confirmed-sim · **Effort** M

**Where.** `src/greenkube/core/optimization/analyzers/rightsizing.py:324-328`; `src/greenkube/core/optimization/analyzers/rightsizing.py:481-483`; `src/greenkube/core/optimization/context_builder.py:181-187`; `src/greenkube/core/reliability_signals.py:293-296`; `src/greenkube/core/reliability_signals.py:175-176`; `src/greenkube/core/optimization/risks.py:274-277`

**Mechanism.** attach_reliability_signals() is best effort: ContextBuilder._attach_reliability_signals() catches every exception ('signals stay unknown; the scan goes on') and apply_signals() only writes oom_kill_count/restarts_in_window when kube-state-metrics answered (`if kills or signals.oom_known`). The module docstring promises 'a signal that cannot be read stays None (unknown), never healthy', but the consumers test truthiness: `if summary.oom_kill_count or summary.restarts_in_window: return None` (cpu_proposal and memory_proposal) and risks.py `if resource == "memory" and profile.oom_events:`. None therefore behaves exactly like 0: the decrease is proposed, with normal risk. The signal query itself is fragile: one instant query evaluates a subquery over the whole lookback at 5-minute resolution for every container (`sum_over_time((... )[1209600s:5m])`), which can exceed the Prometheus query timeout or max-samples limit on a large cluster; any failure of any of the six queries discards all signals of that scan. The hourly rollup already stores restart_count per target container hour (WorkloadUsageSummary.restart_count, and its increase over the window is derivable) but rightsizing never uses it as a fallback.

**Effect on numbers.** A container that is OOM-killed or crash-looping shows low average usage (it spends its time in back-off), so the engine recommends cutting its request. Simulation: container restarting 36 times in 14 days, usage 20m / 100Mi, request 1000m / 1024Mi, signals None -> 'cpu decrease 1000 -> 24', 'memory decrease 1024Mi -> 120Mi'; with the signals known (12 OOM kills, 37 restarts) the same summary yields 'memory increase 1024Mi -> 1536Mi' and no CPU change. The sign of the recommendation flips with the availability of a side query, and the projected savings of the wrong decrease enter the dashboard totals.

**Affected environments.** Clusters without kube-state-metrics (k3s/kubeadm with a minimal Prometheus, managed Prometheus without KSM), any cluster during a Prometheus outage or when the 14-day subquery times out (large clusters, Thanos/Mimir limits), and installs without PROMETHEUS_URL using only the Metrics API.

**Evidence.** audit/work/recommendation-engine/sim_gates.py (real rollup_rows/summarize/cpu_proposal/memory_proposal): '1. crash-looping container, signals unknown (None): ... restart_count(rollup)=36 oom=None restarts_in_window=None -> cpu decrease 1000 -> 24; memory decrease 1024 -> 120' versus '1b. same with signals known: oom=12 restarts_in_window=37 -> memory increase 1024 -> 1536'.

**Fix.** 1) Fail closed: in cpu_proposal()/memory_proposal() a decrease requires `summary.oom_kill_count == 0 and summary.restarts_in_window == 0` (explicit zeros). When either is None, fall back to the rollup: compute restarts in window as (max - min) of restart_count over the summary's hours plus restart-count resets (add `restart_increase` to summary_sql/summarize()); if that is > 0 or also unknown, do not propose the decrease and record a skip reason `reliability_signals_unknown` (same mechanism as analyzer.skipped) that the API/UI shows ('decrease withheld: restart/OOM history could not be read'). 2) risks.py: treat oom_events None as at least MEDIUM for memory decreases and add factor `oom_history_unknown`; eligibility must block automation on it. 3) Make the signal read robust: query per signal independently (one failing query must not drop the others), split the lookback into daily chunks or use `increase(kube_pod_container_status_restarts_total[window])` joined with `kube_pod_container_status_last_terminated_reason` over shorter subqueries, apply an explicit timeout, and record the outcome per signal in the source run so the UI can show 'OOM signal unavailable'. 4) Mark the scan's rightsizing source as incomplete when signals are unknown so reconciliation does not replace a previous OOM-driven increase by a decrease (engine._preserve_last_known already keeps the safer value for incomplete sources).

**Acceptance.** sim_gates.py case 1 returns no proposal and a skip reason. Unit: summary(oom_kill_count=None, restarts_in_window=None, restart_increase=0, low usage, 14 d coverage) -> decrease allowed only if the rollup restart fallback is 0; with restart_increase=3 -> None. Integration with a fake Prometheus that returns HTTP 503 for the OOM query: no active decrease for a container whose rollup restart_count grew, source run status reports the missing signal, a previously active increase for that container is retained.

#### REC-05 · Recommendations are generated for workloads and containers that no longer exist: any target with one hour of usage inside the lookback gets a summary, there is no 'still running' check, so deleted/renamed Deployments, removed containers and finished Jobs keep active (and re-refreshed) rightsizing recommendations for up to the whole lookback.

**Severity** high · **Kind** bug · **Confidence** confirmed-live · **Effort** M

**Where.** `src/greenkube/storage/workload_usage_sql.py:198-288`; `src/greenkube/core/optimization/context.py:129-132`; `src/greenkube/core/optimization/context_builder.py:140-150`; `src/greenkube/core/optimization/analyzers/rightsizing_cpu.py:39-54`; `src/greenkube/core/optimization/analyzers/rightsizing_memory.py:118-133`; `src/greenkube/core/workload_usage.py:705-747`

**Mechanism.** summary_sql()/summarize() return one summary per (namespace, target_kind, target_name, container) that has at least one hourly row in [now - RECOMMENDATION_LOOKBACK_DAYS, now]. The only liveness filter is on namespaces that no longer exist (get_active_k8s_namespaces). OptimizationContext.iter_container_summaries() yields every summary and the CPU/memory analyzers never look at summary.last_hour, at the workload's existence, or at whether the container is still in the pod spec. 'Current request' is the request of the target's last observed hour, which may be days old. Each scan re-emits the recommendation, so it stays `active`, its updated_at and expires_at are pushed forward (TTL never elapses), and reconciliation never marks it stale until the last hour of the dead target leaves the lookback (14 days by default). Increases need no coverage at all, so a finished Job or a pod that lived one step can produce one (sim: one sample at 950m of a 1000m request for Job report-28391234 -> 'cpu increase 1000 -> 1140').

**Effect on numbers.** Counts of active recommendations and the sum of potential savings include workloads that no longer exist, for up to 14 days after deletion; the recommendation states a 'current request' that is not current. Live (kind): Deployment gk-upgrade/gkup-greenkube was replaced by gkup-greenkube-api/-controller/-renderer; its last usage hour is 2026-11-14 22:00, yet at 2026-11-15 10:09 recommendations 35, 36, 37 and 39 (CPU 100m->33m, memory 128->163Mi, 128->175Mi, CPU 50->42m) are still active with updated_at = the last scan and expires_at = +14 days. In a cluster with CronJobs every run can leave a ghost target for 14 days (N runs x containers recommendations).

**Affected environments.** All. Most visible with rollouts that rename workloads (Helm release renames, chart refactors), deleted preview/review environments inside a surviving namespace, CronJobs/Jobs, and containers removed from a pod template.

**Evidence.** Live GK_ENV=kind: `select r.id, r.type, r.owner_name, l.lh from recommendation_history r left join (select namespace,target_kind,target_name,max(hour_bucket) lh from workload_usage_hourly where container_name='' group by 1,2,3) l on ... where r.status='active' and l.lh < now() - interval '2 hours'` -> ids 35,36,37,39 owner gkup-greenkube, last hour 2026-11-14 22:00 (now 2026-11-15 10:10); `kubectl get deploy -n gk-upgrade` lists only gkup-greenkube-api, -controller, -renderer; recommendation_history shows updated_at 2026-11-15 10:09:55 and expires_at 2026-11-29 for these ids. Simulation sim_gates.py case 3 for the single-sample Job.

**Fix.** 1) In the summary (SQL and summarize()) keep last_hour and add `last_sample_at`; in ContextBuilder.build_from_rollup() drop from the analyzers every target container whose last sample is older than max(2 collection steps, 2 hours) before `end` (configurable RECOMMENDATION_TARGET_MAX_STALENESS), unless the workload still exists and is scaled to zero. 2) Cross-check with the cluster: the scan already lists all pods (reliability_signals._list_pods) and HPA targets; build the set of live (namespace, owner kind, owner name, container) from pod specs plus Deployments/StatefulSets/DaemonSets/CronJobs with replicas=0, and skip targets and containers not in it (fail closed when the Kubernetes API cannot be read: keep previous records via the incomplete-source mechanism instead of emitting). 3) Never emit request recommendations for kinds Job and bare Pod that have completed; for CronJobs aggregate runs under the CronJob (owner of the Job) so that the recommendation targets the CronJob template. 4) Recommendations whose target disappeared must be reconciled to `stale` (reason target_gone) by the next scan. No migration needed; the next scan resolves existing ghosts.

**Acceptance.** Integration (SQLite): workload A has 10 days of hourly rows ending 5 hours ago and no live pod -> the scan emits nothing for A and an existing active recommendation for A becomes stale. Workload B scaled to zero 5 hours ago but still present as a Deployment -> existing recommendation kept, flagged 'not running'. A Job with 1-3 samples -> no recommendation. A container removed from the pod template -> no recommendation for it. Live check query above returns no rows.

#### REC-06 · Ignoring a recommendation does not survive the next scan: upsert_recommendations only matches rows in status 'active' (then pr_open/applied family), so the same advice is inserted again as a new active row while the ignored row stays behind; un-ignoring later yields two active rows with one fingerprint.

**Severity** medium · **Kind** bug · **Confidence** confirmed-sim · **Effort** S

**Where.** `src/greenkube/storage/sqlite/recommendation_repository.py:283-290`; `src/greenkube/storage/sqlite/recommendation_repository.py:332-400`; `src/greenkube/storage/postgres/recommendation_repository.py:186-198`; `src/greenkube/storage/postgres/recommendation_repository.py:285-331`; `src/greenkube/core/optimization/lifecycle.py:71-76`; `docs/recommendation.md:62`

**Mechanism.** The docstring of upsert_recommendations() says 'Ignored recommendations are left untouched', and the repository calls ignored rows 'permanently ignored'. The implementation looks up an existing row by identity with status 'active', then a pr_open row, then a row in RECORDED_CHANGE_STATUSES; an 'ignored' row matches none of them, so the incoming recommendation falls through to the INSERT. The engine (_persist) does not filter on ignored fingerprints either. Result: the next scan (hourly by default) re-creates the recommendation as a new active record with a new id; the user's decision and reason remain on a row nobody reads. The lifecycle allows ignored -> active (un-ignore), which then produces two active rows for the same fingerprint (the active lookup uses `ORDER BY id LIMIT 1`, so only the older one is refreshed and the other goes stale/expired later).

**Effect on numbers.** Counts and potential-savings totals include recommendations the user dismissed; with always-on automation the re-created active row is eligible for a pull request although its twin was ignored. Simulation: after ignore -> [(1,'ignored',300)]; after the next scan with the same advice -> [(1,'ignored',300),(2,'active',300)].

**Affected environments.** All; confirmed on SQLite by simulation, PostgreSQL by code reading (same lookup order; the INSERT uses ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING, so a unique index could change the outcome there: to be verified against the migrations).

**Evidence.** audit/work/recommendation-engine/sim_ignore.py (real SQLiteRecommendationRepository + RecommendationLifecycle.ignore): output quoted above. No ignored rows exist in the two live databases, so this was not observable live.

**Fix.** In upsert_recommendations (both backends), before inserting, look for a row of the same identity/fingerprint in status 'ignored': if it exists, do not insert; refresh its updated_at/expires_at and, when the new proposal differs materially (outside REVISION_HYSTERESIS or opposite direction, or a reliability increase with OOM evidence), record a `changed_while_ignored` event and surface it in the ignored list ('advice changed since you ignored it'); add an optional ignore expiry (ignore_until) after which the recommendation is offered again. Add a partial unique index on (fingerprint) WHERE status IN ('active','ignored','pr_open') to make duplicates impossible, with a migration that merges existing duplicates (keep the ignored row, mark active twins stale with reason superseded_by_ignored). Update docs/recommendation.md.

**Acceptance.** sim_ignore.py: after the next scan the table holds exactly one row, status ignored. Un-ignore then scan -> exactly one active row. Changed advice while ignored -> still one row, with an event. Same contract test on PostgreSQL.

#### REC-07 · Lookback 'p95/p99' are nearest-rank percentiles of hourly percentiles (themselves nearly hourly maxima), so the value shown and used as 'p99' is close to the window maximum and is not a percentile of the samples; one 5-minute spike a day makes 'p99' equal to the spike.

**Severity** medium · **Kind** design · **Confidence** confirmed-live · **Effort** L

**Where.** `src/greenkube/core/workload_usage.py:638-667`; `src/greenkube/core/workload_usage.py:62-91`; `src/greenkube/core/workload_usage.py:264-284`; `src/greenkube/core/workload_usage.py:600-603`; `src/greenkube/core/optimization/analyzers/rightsizing_cpu.py:72-83`

**Mechanism.** _stats() computes each lookback percentile as `nearest_rank(present, p)` over the list of hourly values of the same percentile. (a) Per hour, with the default 5-minute step there are 12 points per replica: nearest-rank p95 and p99 of 12 points are both the hourly maximum (index ceil(0.95x12)-1 = 11), and each point is already `max(cpu, peak)`. (b) Over the window, the nearest-rank p99 of N hourly values is the maximum when N < 100 hours (the first 4 days) and the 4th highest hour for 336 hours; hours are unweighted (an hour with 2 samples counts as much as one with 120) and node rows of the same hour are merged with max. So 'p99' is the 99th percentile of hourly maxima, and it equals the spike whenever at least 1% of hours (4 of 336) contain one sample of it, although those samples can be 0.1-0.4% of all samples. The recommendation text nevertheless says 'p99 CPU usage (393m)' / 'per-replica p99 ... x headroom 1.2', and the increase trigger compares this value with 0.9 x request. In addition, an hour re-rolled from the compressed hourly tables (first run after an upgrade, backfill of hours older than raw retention, late rows) contributes one point per pod equal to the hour's peak (`point = max(cpu, peak)` with the row's weight), so for those hours p50, p90, p95 and p99 all equal the hourly maximum.

**Effect on numbers.** The statistic called p99 overstates the true sample p99 by up to max/p50. Simulation: 4032 samples, 14 of them (0.35%) at 500m, the rest at 50m: true p99 = 50m, GreenKube p99 = 500m, CPU increase sized to 600m. Live (minikube): greenkube-ci/test-postgres 'p99 CPU usage (393m) ... Average usage is 9% of the request (18m of 200m)' -> increase 200m->400m; greenkube-review 'p99 (111m)... average 74% of 10m'. Increases are therefore triggered and sized by single spikes outside warm-up, which is exactly what the docs say the p99 basis avoids ('a single startup spike does not').

**Affected environments.** All; strongest at the default 5-minute step (12 points per hour).

**Evidence.** Live minikube, flux-system/kustomize-controller/manager: raw container_metrics (677 samples) give avg 5.23m, percentile_disc(0.95)=7m, percentile_disc(0.99)=8m, max 11m; the hourly rollup p95 values are [10,7,8,0,6,6,6,6,6] and the engine stored 'P95 usage is 10m and observed max is 11m' (recommendation 101, 100m->12m = max(10,(5.23+11)/2)x1.2): the reported p95 is 43% above the sample p95. Four other live recommendations recomputed by hand from workload_usage_hourly match the engine's formulas exactly and show p99 = max in every case (greenkube-api memory: p99 equals max, 194.62Mi -> 234Mi; greenkube-postgres 170.12Mi -> 205Mi; test-postgres CPU p99=max=393m with avg 18m -> capped 400m; metrics-server memory 31.70Mi -> 39Mi). Simulation sim_flap.py: '=== 5-min spike per day: cpu avg=52 p50=50 p95=50 p99=500 max=500' (14 of 4032 samples at 500m).

**Fix.** Store a mergeable distribution per hour instead of four scalars: either a fixed log-scale histogram (for example 40 buckets, VPA-style, exponential 5% growth) or a t-digest/DDSketch blob per (hour, target, container, node) in workload_usage_hourly, and compute lookback percentiles by merging the hourly histograms weighted by their sample counts (SQL: unnest + sum per bucket; reference implementation in summarize()). Keep cpu_usage_max for the peak. Until then, at minimum (1) weight hours by cpu_sample_count/memory_sample_count in _stats() (weighted_nearest_rank already exists), (2) rename the statistic in descriptions and evidence to 'p99 of hourly peaks' and (3) state the fraction of hours above the request. Migration: add the histogram column in a new migration, backfill from raw rows for the retention window and from the compressed hourly tables otherwise (flag those hours as approximate); the rollup refresh already re-rolls the last 24 h.

**Acceptance.** summarize() over 4032 samples with 14 samples at 500m and 4018 at 50m returns p99 within 10% of 50m and max 500m; over a series with 2% of samples at 500m returns p99 about 500m. SQL (Postgres and SQLite) and Python implementations return the same values on the same rows. Live recomputation: stored p95/p99 of a target equal the percentile of its raw combined/container_metrics samples within one bucket width.

#### REC-08 · The decrease coverage gate measures the span between the first and last observed hour, not how much of it was observed: 24 samples in two hours 8 days apart pass the 7-day requirement, and RECOMMENDATION_MIN_SAMPLES is not enforced by any analyzer.

**Severity** medium · **Kind** bug · **Confidence** confirmed-sim · **Effort** S

**Where.** `src/greenkube/models/workload_usage.py:186-191`; `src/greenkube/core/optimization/statistics.py:99-102`; `src/greenkube/core/optimization/analyzers/rightsizing.py:172-182`; `src/greenkube/core/optimization/analyzers/zombie.py:32-36`; `src/greenkube/core/optimization/analyzers/idle_namespace.py:83-89`; `src/greenkube/core/optimization/risks.py:160-176`

**Mechanism.** WorkloadUsageSummary.coverage_seconds is `(last_hour - first_hour) + 3600`. _coverage_ok() compares it with RECOMMENDATION_MIN_DECREASE_COVERAGE_DAYS. hours_observed, sample_count and step_count are available on the summary but unused by the gate, so collection outages, a controller that was down, a workload scaled to zero most of the week, or a freshly re-created pod name all count as observed time. The same span is used as 'unused for N days' evidence by the zombie and idle-namespace analyzers (observed_span). RECOMMENDATION_MIN_SAMPLES (36) only feeds the confidence score in risks.py (+0.25) and the setup page; no analyzer blocks on it. Increases have no evidence requirement at all (by design, but one sample is enough).

**Effect on numbers.** A decrease 'backed by 7 days of weekly cycles' can rest on minutes of data. Simulation: 24 samples in 2 hours, 8 days apart (coverage_days=8.04, hours_observed=2) -> 'cpu decrease 1000 -> 24', 'memory decrease 1024Mi -> 120Mi'. Live minikube shows the gap pattern: the kustomize-controller rollup has 9 hours with data out of a 14-hour span (hour 04:00 holds 2 samples of 0m), which the gate would count as 14 hours.

**Affected environments.** All; triggered by collection gaps (laptop/on-prem clusters that sleep, controller restarts, Prometheus retention shorter than the gap), scale-to-zero workloads and CronJob-like patterns.

**Evidence.** audit/work/recommendation-engine/sim_gates.py case 2 output: '24 samples in 2 hours, 8 days apart: hours_observed=2 samples=24 coverage_days=8.04 -> cpu decrease 1000 -> 24; memory decrease 1024 -> 120'. Live: `select hour_bucket, sample_count from workload_usage_hourly where target_name='kustomize-controller' and container_name='manager'` -> hours 21,22,23 then 04 (2 samples), 06..10. Live kind: recommendation 42 (memory increase 128->191Mi) was created from evidence.sample_count = 1.

**Fix.** Replace the span by observed coverage: add to the summary `observed_hours` (already hours_observed) and `expected_steps` and define coverage_ratio = step_count / (span_seconds / step_seconds) per replica. _coverage_ok() must require (a) span >= RECOMMENDATION_MIN_DECREASE_COVERAGE_DAYS, (b) hours_observed >= 0.8 x span hours (configurable RECOMMENDATION_MIN_COVERAGE_RATIO), (c) every weekday observed at least once when the requirement is >= 7 days, and (d) sample_count >= RECOMMENDATION_MIN_SAMPLES. Use the same helper in zombie.py and idle_namespace.py (the 'unused for N days' statement must be 'observed idle in X of Y hours'). For increases require at least RECOMMENDATION_MIN_SAMPLES samples or two distinct hours unless OOM-backed. Expose hours_observed/expected in the evidence and the description.

**Acceptance.** sim_gates.py case 2 -> no proposal, skip reason insufficient_coverage. A summary with 7.5 days span and 95% of hours observed -> decrease allowed; 7.5 days span and 50% of hours -> blocked. Zombie/idle namespace: 2 observed hours over 8 days -> no recommendation.

#### REC-09 · Static (mirror) control-plane pods are merged into one fake workload 'Node/<node name>' and receive rightsizing and 'use an HPA' recommendations; the pod-level statistics of that target mix etcd, kube-apiserver, scheduler and controller-manager as if they were replicas.

**Severity** medium · **Kind** bug · **Confidence** confirmed-live · **Effort** S

**Where.** `src/greenkube/core/optimization/targeting.py:21-36`; `src/greenkube/collectors/pod_collector.py:111-136`; `src/greenkube/core/workload_usage.py:518-525`; `src/greenkube/core/optimization/analyzers/autoscaling.py:30-52`; `src/greenkube/core/optimization/analyzers/rightsizing.py:199-236`; `src/greenkube/core/optimization/targeting.py:10-18`

**Mechanism.** The pod collector stores the controller ownerReference of a pod as owner_kind/owner_name. Mirror pods of static manifests are owned by `Node/<node>`, so resolve_target() returns (kube-system, 'Node', '<node>') for etcd-<node>, kube-apiserver-<node>, kube-controller-manager-<node> and kube-scheduler-<node>. The rollup groups them as one target: its pod-level summary has pod_count 4 and percentiles/CV computed across four unrelated pods, which (a) feeds AutoscalingAnalyzer (CV across pods of 3m and 70m is high -> 'Node X has highly variable CPU usage ... Consider using HPA'), (b) is the 'pod' bound used by bounded_by_pod() for each container, and (c) yields container rightsizing recommendations labelled "Node 'minikube' container 'etcd'" whose manifests live in /etc/kubernetes/manifests on the node, not in any GitOps repository. Lowering kube-apiserver/etcd requests is not advice GreenKube can deliver or should give by default. Related weakness in the same function: when a pod row has no owner (5% of pod rows on the live minikube database), infer_deployment_target() applies `^(.+)-[a-z0-9]{8,10}-[a-z0-9]{5}$`, which also matches CronJob pods (`report-28391234-x7k2p` -> Deployment 'report') and DaemonSet/Job pods whose name ends with an 8-10 character word (`kps-prometheus-node-exporter-x7k2p` -> Deployment 'kps-prometheus-node').

**Effect on numbers.** Per control-plane node: 4-5 rightsizing recommendations and one AUTOSCALING_CANDIDATE that cannot be acted on; they inflate recommendation counts and the top of the list. Live minikube: ids 10-13, 31 (etcd 100m->16m, kube-apiserver 250m->87m, kube-controller-manager 200m->10m, kube-scheduler 100m->10m, etcd memory 100->176Mi) and id 56 "Node 'minikube' has highly variable CPU usage (CV=1.16, spike ratio=6.6x). Consider using HPA". Live kind: ids 5, 23, 24, 25, 26 and 11 for Node 'gk-cilium-control-plane'. HA control planes multiply this by the number of control-plane nodes.

**Affected environments.** Every self-managed control plane: kubeadm, kind, minikube, k3s/RKE2 static pods, bare metal; not managed control planes (EKS/GKE/AKS), except for other static pods (kube-proxy on some distributions, node-local agents).

**Evidence.** Live: `select id,type,container_name,description from recommendation_history where owner_kind='Node' and status='active'` on both clusters (rows quoted above); `select container_name, max(pod_count) from workload_usage_hourly where target_kind='Node' group by 1` -> pod-level row pod_count 4, containers etcd/kube-apiserver/kube-controller-manager/kube-scheduler with pod_count 1; `select coalesce(owner_kind,'NULL'), count(*) from combined_metrics group by 1` -> NULL 2006 rows / 32 pods (minikube).

**Fix.** 1) In resolve_target() (and target_for_pod) treat owner kind 'Node' as 'no controller': the target is the pod itself (kind 'Pod', name = pod name, plus a `static_pod=True` flag carried on the summary). 2) Exclude static pods from rightsizing/autoscaling/off-peak by default (they are not deliverable through Git); offer them only when RECOMMEND_SYSTEM_NAMESPACES explicitly includes control-plane pods, as manual-only advice. 3) Restrict workload-level analyzers to kinds that have a scale subresource (Deployment, StatefulSet, ReplicaSet, Rollout); never emit AUTOSCALING_CANDIDATE for other kinds. 4) Replace the name regex fallback by owner resolution from the Kubernetes API or from other rows of the same pod across hours (persist a pod->owner map), and when the owner is unknown keep kind 'Pod' and mark the summary `owner_unknown` (no workload recommendation). 5) Migration: delete or re-key workload_usage_hourly rows with target_kind='Node' (re-roll the retention window) and mark the existing Node-owned recommendations stale.

**Acceptance.** resolve_target('kube-system','etcd-cp1','Node','cp1') == ('kube-system','Pod','etcd-cp1'). A scan over a kubeadm-like fixture emits no recommendation with owner_kind 'Node' and no AUTOSCALING_CANDIDATE for kinds other than scalable workloads. infer fallback: pod 'report-28391234-x7k2p' without owner is not attributed to Deployment 'report'.

#### REC-10 · Zombie and idle-namespace detection compare an absolute energy total over a variable window (1000 J) instead of a rate, so they almost never fire with 7+ days of dense data and fire for every namespace when data are sparse or short; the 'unused for N days' evidence is a first-to-last span.

**Severity** medium · **Kind** design · **Confidence** confirmed-code · **Effort** M

**Where.** `src/greenkube/core/optimization/analyzers/zombie.py:56-72`; `src/greenkube/core/optimization/analyzers/idle_namespace.py:66-89`; `src/greenkube/core/config.py:402-408`; `src/greenkube/core/optimization/analyzers/zombie.py:32-36`

**Mechanism.** ZombieAnalyzer flags a target when `total_cost > ZOMBIE_COST_THRESHOLD (0.01) and joules < ZOMBIE_ENERGY_THRESHOLD (1000.0)`; IdleNamespaceAnalyzer when `joules < IDLE_NAMESPACE_ENERGY_THRESHOLD (1000) and cost > 0`. Both sums are totals over whatever part of the lookback has rows (all replicas, all hours). 1000 J over the required 7 days is an average of 1.65 mW; over 14 days 0.83 mW. An idle `sleep` container is attributed about 11 mW in the live clusters (570 J in 14 h), so with dense data the condition is false for any running pod after roughly one day: the analyzers are effectively dead in steady state. Conversely the total is small whenever few samples exist: the span gate (`last_hour - first_hour + 1h >= DELETION_MIN_UNUSED_DAYS`) is satisfied by two observed hours a week apart, so after a collection outage or on clusters that are switched off between sessions, busy namespaces whose few samples sum to less than 1000 J are reported as 'appears idle: consumed only N J over 8.0 days', with an annualized saving equal to the whole namespace cost. The texts state the span as if it were continuously observed.

**Effect on numbers.** False negatives: truly idle workloads/namespaces are not reported once more than about 25 hours of data exist (11 mW x 25 h = 1000 J). False positives: any namespace/workload observed for fewer than 1000 J in total across a 7-day span, e.g. a 5 W service observed for 3 minutes before and after a one-week outage. Live (kind, accelerated DELETION_MIN_UNUSED_DAYS): ids 29/30 'Namespace greenkube-e2e-apps appears idle: it consumed only 570J over 14.0 hours but costs $0.0051' with potential_savings_cost 3.38/year, and id 38 'gk-upgrade ... 224J over 2.0 hours', savings 11.78/year, for the namespace hosting a GreenKube install that had just started; the same namespaces would not qualify 12 hours later with unchanged behaviour (570 J / 14 h -> 1000 J at about 25 h).

**Affected environments.** All. False positives mainly on-prem/dev clusters with intermittent uptime or after controller outages; false negatives everywhere.

**Evidence.** Mechanism established from the code path above (thresholds are totals over the lookback, span is first-to-last hour). Code path above. Live kind: `select id,type,namespace,status,potential_savings_cost,description,reason from recommendation_history where type='IDLE_NAMESPACE'` (rows quoted). Config defaults at src/greenkube/core/config.py:402-408. The live rows show the short-window firing and the wording; the 7/14-day behaviour is arithmetic on the observed 11 mW idle rate, not observed live.

**Fix.** Define idleness as a rate with a coverage requirement: (1) compute average power per replica = joules / duration_seconds (the summary has duration_seconds as replica-seconds) and average CPU usage; flag when average power per replica < ZOMBIE_POWER_THRESHOLD_WATTS (new setting, default e.g. 0.05 W, to be calibrated against the idle allocation of the energy model) AND cpu p95 < a few millicores AND no network activity when available; (2) require hours_observed >= 0.8 x required hours (see the coverage finding) and that the last sample is recent (target still exists); (3) keep the old settings as deprecated aliases converted to a rate (threshold / (lookback seconds)) and document the change in docs/recommendation.md and docs/configuration.md; (4) word the evidence as 'observed idle in X of Y hours since <first>'.

**Acceptance.** Fixture A: one replica, 14 days dense data at 0.01 W and 0-1m CPU -> zombie + idle namespace reported. Fixture B: 5 W workload with 3 minutes of data at day 0 and day 8 -> not reported. Fixture C: fixture A with 2, 7 and 14 days of lookback gives the same verdict. Text contains observed/expected hours.

#### REC-11 · Off-peak 'scale to zero N h/day' recommendations are issued from a single day of data (6 samples in total suffice): each hour of day needs only one observation, unobserved hours silently cut the idle run, and a one-off startup spike is the 'peak', so always-idle workloads, a database and a Git server were told to scale to zero at night.

**Severity** medium · **Kind** bug · **Confidence** confirmed-live · **Effort** M

**Where.** `src/greenkube/core/optimization/analyzers/off_peak.py:20`; `src/greenkube/core/optimization/analyzers/off_peak.py:69-87`; `src/greenkube/core/optimization/analyzers/off_peak.py:124-132`; `src/greenkube/core/workload_usage.py:693-695`; `src/greenkube/core/workload_usage.py:749-752`

**Mechanism.** OffPeakAnalyzer reads snapshot.hour_profiles: the sample-weighted average CPU per UTC hour of day over the lookback. The only evidence gate is `hour_profile_samples >= MIN_PROFILE_SAMPLES (6)`. There is no requirement on days observed per hour of day, on coverage of the 24 hours, or on the absolute level of the peak. `peak_usage = max(hourly_avg.values())` can be the hour in which the pod started once; hours with no data are None and are simply absent, so the 'longest consecutive idle run' is bounded by where data collection happened to stop. Stateful workloads are not excluded (_unsafe_to_stop only excludes DaemonSet/Pod/Job/CronJob, HPA targets, infrastructure namespaces and operator-like names), although scaling a StatefulSet/database to zero daily is not a rightsizing action.

**Effect on numbers.** A daily schedule and its annualized saving are asserted without any repeated daily pattern. Live minikube (about 14 h of history, one night): id 98 e2e-oom 'idle 8h/day (03:00-11:00 UTC)': its hour-of-day profile is 0.361m at 21h (startup, 83 samples) and 0.000m in every other observed hour (22, 23, 3-10) -> it is always idle, and hours 0-2 and 11-20 were never observed; id 97 greenkube-ci/test-postgres (a PostgreSQL instance) 'Scale to 0: 03:00-09:00 UTC'; id 107 gitea 'idle 7h/day (03:00-10:00 UTC)'.

**Affected environments.** All; guaranteed during the first days after installation and after any collection gap.

**Evidence.** Live: `select id, owner_name, cron_schedule, description from recommendation_history where type='OFF_PEAK_SCALING'` (5 rows) and `select hour_of_day, sum(cpu_sample_count), sum(cpu_usage_avg*cpu_sample_count)/sum(cpu_sample_count) from workload_usage_hourly where target_name='e2e-oom' and container_name='' group by 1` (11 hours of day observed, all 0.000 except 21h = 0.361).

**Fix.** 1) Carry per hour of day the number of distinct days observed (add `days` to the hour profile SQL: count(distinct date(hour_bucket)) per hour_of_day) and require all 24 hours observed on at least max(7, RECOMMENDATION_MIN_DECREASE_COVERAGE_DAYS) distinct days, covering both weekdays and weekend. 2) Require the idle condition to hold on at least 90% of those days for each idle hour (store per hour-of-day the share of days below threshold, or the p90 of the hourly averages) rather than on the average. 3) Require a meaningful peak: peak hourly average >= max(10m, 5% of the request) so that a flat-zero workload is routed to the zombie analyzer instead. 4) Exclude StatefulSets (or require an explicit opt-in annotation) and workloads with PVCs. 5) Evidence text must list days observed. Existing active OFF_PEAK_SCALING records are recomputed by the next scan.

**Acceptance.** Fixture with 14 h of data -> no OFF_PEAK_SCALING. Fixture with 14 days where nights 00-06 are below 5% of peak on 13 of 14 days -> recommendation with schedule 00:00-06:00 and evidence '14 days observed'. Always-idle fixture -> no off-peak recommendation. StatefulSet fixture -> none.

#### REC-12 · Revision hysteresis freezes the whole record (evidence, sample count, coverage, description statistics, projected savings, impact, priority) at the first proposal while updated_at keeps advancing, so active recommendations show and are gated on statistics that can be many scans old (live: a recommendation still backed by 'sample_count 1' twelve hours and 729 samples later).

**Severity** medium · **Kind** data-integrity · **Confidence** confirmed-live · **Effort** M

**Where.** `src/greenkube/core/optimization/dedup.py:468-486`; `src/greenkube/core/optimization/dedup.py:489-508`; `src/greenkube/core/optimization/dedup.py:511-540`; `src/greenkube/core/optimization/engine.py:427-431`; `src/greenkube/automation/policy.py:905-957`

**Mechanism.** apply_revision_hysteresis() replaces, for every recommendation whose new proposed request differs from the stored one by more than 0 and at most 5% (same current request, same direction), all `_HELD_FIELDS` by the stored values: recommended_*, description, reason, priority, potential_savings_cost, potential_savings_co2e_grams, impact, evidence, proposal, consensus. The record is then upserted as a 'refresh': updated_at and expires_at move forward, nothing else. As long as each later scan lands within 5% of the stored value (but not exactly on it: `proposed == previous` returns False and refreshes), the record never changes. Evidence therefore keeps the sample_count, coverage_ratio, observation window, usage percentiles, OOM count and cost/CO2 projections of the scan that created the value. Downstream consumers read these as current: confidence scoring (risks._base_confidence uses evidence.sample_count/coverage_ratio), the automation policy gate `sample_count_coverage` (min_samples 36, min_coverage 0.5) and the UI. Because increases are created without any minimum evidence, they are typically created on the first scans after a workload appears, with a handful of samples, and then frozen.

**Effect on numbers.** Displayed usage statistics, sample counts, confidence and projected savings/costs of an active recommendation can be arbitrarily old; the automation gate can reject a well-observed recommendation forever (or, in the other direction, keep approving on old health evidence such as oom_events). Live kind: recommendation 42 (gk-upgrade/gkup-greenkube-api container api, memory 128->191Mi) has evidence.sample_count = 1 and observation_window_end = 2026-11-14T22:06:49Z, description 'uses p99 159MiB and max 159MiB', but updated_at = 2026-11-15 10:15; container_metrics now holds 729 samples for it, avg 164.4Mi, max 165.8Mi (fresh proposal 199Mi, 4.2% from 191Mi, so it is held). 9 of 30 active rightsizing recommendations on kind and 11 of 58 on minikube carry evidence more than one hour older than their last refresh (8 on minikube more than six hours older).

**Affected environments.** All.

**Evidence.** Live: `select count(*), count(*) filter (where (evidence::jsonb->>'observation_window_end')::timestamptz < updated_at - interval '1 hour') from recommendation_history where status='active' and type like 'RIGHTSIZING%'` -> kind 30/9, minikube 58/11; `select id, updated_at, evidence::jsonb->>'observation_window_end', evidence::jsonb->>'sample_count', description from recommendation_history where id in (17,36,42,44)` (kind); `select avg(memory_usage_bytes)/1048576, max(memory_usage_bytes)/1048576, count(*) from container_metrics where namespace='gk-upgrade' and pod_name like 'gkup-greenkube-api-%' and container_name='api'` -> 164.4, 165.8, 729.

**Fix.** Hold only what must stay stable for the action: the proposed request value (and the typed action/limit changes derived from it). In apply_revision_hysteresis() keep `recommended_*` (and `proposal.value`) from the stored record but take evidence, usage statistics, sample_count/coverage, confidence inputs, impact and potential savings from the new scan, recomputed for the HELD value (re-run the Impact projection and planned_limit_changes with the held request; regenerate description/reason from the fresh statistics with the held target, adding 'value held since <date>: new estimate X within 5%'). Add `held_since` and `fresh_estimate` to the consensus/proposal block. Do not create a revision when only evidence changes (compare proposal_signature only). Also bound the hold: release it when the cumulative drift from the value at hold start exceeds the threshold or after N days. No data migration: the next scan refreshes held records.

**Acceptance.** Unit: stored record (proposed 191Mi, evidence.sample_count=1) + new recommendation (proposed 199Mi, evidence.sample_count=729) -> result has recommended 191Mi, evidence.sample_count 729, impact recomputed for 191Mi, no new revision row. Integration: two scans one day apart with 3% drift -> evidence.observation_window_end of the stored record equals the second scan's window end. Live query above returns 0 stale-evidence rows.

#### REC-13 · Evidence coverage_ratio divides replica-samples by single-series expected steps, so a workload with N replicas reports N times its real coverage (capped at 1.0), and a computed coverage of 0 is stored as 1.0; confidence and the automation minCoverage gate rely on it.

**Severity** medium · **Kind** bug · **Confidence** confirmed-live · **Effort** S

**Where.** `src/greenkube/core/optimization/evidence.py:82-90`; `src/greenkube/core/optimization/evidence.py:322-326`; `src/greenkube/core/optimization/evidence.py:335`; `src/greenkube/core/optimization/risks.py:160-178`; `src/greenkube/automation/policy.py:905-957`

**Mechanism.** _utilization_stats() sums `sample_count` over every row of the target's series (all replicas, all nodes) and computes `coverage = min(1.0, sample_count / (window_seconds // step_seconds))`: the denominator is the number of steps one replica can have. Two replicas observed half of the time give 1.0. build_evidence() then stores `coverage_ratio=coverage or 1.0`, turning a computed 0.0 into full coverage. risks._base_confidence adds +0.15 when coverage_ratio >= 0.9 and +0.25 when sample_count >= RECOMMENDATION_MIN_SAMPLES (also replica-inflated), and AutomationPolicy's sample_count_coverage check compares the same fields with minSamples/minCoverage.

**Effect on numbers.** Coverage and confidence are overstated by the replica count. Live kind, same 13.3 h window and same collection gaps: single-replica Deployments (kustomize-controller, metrics-server, opencost, greenkube-api) have coverage_ratio 0.50 and confidence 0.65, while coredns with 2 replicas has sample_count 1584, coverage_ratio 0.996 and confidence 0.80. A 10-replica Deployment observed 10% of the time passes minCoverage 0.5 and gets the 'coverage' confidence bonus.

**Affected environments.** All multi-replica workloads (Deployments, StatefulSets, DaemonSets).

**Evidence.** Live kind: `select id, owner_name, evidence::jsonb->>'sample_count', evidence::jsonb->>'coverage_ratio', confidence, (select max(pod_count) from workload_usage_hourly w where ...) from recommendation_history where status='active' and type like 'RIGHTSIZING%'` -> id 3 coredns 1584 / 0.996 / 0.8 / pods 2; ids 1,2,4,10 single replica 798 / 0.502 / 0.65.

**Fix.** Compute coverage per replica-time: coverage = observed steps / expected steps where observed steps = summary.step_count (distinct collection steps with at least one sample of the target; already in the rollup) and expected = window_seconds / step_seconds since the target's first observation; or duration_seconds / (replicas_avg x window). Report both `sample_count` (replica-samples) and `step_count`. Replace `coverage or 1.0` by the computed value (0.0 stays 0.0; None when unknown, which gates must treat as failing). Use the per-step count for the minSamples gate and the confidence bonus. Recompute on the next scan (see the held-evidence finding so that it actually refreshes).

**Acceptance.** Evidence for a 2-replica workload observed in 50% of steps -> coverage_ratio 0.5 (+-0.02), same as a 1-replica workload with the same gaps. Series with no usable samples -> coverage_ratio 0.0 or None, never 1.0. Policy check rejects a 10-replica workload observed 10% of the time with minCoverage 0.5.

#### REC-14 · AUTOSCALING_CANDIDATE is driven by relative noise on near-zero CPU: 3 samples suffice, there is no minimum absolute usage, warm-up spikes count, and any kind is eligible, so idle workloads (0.01-1m average), single-replica databases and operators are told to 'use an HPA'; each threshold crossing also inserts a new history row.

**Severity** medium · **Kind** bug · **Confidence** confirmed-live · **Effort** S

**Where.** `src/greenkube/core/optimization/analyzers/autoscaling.py:32-58`; `src/greenkube/core/workload_usage.py:268-276`; `src/greenkube/core/workload_usage.py:654-657`; `src/greenkube/core/config.py:404-405`

**Mechanism.** AutoscalingAnalyzer computes `cv = stddev / mean` and `spike_ratio = max / mean` from the pod-level summary and emits when cv > 0.7 and spike_ratio > 3. Gates: `stats.sample_count >= 3`, request != 0, mean != 0. There is no floor on mean usage or on max relative to the request, so integer-millicore quantisation of an idle pod (samples of 0 and 1-2m) produces huge ratios. The variance uses every point including the warm-up after a (re)start (cpu_sum/cpu_sq_sum are accumulated before the warm-up split) and `max` keeps the startup spike by design. The pod-level summary pools all replicas and, for kind 'Node', different static pods. Only existing HPA targets are excluded; StatefulSets, operators/controllers and infrastructure namespaces are not (unlike zombie/off-peak). The condition sits on a noisy boundary without hysteresis: when it flips, reconciliation marks the record stale and the next crossing inserts a new recommendation_history row with the same fingerprint.

**Effect on numbers.** Advisory recommendations with no basis: live minikube, 13 of 17 active AUTOSCALING_CANDIDATE targets average below 1.2m CPU: flux-kustomize-review avg 0.01m, max 2m -> 'CV=19.65, spike ratio=387.0x'; metallb controller avg 0.02m -> 'spike ratio=145.1x'; blackbox-exporter 0.02m; vpa-admission-controller 0.04m; also test-postgres (single-replica PostgreSQL) and "Node 'minikube'". History growth: 18 stale + 16 active AUTOSCALING rows after 12 h, 6 rows for the single fingerprint v3:workload:greenkube-e2e-apps:e2e-oom:::Deployment:e2e-oom:AUTOSCALING_CANDIDATE created between 21:45 and 22:01.

**Affected environments.** All; worst on clusters with many idle or low-traffic workloads (dev, on-prem platforms).

**Evidence.** Live minikube: join of recommendation_history (type AUTOSCALING_CANDIDATE, active) with workload_usage_hourly pod-level averages (query in notes): 17 rows, values quoted above; `select fingerprint, count(*), string_agg(status||':'||id, ',') from recommendation_history group by 1 having count(*)>1`.

**Fix.** In AutoscalingAnalyzer: (1) require a meaningful load: p95 per-replica CPU >= max(50m, 30% of the request) and max >= 80% of the request (the workload actually needs more than one replica's worth at peak), with at least RECOMMENDATION_MIN_DECREASE_COVERAGE_DAYS of coverage and RECOMMENDATION_MIN_SAMPLES samples; (2) compute variability on warm-up-excluded points (add warm-up-excluded sum/sq-sum to the rollup or use p95/p50 instead of CV); (3) restrict to Deployments (and StatefulSets only with opt-in), exclude infrastructure namespaces and event-driven names via deletion_safety helpers, exclude kind Node/Pod/Job; (4) add hysteresis (enter at cv>0.7, leave at cv<0.5) and reactivate the existing stale row of the same fingerprint instead of inserting a new one (general rule for advisory types in upsert_recommendations: a stale row younger than the TTL with the same fingerprint is revived, with an event). Add retention for stale/expired recommendation_history rows.

**Acceptance.** Fixture: pod with samples in {0,1,2}m and request 100m over 14 days -> no AUTOSCALING_CANDIDATE. Fixture: Deployment with p95 600m / p50 100m of a 500m request over 14 days -> candidate. Kind StatefulSet/Node -> none. Condition toggling across 10 scans -> one row in recommendation_history for the fingerprint.

#### REC-15 · Native rightsizing ignores HorizontalPodAutoscalers: a CPU/memory request change on an HPA-scaled workload moves the HPA's utilisation ratio (usage / request), the scan only knows 'an HPA exists' (metrics unknown, risk unchanged), and savings are projected as if the replica count stayed constant.

**Severity** medium · **Kind** design · **Confidence** confirmed-code · **Effort** M

**Where.** `src/greenkube/core/optimization/enrich.py:66-84`; `src/greenkube/core/optimization/risks.py:288-293`; `src/greenkube/collectors/hpa_collector.py:25-50`; `src/greenkube/core/optimization/analyzers/rightsizing.py:282-340`; `docs/recommendation.md:182`

**Mechanism.** HPACollector.collect() returns only the set of scale targets. build_risk_profile() therefore sets hpa_resources = {'*'} and risks.py adds the factor `hpa_present` without raising the level ('An HPA with unknown metrics is only reported'). cpu_proposal()/memory_proposal() never look at context.hpa_targets. For a resource-utilisation HPA the per-replica usage is regulated to targetUtilization x request: (a) lowering the request by a factor k multiplies utilisation by k, the HPA scales out by up to k, and total requested CPU stays about constant: the projected saving (request delta x current replicas) does not materialise; (b) with the workload at minReplicas the per-replica usage observed is low by construction, so the engine keeps proposing decreases; (c) raising the request (the 'p99 > 90% of request' increase) lowers utilisation and makes the HPA scale in, which raises per-replica usage again: the increase rule fights the autoscaler.

**Effect on numbers.** Example: Deployment with HPA target 70% CPU, request 1000m, 4 replicas at 250m average each (at minReplicas). GreenKube proposes about 1000m -> 400m and projects 4 x 600m of released CPU. At the daily peak (600m per replica) utilisation becomes 150%, the HPA scales to about 9 replicas (9 x 400m = 3600m requested versus 4000m before): realised saving at peak about 10% of the projection, plus extra pods (memory requests x 2.25). The measurement stage may then report not_realized, but the recommendation and its projected saving were presented as low risk.

**Affected environments.** Every cluster using HPA on CPU or memory utilisation (the common case); not HPAs on external/custom metrics only.

**Evidence.** Code path: enrich.py:70-75 (`hpa_resources = frozenset({"*"})`, comment 'The HPA collector reports targets, not their metrics'), risks.py:288-293, no reference to hpa in analyzers/rightsizing*.py (grep), docs/recommendation.md:182 documents the 'only reported' behaviour. No live HPA-scaled workload was available to demonstrate it.

**Fix.** 1) Extend HPACollector to return, per scale target, minReplicas, maxReplicas and resource metrics (resource name, target type Utilization/AverageValue, target value, container for ContainerResource metrics). 2) In the rightsizing analyzers: when an HPA scales on Utilization of the resource being changed, do not emit a request change by default; emit instead an advisory that states the coupling and, if wanted, a coupled proposal (new request r' together with a new target utilisation u' = u x r / r' so that the scaling threshold in absolute millicores is unchanged) marked manual-only. For AverageValue or non-resource metrics proceed normally. 3) When the HPA scales on another resource (e.g. CPU HPA, memory change), size per-replica statistics as today but project savings with the observed replica distribution (replica-hours), not the latest replica count. 4) Set risk at least MEDIUM for `hpa_present` with unknown metrics and HIGH for `hpa_scales_on_resource` at scan time (not only from GitOps discovery evidence). 5) Update docs/recommendation.md.

**Acceptance.** Fixture with HPA(cpu Utilization 70%) on a Deployment: scan emits no automatic RIGHTSIZING_CPU change; evidence carries the HPA metrics; RIGHTSIZING_MEMORY is still emitted with risk factor hpa_present. Fixture with HPA on an external metric: both resources emitted. Risk assessment unit test: hpa_resources={'cpu'} and a CPU decrease -> level high, not eligible for auto-approval.

#### REC-16 · OVERPROVISIONED_NODE compares only pod usage with capacity: it ignores requests, has no coverage requirement, flags control-plane and single nodes, and in a namespaced scan the node usage is computed from that namespace's pods only while the cluster-scoped record is still written.

**Severity** low · **Kind** bug · **Confidence** confirmed-code · **Effort** S

**Where.** `src/greenkube/core/optimization/analyzers/nodes.py:41-66`; `src/greenkube/storage/workload_usage_sql.py:303-321`; `src/greenkube/core/optimization/context_builder.py:145-147`; `src/greenkube/core/optimization/engine.py:445-448`

**Mechanism.** OverprovisionedNodeAnalyzer flags a node when average pod CPU usage / capacity < NODE_UTILIZATION_THRESHOLD (0.2) and the same for memory. NodeUsageSummary.cpu_request_avg/memory_request_avg are computed but unused: a node whose capacity is 90% requested cannot be consolidated even at 5% usage. The only evidence gate is `usage.hours_observed > 0`. Usage is the sum of pod-level usage (kubelet, system daemons and unobserved pods excluded). node_sql() applies the scan's namespace filter, so `POST /api/v1/optimization/runs` with a namespace computes each node's utilisation from that namespace alone and the analyzer still emits cluster-scoped OVERPROVISIONED_NODE records, which _persist() upserts (the comment in engine.py acknowledges cluster-scoped records emitted by a namespaced scan).

**Effect on numbers.** Live kind: all three nodes including the control plane are flagged ('gk-cilium-control-plane has 1% average CPU utilization (124m / 14000m)'); minikube's only node was flagged three times (stale ids 68, 71, 75). In a namespaced scan of a small namespace every node appears at about 0% utilisation in the stored description until the next cluster-wide scan.

**Affected environments.** All; namespaced runs are triggered through the API/UI.

**Evidence.** Code path above; live rows `select id, description from recommendation_history where type='OVERPROVISIONED_NODE'` on kind (ids 12-14) and minikube (68, 71, 75).

**Fix.** Skip node analysis when context.namespace is set (or always read node usage cluster-wide: pass namespace=None to node_sql). Flag a node only when both usage and requests are below thresholds (max(usage, request)/allocatable < threshold), with at least DELETION_MIN_UNUSED_DAYS of observed hours, excluding control-plane nodes (node-role.kubernetes.io/control-plane label or taints) and clusters where removing the node leaves fewer nodes than required by the remaining requests; state requested and used shares in the text.

**Acceptance.** Namespaced scan -> no OVERPROVISIONED_NODE record written or modified. Node at 5% usage and 85% requested -> not flagged. Control-plane node -> not flagged. Single-node cluster -> not flagged.

#### REC-17 · The minimum-request floor is applied to increases after the step cap, so containers with tiny requests get 'reliability' increases straight to 10m / 16Mi (beyond the 2x cap) for a few millicores of usage.

**Severity** low · **Kind** bug · **Confidence** confirmed-live · **Effort** S

**Where.** `src/greenkube/core/optimization/thresholds.py:12-57`; `src/greenkube/core/optimization/analyzers/rightsizing.py:300-320`; `src/greenkube/core/optimization/analyzers/rightsizing_cpu.py:72-83`

**Mechanism.** cpu_proposal() caps an increase at RIGHTSIZING_MAX_INCREASE_FACTOR x request ('one step is always capped'). apply_minimum_thresholds() later raises any recommended value below RECOMMENDATION_MIN_CPU_MILLICORES / RECOMMENDATION_MIN_MEMORY_BYTES to the floor, whatever the direction. For a request below the floor the trigger `p99 > 0.9 x request` fires on 1-2m of usage and the final proposal is the floor, with priority 'high' when p99 > request.

**Effect on numbers.** Live minikube id 78: alertmanager 4m CPU request, p99 5m -> raw 6m, cap 8m, stored recommendation 10m (2.5x) with reason 'Reliability: p99 CPU usage (5m) is above 90% of the request ... Recommended value was below the minimum of 10m; floored'. Every sidecar with a 1-9m request produces such an increase and, with automation, a pull request and a negative-saving ledger entry.

**Affected environments.** All (sidecars and exporters with requests below 10m / 16Mi).

**Evidence.** Live: `select id, current_cpu_request_millicores, recommended_cpu_request_millicores, reason from recommendation_history where id=78` (minikube).

**Fix.** Apply the floors only to decreases. For increases, do not trigger when both the current request and p99 x headroom are below the floor (the absolute shortfall is below RECOMMENDATION_MIN_* : add a minimum absolute delta, e.g. 10m / 16Mi, for increases), and never exceed the step cap except for OOM-backed memory steps.

**Acceptance.** Summary request 4m, p99 5m -> no proposal. Request 4m, p99 40m -> increase capped at 8m (2x). Decrease to 3m -> floored to 10m only if 10m < current request.
