#### IMP-01 · Revision hysteresis freezes a recommendation's projected savings (impact, potential_savings_*, evidence) at the values of an earlier scan for as long as the proposal moves by 5% or less.

**Severity** high · **Kind** bug · **Confidence** confirmed-live · **Effort** M

**Where.** `src/greenkube/core/optimization/dedup.py:470-486`; `src/greenkube/core/optimization/dedup.py:489-540`; `src/greenkube/core/optimization/engine.py:428-431`

**Mechanism.** engine._persist calls apply_revision_hysteresis(kept, previous) before the upsert. When the new proposal differs from the stored one by at most REVISION_HYSTERESIS (5%) and the current request is unchanged, the new recommendation is overwritten with the stored record's _HELD_FIELDS: ('recommended_*', 'description', 'reason', 'priority', 'potential_savings_cost', 'potential_savings_co2e_grams', 'impact', 'evidence', 'proposal', 'consensus'). Holding the proposed value is the intent (no new revision for jitter), but the projection and the evidence are functions of the whole lookback (prices, hours observed, coverage, realization factor, grid intensity), not only of the proposed value, and they are held too. within_hysteresis() returns False only when the new proposal is exactly equal to the stored one or moved by more than 5%; a proposal that settles 1-5% away from the stored value (the normal case once more data arrives, e.g. first scan 135 MiB, later scans 137 MiB) is held on every scan, forever. The stored row is then refreshed (updated_at = now) with a projection computed at the revision's creation: typically the first scan after install or after the workload appeared, when the target had seconds or minutes of data, annualized under MIN_COVERAGE_HOURS with confidence 0.225, and possibly with the 'unobserved' realization factor 1.0. Fields that are not held (confidence, value, ranking_score, ranking_factors) are computed from the fresh projection before the hold, so value.components.cost_annual no longer matches impact.expected_billable_cost_annual of the same record.

**Effect on numbers.** potential_savings_cost, potential_savings_co2e_grams, impact.* and evidence statistics of held recommendations are stale by an unbounded amount, in either direction; in practice strongly understated for recommendations created from little data. Live minikube: recommendation 45 (vpa/vpa-recommender memory 500 MiB -> 45 MiB), updated_at 2026-11-15 10:27, first observed 2026-11-14 21:00, still carries impact.assumptions.coverage_hours 0.75, window_hours 1.0, capacity_released.replica_hours 73/yr (one 30 s sample annualized over 1 h) and allocated_cost_annual 0.138, where 0.445 GiB x 8760 h x 0.00423 USD/GiB-h = 16.5 per year (120x too low). Seven active minikube recommendations (30, 31, 34, 36, 44, 45, 73) are in this state: 8 of the 54 active minikube rightsizing rows and 9 of the 30 kind rows refreshed by the latest scan carry a projection with coverage below 6 h although their target was first observed more than 12 h earlier. Record 45's evidence block (also held) has observation_window_end 2026-11-14T21:44:42 and sample_count 1, while the CPU recommendation of the same container (id 23), written by the same scan, has observation_window_end 2026-11-15T10:35:41 and 729 samples. Simulation: stored 0.2065/yr, fresh 24.43/yr, persisted 0.2065/yr.

**Affected environments.** All (every database, every cluster). Most visible on fresh installs and for new workloads, and for memory recommendations (byte-level jitter).

**Evidence.** Live (both clusters): select id, revision, updated_at, impact->'assumptions'->>'coverage_hours', impact->'capacity_released'->>'replica_hours' from recommendation_history where status='active' and type like 'RIGHTSIZING%': rows updated in the latest scan carry coverage 0.75-1.99 h next to rows at 13.46 h. Simulation SIM 5 in audit-workspace/audit/work/impact-projection/sim_projection.py (output: sim_projection.out) drives the real apply_revision_hysteresis with a stored record projected from 30 s of data and a fresh recommendation projected from 14 days: the persisted record keeps the stored impact (window_hours 1.0, 0.2065/yr instead of 24.43/yr).

**Fix.** 1. In dedup.py split _HELD_FIELDS in two: the advice (recommended_cpu_request_millicores, recommended_memory_request_bytes, proposal, description, reason, priority, consensus) stays held; potential_savings_cost, potential_savings_co2e_grams, impact and evidence must be recomputed for the HELD value from the current scan. 2. Give apply_revision_hysteresis a 'reproject' callable (the existing consensus_refiner(context) does exactly this: ImpactProjector.for_context(context).container_resources(ref, context.container_rows(target, container), <resource>=(current, held_value))) and call it for every held recommendation; rebuild evidence with enrich._enrich_one and re-score (score_recommendations) after the hold so value/ranking_score derive from the persisted impact. engine._persist must therefore receive the scan context (today it only gets the recommendations). 3. The revision logic must ignore impact-only changes (no new revision when only the projection moved); check storage upsert/revision comparison (recommendation_revisions) so a refreshed projection does not bump 'revision'. 4. Add an invariant in the upsert path: impact.assumptions.window_hours of a record written by scan S must come from scan S (e.g. persist impact.assumptions.window_end / scan run id and assert it equals the run). 5. Backfill: none needed, the next scan rewrites every active record; applied/verifying records keep the projection they were applied with (see the separate finding on baselines if measurement reads them).

**Acceptance.** Unit: stored record (memory 1 GiB -> 300 MiB, impact projected from one 30 s sample) + fresh recommendation 310 MiB projected from 14 days of hourly rows -> persisted record has recommended 300 MiB, unchanged revision, impact.assumptions.window_hours == 336, potential_savings_cost within 1% of the projection of (1 GiB -> 300 MiB) over the 14-day rows, and value.components.cost_annual == impact.expected_billable_cost_annual. Integration (SQLite): run three scans with proposals 300, 310, 305 MiB while rows accumulate; after each scan impact.assumptions.coverage_hours equals the scan's coverage; recommendation_revisions has one row.

#### IMP-02 · Container rows receive the pod's CPU cost by CPU-usage share (not reservation share), so per-container request cuts are priced with a per-core price that can be 100x too low or several times too high.

**Severity** high · **Kind** bug · **Confidence** confirmed-live · **Effort** L

**Where.** `src/greenkube/core/metric_assembler.py:223-264`; `src/greenkube/core/metric_assembler.py:283-285`; `src/greenkube/impact/recompute.py:80-131`; `src/greenkube/impact/model.py:144-202`; `src/greenkube/core/optimization/context.py:85-91`

**Mechanism.** MetricAssembler.assemble_container_metrics splits the pod aggregate between its containers with cpu_weights = container CPU usage (falling back to memory usage, then requests) and memory_weights = container memory usage: cost_cpu = aggregate.cost_cpu * share, cost_ram = aggregate.cost_ram * memory_share, and co2e_grams, embodied_co2e_grams and joules = aggregate * share (CPU usage share). But the pod's cost is a reservation cost (OpenCost and the Impact Model both charge max(request, usage)), and idle power and embodied emissions are allocated by CPU reservation. The projector prices a container change with sample_prices(): price per reserved core-hour = sample.observed_cost_cpu / (max(request, usage) x hours) of the container rows returned by context.container_rows(). A container that reserves much and uses little (the over-provisioned sidecar rightsizing targets) therefore gets a near-zero price, and the container that does the work gets the cost of its siblings' reservations. The docstring claim 'CPU cost follows the CPU share' hides that the share is usage. BUG-15's guarantee (a cut never claims more than the container is charged) holds only because the container is charged the wrong amount.

**Effect on numbers.** allocated_cost_annual / potential_savings_cost of CPU and memory rightsizing on any container of a multi-container pod; also container_metrics.total_cost, cost_cpu, cost_ram, co2e_grams, embodied_co2e_grams, joules and everything derived (container_metrics_hourly, workload_usage_hourly container rows, per-container dashboards, cost/CO2e baselines of change measurement when they read container rows). Live minikube: recommendation 18 (monitoring/kube-state-metrics container kube-rbac-proxy-main, CPU 20m -> 10m) is priced at 0.00015 USD/core-h where the node's observed price is 0.0316: allocated 0.0058/yr instead of 38.7 core-h x 0.0316 = 1.22/yr (210x too low); recommendation 78 (alertmanager CPU 4m -> 10m) is priced at 0.110 USD/core-h (3.5x too high); config-reloader containers at 0.0000021-0.000035 USD/core-h; opencost-ui RAM at 0.0011 instead of 0.0042 USD/GiB-h. Stored rollup for kube-state-metrics: containers request 20m/10m/10m but hold 0.2%/0.15%/89% of the pod's cost_cpu. Simulation: sidecar 500m -> 50m projected 1.56/yr, truth 118.26/yr; app 100m -> 95m projected 7.80/yr, truth 1.31/yr.

**Affected environments.** All clusters where container usage is collected (metrics-server or cAdvisor), with or without OpenCost; every pod with two or more containers (sidecars, service mesh, config reloaders, kube-rbac-proxy).

**Evidence.** Live: select container_name, max(cpu_request), sum(cost_cpu) from workload_usage_hourly where namespace='monitoring' and target_name='kube-state-metrics' group by 1 -> ''(pod) 40m 0.008366; kube-rbac-proxy-main 20m 0.0000179; kube-rbac-proxy-self 10m 0.0000128; kube-state-metrics 10m 0.007472. impact.assumptions.unit_prices of live recommendations 18, 78, 39, 40, 105, 29, 79 (minikube). Simulation SIM 3 in audit-workspace/audit/work/impact-projection/sim_projection.py (output: sim_projection.out) calls the real assemble_container_metrics then the real projector.

**Fix.** In assemble_container_metrics allocate each quantity with the weight that produced it at pod level: (a) cost_cpu and the CPU-reservation part of energy/CO2e (idle share) and embodied_co2e_grams by container CPU reservation max(cpu_request, cpu_usage) (per container, same step); (b) cost_ram and memory power by container memory reservation max(memory_request, memory_usage); (c) dynamic CPU energy/CO2e by CPU usage share (as today); (d) storage/network/lb/other cost: keep at pod level only or split by reservation, but never by usage. Because the pod row stores only total joules/co2e, either store the idle/dynamic/memory split on the pod row (preferred: the allocator already computes EnergyBreakdown in impact/rows.py::_apply) or run MetricRowAllocator at container granularity (one ResourceSample per container, ref with container) so container rows are produced by the same model as pod rows; the sum over containers must equal the pod row for every column (add this as an invariant test and a controller self-check metric). Native sidecars and containers without usage keep their request as reservation. Projector hardening: in impact/model.py::sample_prices cap/floor the observed per-container price to the node's unit price band (e.g. if observed price deviates more than 2x from node.cpu_price_per_reserved_core_hour record assumptions.price_anomaly and use the node price) so a wrong split can never produce a 100x error silently. Data migration: bump the container row calculation_version; provide a recompute path (greenkube recompute) that re-splits stored container_metrics / container_metrics_hourly / workload_usage_hourly container rows from their pod rows for the retention window, and mark older rows as legacy so projections fall back to pod-level prices for them.

**Acceptance.** Unit: pod (cost_cpu 0.018/h, 0.6 cores reserved) with app (request 100m, usage 90m) and sidecar (request 500m, usage 1m) -> container cost_cpu = 0.003 and 0.015 (+-1%); sum of container rows == pod row for total_cost, cost_cpu, cost_ram, co2e_grams, embodied_co2e_grams, joules. Projection: sidecar 500m -> 50m = 118.26/yr +-2%, app 100m -> 95m = 1.31/yr +-2%, both with unit_prices.cpu_core_hour = 0.03. Live check after recompute: for every multi-container target, sum(cost_cpu)/sum(max(request,usage) core-hours) per container is within 5% of the pod-level value.

#### IMP-03 · On clusters without a detected autoscaler every rightsizing projection is 0 cost and 0 CO2e, reliability increases are classified in the 'savings' lane, and every expected value is negative, so ranking and the savings totals carry no information.

**Severity** high · **Kind** design · **Confidence** confirmed-live · **Effort** M

**Where.** `src/greenkube/impact/realization.py:55`; `src/greenkube/impact/realization.py:494-500`; `src/greenkube/impact/projector.py:633-635`; `src/greenkube/core/optimization/scoring.py:189-200`; `src/greenkube/core/optimization/scoring.py:276-302`; `src/greenkube/core/optimization/eligibility.py:714-717`; `frontend/src/routes/recommendations/+page.svelte:2337-2343`

**Mechanism.** With no Cluster Autoscaler ConfigMap and no Karpenter NodePool, resolve_realization gives the cluster pool FACTOR_NO_AUTOSCALER = 0.0. impact_fields() writes potential_savings_cost = expected_billable_cost_annual = allocated x 0 and potential_savings_co2e_grams = co2e_expected_annual_g = allocated x 0. Consequences: (1) every request change of every size shows 0 (the list and the dashboard hide falsy values, so nothing is shown at all); the allocated value (what chargeback/showback and the change measurement will actually report) is only in impact.allocated_cost_annual. (2) scoring.compute_value takes cost and CO2e from the expected fields, so value_annual = 0 for all of them and EV = -(1 - p) x incident_cost - effort: always negative, and ordered only by risk class and blast radius, never by the size of the change. (3) lane = 'reliability' if value_annual < 0: an increase has allocated cost < 0 but expected cost -0.0, so on fixed nodes it is put in the 'savings' lane, gets no incident_cost_avoided, no 'reliability_change' eligibility warning, and is counted in the savings opportunity. (4) The default RECOMMENDATION_INCIDENT_COST = 100 (a one-off amount) is compared with one year of saving: with p about 0.62 a medium-risk singleton needs more than 23/yr of expected saving to reach EV > 0, which most single-container changes do not reach on any cluster. The projection confidence (impact.confidence, which carries the short-coverage and price-tier penalties) is not used by p_success either.

**Effect on numbers.** Live, both clusters (minikube and kind, fixed nodes): 88 active rightsizing recommendations, all with potential_savings_cost = 0 and potential_savings_co2e_grams = 0 although allocated_cost_annual ranges from -24.4 to +29.2 per year; all 30 request increases (including OOM-driven ones) have value.lane = 'savings' and incident_cost_avoided = 0; ranking_score is negative for all 88 (-9.0 to -67.1). Simulation SIM 4: the same memory increase scores lane 'savings', EV -6.00 at factor 0 and lane 'reliability', EV +5.34 at factor 1. On bare metal / kubeadm / k3s / kind / minikube and on cloud clusters with fixed node groups the product therefore reports 'no savings' for its main recommendation kind while the measured (allocated) savings of the same change are non-zero.

**Affected environments.** On-prem and any cluster without Cluster Autoscaler/Karpenter (or with an undetected autoscaler); the lane and EV-unit problems affect all clusters whenever the pool factor is 0 (Karpenter zero disruption budget) or savings are small.

**Evidence.** Live: select value_score::jsonb->>'lane', direction, count(*), min/max(ranking_score) from recommendation_history where status='active' and type like 'RIGHTSIZING%' -> minikube savings/increase 20, savings/decrease 38; kind savings/increase 10, savings/decrease 20; EV between -67.12 and -9.00. impact of kind recommendation 1: allocated_cost_annual 12.68, expected_billable_cost_annual 0.0, realization_reasons ['No Cluster Autoscaler or Karpenter: fixed nodes, savings stay allocated, not billed']. Simulation SIM 4 in audit-workspace/audit/work/impact-projection/sim_projection.py (output: sim_projection.out).

**Fix.** 1. Decide the lane from the direction of the change, not from the sign of a realization-scaled number: in scoring.compute_value use impact.allocated_cost_annual < 0 (or proposed > current for request kinds) for 'reliability'. 2. Rank by a value that exists on fixed nodes: value_annual = expected billable cost + carbon value + ALLOCATED_VALUE_WEIGHT x (allocated - expected) where the released-capacity value is a documented, configurable weight (default 1.0 for showback users on fixed nodes is defensible: freed capacity is reusable; expose RECOMMENDATION_CAPACITY_VALUE_WEIGHT in config/Helm/docs), and keep both numbers in value.components. 3. Expose two explicit API fields on every recommendation and stop overloading potential_savings_cost: projected_allocated_cost_annual / projected_allocated_co2e_annual_g (always shown, label 'allocated: capacity released') and projected_billable_cost_annual (shown with the realization factor and its reason). The UI list, the dashboard top list and the PR body must show the allocated figure with the note 'fixed nodes: frees capacity, bill unchanged until nodes are removed' instead of hiding a 0. 4. Units of EV: amortize the incident cost or state the horizon explicitly (value over RECOMMENDATION_VALUE_HORIZON_YEARS, default 1) and document it; add a test that a 20m CPU cut and a 2-core cut on the same workload do not get the same EV. 5. Multiply the Beta prior confidence by impact.confidence (or carry it as a separate factor in value.components). 6. Add the 'reliability_change' warning from the change direction. Migration: scores are recomputed at every scan and on read (decide_records); no stored data to migrate, but ranking_score history changes: note it in CHANGELOG.

**Acceptance.** With RealizationContext = fixed nodes: (a) memory increase 1 GiB -> 2 GiB with oom_history -> lane 'reliability', incident_cost_avoided > 0, eligibility warning 'reliability_change'; (b) CPU cut 2000m -> 200m ranks above CPU cut 20m -> 10m on identical workloads; (c) API returns projected_allocated_cost_annual = 236.52 and projected_billable_cost_annual = 0 for the SIM 4 workload and the UI row shows the allocated figure with the fixed-nodes note; (d) opportunityOf() does not count increases.

#### IMP-04 · Projections are annualized over the span since the target's first observed hour, so collection gaps and the hour-floor of the first observation bias every projection low (live: -49%).

**Severity** medium · **Kind** design · **Confidence** confirmed-live · **Effort** M

**Where.** `src/greenkube/impact/model.py:319-356`; `src/greenkube/impact/model.py:627-631`; `src/greenkube/impact/projector.py:484-525`; `src/greenkube/impact/projector.py:199-227`; `docs/methodology/impact-model.md:224-240`

**Mechanism.** annual = window_total x 8760 / window_hours, where window_total is the sum over the target's samples and window_hours = window_end - first_observed (ObservedCoverage). Two biases: (1) hours in which the collector produced no rows (controller down longer than Prometheus retention can backfill, Prometheus outage or missing series, host suspended, rows dropped) stay in the denominator but contribute nothing to the numerator. The model cannot tell 'the workload did not run' (CronJob: intended, documented at impact-model.md:235) from 'nobody was looking'. (2) first_observed comes from the usage summary's first_hour, an hour bucket: a target first sampled at hh:35 gets a coverage that starts at hh:00, so up to one hour without samples is added to the denominator (dominant for young targets, where MIN_COVERAGE_HOURS = 1 also applies). No assumption field reports the observed sample hours, so the bias is invisible in the API, the UI and the PR body.

**Effect on numbers.** allocated_cost_annual, expected_billable_cost_annual, co2e_*_annual_g and capacity_released of every container_resources / workload_deletion / replica_schedule projection, understated by (1 - observed hours / coverage hours). Live kind: both installs lost about 6.6 h of collection during the night (12-23 steps per hour instead of 120 between 2026-11-15 00:00 and 06:00): recommendation 1 (flux-system/kustomize-controller, one always-on replica, CPU 100m -> 10m) reports capacity_released.replica_hours 4454-4463/yr instead of 8760 and allocated_cost_annual 12.68-12.71 instead of 0.09 x 0.0316 x 8760 = 24.9 (-49%); every minikube projection shows 3867 replica-hours/yr (-56%). Hour-floor: simulation SIM 2 gives 216.81 instead of 236.52 (-8%) for a 55-minute-old target; the bias is up to -50% for a target first sampled late in an hour and scanned early in the next.

**Affected environments.** All; proportional to the share of the lookback without collected rows. Largest on laptops/edge/on-prem clusters that stop, after Prometheus outages, and for targets younger than a few hours.

**Evidence.** Live: select date_trunc('hour',timestamp), count(distinct timestamp) from combined_metrics where namespace='flux-system' and pod_name like 'kustomize-controller%' -> 51,120,93,12,23,18,18,14,17,43,120,120,120,54 steps per hour; the stored impact has window_hours 13.44, annualization_factor 651.77, replica_hours 4453.7. Hand recomputation from the stored rollup rows (sum duration 6.83 h, cost_cpu / reserved core-hours = 0.03163) reproduces the stored 12.68 exactly, which shows the arithmetic is right and the denominator is the cause. Simulation SIM 1 and SIM 2 in audit-workspace/audit/work/impact-projection/sim_projection.py (output: sim_projection.out) (12 h gap in 24 h: 118.26 instead of 236.52).

**Fix.** Annualize over the hours the collector actually observed the cluster during the target's coverage: window_hours = sum over collection steps within [first_observed, window_end] of the step duration, taken from a cluster-level source that is independent of the target (the distinct step timestamps of combined_metrics / the rollup's per-hour step_count maximum over all targets, or collection_watermarks). Implementation: (a) add to UsageSnapshot an 'observed_seconds_by_hour' map (per hour bucket: max step_count over targets x step seconds, capped at 3600) computed by one extra SQL aggregate in workload_usage_sql; (b) ImpactProjector.coverage() returns ObservedCoverage with observed_hours = sum of that map from the target's first sample to window_end (so a CronJob keeps its real duty cycle, and a cluster-wide gap is excluded for every target); (c) store first_sample_at (not only first_hour) in workload_usage_hourly/summary, or derive the first hour's observed seconds from the same map, to remove the hour-floor bias; (d) report assumptions.sample_hours, assumptions.collected_hours and assumptions.collection_gap_hours, and lower impact.confidence when collected_hours / coverage < 0.9; (e) update docs/methodology/impact-model.md (Annualization) and bump IMPACT_MODEL_VERSION (numbers change). No stored-data migration: projections are recomputed at each scan.

**Acceptance.** One always-on replica, 1000m -> 100m at 0.03/core-h: with 24 hourly rows -> 236.52/yr; with rows missing for 12 of the 24 hours for ALL targets -> 236.52/yr +-1% and assumptions.collection_gap_hours = 12; a CronJob with rows 1 h per day while other targets have rows all day -> annualized at 1/24 duty cycle (unchanged from today). Target first sampled at hh:35 and scanned at hh+1:30 -> projection within 2% of the continuous value. capacity_released.replica_hours of an always-on single replica is 8760 +-1% in all three cases.

#### IMP-05 · Workload deletions (zombie pods, idle namespaces) are projected with a realization factor of 1 even on fixed nodes, so their 'expected billable' saving is overstated and they outrank every rightsizing.

**Severity** medium · **Kind** design · **Confidence** confirmed-live · **Effort** S

**Where.** `src/greenkube/impact/model.py:370-373`; `src/greenkube/impact/model.py:466-515`; `src/greenkube/core/optimization/analyzers/zombie.py:77-79`; `src/greenkube/core/optimization/analyzers/idle_namespace.py:91-94`; `docs/methodology/impact-model.md:265-269`

**Mechanism.** ReservationAwareImpactModel.project() calls _project_sample_removal(..., realization_factor=1.0, realization_source='deletion') for WorkloadDeletion, while a request cut and a replica schedule on the same samples use pool.realization_for(samples) (0.0 on fixed nodes, 0.6 with Cluster Autoscaler, 0.8 with Karpenter). Deleting pods releases reserved capacity exactly like cutting their requests to zero: the bill only falls when a node goes away. The released idle-power share and embodied share are not saved on a fixed node either; only the dynamic (usage-based) energy really disappears, and that is the smallest part for an idle workload. The documentation states the rule ('Realization factor: 1') without justification.

**Effect on numbers.** potential_savings_cost and potential_savings_co2e_grams of ZOMBIE_POD and IDLE_NAMESPACE are overstated by 1 / pool factor (infinite on fixed nodes, 1.67x with Cluster Autoscaler, 1.25x with Karpenter) relative to every other compute recommendation. Live kind (fixed nodes): GET /api/v1/recommendations/top?metric=cost returns only an orphan PV (6.00) and two IDLE_NAMESPACE rows (3.38 and 1.72 per year 'projected savings') while the 30 rightsizing rows, whose allocated value is up to 29.2 per year each, show 0; minikube returns an empty list. Simulation SIM 4: same workload, CPU cut 236.52 allocated -> 0 expected; deletion 297.84 allocated -> 297.84 expected, CO2e 8257.8 g expected of which only the dynamic part is physical.

**Affected environments.** All; worst on fixed-node (on-prem) clusters.

**Evidence.** Live kind recommendation_history ids 29, 30 (impact.realization_factor 1.0, realization_factor_source 'deletion') next to id 1 (factor 0.0, 'realization_context'); live API top list above. Simulation SIM 4 in audit-workspace/audit/work/impact-projection/sim_projection.py (output: sim_projection.out). Code path model.py:370-373.

**Fix.** In ReservationAwareImpactModel.project() resolve the factor of WorkloadDeletion like ReplicaScheduleChange: factor, source, reasons, pools = pool.realization_for(baseline.samples); pass them to _project_sample_removal and _record_realization. For CO2e apply the factor per component in _finalize: dynamic_operational and gpu_operational of a removed workload are saved at factor 1 (the energy is no longer consumed), idle_operational, memory_operational and embodied at the pool factor; expose co2e_expected_annual_g = sum(component x its factor) and keep co2e_measurable_expected_annual_g consistent (types.ImpactDelta.co2e_measurable_expected_annual_g currently multiplies everything by one factor). Storage/LB cost components of the deleted workload's observed cost (cost_storage, cost_lb, cost_network in observed_cost_breakdown) are billed per asset and keep factor 1. Update docs/methodology/impact-model.md (Workload deletion, Realization factor) and bump IMPACT_MODEL_VERSION.

**Acceptance.** Fixed-node context: deletion of a workload with allocated 297.84/yr -> expected_billable_cost_annual 0, realization_factor_source 'realization_context', co2e_expected_annual_g == annualized dynamic CO2e only. Cluster Autoscaler context: expected = 0.6 x (cpu + ram) + 1.0 x (storage + lb + network). A CPU cut to the usage level and a deletion of the same idle workload differ only by the usage-based terms.

#### IMP-06 · The 'projected' savings of applied changes and the user-declared bound use the realization-scaled (billable) projection, while measurement and the ledger use allocated values, so pending and realized panels are on different bases.

**Severity** medium · **Kind** data-integrity · **Confidence** confirmed-live · **Effort** M

**Where.** `src/greenkube/core/recommendation_realization.py:31-47`; `src/greenkube/core/optimization/lifecycle.py:381-391`; `src/greenkube/core/savings_attributor.py:150-165`; `src/greenkube/core/savings_attributor.py:236-255`; `src/greenkube/impact/projector.py:633-635`

**Mechanism.** When a recommendation is applied, lifecycle stores cost_saved / carbon_saved_co2e_grams = estimate_realized_savings() = potential_savings_* x share, i.e. the expected billable projection (allocated x realization factor). savings_attributor._projection_components accrues these as method 'projected' (projected_annual, projected_to_date / prorated_* of GET /recommendations/savings) until the change is measured; the measurement and the ledger then report allocated savings (measured_basis 'measured_allocated', compared with impact.allocated_cost_annual per docs/methodology/savings-measurement.md). The same quantity therefore changes basis when it moves from 'projected' to 'measured'. validate_user_declared_savings() bounds a declared saving by 10x potential_savings_* and rejects any declaration when the projection is 0, which is every rightsizing on fixed nodes.

**Effect on numbers.** projected_annual / projected_to_date / prorated_* understate the pending allocated saving by the realization factor (100% on fixed nodes, 40% with Cluster Autoscaler, 20% with Karpenter) and jump at verification. Live: 6 applied changes on minikube and 2 on kind have cost_saved = 0 and carbon_saved_co2e_grams = 0 while their impact.allocated_cost_annual sums to 10.40 and 20.14 per year; GET /api/v1/recommendations/savings on kind returns projected_annual.cost 0.0 with applied_count 2. On-prem users cannot declare a saving on PATCH /apply (ValueError 'no projected cost_saved to bound a declared value').

**Affected environments.** All clusters whose realization factor is below 1 (every cluster once the context is observed).

**Evidence.** Live: select status, verification_status, count(*), sum(cost_saved), sum(potential_savings_cost), sum(impact->>'allocated_cost_annual') from recommendation_history where status not in ('active','stale') -> applied|inconclusive|6|0|0|10.401 (minikube), applied|inconclusive|2|0|0|20.141 (kind); live /recommendations/savings payload. Code path read end to end.

**Fix.** Carry both bases explicitly and never mix them: (1) at apply time store projected_allocated_cost_annual / projected_allocated_co2e_annual_g (from impact.allocated_cost_annual and comparison.measurable_co2e_projection(impact, allocated=True), scaled by _realized_share) and projected_billable_* (allocated x realization factor) in the record baseline; (2) _projection_components accrues the allocated projection for the 'projected' panels that sit next to 'measured allocated', and the realization block of /recommendations/savings derives the billable expectation from it (it already does so for measured values); (3) validate_user_declared_savings bounds against the allocated projection and accepts a declaration when only the billable projection is 0; (4) label the API fields with their basis (basis: 'allocated' | 'billable'). Backfill: recompute cost_saved / carbon_saved_co2e_grams of records in applied/verifying from their stored impact (one migration, idempotent); ledger entries with method 'projected' written on the billable basis must be restated by an appended correction entry, not edited (hash chain).

**Acceptance.** Fixed-node cluster, CPU cut with impact.allocated_cost_annual 236.52 applied in full -> /recommendations/savings.projected_annual.cost == 236.52 with basis 'allocated' and realization.realized_billed_cost_annual_expected == 0; after a 'realized' measurement of 230/yr the projected block drops to 0 and run_rate_annual.cost == 230 (same basis, no jump larger than the projection error). PATCH /apply with cost_saved=200 is accepted on a fixed-node cluster; 3000 is rejected.

#### IMP-07 · The UI sums the projected savings of overlapping recommendations (namespace deletion, workload deletion, off-peak, rightsizing, node consolidation) and the two totals disagree on negative values.

**Severity** medium · **Kind** ux · **Confidence** confirmed-code · **Effort** M

**Where.** `frontend/src/routes/recommendations/+page.svelte:380-381`; `frontend/src/routes/recommendations/+page.svelte:2204-2213`; `frontend/src/lib/overview.js:55-79`

**Mechanism.** 'Potential CO2 Savings' and 'Potential Cost Savings' on the recommendations page are actionableActiveRecs.reduce((s, r) => s + (r.potential_savings_* ?? 0), 0): every active record is added, including request increases (negative values) and records whose targets overlap. overview.js opportunityOf() adds only positive values and skips the reliability lane, so the dashboard and the recommendations page show different totals for the same set. Overlaps that are added: IDLE_NAMESPACE (all workloads of the namespace, factor 1) + ZOMBIE_POD / RIGHTSIZING_* / OFF_PEAK_SCALING of workloads in that namespace; OFF_PEAK_SCALING + RIGHTSIZING_* of the same workload (the rightsizing is annualized over all hours, including those the schedule removes); OVERPROVISIONED_NODE (node removal at factor 1) + the realization-scaled rightsizing of the pods on those nodes, which is the same node-hours counted twice. CPU and memory records of one container are additive (different resources) and are not a problem.

**Effect on numbers.** Example on a Karpenter pool: a namespace with 10 workloads allocated 1000/yr, each also flagged for a 50% CPU cut (400/yr expected at 0.8): the page shows 1000 + 400 = 1400 'potential' for at most 1000 achievable. With a consolidation recommendation for the pool the node-hours are added a third time. With increases present the recommendations page total is lowered by their cost while the dashboard total is not.

**Affected environments.** All; visible wherever deletion, schedule or node recommendations coexist with rightsizing (autoscaled clusters; on fixed nodes only the deletion/asset rows are non-zero).

**Evidence.** Code read: +page.svelte:380-381 and overview.js:55-79. Live kind has IDLE_NAMESPACE rows for namespaces whose workloads also carry RIGHTSIZING rows (ids 29/30 with workloads of gk-waste and greenkube-e2e-apps).

**Fix.** Compute the opportunity server side, once, as a de-duplicated total and return it from GET /recommendations/active (or /recommendations/summary): group active records by target containment (namespace > workload > container; node pool > node) and for each compute scope keep max(parent, sum of children) for allocated and for billable separately; treat OFF_PEAK + RIGHTSIZING of one workload as max(); never add OVERPROVISIONED_NODE to pod-level billable savings of the same pool (report 'billable via consolidation' = max of the two); exclude the reliability lane and report its cost apart. Both pages must render that one figure with the label 'Projected (not additive across overlapping recommendations)' and show allocated and billable separately. Add a frontend unit test and an API test.

**Acceptance.** Fixture: IDLE_NAMESPACE ns1 (100/yr) + two RIGHTSIZING_CPU in ns1 (20/yr, 30/yr) + one RIGHTSIZING_MEMORY increase in ns2 (-5/yr) + one RIGHTSIZING_CPU in ns2 (10/yr) -> opportunity = 110, reliability cost = 5, identical on the dashboard and on the recommendations page.

#### IMP-08 · There is no feedback loop from measured outcomes to projections: realization factors, the incident cost and the annualization are constants, and the measured projection accuracy is never used.

**Severity** medium · **Kind** architecture · **Confidence** confirmed-code · **Effort** XL

**Where.** `src/greenkube/impact/realization.py:55-59`; `src/greenkube/core/optimization/track_record.py:1-15`; `src/greenkube/core/optimization/scoring.py:203-222`; `src/greenkube/core/optimization/dedup.py:194-207`; `docs/roadmap.md:2381`; `docs/roadmap.md:2490`

**Mechanism.** The documented target is a median measured / projected (allocated) ratio of 0.7-1.3, then 0.8-1.2 with 90% CI coverage >= 85% (docs/roadmap.md:2490) and 'within 20%' on the quickstart (2381). The only feedback that exists is binary: TrackRecordBook counts realized / not_realized outcomes per (source family, capability) and moves p_success and the weighted consensus. The measured projection_accuracy stored in change_measurements.estimates is not read by anything that produces a projection; the realization factors (0.0 / 0.3 / 0.6 / 0.8, x0.5 with blockers) are unreferenced constants that no observation ever corrects (node-hours actually removed after a change are never compared with the released capacity); the projection's own confidence is not validated against outcomes. A systematic bias such as the -49% gap bias or the container price error above is therefore invisible to the product: no metric, no alert, no API field reports the running accuracy of projections.

**Effect on numbers.** All projected figures: any systematic error stays uncorrected and unreported. On the live clusters no measurement carries a projection_accuracy (all 8 are inconclusive), so the documented accuracy target cannot currently be demonstrated at all.

**Affected environments.** All.

**Evidence.** Code read: grep for projection_accuracy shows writers in verify/strategy.py and readers only in API/UI rendering; realization constants in realization.py:55-59; track_record.py docstring. Live: select outcome from change_measurements -> inconclusive only.

**Fix.** Add a ProjectionCalibration component (new module greenkube/impact/calibration.py + table projection_calibration). (1) Inputs: every decided, non-contaminated measurement with a claimed allocated_cost / co2e estimate and a projection: ratio r = measured_annual / projected_allocated_annual, keyed by (impact model version, change kind, capability, source family, cost tier, coverage bucket <1d / 1-7d / >7d). (2) Statistic: robust log-ratio estimate per key (median and MAD of ln r, shrunk toward 0 with a prior worth 10 observations; require n >= 5 before use). (3) Use: do NOT silently rescale the point estimate; attach to every projection impact.calibration = {ratio_median, ci80_low, ci80_high, n, key} and report projected_low / projected_high = projection x CI bounds; use the calibrated median in value_annual for ranking only when n >= 20, with the factor recorded in value.components. (4) Realization: per pool, estimate the realized factor = (node-hours removed attributable to released capacity over the 30 days after changes) / (capacity released in node-hour equivalents), from node_snapshots_scd and applied changes; report it next to the constant and replace the constant once n >= 10 changes (realization_factor_source 'measured'). (5) Observability: Prometheus gauges greenkube_projection_accuracy_ratio{kind,capability,quantile} and greenkube_projection_accuracy_samples, an API block in GET /recommendations/track-record, and an alert rule in the chart when the median leaves 0.7-1.3 with n >= 10. (6) Invariant checks at projection time (cheap, no measurement needed): replica_hours of an always-on target within 5% of 8760 x replicas; observed unit price within 2x of the node price; allocated saving <= observed workload cost. Violations set impact.assumptions.anomalies and lower impact.confidence. Docs: add 'Calibration' to docs/methodology/impact-model.md and savings-measurement.md.

**Acceptance.** Seed 30 measurements with measured / projected = 0.5 for (container_resources, cpu_rightsizing, greenkube, opencost, >7d): new projections of that key carry impact.calibration.ratio_median in [0.45, 0.55], n = 30, the gauge reports 0.5 and the alert fires; with 3 measurements no calibration is applied and impact.calibration.n == 3. A projection whose replica_hours is 4454 for a single always-on replica carries assumptions.anomalies ['replica_hours_below_expected'].

#### IMP-09 · Minimum-request clamping is applied after the projection, so a clamped recommendation stores one value and the projection (and description) of another.

**Severity** medium · **Kind** bug · **Confidence** confirmed-live · **Effort** S

**Where.** `src/greenkube/core/optimization/dedup.py:455-458`; `src/greenkube/core/optimization/thresholds.py:12-57`; `src/greenkube/core/optimization/analyzers/rightsizing.py:305-306`

**Mechanism.** finalize_entries() projects consolidated values (analyzer projection, or materialize -> refine) and then runs apply_minimum_thresholds(rec, config), which raises recommended_cpu_request_millicores / recommended_memory_request_bytes to RECOMMENDATION_MIN_* and appends '(Floored to minimum ...)' to the description, but leaves potential_savings_*, impact and impact.assumptions.change untouched. The native analyzers floor decreases before projecting, but not increases (rightsizing.py: proposed = max(_capped(raw, request, factor), request + 1)); VPA, Goldilocks, KRR, Kubecost and pushed signals are not floored either. The stored record then proposes the minimum while its projection, its description ('Recommend increasing to 6m') and the baseline used later for projection accuracy describe another value. For a decrease from a request below the minimum, the clamp turns the advice into an increase that keeps a positive saving.

**Effect on numbers.** Live minikube recommendation 78 (monitoring/alertmanager-main container alertmanager, 3 replicas): current 4m, recommended 10m, description 'Recommend increasing to 6m. (Floored to minimum: 10m CPU.)', impact.assumptions.change.cpu_request_cores [0.004, 0.006], allocated_cost_annual -2.61 where the projection of 4m -> 10m from the same rows is -7.83 (the cost of the increase is understated 3x; it is the only one of 41 hand-recomputed CPU projections that does not match). Third-party example: current 100m, VPA target 2m, minimum 10m: stored proposal 10m with a projection for 98m released instead of 90m.

**Affected environments.** All clusters with requests near or below RECOMMENDATION_MIN_CPU_MILLICORES (10m) / RECOMMENDATION_MIN_MEMORY_BYTES, and clusters with third-party sources proposing values below the minimums.

**Evidence.** Live: select current_cpu_request_millicores, recommended_cpu_request_millicores, impact->'assumptions'->'change' from recommendation_history where id=78 (minikube) -> 4 | 10 | {cpu_request_cores: [0.004, 0.006]}. Hand recomputation (audit-workspace/audit/work/impact-projection/recompute.sql) gives -7.832 against the stored -2.611. Code read: dedup.py:455-458; thresholds.py has no projector call.

**Fix.** Clamp before projecting. (1) In analyzers/rightsizing.py floor increase proposals too (proposed = max(proposed, RECOMMENDATION_MIN_*)) and build the description from the floored value. (2) In dedup, apply the minimum inside materialize() on consensus.value, before refine, so a changed value is always re-projected and re-described; keep apply_minimum_thresholds only as an assertion. (3) Drop the recommendation when the clamped value equals the current request, and when a clamped decrease becomes an increase without a reliability trigger. (4) Invariant at RecommendationRecord creation: impact.assumptions.change proposed value == recommended_* (unit-converted, 1m / 1 MiB tolerance); reject or re-project otherwise. No migration: the next scan rewrites active records.

**Acceptance.** Analyzer increase 4m -> 6m with minimum 10m: stored recommended 10m, description 'increasing to 10m', impact.assumptions.change.cpu_request_cores == [0.004, 0.010] and allocated_cost_annual equal to the projection of 4m -> 10m. VPA signal 100m -> 2m: stored 10m with change [0.1, 0.01]. VPA signal 8m -> 5m: no recommendation. A query for records whose change differs from recommended_* returns no row after a scan.

#### IMP-10 · The native node analyzer emits OVERPROVISIONED_NODE without any projection (and for control-plane nodes), although node removal is the only change that lowers the bill on fixed nodes and the projector can price it.

**Severity** medium · **Kind** design · **Confidence** confirmed-live · **Effort** M

**Where.** `src/greenkube/core/optimization/analyzers/nodes.py:23-91`; `src/greenkube/impact/projector.py:579-583`; `src/greenkube/impact/model.py:555-609`

**Mechanism.** NodesAnalyzer builds its Recommendation without impact_fields(): potential_savings_cost, potential_savings_co2e_grams and impact stay NULL, so the record has value_annual 0 and a negative EV (node scope: blast radius x3). ImpactProjector.node_removal() / NodePoolChange exist and are used only by the Karpenter provider. The analyzer also flags every under-used node independently, including control-plane nodes, so a 3-node cluster gets three 'consolidate' records that cannot all be applied. On fixed-node clusters this is the one recommendation kind whose saving is fully billable (realization 1 by construction: the action is the node removal), and it is the one shown without a number, while request cuts show 0.

**Effect on numbers.** Missing number rather than a wrong one: live kind has 3 active OVERPROVISIONED_NODE rows (nodes at 1% CPU, 4% memory of 14 cores / 39 GiB each) with potential_savings_cost NULL. With the stored observed prices (0.0316 USD/core-h, 0.0042 USD/GiB-h) one worker is 14 x 0.0316 + 39.1 x 0.0042 = 0.61 USD/h, 5320 USD per year of capacity that the page reports as no saving; GET /recommendations/top?metric=cost omits them.

**Affected environments.** All clusters without Karpenter (the Karpenter provider projects its own NodePool advice); most relevant on-prem.

**Evidence.** Live kind: select id, target_node, potential_savings_cost, impact from recommendation_history where type='OVERPROVISIONED_NODE' and status='active' -> 3 rows, NULL, NULL (one is gk-cilium-control-plane). Code read of nodes.py (no projector call).

**Fix.** Rework NodesAnalyzer into a pool-level consolidation estimate: (1) exclude control-plane nodes (node-role.kubernetes.io/control-plane label or NoSchedule taint) and unschedulable nodes; (2) compute how many schedulable nodes are needed for the pool's summed reservations (max of CPU and memory request utilization, plus a configurable headroom, the logic of providers/karpenter._removable_nodes) and emit ONE recommendation per pool/cluster naming the removable nodes (cheapest first), not one per node; (3) project it with ImpactProjector.node_removal(removed_nodes, action_is_consolidation=True) and attach impact_fields(delta); (4) in the UI and PR text cross-reference it from request-cut records on fixed nodes ('billable only once N nodes are removed: see recommendation #x'); (5) the de-duplicated total (separate finding) must not add this figure to pod-level billable savings of the same pool. Node price must come from the node's resolved UnitPrices with its tier recorded; on-prem without configured rates label it custom_rate/opencost_default with confidence 0.5.

**Acceptance.** 3 identical workers at 5% request utilization and one control-plane node -> exactly one OVERPROVISIONED_NODE record, removed_nodes = 2 workers, impact.kind 'node_pool', expected_billable_cost_annual == 2 x node hourly price x 8760, realization_factor_source 'consolidation_action'; no record names the control-plane node.

#### IMP-11 · Orphaned PersistentVolumes on non-cloud CSI or NFS storage are projected at the cloud default rate as an expected billable saving, and OpenCost PV/LB costs are annualized over the whole lookback.

**Severity** low · **Kind** portability · **Confidence** confirmed-code · **Effort** M

**Where.** `src/greenkube/core/optimization/analyzers/orphaned_pv.py:57-86`; `src/greenkube/collectors/pv_collector.py:283-289`; `src/greenkube/collectors/lb_collector.py:540-546`; `src/greenkube/impact/model.py:517-549`

**Mechanism.** orphaned_pv.py prices a Released/Failed PV without an OpenCost cost at capacity_gib x STORAGE_COST_PER_GIB_MONTH (default 0.10) x 12 and projects it through asset_deletion with realization factor 1 ('expected billable'). Local and hostPath volumes are excluded by the collector, but NFS, Ceph/Rook, Longhorn, OpenEBS and other on-prem CSI volumes are not billed per GiB; the LoadBalancer analyzer has an explicit bare-metal branch (cost 0, CostSource.NONE), the PV analyzer has none. When OpenCost does return a cost, enrich_orphaned_pv_costs / enrich_orphaned_lb_costs compute annual_cost = window_cost x 365 / RECOMMENDATION_LOOKBACK_DAYS: an asset (or an OpenCost install) younger than the lookback is annualized as if its cost were spread over the whole window, the bias that model 2.0.0 removed for workloads. The PV cost is read from the OpenCost allocation API aggregated by persistentvolume, which reports volumes mounted by pods; a Released volume is unlikely to appear there, so the default rate is probably always used (not verified live).

**Effect on numbers.** potential_savings_cost of ORPHANED_PERSISTENT_VOLUME: 0.10 x GiB x 12 shown as billable on clusters where the true billable saving is 0 (a 500 GiB Ceph volume shows 600/yr); OpenCost-priced orphan PV/LB understated by lookback / asset age (a 2-day-old LoadBalancer in a 14-day lookback: 7x too low). Live kind: the only orphan PV (5 GiB, test CSI driver) is projected at 6.00/yr from STORAGE_COST_PER_GIB_MONTH with confidence 0.5 and is rank 1 of /recommendations/top?metric=cost.

**Affected environments.** On-prem and any cluster with self-hosted storage; clusters where OpenCost is younger than the lookback.

**Evidence.** Code read end to end; live kind recommendation 31 (impact.assumptions.cost_detail 'STORAGE_COST_PER_GIB_MONTH', realization_factor 1.0).

**Fix.** (1) Classify the volume's billing in pv_collector: cloud CSI drivers and in-tree cloud volume sources (ebs.csi.aws.com, pd.csi.storage.gke.io, disk.csi.azure.com, cinder.csi.openstack.org, Scaleway, OVH...) are billable; anything else is 'self_hosted': project allocated = capacity x configured rate but expected billable = 0 with cost_source NONE and a note 'self-hosted storage: capacity released, no bill', unless the operator sets STORAGE_COST_PER_GIB_MONTH explicitly (then CUSTOM_RATE 'configured'). AssetDeletion needs a realization_factor field instead of the hard-coded 1.0. (2) Annualize OpenCost asset costs over min(lookback, asset age, OpenCost data span): use the per-asset 'minutes' / start-end returned by OpenCost (annual = window_cost x 525600 / minutes). (3) Read orphan PV cost from the OpenCost assets API (/assets?filter type=Disk) rather than allocation. (4) Document in docs/recommendation.md.

**Acceptance.** Released 100 GiB PV with csi.driver rook-ceph.rbd.csi.ceph.com and no configured rate -> expected_billable_cost_annual 0, allocated 120, note present; same PV with ebs.csi.aws.com -> 120 billable at confidence 0.5. OpenCost returns 0.10 for a LoadBalancer observed 2880 minutes in a 14d window -> annual 18.25, not 2.61.

#### IMP-12 · The realization factor silently defaults to 1.0 until the controller process has completed a first autoscaler read (or when that read fails), and to 0.0 for any autoscaler that is not detected.

**Severity** low · **Kind** reliability · **Confidence** confirmed-code · **Effort** M

**Where.** `src/greenkube/impact/realization.py:70`; `src/greenkube/impact/realization.py:335-354`; `src/greenkube/impact/realization.py:640-659`; `src/greenkube/impact/projector.py:378-387`; `src/greenkube/core/historical_range_processor.py:588-593`; `src/greenkube/collectors/autoscaler_collector.py:258-270`

**Mechanism.** The realization context is a process-local cache (_STATE) filled only inside HistoricalRangeProcessor's collection window, after the OpenCost read. It is not persisted. After every controller restart, if the first collection window fails before that line (Prometheus unreachable at start-up) or the Kubernetes read returns None, the forced initial scan projects with UNOBSERVED_FACTOR = 1.0 (expected = allocated). A pool whose autoscaler input cannot be read (RBAC, timeout) and has no previous observation also gets 1.0. Combined with the revision hysteresis finding, a projection made with 1.0 can then be kept on the record for a long time. In the other direction, detection only knows the kube-system/cluster-autoscaler-status ConfigMap and karpenter.sh NodePools: a Cluster Autoscaler run with another --status-config-map-name or namespace, or a managed control-plane autoscaler that publishes neither, is classified 'none' and gets 0.0 (plausible; not verified on a cloud provider).

**Effect on numbers.** expected_billable_cost_annual and co2e_expected_annual_g (and potential_savings_*) switch between the full allocated amount and 0 depending on process state: the SIM 4 CPU cut is 236.52 with an unobserved context and 0 once fixed nodes are observed. The source is recorded in impact.assumptions ('realization_context:unobserved') but is not surfaced as a warning in the UI or the PR body.

**Affected environments.** All controllers after a restart with a failing first collection; clusters with restricted RBAC; clusters whose autoscaler is not one of the two detected forms.

**Evidence.** Code read end to end. Live controllers show realization_factor_source 'realization_context' for all request changes, so the normal start-up order (collection, then scan) works; the failure paths were not reproduced live.

**Fix.** Persist the resolved RealizationContext (table realization_context: observed_at, pools JSON) at each refresh and load it at process start (current_realization_context falls back to the stored one when the process cache is empty and it is younger than 24 h). Refresh it before, and independently of, the Prometheus/OpenCost reads. When no context exists, project expected = None (unknown) instead of 1.0 and render 'billable saving unknown: cluster not observed yet'. Add IMPACT_AUTOSCALER_HINT (none | cluster_autoscaler | karpenter) and the Cluster Autoscaler ConfigMap name/namespace to the configuration and Helm values; log the detected autoscaler per pool at start-up and expose it in /api/v1/health.

**Acceptance.** Controller restarted with Prometheus down and a stored fixed-node context: the initial scan projects expected_billable_cost_annual 0 with realization_factor_source 'realization_context'. With no stored context: expected billable is null, allocated 236.52, and the UI shows the 'not observed yet' note. IMPACT_AUTOSCALER_HINT=cluster_autoscaler yields factor 0.6 without the ConfigMap.

#### IMP-13 · The 5% revision hysteresis is a one-step relative band without memory: proposals that alternate between two nearby values create a new revision at every flip.

**Severity** low · **Kind** reliability · **Confidence** confirmed-live · **Effort** S

**Where.** `src/greenkube/core/optimization/dedup.py:468-508`

**Mechanism.** within_hysteresis() compares the new proposal with the stored one only: abs(proposed - previous) <= 0.05 x previous. For small integers (millicores) 5% is less than the natural step of a percentile-based proposal: 56m and 60m differ by 7%, so a workload whose p99 alternates between two samples flips the stored advice on every flip, each time superseding the previous revision, resetting the description and re-arming eligibility/warm-up logic that keys on the revision. Inside the band the opposite problem applies (projection frozen, see the first finding).

**Effect on numbers.** Live kind recommendation 6 (monitoring/prometheus CPU, current 200m): 11 revisions in 13 h, proposals 16, 19, 26, 29, 36, 56, 60, 56, 60, 56, 51 with allocated projections between 3.82 and 25.84 per year; greenkube-postgres CPU has 9 revisions, greenkube-api CPU 8. The displayed saving of one unchanged workload moves by up to 7x across scans (mostly the short-coverage annualization during the first hours).

**Affected environments.** All; strongest with short scan intervals, small requests and young targets.

**Evidence.** Live: select revision, created_at, snapshot->>'recommended_cpu_request_millicores', snapshot->'impact'->>'allocated_cost_annual' from recommendation_revisions where recommendation_id=6 (kind).

**Fix.** Use a deadband with an absolute floor and with memory: hold when abs(new - stored) <= max(5% x stored, RECOMMENDATION_MIN_CPU_MILLICORES for CPU, 16 MiB for memory); when outside the band, require the new value to be confirmed by K consecutive scans (default 3) or to move in the safer direction (a higher request is accepted at once) before creating a revision; quantize analyzer proposals to a geometric grid (for example round up to the next 5% step, minimum 5m) so jitter maps to the same value. Do not create revisions for targets whose coverage is below 1 h (keep the record as 'warming up' with no projection). Always refresh the projection for the held value (first finding).

**Acceptance.** Sequence of scan proposals 56, 60, 56, 60, 56 for a 200m request -> one revision (56 or 60, the higher under 'safest'), projection refreshed at each scan. Sequence 56, 80, 80, 80 -> second revision created at the third 80.
