#### LED-01 · The ledger accrues 'projected' savings on the expected-billable basis (allocated x realization factor, 0 on fixed-capacity clusters) and 'measured' savings on the allocated basis, so the two methods are not comparable, nothing accrues before verification on-prem, and restatement cannot credit the pre-verification period.

**Severity** high · **Kind** design · **Confidence** confirmed-live · **Effort** L

**Where.** `src/greenkube/impact/projector.py:634`; `src/greenkube/core/savings_attributor.py:150-164`; `src/greenkube/core/savings_attributor.py:167-192`; `src/greenkube/verify/service.py:1167-1186`; `src/greenkube/verify/service.py:1244`; `src/greenkube/verify/ledger/projector.py:276-282`; `src/greenkube/verify/ledger/projector.py:351-390`; `src/greenkube/core/savings_report.py:113-169`; `src/greenkube/api/metrics_endpoint.py:1105-1121`

**Mechanism.** (1) The recommendation columns potential_savings_cost / cost_saved (and the CO2e columns) are filled from the Impact Model's expected_billable_cost_annual = allocated_cost_annual x realization_factor (impact/projector.py:634 `"potential_savings_cost": delta.expected_billable_cost_annual`). (2) SavingsAttributor._projection_components accrues record.cost_saved / carbon_saved_co2e_grams under method 'projected'. (3) After a REALIZED measurement, ChangeMeasurementService stores measured_cost_saved = cost.claimed_annualized, where the cost estimate is the member's share of the measured ALLOCATED cost delta (service.py:1244 compares it with allocated_projection(member)); the attributor accrues it as 'measured' (v2: measured_allocated). (4) The v2 projector skips zero amounts (`if value == 0.0: continue`), and _restate only restates existing projected entries. Consequences: on any cluster whose realization factor is 0 (single node, fixed bare-metal capacity, no autoscaler: minikube, kind, most on-prem) the projection is exactly 0, so NOTHING accrues between applied_at and verified_at, projected_annual and projected_to_date are 0, and the restatement has no projected entry to replace: the saving of the whole measurement window (default 72 h, up to weeks when extended) is never booked although the measurement proved it. Where the factor is between 0 and 1, 'projected' and 'measured' totals differ by 1/factor for a perfectly accurate projection, greenkube_savings_measured_vs_projected_ratio and the report's side-by-side blocks compare different bases, and a restatement replaces a billable-basis amount with an allocated-basis amount without saying so. The ledger entry carries no 'basis' field, and method 'projected' is documented only as 'projection of an applied change that is not measured yet'.

**Effect on numbers.** Live (both clusters): all 8 applied rightsizing recommendations have cost_saved = 0 and carbon_saved_co2e_grams = 0 while impact.allocated_cost_annual is 0.43 to 18.10 USD/yr (realization_factor 0.0); savings_ledger_entries and recommendation_savings_ledger are empty after 12 h of applied changes. Simulation SIM2: change applied 96 h ago, verified 24 h ago, measured allocated 0.01/h. Factor 0: ledger shows measured 0.24 in total (true allocated saving since apply: 0.96, 75% missing; with the default 72 h window every change loses at least 3 days of saving). Factor 0.3: projected accrued 0.216 for 72 h against a measured 0.72 for the same hours (x3.3 jump at restatement although the projection was exact on its own basis). Direction: projected understated by (1 - factor); realized-to-date understated by the measurement window on fixed-capacity clusters.

**Affected environments.** All; worst on-prem and single-node clusters (realization factor 0), any cloud cluster without a node autoscaler, and every namespace whose pool cannot scale down.

**Evidence.** Live SQL (minikube and kind): select id, cost_saved, potential_savings_cost, impact::jsonb->>'allocated_cost_annual', impact::jsonb->>'expected_billable_cost_annual', impact::jsonb->>'realization_factor' from recommendation_history where applied_at is not null -> cost_saved 0, allocated 4.0152/0.4350/5.3998/0.6669/-0.0580/-0.0580 (minikube) and 2.0367/18.1046 (kind), factor 0.0 everywhere; select ... from savings_ledger_entries -> 0 rows. Simulation audit-workspace/audit/work/ledger-savings/sim_ledger.py (function sim2), output: factor 0 -> only 'measured_allocated cost accrued +0.24'; factor 0.3 -> projected accrued +0.216, restatement reversal -0.216, restatement measured +0.72, accrued measured +0.24.

**Fix.** Make the basis explicit and keep one basis per method. (a) Add a `basis` column to recommendation_savings_ledger and savings_ledger_entries (values allocated | billable_expected | billed; part of the canonical hash payload for new entries, via a new method_version so old hashes stay valid) and expose it in /api/v2/ledger/entries, /summary totals, CSV and FOCUS exports, and in SavingsBlock. (b) Accrue the projection on the ALLOCATED basis: in SavingsAttributor._projection_components use impact.allocated_cost_annual (fallback potential_savings_cost when the impact has none) and the matching allocated/measurable CO2e component (the same value verify/attribution.py allocated_projection and _measurable_co2e use), so 'projected' and 'measured_allocated' are the same quantity before and after measurement and restatement is like for like. Keep the billable expectation as a derived, clearly labelled figure (allocated x realization_factor) in the report (realization block) and never store it as an accrual. (c) Restatement must not depend on a non-zero projected entry: for each verified recommendation, generate restatement entries for every grid slot in [applied_at, verified_at) (entry_key restatement:slot:{rec}:{slot}:{quantity}); when a projected entry exists for the slot, reverse it as today. (d) Rename/re-document greenkube_savings_measured_vs_projected_ratio, or compute it on the allocated basis only. (e) Migration: existing v1 projected rows keep basis billable_expected (backfill the column from the recommendation's impact); do not rewrite them; on upgrade run one pass that writes the missing slot restatements for already verified recommendations. (f) Update docs/methodology/savings-measurement.md section 12 and the UI vocabulary (frontend/src/lib/savingsVocabulary.js) to state the basis of each block.

**Acceptance.** 1. Record with impact.allocated_cost_annual=87.66 (0.01/h), realization_factor=0, applied 96 h ago, pending: after accrue() the ledger holds 96 projected rows summing 0.96 with basis=allocated; report.realization shows billable expected 0. 2. Same record verified 24 h ago with measured 0.01/h: v2 restated measured_allocated = 0.96, restated projected = 0, and the v1-compatible report shows realized_to_date 0.96 (see LED-04). 3. Factor 0.3 and an exact projection: projected accrued over 72 h equals measured restated over the same 72 h (ratio 1.0, not 0.3). 4. Every entry returned by /api/v2/ledger/entries has a non-null basis; the FOCUS export carries x_greenkube_basis.

#### LED-02 · The v1 ledger, the accrual watermark and the v2 projection are keyed by CLUSTER_NAME, which falls back to the pod's node name; when the controller pod moves to another node the savings history is split and the rows written under the old name are never reported nor projected.

**Severity** high · **Kind** data-integrity · **Confidence** confirmed-live · **Effort** M

**Where.** `src/greenkube/core/config.py:1141-1184`; `src/greenkube/core/savings_attributor.py:98-102`; `src/greenkube/storage/postgres/savings_repository.py:150-157`; `src/greenkube/storage/postgres/savings_repository.py:179-224`; `src/greenkube/storage/ledger_repository.py:354-409`; `src/greenkube/verify/ledger/projector.py:202-241`; `src/greenkube/api/routers/ledger.py:162-170`; `src/greenkube/api/routers/recommendations.py:349-351`; `helm-chart/templates/configmap.yaml:66`

**Mechanism.** config.clusterName defaults to '' in the chart, so CLUSTER_NAME is auto-detected. When the synchronous node listing in Config._list_nodes_in_cluster returns None (any exception is swallowed at DEBUG level), the name is K8S_NODE_NAME, the node the pod runs on. The live 3-node kind cluster shows exactly this: cluster_identities has two rows for one cluster UID (gk-cilium-worker resolved 21:38, gk-cilium-worker2 resolved 21:49) and accrual_watermarks has one row per name, although the service account may list nodes (kubectl auth can-i list nodes = yes) and the documented result for a multi-node cluster without a label is 'default'. Everything in the v1 ledger is keyed by that name: accrual_watermarks(cluster_name, stream), recommendation_savings_ledger.cluster_name, get_accrual_breakdown / get_lifetime_counters WHERE cluster_name = $1, and the projector reads `l.cluster_name = ?` with the current name (ledger_repository._v1_scope). After a name change: (1) GET /recommendations/savings, the dashboard gauges and the lifetime counters only see rows written under the new name; (2) the watermark of the new name is absent, so a new 7-day initial backfill runs (only the per-recommendation floor prevents double accrual); (3) v1 rows of the old name that were not yet projected are never projected, and greenkube_ledger_unprojected_rows stays 0 because count_unprojected_v1_rows also filters on the current name, so the GreenKubeLedgerProjectionBacklog alert cannot fire; (4) the v2 chain (keyed by UID) contains entries of both names, so the 'accrued book reconciles with v1' invariant is broken; (5) if the API pod and the controller pod run on different nodes, the API resolves another name: get_identity(name) returns None and every /api/v2/ledger endpoint answers an empty ledger while /recommendations/savings reads the wrong name.

**Effect on numbers.** SIM3: a change saving 0.01/h, verified 48 h ago; controller on node 'worker' for 24 h, then on 'worker2'. Report under the current name: 0.24 instead of 0.48 (50% lost; in general everything accrued before the last pod move disappears from realized_to_date, projected_to_date, the *_attributed_* counters, which reset, and the dashboard windows). 24 v1 rows are left without v2 entry while the backlog gauge reports 0. Direction: always understated; counters are non-monotonic across the move.

**Affected environments.** Multi-node clusters where the name is not set explicitly and node listing fails at start-up (seen live on kind with Cilium, 3 nodes); any install where config.clusterName is later changed; HA setups with API and controller on different nodes. Single-node clusters are stable only while the node name is stable.

**Evidence.** Live (GK_ENV=kind): select * from cluster_identities -> 'gk-cilium-worker | 97d1af52-... | kube-system | 21:38:40' and 'gk-cilium-worker2 | 97d1af52-... | kube-system | 21:49:43'; select * from accrual_watermarks -> one row per name; GET api/v2/ledger/summary -> cluster_name gk-cilium-worker2; kubectl get nodes -> 3 nodes; kubectl auth can-i list nodes --as=system:serviceaccount:greenkube:<sa> -> yes. The reason the node listing failed is not established (logged at DEBUG only). Simulation audit-workspace/audit/work/ledger-savings/sim_ledger.py (sim3): 'v1[worker2] measured cost=+0.24', 'v1[worker] measured cost=+0.24', 'unprojected v1 rows counted for worker2: 0 | really without v2 entry (name worker): 24', identity of 'worker' = None.

**Fix.** 1. Key all ledger state by the stable cluster UID, not by the label: add cluster_uid to recommendation_savings_ledger, recommendation_savings_ledger_hourly and accrual_watermarks (migration: fill from cluster_identities by cluster_name; rows whose name has no identity get the UID of the only identity when exactly one UID exists, else stay NULL and are reported). SavingsAttributor, both savings repositories (breakdown, counters, floors, watermark, has_legacy_compressed_rows), the projector's _v1_scope / count_unprojected_v1_rows and the API (ledger.py _cluster, recommendations.py) must filter on cluster_uid. cluster_name stays a display label. 2. Merge existing split watermarks: for one UID keep MAX(watermark). 3. Config._auto_detect_cluster_name: never return a node name when more than one node may exist; retry the node listing (3 attempts with backoff) and log the failure at WARNING; when detection is impossible use the stored cluster_identities name of the UID if one exists (resolve the name after the DB is reachable) else 'default'. 4. Add a start-up invariant: if cluster_identities holds another name for the same UID, log an error, expose greenkube_cluster_name_conflict{uid} = 1 and reuse the first name for writes. 5. Helm: document that config.clusterName should be set, and make NOTES.txt warn when it is empty. 6. One-off repair command (greenkube ledger repair-cluster-name) that reassigns v1 rows of old names to the UID and lets the projector catch up.

**Acceptance.** 1. Unit: with K8S_NODE_NAME=a then b and the node listing failing, savings_cluster identity is the same and get_accrual_breakdown returns the rows written under both. 2. SIM3 rerun: report = 0.48, v2 measured = 0.48, greenkube_ledger_unprojected_rows = 0 with 48 entries. 3. Migration test: a database with two names for one UID ends with one watermark (the max) and every v1 row carrying the UID. 4. API pod and controller pod with different K8S_NODE_NAME return the same /api/v2/ledger/summary.

#### LED-03 · Projected savings accrued before a NOT REALIZED or INCONCLUSIVE verdict stay in the ledger for ever (never reversed, superseded or restated), and a measured loss is never booked.

**Severity** medium · **Kind** data-integrity · **Confidence** confirmed-sim · **Effort** M

**Where.** `src/greenkube/core/savings_attributor.py:187-192`; `src/greenkube/verify/ledger/projector.py:84`; `src/greenkube/verify/ledger/projector.py:351-390`; `src/greenkube/core/optimization/lifecycle.py:855-915`; `src/greenkube/cli/start.py:387-401`; `docs/methodology/savings-measurement.md:331`

**Mechanism.** BUG-30 was fixed only going forward: accrual_components returns [] once verification_status is failed/inconclusive, so no new row is written. The rows written between applied_at and the verdict are kept: mark_not_realized and mark_inconclusive leave the status at 'applied' and call nothing on the ledger; supersede_for_recommendation is only called for rollback_review (start.py) and phantom repair; the projector restates only RESTATED_STATUSES = ('verified', 'regressed'). For not_realized the signed measured value (possibly negative: the change costs more) is stored on the recommendation but never reaches the ledger. docs/methodology/savings-measurement.md documents this as a 'restatement limit', but neither the API nor the UI flags it: projected_to_date, the v2 'projected' totals (both books), greenkube_*_savings_attributed_*{method=projected} and the exports keep presenting, as savings to date, projections that a measurement has refuted.

**Effect on numbers.** SIM1: two changes projected at 0.01/h, applied 72 h before their verdicts (one not_realized with a measured loss of 0.005/h, one inconclusive). Ledger after the verdicts: projected cost +1.44 (144 entries), 0 reversal, 0 restatement, measured 0. Correct books: projected 0 for the refuted change and a measured -0.36. Per failed change the overstatement is projected_rate x (verdict time - applied_at), i.e. at least the 72 h default window and up to the extended window; it never decays, so the all-time 'projected to date' only grows with every failed change.

**Affected environments.** All.

**Evidence.** Simulation audit-workspace/audit/work/ledger-savings/sim_ledger.py (sim1) output: 'projected cost accrued accrual value=+1.440000 n=144', 'v2 superseded 0 restated 0', v1 projected cost=+1.44. Code: lifecycle.mark_not_realized / mark_inconclusive contain no ledger call; grep supersede_for_recommendation -> only cli/start.py:399 and core/optimization/phantom_repair.py:291.

**Fix.** Derive corrections from the recommendation state in the projector instead of one-shot side effects. In LedgerProjector add a step 'refuted projections': load recommendations whose latest decided measurement is not_realized (status applied, verification_status failed) or that are failed/reverted; for every unreversed projected accrual of such a recommendation with period_end <= decided_at of that measurement write (a) a reversal (reason 'not_realized', book restatement) and (b) when the measurement has a signed measured value, a restatement entry at that measured annual rate (method measured_allocated, may be negative, linked to measurement_id). For inconclusive verdicts keep the projection but tag it: add reason/flag 'unverified' in the summary (a separate total `projected_unverified`), and stop presenting it inside projected_to_date without that label. Mirror in v1 so the legacy report agrees: either set superseded on those v1 rows or (preferred, see LED-04) serve the report from the v2 restated totals. A later successful re-measurement (request_remeasure -> verified) must restate from the new measurement: allow a second restatement keyed by measurement_id (entry_key restatement:{projection}:{measurement_id}) that reverses the previous restatement first. Add REASONS 'not_realized'. Backfill: the step is idempotent and state-derived, so the first pass after upgrade corrects existing rows.

**Acceptance.** SIM1 after the fix: v2 restated projected cost = 0.72 only for the inconclusive change and reported under projected_unverified; change 1 shows projected restated 0 and measured_allocated restated -0.36; GET /recommendations/savings projected_to_date excludes the refuted change; running the pass twice writes nothing new; chain verification passes.

#### LED-04 · The numbers users actually see (GET /recommendations/savings, overview hero, Prometheus counters and dashboard gauges) come from the mutable v1 ledger and never include restatements, so they disagree with the hash-chained v2 ledger and understate realized savings by the whole pre-verification period.

**Severity** medium · **Kind** design · **Confidence** confirmed-sim · **Effort** L

**Where.** `src/greenkube/api/routers/recommendations.py:327-360`; `src/greenkube/core/savings_report.py:113-169`; `src/greenkube/api/metrics_endpoint.py:433-492`; `src/greenkube/api/metrics_endpoint.py:722-756`; `frontend/src/lib/api.js:450`; `frontend/src/lib/overview.js:81`; `src/greenkube/verify/ledger/projector.py:422-457`; `src/greenkube/core/migrations/scripts/postgres/0042_savings_ledger_accrual_grid.sql:1`

**Mechanism.** Restatement (BUG-39) exists only in the v2 'restatement' book. The v1 table recommendation_savings_ledger is still the source of realized_to_date / projected_to_date, of the greenkube_*_savings_attributed/reversed counters, of greenkube_dashboard_savings_* and of the UI hero. In v1 the projected rows of a verified change stay 'projected' for ever and the measured rows start at verified_at. Two official figures therefore exist for the same question: v1 'realized_to_date' and v2 measured_allocated 'restated'. In addition the v1 table has no append-only trigger and no hash chain (only savings_ledger_entries has), and its `superseded` flag is mutable, so the tamper evidence built in v2 does not protect the headline numbers.

**Effect on numbers.** SIM2 (factor 0.3 case): v1 report realized_to_date = 0.24 and projected_to_date = 0.216, while v2 says measured_allocated restated = 0.96 and projected restated = 0. The UI hero therefore understates realized savings by rate x (verified_at - applied_at) per change (>= 72 h of saving each) and keeps showing already measured periods as 'projected to date' for ever.

**Affected environments.** All.

**Evidence.** Simulation audit-workspace/audit/work/ledger-savings/sim_ledger.py (sim2), lines 'v1[sim2] measured cost=+0.24', 'v1[sim2] projected cost=+0.216' versus 'measured_allocated cost restatement restatement value=+0.72' + accrued 0.24. frontend grep: the only savings total call is request(`${BASE}/recommendations/savings`) (api.js:450); /api/v2/ledger is used only by the /ledger page.

**Fix.** Make v2 the single read model. 1. assemble_savings_report must take its *_to_date blocks from LedgerRepository.summary(cluster_uid, start, end, namespace): realized_to_date = measured_allocated restated (accrued + restatement), projected_to_date = projected restated, user_declared_to_date likewise; add fields `accrued_only` for transparency during the transition. 2. Lifetime counters and dashboard gauges: compute from savings_ledger_entries (accrued = sum of positive entries, reversed = sum of negative entries, per method) so reversals/restatements are visible to Prometheus. 3. Keep v1 as a write-ahead staging table only, add the same append-only triggers (UPDATE allowed only for superseded FALSE -> TRUE) and document that it is not a reporting source; plan its removal (accrual should write v2 drafts directly: entry_key accrual:{rec}:{method}:{slot}:{quantity}). 4. Add a reconciliation invariant in the ledger-chain loop: sum(v2 accrued book) == sum(v1 non-superseded) per method within 1e-9 relative, exported as greenkube_ledger_reconciliation_ok and alerted.

**Acceptance.** SIM2 rerun: GET /recommendations/savings realized_to_date.cost = 0.96 and projected_to_date.cost = 0 for the verified change; equals /api/v2/ledger/summary totals[measured_allocated].restated; reconciliation gauge = 1; an UPDATE of cost_saved_dollars on recommendation_savings_ledger is rejected by the database.

#### LED-05 · The hash chain is not tamper-evident against anyone who can write to the database: there is no secret or external anchor, the verified head is stored in the same database, and a full re-chain plus an edit of ledger_chain_status passes every verification.

**Severity** medium · **Kind** security · **Confidence** confirmed-sim · **Effort** L

**Where.** `src/greenkube/verify/ledger/model.py:194-199`; `src/greenkube/verify/ledger/verification.py:126-161`; `src/greenkube/verify/ledger/verification.py:164-227`; `src/greenkube/storage/ledger_repository.py:676-694`; `src/greenkube/core/migrations/scripts/postgres/0079_savings_ledger_entries.sql:62-77`; `docs/methodology/savings-measurement.md:321-323`

**Mechanism.** hash = sha256(prev_hash || canonical_json(entry)) uses no key, so anyone able to modify rows can recompute the chain. The triggers are ordinary triggers the table owner (the GreenKube database role itself) can drop or bypass (ALTER TABLE ... DISABLE TRIGGER, session_replication_role). Tail truncation and rewrite are only detected against ledger_chain_status.head_seq/head_hash, a plain updatable row in the same database (the documentation even tells operators to delete it to reset). The in-process checkpoint is lost at restart. verify_chain checks structure only: it does not check that a reversal's value is the negative of the entry it reverses, that source rows exist, or that totals reconcile. After a backup restore or a failover that lost the last commits, the chain and the status row go back together, so the loss of entries is silent as well (the v1 rows are lost with them and re-accrued with new created_at).

**Effect on numbers.** SIM4: 20 chained entries, total cost 0.10. After dropping the triggers, multiplying every cost by 100, deleting the last 4 entries, re-hashing and updating ledger_chain_status, a full verification in a fresh process reports ok=True for 16 entries and the summary shows total cost 8.0 (x80). Any figure can be produced with a valid chain.

**Affected environments.** All (SQLite and PostgreSQL); most relevant where the ledger is used as evidence (chargeback, CSRD reporting).

**Evidence.** Simulation audit-workspace/audit/work/ledger-savings/sim_ledger.py (sim4) output: 'before: {uid: (True, 20)} total cost = 0.1' then 'after rewrite (full verification, new process): {uid: (True, 16, None)} total cost = 8.0'.

**Fix.** State the real guarantee and add an anchor outside the database. 1. Chain with an HMAC: hash = HMAC-SHA256(key, prev_hash || canonical) with a key from a Kubernetes Secret (LEDGER_CHAIN_KEY, generated by the chart, never stored in the database); keep 'ledger/1' entries verifiable with the old rule and switch by method_version/chain_version column. 2. Publish the head outside the database on every verification: (a) Prometheus gauge greenkube_ledger_chain_head_seq{cluster_uid} and an info metric with the head hash (Prometheus retention becomes an independent witness); (b) optional signed checkpoint written to a ConfigMap/annotation or committed to the GitOps repository (LEDGER_CHECKPOINT_TARGET). Verification compares the database with the last external checkpoint and fails with reason 'checkpoint_mismatch'. 3. PostgreSQL: run the application with a role that does not own savings_ledger_entries (owner = migration role; app role has INSERT/SELECT only), document it in the chart. 4. Add semantic checks to verify_chain: reversal.value == -target.value and same quantity/unit; restatement has a reversal of the same projection; entry cluster_uid equals the chain's. 5. Document backup-restore behaviour: after a restore the external checkpoint is ahead; the operator must acknowledge with an explicit, logged command (greenkube ledger accept-head) instead of deleting the status row. 6. Fix the docs sentence that presents the chain as tamper detection without qualification.

**Acceptance.** 1. SIM4 rerun with a chain key: verification fails with hash_mismatch (the attacker cannot recompute HMACs). 2. Deleting the tail and editing ledger_chain_status: verification fails with checkpoint_mismatch against the external checkpoint. 3. With the app role, DROP TRIGGER and ALTER TABLE on savings_ledger_entries are denied on PostgreSQL. 4. A reversal whose value was inserted with the wrong sign fails verification with reason 'reversal_mismatch'.

#### LED-06 · A forward clock jump makes the attributor accrue up to 168 future hourly slots per run and then freezes all accrual until real time catches up; rows are written with the current status for periods that have not happened.

**Severity** medium · **Kind** reliability · **Confidence** confirmed-sim · **Effort** S

**Where.** `src/greenkube/core/savings_attributor.py:311-318`; `src/greenkube/core/savings_attributor.py:409-470`; `src/greenkube/core/savings_attributor.py:72`

**Mechanism.** accrue() takes `now` from the wall clock, builds every slot from the watermark to floor_to_slot(now) (capped at MAX_SLOTS_PER_RUN = 168) and advances the watermark to the last slot. There is no check that `now` is plausible (no comparison with the database clock, the previous run or the newest collected metric). With a wrong clock each run (every collection step, 5 min by default) writes one more week of accruals in the future. Once the clock is right, _slots() is empty until real time passes the watermark, so `if not slots: return` skips everything, including the catch-up of newly applied changes and the stop of changes that were refuted meanwhile: the future rows stay as written.

**Effect on numbers.** Simulation: clock +400 days for 3 runs (15 min): 504 projected rows for the next 21 days are written (ledger total 5.09 instead of 0.05), the watermark is 21 days ahead; a verdict 'not realized' the next day does not remove them, and a change applied afterwards accrues nothing for 21 days (3.35 missing in the example at 0.01/h for 14 days). At a 5 min step, one hour of wrong clock books 84 days of future savings.

**Affected environments.** All; more likely on-prem / edge / VMs resumed from snapshot without NTP.

**Evidence.** Simulation audit-workspace/audit/work/ledger-savings/sim_clock.py output: 'bad-clock run 0..2: rows 168' with watermark 2026-11-22, 2026-11-29, 2026-12-06; 'clock fixed, +2 h / +24 h / +336 h: slots 0 rows 0'; 'ledger total: projected 5.09'.

**Fix.** In SavingsAttributor.accrue: (1) read the database time in the same transaction (SELECT now() / strftime) and refuse to run (counter greenkube_attribution_errors_total{reason=clock_skew}, log error) when |process now - database now| > 5 min; (2) never accrue a slot whose end is later than the newest complete collection watermark (collection_watermarks) + one slot: savings can only be earned for hours the collector has seen; (3) when the stored watermark is in the future of the trusted now, expose greenkube_accrual_watermark_ahead_seconds and alert; provide a repair command that supersedes rows with period_start > now and resets the watermark. (4) Process never-accrued records (catch-up) even when there is no new slot.

**Acceptance.** Rerun sim_clock.py: the bad-clock runs write 0 rows and increment the clock_skew counter; watermark unchanged; after the clock is fixed accrual continues normally and the change applied at +1 h accrues from its applied_at.

#### LED-07 · The 'compare realized to projected to refine the projection model' loop is not implemented: only a binary realized/not-realized count per source feeds the ranking probability; measured magnitudes never calibrate the projections.

**Severity** medium · **Kind** design · **Confidence** confirmed-code · **Effort** XL (split: 1 calibration table + refresh job (M); 2 Impact Model use and versioning (M); 3 API/UI (M); 4 track record per change (S))

**Where.** `src/greenkube/core/optimization/track_record.py:63-113`; `src/greenkube/core/optimization/track_record.py:135-147`; `src/greenkube/storage/measurement_repository.py:128-169`; `src/greenkube/core/optimization/scoring.py:203-222`; `src/greenkube/verify/strategy.py:412`; `src/greenkube/api/routers/changes.py:171-200`

**Mechanism.** What exists: (1) per-measurement projection_accuracy = measured annualized / projected annual, stored inside change_measurements.estimates and returned by GET /api/v2/changes/{id}/measurements, used once as a not_realized threshold; (2) TrackRecord: Beta(1 + realized, 1 + not_realized + rollback) per (source family, capability), read by scoring.success_probability to adjust p_success in the ranking. What is missing: nothing aggregates projection_accuracy, nothing feeds it back into the Impact Model, the analyzers or the displayed projection; no calibration factor, no error bands on projections, no API or UI that shows projected versus realized over time per source/capability/namespace; confidence of new recommendations is not updated. Also: the track record counts recommendations, not changes (a CPU+memory bundle counts twice for one outcome); `regressed` counts as realized in the measurement path (measurement_repository) but as rollback in the fallback path (track_record.outcome_of); inconclusive outcomes (the only outcome seen live: 8 of 8) carry no information, so on short-history or accelerated installs the record never moves; on the live clusters projected cost is 0 (LED-01), so a ratio cannot even be computed on the billable basis.

**Effect on numbers.** Projected savings shown on recommendations and in projected_annual are never corrected by experience: a source whose projections are systematically 2x too high keeps showing 2x-too-high projections; only its ranking probability changes. Live: track record empty on both clusters (all outcomes inconclusive).

**Affected environments.** All.

**Evidence.** Code read end to end: grep projection_accuracy -> only produced in verify/strategy.py:412, verify/service.py:1488, verify/deletion.py:190, verifier.py:1504 and returned by api/routers/changes.py:197; no consumer. track_record users: recommendation_ranking.py:216-268, enrich.py:135-150, scoring.py:203-222 (alpha/beta only). Live: select outcome, count(*) from savings_measurements -> inconclusive only.

**Fix.** Build the loop on stored evidence. 1. New table projection_calibration(cluster_uid, source_family, capability, quantity, basis, n, sum_log_ratio, sum_sq_log_ratio, median_ratio, p10_ratio, p90_ratio, updated_at, model_version) refreshed by the ledger-chain loop from change_measurements: one sample per CHANGE and quantity = measured annualized / allocated projection (same basis, LED-01), only decided outcomes (realized and not_realized, signed, winsorized to [-1, 3]), latest measurement per change, excluding contaminated/inconclusive, weighted by measurement confidence. 2. Shrinkage estimator: calibrated_factor = exp((n x mean_log + k x 0) / (n + k)) with k = 5 pseudo-observations at ratio 1, clipped to [0.25, 2]; require n >= 5 before applying. 3. Impact Model: multiply new projections by the factor of (source family, capability) and store both raw and calibrated values plus the calibration version in impact (impact.calibration = {factor, n, version}); the ledger entry method_version records it. Never rewrite past projections. 4. Expose GET /api/v2/calibration (per source/capability: n, median, p10-p90, factor, last update) and a UI panel 'Projected vs realized' (scatter and ratio trend), and show an interval on each recommendation (projection x [p10, p90]). 5. Track record: count per change, treat regressed consistently (count as realized for the projection ratio but expose a separate `regressed` rate), and feed p_success from change-level outcomes. 6. Metrics: greenkube_projection_ratio{source_family, capability, quantity, stat}. 7. Tests with synthetic measurements (ratios 0.5 for vpa/cpu) proving the factor converges to 0.5 and new recommendations are scaled.

**Acceptance.** Given 10 decided change measurements for (vpa, rightsizing_cpu) with measured/projected = 0.5 each: GET /api/v2/calibration returns factor in [0.55, 0.65] (shrinkage), n = 10; a new vpa CPU recommendation with raw allocated projection 100 is stored with impact.allocated_cost_annual about 60 and impact.raw 100; with n = 3 the factor is not applied; a bundle of two recommendations in one change counts as one sample.

#### LED-08 · Ledger corrections on rollback are a one-shot best-effort side effect: a failed supersede call is never retried, late accrual rows written after it stay counted, and dismissing a false-positive rollback review leaves the whole earlier history reversed.

**Severity** medium · **Kind** reliability · **Confidence** confirmed-sim · **Effort** M

**Where.** `src/greenkube/cli/start.py:387-401`; `src/greenkube/storage/postgres/savings_repository.py:131-144`; `src/greenkube/core/optimization/lifecycle.py:604-652`; `src/greenkube/storage/ledger_repository.py:411-444`

**Mechanism.** run_recommendation_lifecycle supersedes v1 rows only for records returned as rollback_review by that very run: `try: await savings_repo.supersede_for_recommendation(rec_id) except Exception: logger.warning(...)`. If the UPDATE fails (database restart, lock timeout) or the process stops between the status transition and the call, nothing retries: the recommendation is already rollback_review, so it is not in a later `verified` list, and all its accruals stay counted. The attribution loop runs concurrently with the lifecycle loop on a list of records loaded earlier, so it can insert one more slot after the supersession; that row is never flagged. In the other direction, dismiss_rollback_review returns the record to applied/pending, but the rows superseded at review time stay superseded (v2: reversed by 'supersession' entries) and the accrual floor (MAX(period_end) including superseded rows) prevents re-accrual: a false-positive review permanently deletes the savings accrued before it.

**Effect on numbers.** A rolled-back change whose supersede call failed keeps 100% of its accrued projected/measured savings in every total for ever (for a change at 100 USD/yr reviewed after 30 days: 8.2 USD wrongly kept). A dismissed false positive loses the same amount.

**Affected environments.** All; more likely with PostgreSQL restarts or failovers.

**Evidence.** Simulation audit-workspace/audit/work/ledger-savings/sim_rollback.py: change projected at 0.01/h applied 48 h ago, review after 24 h (24 rows superseded), one attribution run that still held the pre-review record wrote 1 more row that is never superseded, review dismissed: final v2 projected accrual +0.48, supersession reversal -0.24, v1 total 0.24 for a change that was in place for 48 h (0.48). The never-retried failure path is from code: cli/start.py:387-401 (warning only), grep supersede_for_recommendation (two call sites), lifecycle.dismiss_rollback_review (no ledger call).

**Fix.** Move supersession into the state-derived projector/reconciler: on every ledger-chain tick, select recommendations with status in (rollback_review, reverted, failed) that still have non-superseded v1 rows (or unreversed v2 entries) and supersede them; and select recommendations that are applied/verifying/verified again after a ROLLBACK_DISMISSED event and write 'reinstatement' entries (new reason) re-crediting the entries reversed by that review (v1: new rows, since superseded cannot be unset in an append-only model). Remove the one-shot call from cli/start.py or keep it as a fast path. Add greenkube_ledger_unreconciled_recommendations gauge and an alert.

**Acceptance.** 1. Test: make supersede_for_recommendation raise once; after the next ledger-chain tick the v2 net of the recommendation is 0. 2. Test: an accrual row inserted after the supersession is reversed by the next tick. 3. Test: rollback_review then dismiss -> v2 net equals the accruals before the review plus those after the dismissal.

#### LED-09 · The ledger grows without bound at one v1 row and two v2 entries per accruing recommendation per hour, with full-table anti-join sweeps, an all-time summary scan per API call and a daily full re-hash; there is no rollup or archival design.

**Severity** medium · **Kind** performance · **Confidence** confirmed-code · **Effort** L

**Where.** `src/greenkube/core/savings_attributor.py:347-385`; `src/greenkube/verify/ledger/projector.py:202-241`; `src/greenkube/storage/ledger_repository.py:354-409`; `src/greenkube/storage/ledger_repository.py:622-670`; `src/greenkube/verify/ledger/verification.py:43-44`; `src/greenkube/verify/ledger/projector.py:351-368`

**Mechanism.** Every accruing recommendation writes one v1 row per hour and method and the projector turns it into one entry per non-zero quantity (cost, CO2e); verified changes accrue for ever (until drift, deletion or regression). Restatement adds two more entries per projected entry. Nothing is ever compressed (by design), yet: the full sweep (every ledger-chain tick and once an hour per attributor) runs `NOT EXISTS (SELECT 1 FROM savings_ledger_entries ...)` over the whole v1 table; GET /api/v2/ledger/summary and the savings report aggregate all rows on every call; the full chain verification re-hashes every entry in Python once a day and at every controller start; _restate loads all verified/regressed recommendations on every pass. The savings_ledger_daily_rollup view is computed at read time.

**Effect on numbers.** 1,000 accruing recommendations: 8.8 million v1 rows and 17.5 million v2 entries per year (about 0.6 to 1 kB each with keys, UUIDs and two 64-char hashes: 10 to 18 GB/yr before indexes). API latency and the daily verification grow linearly for ever; on SQLite (the default for small installs) the write lock is held during appends.

**Affected environments.** All; large clusters and long-running installs first.

**Evidence.** Code read: entry creation per slot and quantity (savings_attributor._accruals_for, projector._row_drafts), full sweep `after = 0 if full`, summary SQL without materialization. Not load-tested (live ledgers are empty).

**Fix.** Keep the append-only guarantee but change the grain and the read path. 1. Accrue per recommendation per UTC day once a slot day is complete and stable (keep hourly only for the current day in a staging table), or keep hourly v1 rows but project one v2 entry per (recommendation, method, quantity, day) with entry_key day-based; partial days at apply/verification boundaries keep exact period bounds. 2. Maintain a materialized ledger_totals(cluster_uid, day, namespace, method, quantity, unit, book, entry_kind) updated in the append transaction; summary and report read it (windows prorate only boundary days from entries). 3. Sweep by an indexed 'projected' marker (a projection watermark per v1 id plus a small pending table) instead of a full anti-join. 4. Verification: checkpoint signed heads every N entries (LED-05) so the daily full verification can run in segments and be resumed; bound per-tick work. 5. PostgreSQL: partition savings_ledger_entries by month on period_start. 6. Document expected growth and a cold-archive export.

**Acceptance.** Load test with 1,000 recommendations x 365 days: summary p95 < 200 ms, ledger-chain tick < 60 s, table growth < 1.5 GB/yr; totals identical (1e-9) to the hourly-grain implementation on the same inputs.

#### LED-10 · The FOCUS export books projected and user-declared amounts as negative EffectiveCost 'Credit' rows, so summing the standard cost column mixes methods and subtracts unverified projections from spend.

**Severity** medium · **Kind** design · **Confidence** confirmed-code · **Effort** S

**Where.** `src/greenkube/api/routers/ledger.py:460-509`; `src/greenkube/api/routers/ledger.py:512-543`; `docs/methodology/savings-measurement.md:337`

**Mechanism.** _focus_row writes EffectiveCost = -saving for every entry whatever its method (projected, user_declared, measured_allocated) and ChargeCategory 'Credit' for accruals. A FOCUS consumer (FinOps tool, BI) aggregates EffectiveCost without knowing the x-greenkube-method extension, so projected, user-declared and measured-allocated amounts are summed together and netted against real spend, which is exactly what product principle 3 forbids; measured_allocated savings are not on the bill either. The extension columns use the `x-greenkube-` prefix although FOCUS 1.0 requires `x_` (documented as a known gap). When only a CO2e entry exists for a group the row has an empty BillingCurrency. Groups evicted from the 5,000-group buffer before their second quantity arrives are emitted as two rows.

**Effect on numbers.** A consumer summing EffectiveCost gets projected + user-declared + measured-allocated in one figure. SIM1 data: -1.44 of 'credits' for two changes whose savings were refuted or undecided (LED-03). SIM2 data (factor 0.3): -0.456 before the restatement and -0.96 after it for the same hours, none of it being a billed amount.

**Affected environments.** Users of the FOCUS export.

**Evidence.** Code read: ledger.py:481-482 `"BilledCost": repr(-saving if entry.method == "realized_billed" else 0.0), "EffectiveCost": repr(-saving)`.

**Fix.** EffectiveCost and BilledCost must be 0 for every method except realized_billed; carry the amount in x_greenkube_saving with x_greenkube_method and x_greenkube_basis. Offer `format=focus&methods=measured_allocated` only as an explicit opt-in that sets ChargeDescription 'allocated saving (not billed)'. Rename extension columns to the x_ prefix (keep the old names behind `compat=1` for one release). Fill BillingCurrency from the entry unit/currency of the group or the cluster pricing currency. Flush incomplete groups by group_key order (entries of one v1 row are adjacent) instead of the eviction heuristic. Add a FOCUS validator test in CI.

**Acceptance.** Export of a ledger with projected, user_declared and measured_allocated entries: sum(EffectiveCost) = 0 and sum(BilledCost) = 0; sum(x_greenkube_saving) grouped by x_greenkube_method equals /ledger/summary; the official FOCUS 1.0 validator reports no column-name error.

#### LED-11 · Ledger entries cannot be reproduced from their own provenance: price_card_version, intensity_g_per_kwh and intensity_source are never filled, projected entries carry only a generic method_version, and the v1 ledger has no currency.

**Severity** low · **Kind** data-integrity · **Confidence** confirmed-code · **Effort** M

**Where.** `src/greenkube/verify/ledger/projector.py:283-313`; `src/greenkube/verify/ledger/projector.py:432-456`; `src/greenkube/verify/ledger/model.py:132-134`; `src/greenkube/storage/ledger_repository.py:493-542`; `src/greenkube/core/savings_report.py:96-110`

**Mechanism.** LedgerEntryDraft has price_card_version, intensity_g_per_kwh and intensity_source, and the CSV export has the columns, but no code path sets them (the projector never passes them), so every entry has NULL. Projected entries get method_version 'ledger/1:projected' (no Impact Model version, no recommendation revision). Provenance is resolved at projection time as the LATEST change and measurement of the recommendation, so a row projected late (catch-up after an outage, re-measurement in between) can be linked to a measurement that did not produce its rate. The v1 rows store amounts in a column named cost_saved_dollars without currency; the v2 currency is taken from the recommendation's impact.currency at projection time, and GET /recommendations/savings sums v1 amounts across currencies if the pricing currency was changed.

**Effect on numbers.** No total is changed, but an auditor cannot recompute an entry (rate x period) from the ledger alone or prove which price card and grid intensity it rests on; after a currency change the v1 report adds amounts of two currencies.

**Affected environments.** All.

**Evidence.** grep price_card_version|intensity_g_per_kwh in src/greenkube/verify/ledger and storage/ledger_repository.py: only declarations and column lists, no assignment.

**Fix.** At accrual time store on the v1 row (new columns) and copy into the entry: annual_rate, currency, basis, impact_model_version, recommendation revision id, measurement_id (the one whose rate is used, taken from baseline.latest_measurement_id at accrual time, not at projection time), price card id/version and the intensity value and source used by the projection or the measurement snapshot (baseline_snapshots.price_card / intensity_source). Add annual_rate to the hash payload under a new method_version. Group v1 report totals by currency.

**Acceptance.** For any entry: value == annual_rate x (period_end - period_start) / 31557600 within 1e-12; price_card_version and intensity_source are non-null for cost and CO2e entries respectively; a measured entry's measurement_id equals the measurement whose claimed_annualized equals annual_rate.

#### LED-12 · Minor ledger inconsistencies: drift reversals with mixed signs are projected as accruals, zero-length periods are dropped from windowed summaries, and a record that errors or is skipped for some runs loses those slots for good.

**Severity** low · **Kind** bug · **Confidence** confirmed-code · **Effort** M

**Where.** `src/greenkube/verify/ledger/projector.py:129-135`; `src/greenkube/storage/ledger_repository.py:636-651`; `src/greenkube/api/routers/ledger.py:261-270`; `src/greenkube/core/savings_attributor.py:437-447`; `src/greenkube/verify/drift.py:746-786`

**Mechanism.** (a) _is_drift_reversal requires co2e <= 0 and cost <= 0; a verified recommendation with a positive cost saving and a negative CO2e saving (or the reverse) produces a reversal row with mixed signs, which is then projected as two 'accrual' entries created_by 'savings-attributor' with reason 'accrual' instead of a 'reversal' with reason 'drift' (totals stay right, classification and reverses_entry_id are lost; the v1 lifetime counters count the positive part as newly 'accrued'). (b) In LedgerRepository.summary a window divides by NULLIF(period length, 0): an entry with period_start == period_end gets a NULL share and vanishes from SUM, while the export's window_share returns 1.0 or 0.0 for the same entry. (c) accrue() advances the global watermark even when a record raised (errors += 1): once the record is valid again it has a floor, so the catch-up (only for floor is None) does not run and the slots of the failing runs are never accrued. (d) The drift reversal amount is rate x (floor - start) and assumes measured accruals exist for the whole interval; with gaps from (c) it reverses more than was accrued.

**Effect on numbers.** (a) reason/kind mislabelled, attributed counter inflated by the positive part. (b) windowed total differs from the export for zero-length entries. (c) understatement by rate x duration of the failing runs. (d) over-reversal by the same amount (net negative total for the recommendation).

**Affected environments.** All (edge cases).

**Evidence.** Code read; not simulated.

**Fix.** (a) Mark drift reversals explicitly: add a `kind`/`reason` column on v1 rows written by DriftMonitor._reversal ('drift_reversal') and use it in the projector instead of the sign heuristic. (b) Use the same rule in SQL and Python (zero-length entry inside the window -> share 1). (c) Track a per-recommendation accrual cursor (last accrued slot per recommendation and method) instead of one global watermark plus floor, and backfill from the cursor. (d) Compute the reversal from the measured rows actually present in [start, floor): reversal = -SUM(rows).

**Acceptance.** (a) reversal row with cost -1, co2e +2 -> two v2 entries entry_kind reversal, reason drift. (b) summary(window) == sum(export window_value). (c) a record invalid for 3 runs then fixed has no missing slot. (d) net of a regressed recommendation over the reversed interval == 0 exactly, with a gap in accruals.

core/observability.py (metric definitions only); core/health.py (function list only)', 'storage/postgres/repository.py read methods (379-457, 665-1565), summary_repository.py, timeseries_cache_repository.py; storage/base_repository.py 239-705; storage/sqlite/repository.py (cursor read, aggregate_summary/timeseries split and bucketing)', 'core/metrics_compressor.py (limits, watermark, namespace cache), core/savings_report.py, core/savings_attributor.py (accrual components, run rates), core/recommendation_ranking.py, impact/realization.py (header)', 'frontend/src/routes/footprint/+page.svelte, frontend/src/lib/utils/format.js, dashboards/greenkube-grafana.json (all panel expressions), helm-chart/templates/servicemonitor.yaml'], 'ran': ['audit-workspace/audit/work/api-read-path/sim_report.py: real SQLite repository + real MetricsCompressor + real report/metrics route functions (20 days, 2 pods)', 'audit-workspace/audit/work/api-read-path/sim_exposition.py: real metrics_endpoint refresh with fake repositories (stall, overlapping scrapes, UI request during scrape)', 'Live (minikube and kind): /metrics/summary, /metrics/dashboard-summary, /metrics/dashboard-timeseries/24h, /metrics/timeseries, /metrics/by-namespace, /metrics/top-pods, /report/summary, /report/years, /nodes, /recommendations/savings, /recommendations/top compared with direct SQL; Prometheus queries of the greenkube_* series on kind (minikube Prometheus does not scrape GreenKube)'], 'not_covered': ['api/routers/optimization.py and setup.py beyond the parts listed; core/health.py probes; PostgreSQL tie-order instability of OFFSET paging was not reproduced on a live database', 'Raw + hourly behaviour on the live installs: both hourly tables are empty (13 h of data), so the seam was only tested in simulation', 'float4 SUM precision (NRG-06) and session-time-zone bucketing (ROL-08, STO-10) were not re-tested; live side observation: /metrics/summary and the sum of /metrics/by-namespace for the same range differ by 0.01% (0.89972 versus 0.89963)', 'The React/Svelte chart builders (frontend/src/lib/charts.js) and the report page UI']}

**Earlier defects not fully fixed.**

- BUG-02 (partially_fixed): Compressor is hour-aligned and watermark-driven, but every reader still splits at an unaligned wall-clock cutoff (API-03 here, ROL-01).
- BUG-19 (partially_fixed): Cost and energy now share one boundary (idle goes to node_idle_metrics for both), but the API never adds the idle share back (API-04).
