#### API-01 · An aggregated report export (aggregate=true) is aggregated per 7-day read chunk, so the file holds several rows for the same (namespace, pod, period) key and the preview row count is wrong.

**Severity** high · **Kind** bug · **Confidence** confirmed-sim · **Effort** M

**Where.** `src/greenkube/api/routers/report.py:276-291`; `src/greenkube/api/routers/report.py:189-208`; `src/greenkube/core/aggregator.py:70-172`

**Mechanism.** report_export streams the range in windows of chunk_days=7 (`_iter_metrics_chunks`). When aggregate=true it calls `aggregate_metrics(batch, **granularity_flags, group_by=group_by)` on EACH 7-day batch and yields the result, so the grouping key (namespace, pod, period) is only unique inside one chunk. Any period longer than, or not aligned with, a 7-day chunk is emitted once per chunk: monthly, yearly and 'no granularity' always; weekly because ISO weeks and the chunks start on different days; daily and hourly whenever the range start is not on a day/hour boundary (every `last=` range). /report/summary computes `total_rows` with `aggregate_grouped_row_count` (COUNT DISTINCT over the whole range), so the preview announces fewer rows than the file contains. Each partial row also carries a `timestamp` (first sample of the chunk), `duration_seconds` and averaged usage of its chunk only. The aggregated rows are also semantically wrong for group_by=namespace (`aggregate_metrics`): `cpu_request` and `memory_request` are `max(...)` over the rows of all pods (one pod's request, not the namespace's), `duration_seconds` is the sum over pods (the simulation shows 1,209,600 s = 14 days for a 7-day chunk of 2 pods), usage is an unweighted mean over rows, and the cost components (cost_cpu, cost_ram...), `cost_source` and the other provenance fields are not carried, so an aggregated export has no cost breakdown and no provenance at all (see also ROL-10).

**Effect on numbers.** Sums over the file are right, but every per-key figure is split. Simulation (2 pods, 1.0 cost per 5-minute sample, range 2026-10-26..2026-11-12, group_by=namespace, granularity=monthly): the preview says 2 rows, the export has 4: 2026-10 = 3456, 2026-11 = 576, 2026-11 = 4032, 2026-11 = 2280. A reader (or a spreadsheet lookup) that takes the first '2026-11' row reports 576 instead of 6888 (-92%). granularity=yearly: 3 rows for 2026 instead of 1; granularity=None: 3 rows instead of 1. A yearly report per namespace has about 53 rows per namespace instead of 1.

**Affected environments.** All installs, both databases; every aggregated export longer than 7 days (and shorter ones that cross a chunk boundary).

**Evidence.** Simulation audit-workspace/audit/work/api-read-path/sim_report.py (real SQLite repository, real MetricsCompressor, real router functions), output section [2]: 'aggregate=true group_by=namespace granularity=monthly: preview total_rows=2 exported rows=4', 'granularity=yearly: preview total_rows=1 exported rows=3', 'granularity=None: preview total_rows=1 exported rows=3'.

**Fix.** Aggregate in the database, not per chunk. Add a repository method `aggregate_report_rows(start, end, namespace, granularity, group_by)` on CombinedMetricsRepository (PostgreSQL and SQLite) that runs one GROUP BY over the raw+hourly union (same split rule as the other aggregates, i.e. the compression watermark once that fix lands) with the bucket expression in UTC and returns one row per (group key, bucket): SUM of cost, cost_* components, co2e, embodied, joules; duration-weighted grid_intensity and pue; time-weighted average usage; for group_by=namespace the request columns must be the time-weighted average of the per-step SUM over pods (not MAX over rows). report_export streams these rows (they are bounded by groups x buckets) and /report/summary counts the same query so `total_rows` equals the number of exported rows by construction. If a streaming Python fallback is kept, carry an accumulator dict keyed by (namespace, pod, period) across chunks and emit a key only when the chunk cursor has passed the end of its period. Remove `aggregate_metrics` from the export path or restrict it to the CLI. No data migration.

**Acceptance.** 1) Seed 2 pods x 20 days of 5-minute rows with cost 1.0, compress, call /report/export?aggregate=true&granularity=monthly&group_by=namespace&start=D0&end=D17&format=json: exactly one row per (namespace, month); row costs equal the SQL sums; 2) the number of rows equals /report/summary total_rows for granularity in hourly, daily, weekly, monthly, yearly and none, for group_by pod and namespace, for `last=30d`, a custom range and `years=`; 3) no two exported rows share (namespace, pod_name, period).

#### API-02 · The Prometheus exposition publishes the cost, CO2e and energy of ONE collection step as unit-less gauges named *_total, so every greenkube_pod_*, greenkube_namespace_*_total and greenkube_cluster_*_total value scales with the collection step and the bundled Grafana panels show them as 'Cost' and 'CO2e'.

**Severity** high · **Kind** design · **Confidence** confirmed-live · **Effort** M

**Where.** `src/greenkube/api/metrics_endpoint.py:61-84`; `src/greenkube/api/metrics_endpoint.py:151-210`; `src/greenkube/api/metrics_endpoint.py:853-856`; `src/greenkube/api/metrics_endpoint.py:906-923`; `src/greenkube/api/metrics_endpoint.py:1260-1275`; `dashboards/greenkube-grafana.json`

**Mechanism.** `refresh_metrics_from_db` reads `read_latest_per_pod(now - 20 min, now)` (one row per pod: its most recent step) and `update_cluster_metrics` copies `m.total_cost`, `m.co2e_grams`, `m.embodied_co2e_grams`, `m.joules` of that single row into `greenkube_pod_cost_dollars`, `greenkube_pod_co2e_grams`, `greenkube_pod_energy_joules`, then sums them into `greenkube_namespace_*_total` and `greenkube_cluster_*_total`. A stored row is the amount of one collection step (PROMETHEUS_QUERY_RANGE_STEP, 5 minutes by default), so the gauge is 'dollars per step' with no step in the name, the help text ('Cloud cost per pod in dollars', 'Total cost across all pods in dollars') or a label. The `_total` suffix announces a counter but the series is a gauge that never accumulates: rate(), increase() and sum_over_time() all give meaningless results (sum_over_time depends on scrape interval / step). The bundled Grafana dashboard plots them directly: 'Cost by Namespace' = `sum by (namespace) (greenkube_namespace_cost_dollars_total)` with unit currencyUSD, 'Top 15 Pods - Cost', 'CO2e by Namespace', and uses `greenkube_cluster_cost_dollars_total` as the `or` fallback of the windowed 'Footprint & Cost Mix' stat when the summary series is absent, so a per-step amount is shown where a 24h/7d/30d total is expected. The latest-row set also mixes timestamps: a pod deleted up to 20 minutes ago still contributes its last row, and a pod whose last row came from a catch-up chunk contributes a row of a different duration.

**Effect on numbers.** Values are proportional to the step. Live (kind): two installs of the same cluster expose greenkube_cluster_cost_dollars_total = 0.000715 (30 s step) and 0.00716 (5 min step) for the same 34 pods at the same moment: a factor 10. With the default 5-minute step the 'Cost by Namespace' panel shows 1/288 of the daily cost (e.g. $0.0012 for a namespace that costs $0.35 per day) under a plain dollar unit; when the fallback is used the 'Footprint & Cost Mix' stat for a 30d window is understated by a factor 8640.

**Affected environments.** All installs that scrape /prometheus/metrics or use the bundled Grafana dashboard.

**Evidence.** Live kind Prometheus: `greenkube_cluster_cost_dollars_total` = 0.0007153 (instance 10.244.2.44, release with 30 s step) and 0.007163 (instance 10.244.1.63, release gk-upgrade with 5 min step); SQL on the first install: latest row per pod in 20 minutes = 34 rows, sum(total_cost) = 0.0007157556, equal to the sum of the single latest step. Panel expressions extracted from dashboards/greenkube-grafana.json.

**Fix.** Expose rates and real counters instead of per-step amounts. (1) Replace the per-step gauges by rate gauges with the unit in the name, computed as amount / duration_seconds of the row: greenkube_pod_cost_dollars_per_hour, greenkube_pod_co2e_grams_per_hour, greenkube_pod_power_watts (joules / duration), and the same at namespace and cluster level (sum of pod rates of ONE common timestamp: the latest completely collected step, taken from the collection watermark, not 'latest row per pod in 20 minutes'). (2) If cumulative series are wanted, publish true counters (CounterMetricFamily fed from SUM over all stored history per namespace, or from a persisted cumulative table) so increase() over any range equals the stored total. (3) Keep the old names for one release as deprecated aliases with the help text 'amount of the last collection step (N seconds)' and a `step_seconds` gauge, then remove them. (4) Update dashboards/greenkube-grafana.json: per-namespace and top-pod panels use the hourly rate or the windowed greenkube_dashboard_summary_* series; remove the `or greenkube_cluster_*_total` fallbacks. (5) Update docs/prometheus-grafana.md with units.

**Acceptance.** With the same stored data re-collected at 30 s and 5 min steps, every exposed cost/CO2e/energy series differs by less than 1%; `greenkube_cluster_cost_dollars_per_hour * 24` over a steady day equals SUM(total_cost) of that day within 1%; promtool check metrics reports no counter/gauge naming violation; no Grafana panel shows a per-step amount under a currency or gram unit.

#### API-03 · Every API aggregate that spans more than 24 hours reads hourly rows up to wall-clock now - 24 h and raw rows after it, while the compressor only fills hourly rows up to the last closed hour: 0 to about 2 hours of data are missing from the 7d, 30d, 1y and YTD totals, charts, namespace and pod breakdowns and reports.

**Severity** high · **Kind** bug · **Confidence** confirmed-sim · **Effort** M

**Where.** `src/greenkube/storage/postgres/repository.py:721-765`; `src/greenkube/storage/postgres/repository.py:932-977`; `src/greenkube/storage/postgres/repository.py:1054-1094`; `src/greenkube/storage/postgres/repository.py:1130-1170`; `src/greenkube/storage/postgres/repository.py:1416-1491`; `src/greenkube/storage/sqlite/repository.py:906-941`; `src/greenkube/storage/base_repository.py:316-342`; `src/greenkube/core/metrics_compressor.py:329-340`

**Mechanism.** Same root cause as ROL-01 / RECON-03 / MEA-18, restated here because it is the main correctness defect of the read path. aggregate_summary, aggregate_timeseries, aggregate_by_namespace, aggregate_top_pods, aggregate_grouped_row_count, read_combined_metrics_page and read_combined_metrics_smart all compute `cutoff = now - METRICS_COMPRESSION_AGE_HOURS` and read `combined_metrics` for `timestamp >= cutoff - 1 min` and `combined_metrics_hourly` for `hour_bucket < cutoff`. The compressor writes an hourly row only for hours that end at or before `floor_hour(now - 24 h)` and only when its hourly loop runs. The raw rows between the last compressed hour and `cutoff - 1 min` still exist (raw retention is 7 days) but no query reads them. In addition the hourly branch uses `hour_bucket >= start`, so the hour that contains an unaligned start is dropped entirely. If compression stops or lags (error, long backfill, controller down), the hole grows without bound although the raw rows are in the table, and nothing in the response says so.

**Effect on numbers.** All totals and breakdowns of windows longer than 24 h are understated by 0-2 h of cluster data at the seam plus up to 1 h at the window start: about -0.4% to -1.8% for 7d, more for 2d-3d ranges, and -100% of everything older than 24 h if the hourly table is not being filled. Simulation through the real route functions (2 pods, 1.0 per 5-minute sample, last=10d): truth 5760, /metrics/summary 5736.0, /metrics/timeseries sum 5736.0, /metrics/by-namespace 5736.0.

**Affected environments.** All installs once data is older than 24 h, PostgreSQL and SQLite. Not visible on the two live installs (13 h of data, hourly tables empty).

**Evidence.** audit-workspace/audit/work/api-read-path/sim_report.py output section [4]; live: `select count(*) from combined_metrics_hourly` = 0 on both installs, so the live comparisons below only exercise the raw branch.

**Fix.** Split on the compression watermark, not on the clock. Add one helper (for example `MetricsReadSplit.resolve(conn, table)`) that returns `seam = metrics_compression_watermarks.watermark` for the raw table (exclusive end of the last compressed hour; when absent, the minimum raw timestamp floored to the hour) and make every reader use: hourly rows with `hour_bucket < seam`, raw rows with `timestamp >= seam`. For the window start, either floor `start` to the hour when the hourly branch is used (and return the effective start in the response) or read the first partial hour from raw rows when they still exist. Replace the seven copies of the split logic by this helper. Read the seam in the same transaction as the aggregate so a compression that commits between the two reads cannot double count. No migration; the cached metrics_summary and metrics_timeseries_cache rows correct themselves at the next refresh.

**Acceptance.** Frozen-clock test at hh:37 with 10 days of 5-minute rows and a compressor that last ran at (hh-1):50: /metrics/summary, /metrics/timeseries (sum), /metrics/by-namespace, /metrics/top-pods (sum over all pods), /report/summary and the sum of /report/export for last=10d all equal the SQL sum over raw + hourly rows of the same instants; the same test with the compressor stopped for 3 days still returns the full total; no row is counted twice when the compressor runs between two requests.

#### API-04 · No API total, chart, breakdown, report or Prometheus series includes the node idle share (node_idle_metrics is read by no endpoint), so 'Total Cost', 'Total Energy' and 'Total CO2' are the allocated-to-pods part only, without saying so.

**Severity** high · **Kind** design · **Confidence** confirmed-live · **Effort** L

**Where.** `src/greenkube/storage/postgres/repository.py:582-600`; `src/greenkube/storage/postgres/repository.py:709-803`; `src/greenkube/api/schemas.py:337-351`; `frontend/src/routes/footprint/+page.svelte:173-180`; `src/greenkube/api/metrics_endpoint.py:919-923`

**Mechanism.** Same subject as NRG-02 and COST-13, seen from the API. The Impact Model writes the unreserved share of each node to `node_idle_metrics` (cost, joules, co2e, embodied). `read_idle_metrics` exists in the repository but no router, no summary refresher and no exposition code calls it: every aggregate reads `combined_metrics` / `combined_metrics_hourly` only. The response schemas call the result `total_cost` ('Total cost in dollars'), `total_energy_joules`, `total_co2e_grams`, the UI cards are labelled 'Total Cost', 'Total Energy', 'Total CO2 (Scope 2+3)', and the Prometheus help says 'Total cost across all pods'. Nothing reconciles pods + idle with the node totals.

**Effect on numbers.** Cluster totals are understated by the idle share, which dominates on lightly loaded clusters. Live kind (13 h): pods total_cost 0.58, idle total_cost 12.24: the dashboard shows 4.5% of the modelled cluster cost; energy 100,442 J shown versus 1,853,116 J idle (5.1%); CO2e 0.94 g shown versus 17.4 g idle. Live minikube: cost 0.877 shown versus 0.739 idle (54% shown), energy 165,869 J versus 98,863 J idle.

**Affected environments.** All installs; worst on over-provisioned and on-prem clusters.

**Evidence.** Live SQL on both installs: `select sum(total_cost), sum(co2e_grams), sum(joules) from node_idle_metrics` versus the same on combined_metrics, compared with GET /api/v1/metrics/summary?last=7d (equal to the combined_metrics sums); grep shows no caller of read_idle_metrics under src/greenkube/api or core/summary_refresher.py.

**Fix.** Add the idle share to every cluster-level read: aggregate_summary and aggregate_timeseries return `idle_cost`, `idle_energy_joules`, `idle_co2e_grams`, `idle_embodied_co2e_grams` next to the allocated figures and `cluster_total_* = allocated + idle`; rename the existing fields in the schema descriptions to 'allocated to pods'. Store the idle columns in metrics_summary and metrics_timeseries_cache (migration adding four columns each), expose `greenkube_cluster_idle_*` series, show 'Allocated' and 'Idle (unallocated)' in the UI cards and add an `__idle__` slice to the namespace donut. Namespace-filtered requests keep idle out and say so. This needs the hourly idle aggregate of ROL-07 so that windows older than raw retention keep their idle part. Add a reconciliation check: pods + idle of a step equals the node figure within 1%.

**Acceptance.** On a seeded database with node total 10.0 per step, pods 4.0 and idle 6.0: /metrics/summary returns allocated 4.0 x steps, idle 6.0 x steps and cluster_total 10.0 x steps; the UI shows the three figures; the sum of the timeseries buckets equals the summary; a 30-day window still contains the idle part after raw retention.

#### API-05 · The report export loses the rows stamped exactly on the first 7-day chunk boundary (a whole hour of every pod for date-aligned ranges), so the file total is lower than the /report/summary preview of the same range.

**Severity** medium · **Kind** bug · **Confidence** confirmed-sim · **Effort** S

**Where.** `src/greenkube/api/routers/report.py:280-291`; `src/greenkube/api/routers/report.py:42-49`

**Mechanism.** The chunk loop reads `[cursor, window_end)` (the repositories are half-open: `timestamp >= $1 AND timestamp < $2`, `hour_bucket >= $1 AND hour_bucket < $2`) and then advances with `cursor = window_end + timedelta(seconds=1)`. The code was written for inclusive ends; with half-open reads the interval `[window_end, window_end + 1s)` is never read. Ranges built from dates or years start at 00:00:00, so the first boundary is exactly start + 7 days 00:00:00, which is the timestamp of an hourly row (hour_bucket) of every pod, or of a raw 5-minute step. Later boundaries drift by one second per chunk (00:00:01, 00:00:02...) and only hit rows stamped on those seconds. Separately, calendar years and date-only ends use 23:59:59.999999 as an exclusive end, which is harmless for aligned rows but is one more inclusive/exclusive mismatch.

**Effect on numbers.** Export total = preview total minus one hour of all pods (compressed data) or one step of all pods (raw data). Simulation: 18 full days, truth 10368, /report/summary total_cost 10368.0 and total_rows 864, /report/export sum 10344.0 and 862 rows; hour 2026-11-02T00:00Z is absent (-0.23%). On a yearly report the loss is one hour of 8760 (-0.011%), but the file no longer reconciles with the preview, the dashboard or the SQL total.

**Affected environments.** All installs; deterministic for start=/end= and years= ranges, occasional for last= ranges.

**Evidence.** audit-workspace/audit/work/api-read-path/sim_report.py output section [1]: 'hours absent from the export: [2026-11-02T00:00:00Z]', missing=24.0 (0.231%).

**Fix.** In `_iter_metrics_chunks` use half-open chunks: `while cursor < range_end: window_end = min(range_end, cursor + delta); read [cursor, window_end); cursor = window_end`. Make every range half-open at the source: `_get_calendar_year_range` returns (Jan 1 year, Jan 1 year+1); a date-only `end` means the start of the next day; document `end` as exclusive for full datetimes. Add an invariant test that sums the export and compares it with aggregate_summary.

**Acceptance.** For a seeded database, for ranges start=2026-10-26&end=2026-11-12, years=2026 and last=30d: sum(total_cost), sum(co2e_grams), sum(joules) and the row count of /report/export (aggregate=false) equal /report/summary exactly; a row stamped at start + 7 days 00:00:00 is present in the file.

#### API-06 · The sustainability score is computed from one row per pod without node or HPA data: three of its seven dimensions are constants (50, 50, 100), two depend on the collection step, and an empty database scores 50.

**Severity** medium · **Kind** design · **Confidence** confirmed-live · **Effort** M

**Where.** `src/greenkube/api/metrics_endpoint.py:970-982`; `src/greenkube/api/metrics_endpoint.py:818`; `src/greenkube/core/sustainability_score.py:101-103`; `src/greenkube/core/sustainability_score.py:216-254`; `src/greenkube/core/sustainability_score.py:266-267`; `src/greenkube/core/sustainability_score.py:325-328`; `src/greenkube/core/sustainability_score.py:392-435`; `src/greenkube/core/sustainability_score.py:441-451`; `docs/sustainability-score.md`

**Mechanism.** The only caller is `update_cluster_metrics`, which runs `SustainabilityScorer().compute(metrics)` on the latest row of each pod, with no `node_infos` and no `hpa_targets`. Consequences, dimension by dimension: node_efficiency (15%) returns `_NEUTRAL` = 50 because `node_infos` is None; scaling_practices (10%) needs `len(usages) >= 3` per pod and gets 1, so it returns 50; carbon_aware_scheduling (10%) compares each pod's average intensity with its zone average over the same single instant, so the ratio is 1 and the score is always 100; stability (5%) uses the lifetime restart counter. waste_elimination (15%) applies ZOMBIE_COST_THRESHOLD=0.01, ZOMBIE_ENERGY_THRESHOLD=1000 J and IDLE_NAMESPACE_ENERGY_THRESHOLD=1000 J to the amounts of ONE step: a namespace is 'idle' below 3.3 W with a 5-minute step and below 33 W with a 30-second step, and a pod must cost more than $0.01 per step ($2.88 per day at 5 minutes) to be a zombie. resource_efficiency (25%) caps usage/request at 1 (a pod above its request is 'perfect') and skips pods without requests, which removes the least governed pods from the score. With no metrics the score is published as 50, and per-namespace scores are published the same way. The documentation describes time windows and HPA and node inputs that the running code never receives.

**Effect on numbers.** 22.5 of the 100 points are fixed (0.15*50 + 0.10*50 + 0.10*100) and stability adds a near-constant 5: the score can only move inside roughly [27.5, 87.5] and reflects only request utilisation, grid intensity x PUE and the step-dependent waste test. Live kind: node_efficiency 50, scaling_practices 50, carbon_aware_scheduling 100, stability 100, waste_elimination 73.3, resource_efficiency 35.1, carbon_efficiency 95.8, overall 66.4.

**Affected environments.** All installs that read greenkube_sustainability_score or the Grafana 'Sustainability Score Radar'.

**Evidence.** Live kind Prometheus: greenkube_sustainability_dimension_score{namespace='__all__'} = resource_efficiency 35.1, carbon_efficiency 95.8, waste_elimination 73.3, node_efficiency 50, scaling_practices 50, carbon_aware_scheduling 100, stability 100; code path read end to end; grep shows no other caller of SustainabilityScorer.

**Fix.** Either remove the score from the default dashboard until it is defined on real inputs, or compute it in the controller once per hour over a fixed window (for example the last 24 h read from the hourly rollup), with node snapshots and HPA targets passed in, and store the result (overall, dimensions, window, inputs available) in a table that the API exposes. A dimension without its input must be published as absent (no series) and its weight redistributed, never as 50. Express the waste thresholds as rates (watts, dollars per hour). Use restarts inside the window, not the lifetime counter. Do not cap usage/request at 1 without also penalising pods above their request, and include pods without requests in a 'governance' dimension. With no metrics publish no series. Align docs/sustainability-score.md with the code.

**Acceptance.** Unit tests: the same 24 h of data scored at 30 s and 5 min steps gives the same score (+-1); a cluster with nodes at 10% utilisation scores lower on node_efficiency than one at 60%; with node data missing the dimension is absent and the response lists it under `missing_inputs`; an empty database yields no greenkube_sustainability_score series.

#### API-07 · GET /api/v1/metrics pages with LIMIT/OFFSET ordered by timestamp only, and its cursor mode reads a different data set (raw table only) that no response ever hands a cursor for.

**Severity** medium · **Kind** bug · **Confidence** confirmed-sim · **Effort** M

**Where.** `src/greenkube/api/routers/metrics.py:82-119`; `src/greenkube/storage/postgres/repository.py:1506-1512`; `src/greenkube/storage/postgres/repository.py:1196-1257`; `src/greenkube/storage/sqlite/repository.py:609-690`; `src/greenkube/storage/base_repository.py:366-391`

**Mechanism.** Offset mode: `read_combined_metrics_page` runs `SELECT * FROM (raw UNION ALL hourly) ORDER BY timestamp LIMIT $n OFFSET $m`. All pods of a step share one timestamp, so the order inside a step is not defined; PostgreSQL may return ties in a different order for each page query, and rows inserted or compressed between two page requests shift the offsets. A client that pages through and sums gets duplicated and missing pod rows. The SQLite backend does not override the method, so it loads the whole range in memory for every page (the log says 'using Python fallback; override in SQL backend to avoid OOM'). Cursor mode: only entered when the client sends `cursor`, but the offset response never returns `next_cursor`, so the first cursor cannot be obtained from the API. If used, `read_combined_metrics_cursor` reads `combined_metrics` only: `total` and the rows cover the raw table (raw retention, 7 days by default) instead of the raw + hourly union of offset mode, and anything older than raw retention is silently absent while `total` looks complete. The page size is also silently capped to 1000 in cursor mode although `limit` accepts 10000.

**Effect on numbers.** Simulation (20 days of data, last=10d): offset mode total=1004 rows starting 2026-11-05 (hourly rows + last 24 h raw), cursor mode total=4044 rows starting 2026-11-08 (raw rows only, nothing before raw retention). A client summing cost over the cursor pages gets 7 days of a 10-day range (-30%) with HTTP 200.

**Affected environments.** All installs; API consumers and integrations that list raw metrics.

**Evidence.** audit-workspace/audit/work/api-read-path/sim_report.py output section [3]; code read end to end for both backends.

**Fix.** One pagination mode: keyset on (timestamp, namespace, pod_name) over the same raw + hourly union used by the aggregates. The first page (no cursor) must return `next_cursor`; `offset` stays accepted for compatibility but uses the same ordered query `ORDER BY timestamp, namespace, pod_name`. Implement the SQL page for SQLite. `total` must be computed on the same union as the rows. Reject `limit` above the real maximum with 422 instead of capping silently. Add a `resolution` field per row (raw step or 1h) so a client knows what a row represents.

**Acceptance.** Seed 3 pods x 10 days, compress; page through /metrics?last=10d&limit=100 following next_cursor: the union of pages has no duplicate (timestamp, namespace, pod_name), its length equals `total`, and the sum of total_cost equals /metrics/summary?last=10d for the same instant (frozen clock); same result on PostgreSQL and SQLite.

#### API-08 · When no metric row is younger than 20 minutes, /prometheus/metrics publishes cluster cost, CO2e, energy and pod count as 0 and a sustainability score of 50 instead of dropping the series; when a refresh step fails, the previous values are served again with HTTP 200.

**Severity** medium · **Kind** reliability · **Confidence** confirmed-sim · **Effort** S

**Where.** `src/greenkube/api/metrics_endpoint.py:808-821`; `src/greenkube/api/metrics_endpoint.py:1250-1277`; `src/greenkube/api/metrics_endpoint.py:1279-1354`; `src/greenkube/core/config.py:907-921`

**Mechanism.** `refresh_metrics_from_db` reads the latest row per pod in `[now - 20 min, now)`. If the result is empty, `update_cluster_metrics([])` sets `greenkube_cluster_*_total`, `greenkube_cluster_pod_count`, `greenkube_cluster_namespace_count`, `greenkube_metrics_total` and `greenkube_carbon_intensity_score` to 0 and `greenkube_sustainability_score` to 50, and leaves `greenkube_last_collection_timestamp_seconds` at its old value. This happens whenever collection is late by 20 minutes (controller down, Prometheus down, a catch-up after an outage) and permanently between collections when PROMETHEUS_QUERY_RANGE_STEP is 30m or 1h (allowed: any divisor of 24 h). In the other direction every block is wrapped in `except Exception: logger.warning(...)`: when the database read fails the gauges keep the values of the last successful scrape and the endpoint still answers 200, so a broken API replica exports frozen numbers indefinitely.

**Effect on numbers.** Simulation: with the last row 2 minutes old the scrape gives cluster cost 0.5, CO2e 2.0, pods 1, score 41.0; with the last row 25 minutes old it gives cost 0.0, CO2e 0.0, pods 0, score 50.0 while last_collection_timestamp is unchanged. Any Grafana panel or alert on these series reads an outage of GreenKube as a cluster that costs nothing.

**Affected environments.** All installs that scrape the API; systematic with a collection step of 30 minutes or more.

**Evidence.** audit-workspace/audit/work/api-read-path/sim_exposition.py (real metrics_endpoint code, fake repositories), first two output lines.

**Fix.** Never publish a value that was not read. (1) Select the rows of the latest completely collected step (collection watermark) instead of a fixed 20-minute window; if that step is older than max(3 x step, 15 min), clear the pod, namespace, cluster and score gauges (no series) and keep only `greenkube_last_collection_timestamp_seconds` and a new `greenkube_collection_lag_seconds`. (2) When a refresh block raises, clear the gauges it owns and increment `greenkube_exposition_refresh_errors_total{block=...}`; return 503 when the cluster block cannot be read. (3) Ship an alert rule in the chart on collection lag.

**Acceptance.** Test with fake repositories: last row 25 minutes old and step 5m -> the output contains no greenkube_cluster_*, greenkube_pod_* or greenkube_sustainability_score sample and contains greenkube_collection_lag_seconds >= 1500; step 1h and last row 40 minutes old -> the values of that row are still exposed; a repository that raises -> HTTP 503 or the affected families absent, never the previous values.

#### API-09 · The exposition is rebuilt inside each scrape by clearing shared gauges and refilling them across awaits, so overlapping scrapes (an HA Prometheus pair) or a UI request to /metrics/dashboard-summary return a partial set of series.

**Severity** medium · **Kind** bug · **Confidence** confirmed-sim · **Effort** M

**Where.** `src/greenkube/api/metrics_endpoint.py:1344-1354`; `src/greenkube/api/metrics_endpoint.py:1318-1331`; `src/greenkube/api/metrics_endpoint.py:672-689`; `src/greenkube/api/routers/dashboard.py:49-51`; `src/greenkube/api/routers/dashboard.py:121-125`; `src/greenkube/api/app.py:432-454`

**Mechanism.** All gauges live in one process-wide registry. `refresh_metrics_from_db` does `update_dashboard_summary_metrics(rows, reset=True)` (clears every namespace) and then awaits `summary_repo.get_rows(namespace)` once per namespace before setting that namespace; the savings block does `clear_dashboard_savings_metrics()` and then awaits one ledger query per window. Any other coroutine that runs during those awaits sees or causes a partial registry: a second scrape clears what the first one filled and the first one then serialises the registry; `GET /api/v1/metrics/dashboard-summary` without a namespace also calls `update_dashboard_summary_metrics(rows, reset=True)` from the request handler, and the refresh POST does it from a background task. The savings TTL does not protect: `_mark_savings_refreshed` is only called after the fill. The refresh also runs N+7 sequential queries on every 30-second scrape with a 10-second scrape timeout.

**Effect on numbers.** Simulation with 20 namespaces and 5 ms per query: two overlapping scrapes -> the worse output holds the 24h summary series of 5 namespaces out of 20; one UI summary request during a scrape -> 12 of 20 namespaces, and the Grafana expression `sum(max by (cluster, namespace)(greenkube_dashboard_summary_cost_dollars_total{namespace!='__all__'}))` gives 120 instead of 200 (-40%) for that scrape. The result is intermittent dips and gaps in the Grafana totals.

**Affected environments.** Installs scraped by more than one Prometheus (kube-prometheus default is 2 replicas), or scraped while the UI is in use; worse with many namespaces or a slow database.

**Evidence.** audit-workspace/audit/work/api-read-path/sim_exposition.py, last two output lines.

**Fix.** Build each scrape from an immutable snapshot. Replace the module-level Gauge objects by a custom collector whose `collect()` yields metric families from a snapshot object; `refresh_metrics_from_db` builds a new snapshot in local variables and swaps the reference in one assignment at the end. Guard the refresh with an asyncio.Lock and a short TTL (for example 15 s) so concurrent scrapes share one refresh. Remove the gauge writes from the API handlers in routers/dashboard.py (reading the summary must not mutate the exposition). Read all namespaces' summary rows in one query.

**Acceptance.** Concurrency test: 10 overlapping calls of the scrape handler plus 10 calls of GET /metrics/dashboard-summary with a repository that sleeps 5 ms per query: every scrape output contains all 20 namespaces and all 7 windows; the number of summary queries per scrape is 1; two scrapes inside the TTL trigger one refresh.

#### API-10 · pod_count is the number of distinct pod names seen in the whole window, but the UI shows it as 'Active Pods' and divides the footprint by it for 'Avg CO2/Pod'.

**Severity** medium · **Kind** ux · **Confidence** confirmed-live · **Effort** S

**Where.** `src/greenkube/storage/postgres/repository.py:785-786`; `src/greenkube/core/summary_refresher.py:171-172`; `frontend/src/routes/footprint/+page.svelte:180`; `frontend/src/routes/footprint/+page.svelte:203-205`; `src/greenkube/api/routers/report.py:141-142`; `src/greenkube/api/routers/metrics.py:172-173`

**Mechanism.** `aggregate_summary` returns `COUNT(DISTINCT pod_name)` over all rows of the window (and ignores the namespace, see STO-13). Every pod that existed at any time in the window counts once: each rollout, job run or restart of a ReplicaSet adds names. The Footprint page shows the value under 'Active Pods' and computes 'Avg CO2/Pod (Scope 2+3)' as total / pod_count, which mixes a cumulative head count with a cumulative amount. For multi-year reports `_aggregate_summary_for_ranges` takes the maximum of the per-range counts ('slightly conservative'), which is neither a union nor a current count. /metrics/timeseries returns `pod_count: 0` and `namespace_count: 0` for every bucket (hard-coded, 'not aggregated at timeseries level') although the schema describes them as 'Number of unique pods in this bucket'.

**Effect on numbers.** Live minikube: the 24h card says 89 'Active Pods' while 59 pods are running (+51%); live kind: 48 versus 34 (+41%). With a daily deployment of 100 pods the 30d card shows about 3,000 'active' pods and an average footprint per pod 30 times too low. Timeseries consumers read 0 pods in every bucket.

**Affected environments.** All installs; grows with pod churn and window length.

**Evidence.** Live: GET /api/v1/metrics/dashboard-summary windows.24h.pod_count = 89 (minikube) and 48 (kind); `count(kube_pod_status_phase{phase='Running'} == 1)` = 59 and 34; rows of the last 90 seconds: 59 and 34 distinct pods.

**Fix.** Return two explicit figures from aggregate_summary: `active_pods` (distinct (namespace, pod_name) in the latest collected step) and `pods_seen` (distinct (namespace, pod_name) over the window), plus `pod_hours` (sum of duration_seconds / 3600). Store them in metrics_summary (migration: add columns; keep pod_count as a deprecated alias of pods_seen). The UI card 'Active Pods' uses active_pods; replace 'Avg CO2/Pod' by CO2e per pod-hour. For several ranges compute the union in SQL. In TimeseriesPoint either compute the per-bucket counts or remove the two fields.

**Acceptance.** Seed 10 pods replaced every day for 7 days (70 names, 10 alive at any time): /metrics/summary?last=7d returns active_pods 10, pods_seen 70, pod_hours 1680; the UI shows 10 active pods; two namespaces with a pod of the same name count 2.

#### API-11 · The Footprint page mixes KPI cards read from an hourly cache with breakdowns computed live, and the cache keeps rows that are no longer refreshed, so figures shown together cover different periods.

**Severity** medium · **Kind** data-integrity · **Confidence** confirmed-live · **Effort** M

**Where.** `frontend/src/routes/footprint/+page.svelte:27`; `frontend/src/routes/footprint/+page.svelte:72-92`; `src/greenkube/core/summary_refresher.py:96-117`; `src/greenkube/storage/postgres/timeseries_cache_repository.py:22-25`; `src/greenkube/storage/postgres/summary_repository.py:91-135`; `src/greenkube/api/metrics_endpoint.py:672-689`; `src/greenkube/cli/start.py:796-802`

**Mechanism.** For the 24h, 7d, 30d, 1y and YTD ranges the cards and the chart come from `metrics_summary` / `metrics_timeseries_cache`, refreshed once per hour by the controller with `end = refresh time`; the namespace donut and the top-pods chart on the same page call /metrics/by-namespace and /metrics/top-pods with `end = now`. The two halves of the page therefore describe windows shifted by up to one hour (the page prints 'Summary last refreshed ...' but not that the breakdown is from another period). Stale entries (see also ROL-13): `upsert_points` returns without deleting when the new point list is empty, so a namespace or window that has no data any more keeps its old buckets; `metrics_summary` rows of a namespace that left `namespace_cache` are never deleted nor refreshed and `get_rows(namespace=X)` still serves them; a new namespace gets no row until the next hourly compression has added it to `namespace_cache` and the next hourly refresh has run. The `greenkube_dashboard_summary_*` Prometheus series carry no refresh timestamp, so Grafana cannot tell a one-hour-old cache from a three-day-old one (controller down).

**Effect on numbers.** Live minikube at 10:36:58: 24h card total_cost 0.8668 (refreshed 10:22:11) versus 0.8996 as the sum of the namespace breakdown on the same page (+3.8%); live kind: 0.5664 versus 0.5950 (+5.0%). On a steady cluster the 24h figures differ by up to 4%; a selected namespace that stopped running keeps showing its last cached chart indefinitely.

**Affected environments.** All installs.

**Evidence.** Live GET /api/v1/metrics/dashboard-summary, /metrics/by-namespace?last=24h and /metrics/summary?last=24h issued in the same second on both installs; code read for the cache repositories and the refresher.

**Fix.** One time base per page: store `window_start` and `window_end` in metrics_summary and return them; the UI passes the cached `window_end` as `end` to /metrics/by-namespace and /metrics/top-pods (add `start`/`end` parameters to these endpoints), or all four blocks are served by one endpoint that reads one snapshot. In `upsert_points` always delete the (window, namespace) rows, inside one transaction with the inserts, and add `delete_missing(namespaces)` to both cache repositories so the refresher removes rows of namespaces it no longer computes. Refresh the cache at the end of each collection cycle (it is seven aggregates per namespace) instead of hourly. Expose `greenkube_dashboard_summary_updated_timestamp_seconds{window,namespace}` and return `stale: true` from the API when updated_at is older than twice the refresh interval.

**Acceptance.** With a frozen clock and a refresh 40 minutes old, the sum of the namespace breakdown equals the card total exactly; after a namespace stops producing rows for longer than a window, its cached chart for that window is empty and its summary row is zero or absent; the Prometheus output contains the refresh timestamp series.

#### API-12 · On clusters without a node autoscaler every rightsizing recommendation has potential_savings_cost = 0 and potential_savings_co2e_grams = 0, so /recommendations/top (default metrics), the Prometheus savings gauges and the Grafana 'Top Actionable Recommendations' table are empty or zero while dozens of recommendations are active.

**Severity** medium · **Kind** portability · **Confidence** confirmed-live · **Effort** M

**Where.** `src/greenkube/core/recommendation_ranking.py:61-68`; `src/greenkube/core/recommendation_ranking.py:144`; `src/greenkube/impact/realization.py:55`; `src/greenkube/api/routers/recommendations.py:265-273`; `src/greenkube/api/metrics_endpoint.py:1059-1100`; `frontend/src/routes/+page.svelte:179-182`

**Mechanism.** The stored `potential_savings_*` columns hold the expected billable saving = allocated saving x realization factor, and the factor is `FACTOR_NO_AUTOSCALER = 0.0` on fixed-node clusters (minikube, kind, kubeadm, k3s, bare metal, any cloud node group without Cluster Autoscaler or Karpenter). The allocated projection is only inside the `impact` JSON (`allocated_cost_annual`, `co2e_allocated_annual_g`). `rank_recommendations` with metric co2 or cost keeps only `_sort_value(rec, metric) > 0`, the exposition sums `potential_savings_cost` into `greenkube_recommendations_savings_cost_dollars` and `greenkube_namespace_recommendation_savings_*`, and the overview page prints 'Projected {potential_savings_cost}/yr'. The API has no top-level field for the allocated saving, which is the quantity the Prove step later measures and reports ('measured_allocated').

**Effect on numbers.** Live minikube: 80 active recommendations, 58 rightsizing ones with sum(impact.allocated_cost_annual) = 228.7 per year and sum(potential_savings_cost) = 0; GET /api/v1/recommendations/top?metric=cost returns []. Live kind: 30 rightsizing recommendations with 203.5 per year allocated and 0 stored; greenkube_recommendations_savings_cost_dollars = 0 for RIGHTSIZING_CPU and RIGHTSIZING_MEMORY; the top list only holds the orphan PersistentVolume and idle namespaces. The projected column (0) can then never be compared with the measured allocated saving of the same change.

**Affected environments.** Every cluster without Cluster Autoscaler or Karpenter: all on-prem distributions and fixed cloud node pools.

**Evidence.** Live SQL: `select type, status, count(*), sum(potential_savings_cost), sum((impact::jsonb->>'allocated_cost_annual')::float) from recommendation_history group by 1,2`; live GET /api/v1/recommendations/top?limit=3&metric=cost -> [] (minikube); live kind Prometheus greenkube_recommendations_savings_cost_dollars.

**Fix.** Expose both quantities under explicit names everywhere: `allocated_savings_cost_annual` / `allocated_savings_co2e_grams_annual` and `expected_billable_savings_*` with `realization_factor`, as top-level fields of RecommendationRecord, TopRecommendation and RecommendationV2 (filled from `impact`; add stored columns and a backfill from the impact JSON for existing rows). `rank_recommendations` for metric co2/cost ranks by the allocated saving when the billable one is 0 and never filters a recommendation out because its saving is 0 (sort it last). Prometheus: add `basis="allocated"|"billable"` to the savings gauges. UI: show 'Frees {allocated}/yr of allocated capacity; expected on the bill: {billable}/yr (no autoscaler)'.

**Acceptance.** On a fixture without autoscaler and three rightsizing recommendations of allocated 12, 5 and 2 per year: /recommendations/top?metric=cost returns the three in that order with allocated 12/5/2 and billable 0; the Prometheus output has greenkube_recommendations_savings_cost_dollars{basis='allocated'} = 19 and {basis='billable'} = 0; the UI shows both figures.

#### API-13 · Read endpoints carry no coverage or freshness information: no collected-until time, no hole indicator, no estimated share, partial first and last buckets, and health and setup checks stay 'ok' when collection has stopped.

**Severity** medium · **Kind** design · **Confidence** confirmed-code · **Effort** L

**Where.** `src/greenkube/api/schemas.py:337-368`; `src/greenkube/api/schemas.py:406-442`; `src/greenkube/api/routers/metrics.py:122-176`; `src/greenkube/api/routers/config.py:21-55`; `src/greenkube/api/routers/setup.py:143-148`; `src/greenkube/api/routers/setup.py:570-640`; `src/greenkube/storage/postgres/repository.py:1038-1040`

**Mechanism.** MetricsSummaryResponse, TimeseriesPoint, NamespaceBreakdownItem, TopPodItem and ReportSummaryResponse contain amounts only. A request for last=7d on an install that has 13 hours of data, or that lost a day of collection, returns a plausible total with nothing that says which part of the window is covered. Buckets without rows are omitted from /metrics/timeseries (live minikube: 11 hourly buckets for a 14-hour span) and the first and last buckets are partial without a flag, so charts dip at both ends. Rows flagged `is_estimated`, or priced by the default rate (`cost_source`), or computed with the default intensity (`intensity_source`) are summed with measured rows and the share is not returned (COST-08 covers cost). No route returns the collection watermark. GET /api/v1/health only checks the schema and an in-process loop; the setup 'Evidence warm-up' check uses the earliest input time only (`earliest + required <= now` -> 'ok'), so it stays green after collection stops. `list_namespaces` returns [] with HTTP 200 on a database error. `last=5m` is parsed as 5 months (see the low finding on durations).

**Effect on numbers.** A total that covers 13 hours is displayed as the 7d, 30d, 1y and YTD figure (live: all four windows equal the 24h value); a window with a 6-hour collection hole reads 25% low for 24h with no indication; there is no way for a consumer to compute a correct rate (per hour, per day) from a total because the covered duration is unknown.

**Affected environments.** All installs; most visible on new installs, after outages, and where OpenCost or Electricity Maps is absent.

**Evidence.** Live: metrics_summary rows 24h, 7d, 30d, 1y, ytd identical on both installs; dashboard-timeseries/24h has 11 points on minikube for 21:00-10:00; schemas and routers read in full.

**Fix.** Add a `coverage` object to every aggregate response and to the cached rows: `requested_start`, `requested_end`, `data_start`, `data_end` (collection watermark), `covered_seconds` (sum of distinct collected steps), `expected_seconds`, `coverage_ratio`, `estimated_cost_ratio`, `estimated_intensity_ratio`, `default_power_profile_ratio` (cost- and energy-weighted shares by provenance). Zero-fill missing buckets with `complete: false` and mark partial edge buckets. UI: show 'based on N hours of data' when coverage_ratio < 0.95 and a provenance badge. Add GET /api/v1/metrics/freshness (watermark, lag, last hole) and include collection lag in /api/v1/health/services and in the setup evidence check (latest input time, not only earliest). `list_namespaces` must raise on a database error.

**Acceptance.** Install with 13 h of data: /metrics/summary?last=7d returns coverage_ratio = 13/168 +- 0.01 and the UI shows the notice; a seeded 6-hour hole yields coverage_ratio 0.75 for 24h and six buckets with complete=false and value 0; with collection stopped for 1 hour the setup evidence check and /health/services report a collection lag.

#### API-14 · The duration suffix 'm' means months: last=5m returns 150 days of data, and the parser's own docstring presents '5m' as a dashboard duration.

**Severity** low · **Kind** ux · **Confidence** confirmed-live · **Effort** S

**Where.** `src/greenkube/utils/time.py:51-66`; `src/greenkube/api/routers/metrics.py:61`

**Mechanism.** `parse_duration` accepts `(\d+)(min|[hdwmy])` and maps `m` to `timedelta(days=amount * 30)` and `y` to 365 days. The docstring says 'Parse dashboard durations such as 5m', and Prometheus, Grafana and Kubernetes users read 5m as five minutes. The API accepts it silently and the response does not echo the resolved window.

**Effect on numbers.** Live: GET /api/v1/metrics/summary?last=5m returns total_cost 0.8997 and 89 pods (all stored data) while last=10min returns 0.0204 and 59 pods: a factor 44 for a client that meant five minutes.

**Affected environments.** All API clients and scripts.

**Evidence.** Live calls on minikube and kind (last=5m equals the all-time total).

**Fix.** Accept `m` as minutes (and keep `min`), use `mo` for months, or reject bare `m` with a 400 that lists the valid units; return `window_start` and `window_end` in every aggregate response. Update docs/api.md and the frontend range selector if it sends `1m`.

**Acceptance.** last=5m returns the same as last=5min (or 400); last=1mo returns 30 days; responses echo the resolved window.

#### API-15 · Prometheus exposition naming and labels: gauges named *_total, a currency unit hard-coded as dollars, measured values inside label values, rank labels, and 'implemented' counts that include changes that were not realized.

**Severity** low · **Kind** design · **Confidence** confirmed-code · **Effort** M

**Where.** `src/greenkube/api/metrics_endpoint.py:151-210`; `src/greenkube/api/metrics_endpoint.py:242-296`; `src/greenkube/api/metrics_endpoint.py:335-340`; `src/greenkube/api/metrics_endpoint.py:948-968`; `src/greenkube/api/metrics_endpoint.py:407-417`; `src/greenkube/api/metrics_endpoint.py:516-533`; `src/greenkube/storage/postgres/recommendation_repository.py:713-723`; `dashboards/greenkube-grafana.json`

**Mechanism.** (1) `greenkube_*_total` families are Gauges (namespace, cluster, dashboard summary, dashboard savings, recommendations_total, recommendations_implemented_total, cluster_*_saved_*_total, which is an annual rate): promtool and any rate()/increase() user are misled. (2) `greenkube_zone_grid_intensity_gco2_kwh` puts the rounded intensity and the node list in the labels `bubble_label` and `map_label`, so each change of intensity creates a new series per zone (about 24 per day per zone) and range queries return several bubbles for one zone. (3) `greenkube_top_recommendations` exposes every active recommendation four times with a `rank` label that changes whenever the order changes. (4) Pod series carry `node` and `region`, so a pod that moves produces new series while the old ones stay until the next clear. (5) Names end in `_dollars` although the pricing currency is configurable. (6) `greenkube_recommendations_implemented_total` counts status applied, verifying, verified and rollback_review, including inconclusive and not-realized changes, and the 'GreenKube Impact' panel shows it as implemented actions. (7) The bundled dashboard uses `sum by (namespace) (greenkube_namespace_cost_dollars_total)` and `avg by (...)` without de-duplication, so two API replicas or two releases in one Prometheus double the per-namespace panels (live kind: two releases scraped, values summed).

**Effect on numbers.** Series churn of roughly 24 x zones per day for the map gauge and 4 x active recommendations per reorder; per-namespace Grafana panels multiplied by the number of scraped API replicas (2 by default in the documented HA setup).

**Affected environments.** Installs using the ServiceMonitor and the bundled Grafana dashboard.

**Evidence.** Code and dashboard JSON read; live kind: `greenkube_namespace_cost_dollars_total{namespace='kube-system'}` returns two series (two releases) that the panel expression sums.

**Fix.** Rename gauges without `_total` (keep aliases for one release), put the currency in a label of a `greenkube_pricing_info` series and name amounts `_cost`, move display strings out of labels (Grafana can build them from `zone` and the value), drop `rank` in favour of topk() in the query, publish implemented counts by outcome (`outcome=realized|not_realized|inconclusive|pending`), and wrap every dashboard expression in `max by (cluster, ...)` before summing. Add a promtool check metrics step to CI.

**Acceptance.** promtool check metrics passes on the exposition; the dashboard totals are unchanged when a second API replica is scraped; the map gauge has one series per zone over a 24-hour range query.

#### API-16 · CSV/JSON export details: negative numbers are written as text with a leading apostrophe, a failure in the middle of a stream produces a truncated file after HTTP 200, and the UI formats negative CO2e amounts in milligrams.

**Severity** low · **Kind** bug · **Confidence** confirmed-code · **Effort** S

**Where.** `src/greenkube/api/routers/report.py:365-370`; `src/greenkube/api/routers/report.py:276-335`; `frontend/src/lib/utils/format.js:10-15`

**Mechanism.** `_sanitize_cell` prefixes every cell whose text starts with '=', '+', '-' or '@' with an apostrophe, including numeric cells: a negative value (for example a negative cost delta or a negative float after a correction) becomes the string '-0.5 and is no longer summed by a spreadsheet. The export is a StreamingResponse: a database error after the first chunk cannot change the status code, the CSV simply ends (the JSON variant ends without the closing bracket). `formatCO2` tests `grams >= 1000` and `grams >= 1` only, so any negative amount falls to the last branch and is printed as `grams * 1000` mg (-1200 g is shown as '-1200000.0 mg').

**Effect on numbers.** Negative cells drop out of spreadsheet sums; a truncated export under-reports by the missing chunks with no error visible in the file; negative CO2e savings are displayed with a misleading unit.

**Affected environments.** All installs.

**Evidence.** Code read; `formatCO2(-1200)` evaluated by hand from the three branches.

**Fix.** Sanitise only string-typed fields (pod_name, namespace, node, owner_name, labels), never numbers. End the CSV with a trailer line `# rows=<n> sha256=<digest>` (or send the totals in a first comment line) and the JSON with a final metadata object so a truncated file is detectable; alternatively build the file to a temporary object and send it with Content-Length. In format.js use `Math.abs(grams)` for the unit choice.

**Acceptance.** A row with total_cost -0.5 is exported as -0.5; a repository that raises on the second chunk yields a file without the trailer and the client helper reports an incomplete download; formatCO2(-1200) returns '-1.20 kg'.
